# Abductor digiti minimi

*Basic Science · 1 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy — foot ADM (Orthobullets)

| | Abductor digiti minimi (foot) |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | Calcaneal tuberosity |
| **Insertion** | Base of 5th toe |
| **Action** | Abducts small toe |
| **Innervation** | **Lateral plantar nerve** |
| **Arterial supply** | Lateral plantar artery |

![Abductor digiti minimi of the foot](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10094-ll1-moved.jpg)

### Blood supply — hand ADM

**Hand ADM** — hypothenar · abducts 5th digit · **deep branch of ulnar nerve (C8, T1)**

**Main blood supply** — ✓ **ulnar artery** (deep branch) · 1 major + 2 constant minor pedicles · major pedicle leaves ulnar artery just distal to **pisiform**

**Flap use** — ADM can be used as a local flap in crushed hands

![Ulnar artery supplying ADM and FDM (hypothenar excised)](../../MCQ/attachments/q1267-illb-adm-ulnar-artery.jpg)

## X vs Y

| Artery | Supplies |
|---|---|
| **Ulnar artery** | ✓ **Hypothenar muscles (ADM, FDM)** |
| Radial artery | Thenar muscles |
| Posterior interosseous artery | Superficial + deep extensor compartments of forearm |
| Anterior interosseous artery | Forearm flexor compartment |
| Palmar metacarpal artery | Palmar interossei |

| | Hand ADM | Foot ADM |
|---|---|---|
| Nerve | Deep branch **ulnar** | **Lateral plantar** |
| Artery | **Ulnar** | Lateral plantar |

## How it's asked

**"Main blood supply to abductor digiti minimi (hand)?"** → **ulnar artery**\
✗ Wrong options: PIA · AIA · palmar metacarpal · radial

**"Nerve to abductor digiti minimi of the foot?"** → **lateral plantar nerve**

# Adductor longus

*Basic Science · 2 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Adductor longus |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | Anterior body of pubis, just lateral to symphysis |
| **Insertion** | **Middle third of linea aspera** · between adductor magnus/brevis (medial) and vastus medialis origin (lateral) |
| **Action** | Adducts + flexes thigh · helps laterally rotate hip |
| **Innervation** | **Anterior division of obturator nerve (L2, L3, L4)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Obturator artery + medial circumflex femoral artery |

![Adductor longus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10065-adductor-longus.jpg)

### Cross-sectional anatomy — proximal thigh

**Adductor longus** — lies **just deep to the superficial femoral artery**

**Adductor brevis** — between longus and magnus · **deep to the deep femoral vessels**

**Adductor magnus** — largest, most posterior adductor

**Sartorius** — superficial · **covers the superficial femoral vessels**

**Gracilis** — medial + superficial

![Proximal thigh cross-section — identify the arrowed muscle](../../MCQ/attachments/q7426-a-38a.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### L3 radiculopathy vs femoral neuropathy

**Key test** — **hip adduction** (obturator nerve) · weak in **L2/L3 radiculopathy**, spared in **femoral neuropathy (L2–4)**

**Femoral-innervated** — quadriceps · iliacus · sartorius · psoas\
✗ Wrong options: distinguishing

## X vs Y

| Muscle | Nerve | Distinguishes L3 root from femoral nerve? |
|---|---|---|
| **Adductor longus** | **Obturator (ant. division)** | Yes |
| Quadriceps | Femoral | No |
| Iliacus | Femoral | No |
| Sartorius | Femoral | No |
| Psoas | Femoral (listed as such in the OB key) | No |

## How it's asked

**"L3 radiculopathy best differentiated from femoral neuropathy by testing?"** → **adductor longus** (obturator)\
✗ Wrong options: quadriceps · iliacus · sartorius · psoas

**"Muscle just deep to the superficial femoral artery on proximal-thigh cross-section?"** → **adductor longus**\
✗ Wrong options: magnus (large, posterior) · brevis (deep to profunda) · sartorius (covers SFA) · gracilis (medial)

# Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis (ALS)

*Basic Science · 3 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Pathology

**Definition** — **Lou Gehrig's disease** · progressive motor neuron disorder · **degeneration of anterior horn cells** · commonest degenerative disease of the motor neuron system

**LMN component** — loss of motor neurons in **anterior horns** + motor cranial nerve nuclei

**UMN component** — loss of myelinated fibres in **corticospinal + corticobulbar** tracts

**Hallmark** — **abnormal motor conduction, normal sensory conduction**

### Genes & lab markers

**Familial** — ~**5%** · some map to **superoxide dismutase** gene on **chromosome 21** · cause otherwise unknown

**Labs** — **no lab test confirms ALS** · SPEP/immunoelectrophoresis only exclude differentials

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **painless weakness** of one limb → spreads · fasciculations · cramps · dysarthria/dysphagia · ↓ head control · breathing difficulty · urinary frequency/incontinence (late) · **sensation normal**

**Neck ptosis** — neck drop from neck-extensor weakness

| UMN signs | LMN signs |
|---|---|
| Spasticity | Atrophy |
| **Hyperreflexia** | Weakness |
| **(+) Hoffmann** | **Fasciculations** |
| **(+) Babinski** | Clumsiness |
| Spastic dysarthria | ↓ reflexes, ↓ tone |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria

**Clinical** — **UMN + LMN signs in the same limb** · no pain, no sensory symptoms · ± cranial-nerve findings → highly indicative

**Period of observation** — documents progressive loss of UMN + LMN function

**Often misdiagnosed** — cervical myelopathy / radiculopathy

### Electrodiagnostics — definitive test

**Definitive diagnosis** — ✓ **electrodiagnostic studies (NCS + EMG)** · also exclude peripheral mimics

| EMG/NCS finding in ALS | |
|---|---|
| Motor | **↓ CMAP amplitude** widespread · slowed motor CV |
| Denervation | **Fibrillations + positive sharp waves** + ↓ recruitment in **≥3 extremities** |
| Spontaneous | Fibrillation + **fasciculation** potentials |
| Reinnervation | Present (**denervation + reinnervation**) |
| Sensory | **Normal SNAPs** |

**Not definitive** — genetic testing (only some familial types) · MRI brain/cord (rules out structural mimics) · muscle biopsy (not required) · SPEP

## 5 Management

**No cure** — supportive · maintain function + comfort

**Riluzole** — blocks **tetrodotoxin-sensitive Na⁺ channels** in damaged neurons · delays ventilator dependence · prolongs life **2–3 months**

## X vs Y

| | **ALS** | Peripheral compressive neuropathy (CTS/cubital) |
|---|---|---|
| UMN signs (Hoffmann, Babinski, hyperreflexia) | **Present** | Absent |
| Sensory symptoms | **None** | Present |
| SNAPs | **Normal** | Abnormal |
| Distribution | Spreads, multiple limbs | Single nerve territory |

## How it's asked

**"Most useful study for definitive diagnosis of ALS?"** → **electrodiagnostic studies**\
✗ Wrong options: genetic testing · MRI · muscle biopsy · SPEP

**"Hallmark EMG/NCS pattern?"** → **abnormal motor, normal sensory conduction** · denervation + reinnervation

**"Hand weakness + wasting + hyperreflexia/Hoffmann, normal sensation?"** → **ALS**\
✗ Wrong options: carpal/cubital tunnel

**"Riluzole mechanism?"** → **blocks tetrodotoxin-sensitive sodium channels** · +2–3 months survival

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Anterior interosseous nerve

*Basic Science · 4 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Innervated muscles

**AIN muscles** — **FPL** · **FDP index + long** · **pronator quadratus**

**Motor only** — no cutaneous sensation

![AIN branching from the median nerve](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10107-graphic-2-large.jpg)

### Gantzer muscle (TO)

**Gantzer muscle** — ✓ **accessory head of FPL** (or of FDP) · anatomical variant in up to **70%** **(TO)**

**Origin** — deep surface of FDS, coronoid process or medial epicondyle **(TO)**

**Insertion** — FPL or FDP **(TO)**

**Innervation** — exclusively **AIN** **(TO)**

**Traps** — ✗ Wrong options: accessory popliteus · accessory biceps femoris · accessory rectus femoris **(TO)**

## 3 Presentation

**Supracondylar humerus fracture (extension type)** — **AIN = commonest nerve injury** in children

**Muscle affected** — ✓ **pronator quadratus** (most common electrophysiological abnormality) · also FPL, FDP index/long

**Often misdiagnosed** — as tendon injury (only 3 of 13 correct initially in Seror's series)

![Inability to make an "OK" sign — AIN lesion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10107-anterior-interosseous-lesion1345527186191.jpg)

### Book test (TO, FNB 2024)

**"Book test assesses?"** — answer key not provided by source · options: adductor pollicis, opponens pollicis, FPL, dorsal interossei · module files it under **FPL** (AIN muscle) — read the options

## X vs Y

| Muscle | Nerve | Lost in AIN palsy? |
|---|---|---|
| **Pronator quadratus** | **AIN** | Yes |
| FPL | AIN | Yes |
| FDP index/long | AIN | Yes |
| FDP ring/small | Ulnar | No |
| FCR | Median (main trunk) | No |
| FCU | Ulnar | No |
| EDC | PIN | No |

## How it's asked

**"Extension supracondylar fracture, 4-yr-old, nerve deficit — muscle most likely affected?"** → **pronator quadratus** (AIN)\
✗ Wrong options: EDC · FCR · FDP ring/small · FCU

**"True about Gantzer muscle (TO)?"** → **accessory head of FPL** · AIN-innervated\
✗ Wrong options: popliteus · biceps femoris · rectus femoris

**"Book test (FNB, TO)?"** → answer key not provided · module = **FPL**

# Anti-inflammatory Medications

*Basic Science · 5 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### NSAID mechanism (COX-1, COX-2)

**NSAID effects** — anti-inflammatory · antipyretic · analgesic · **antiplatelet**

**Arachidonic acid** — directly metabolised by ✓ **cyclooxygenase** → prostaglandins, prostacyclin, **thromboxane**

**5-lipoxygenase** — arachidonic acid → **leukotrienes**\
✗ Wrong options: "1-lipoxygenase"

**HMG-CoA reductase** — mevalonate / cholesterol pathway (statins) · not arachidonic acid

**Steroids** — inhibit ✓ **phospholipase A2** → no arachidonic acid from membrane phospholipids · earliest step of cascade

![Arachidonic acid metabolism — steroids block PLA2, NSAIDs block COX](../../MCQ/attachments/q867-illa-aa-metabolism.jpg)

| Enzyme | Role |
|---|---|
| **COX-1** | Constitutive · **gastric mucosal protection (PGE2)** · renal blood flow · **platelet TXA2** |
| **COX-2** | Inducible · **inflammation, pain, fever** · no platelet effect · **needed for endochondral ossification** |

### Drug classes

| Drug | Binding | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **Aspirin** | **Irreversible** · acetylates **serine 530** of COX | Blocks **TXA2** → antiplatelet · effect >1 wk (platelet life) |
| Ibuprofen | **Reversible**, competitive · non-selective COX-1 + COX-2 | GI ulcer, ↓ TX + ↓ PG |
| Indomethacin, naproxen, diclofenac, meloxicam | **Reversible** COX-1 | Indomethacin = **HO prophylaxis** · **highest GI toxicity** |
| Ketorolac (Toradol) | COX-1 selective (OB) | — |
| **Celecoxib**, rofecoxib | **COX-2 selective** | Spare platelets + gastric mucosa · **cardiac toxicity** |

### Aspirin — dose-dependent action

| Dose | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Low 75–81 mg/day** | Irreversibly acetylates COX-1 → ↓ platelet TXA2 → **antithrombotic** |
| **Intermediate 650 mg–4 g/day** | Inhibits COX-1 + COX-2 → ↓ PG → **analgesic, antipyretic** |

## 5 Management

**Indications** — pain · **heterotopic ossification prophylaxis**

**Contraindications** — severe renal disease · gastric ulcers

**COX-2 selective** — anti-inflammatory without COX-1 loss (gastric mucosa, renal blood flow, platelets) · usable **perioperatively** (no platelet effect) · **no more effective for OA** than non-selective

**Perioperative NSAID (OB)** — dose- and time-dependent effect on bone healing → **lowest effective dose, ≤7 days postop**

### Corticosteroids

**Systemic GC bone loss** — adverse skeletal effects **within 3 months** · at doses **as low as 2.5–5.0 mg/day** prednisone-equivalent · dose-dependent

**High fracture risk on long-term oral GC** — ✓ give **bisphosphonate** (antiresorptive) + calcium + vitamin D

**After stopping GC** — fracture risk normalises unless previously high-dose

**Epidural steroid injection** — ✓ **↓ lumbar BMD** (esp. postmenopausal women) · knee/other large-joint injections do not ↓ lumbar BMD

**Other GC effects** — transient **hyperglycaemia** (caution in diabetics) · interfere with RANKL pathway

## 6 Complications

### NSAID side effects & fracture healing

**Most strongly associated** — ✓ **renal impairment** (all NSAIDs, incl. COX-2)\
✗ Wrong options: hepatic · QTc · seizures · haematuria

**GI** — dyspepsia · ulcer perforation/bleeding/obstruction **2–4%** of chronic users · elderly **4–5×** risk of death from GI haemorrhage

**GI mechanism** — **non-specific COX-1 + COX-2 inhibition → ↓ thromboxane + ↓ prostaglandin** → uncoupled mitochondrial oxidative phosphorylation, ↑ permeability, **disruption of intestinal phospholipid bilayer**

**GI risk factors** — **concurrent anticoagulant (most important)** · age >60 · prior GI disorder

**Other** — **↑ bleeding time** · **↑ creatinine** · **tinnitus**/sensorineural hearing loss (reversible) · cardiac toxicity · NOT ↑ IL-6 (NSAIDs ↓ IL-6)

**Indomethacin for HO** — most likely side effect = ✓ **GI ulceration**

![Peptic ulcer from NSAID use](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9060-ulcer.jpg)

### Fracture healing

**Mechanism** — ↓ COX-2 → **↓ PGE2** → ↓ mesenchymal stem cell → osteoblast/chondrocyte differentiation (PGE2 acts via RANKL/OPG balance)

**Effect** — ✓ **↓ endochondral ossification** → ↓ callus → delayed union/nonunion

**Which healing** — **secondary (callus) > primary**

**Risk ↑** — prolonged use · large doses · **adults** · no effect shown in **children**

**COX-2** — thought **necessary for normal endochondral ossification** (animal data) · not "definitively shown in human studies"

### Intra-articular corticosteroid injection complications

**Described** — local flare · fat atrophy · skin pigmentation (depigmentation) · facial flushing · injection-site pain, bleeding

**Not described** — **apoptosis of myocytes**

## X vs Y

| | Steroids | NSAIDs |
|---|---|---|
| Enzyme inhibited | **Phospholipase A2** | **COX-1/COX-2** |
| Level in cascade | Earliest (no arachidonic acid) | Arachidonic acid → prostanoids |
| Leukotrienes | ↓ | Unaffected (5-LOX intact) |

| | COX-1 | COX-2 |
|---|---|---|
| Expression | Constitutive | Inducible (inflammation) |
| Function | Gastric protection, platelets, renal flow | Pain, fever, inflammation, **endochondral bone** |
| Selective drug | Ketorolac (OB) | **Celecoxib** |
| Inhibiting it causes | GI ulcer, bleeding | ↓ fracture healing, cardiac events |

## How it's asked

**"Arachidonic acid is directly metabolised by?"** → **cyclooxygenase**\
✗ Wrong options: 1-lipoxygenase · HMG-CoA reductase · carbonic anhydrase · thromboxane synthetase

**"All bind COX-1 reversibly EXCEPT?"** → **aspirin** (irreversible)\
✗ Wrong options: meloxicam, diclofenac, indomethacin, naproxen

**"Mechanism of aspirin?"** → **binding of COX-1 and COX-2**\
✗ Wrong options: vitamin K carboxylation · Xa via antithrombin · direct thrombin inhibition

**"Chronic ibuprofen → GI ulcer mechanism?"** → **non-specific COX-1 + COX-2 inhibition → ↓ thromboxane + ↓ PG → phospholipid bilayer disruption**

**"Indomethacin for HO prophylaxis — likely side effect?"** → **GI ulceration**

**"Side effect most strongly associated with NSAIDs?"** → **renal impairment**

**"NOT a side effect of prolonged NSAIDs?"** → **↑ circulating IL-6**\
✗ Wrong options: ulcer · ↑ creatinine · ↑ bleeding time · tinnitus

**"True about COX-2?"** → **necessary for normal endochondral ossification**\
✗ Wrong options: constitutive form · more gastric irritation · proven in humans

**"True about NSAIDs in femoral nailing?"** → **prolonged use ↓ endochondral ossification**\
✗ Wrong options: inhibits PLA2 · COX-1 selectivity ↓ GI effects · adults lower risk than children · primary > secondary healing

**"How do NSAIDs impair bone healing?"** → **↓ PGE2 and COX-mediated osteoblastic MSC differentiation**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ osteoclastogenesis · ↑ COX-2

**"Corticosteroids inhibit?"** → **phospholipase A2**\
✗ Wrong options: lipoxygenase · COX-1 · TXA2 · prostacyclin

**"NOT a complication of corticosteroid injection?"** → **apoptosis of myocytes**

**"Most accurate about corticosteroid complications?"** → **epidural steroid injection ↓ lumbar BMD**\
✗ Wrong options: no effect <1 yr · bisphosphonate contraindicated · 2.5–5 mg safe · knee injection ↓ lumbar BMD

# Antibiotic Classification & Mechanism

*Basic Science · 6 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Mechanism of action by class

**Five basic mechanisms** — cell-wall synthesis · membrane permeability · ribosomal (protein) inhibition · DNA metabolism · antimetabolite (folate)

![Antibiotic mechanisms of action](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9059-antibiotics-mechanisms-of-action.jpg)

| Target | Drugs | Cidal / static |
|---|---|---|
| **Cell wall** (transpeptidase / PBP) | **Penicillins, cephalosporins**, carbapenems (imipenem), aztreonam, β-lactamase inhibitors | Bactericidal |
| **Cell wall — D-Ala-D-Ala** | **Vancomycin** (binds D-Ala-D-Ala terminal → blocks transpeptidation, peptidoglycan cross-linking) | Bactericidal |
| Membrane permeability | Bacitracin · polymyxin | — |
| Membrane depolarisation | **Daptomycin** | Bactericidal |
| Fungal membrane sterols | **Amphotericin, nystatin** — bind sterols → disrupt membrane | — |
| **30S ribosome** | **Aminoglycosides** (gentamicin, tobramycin — **irreversible**) · **tetracyclines** (doxycycline — block tRNA binding) | AG cidal · tetracycline static |
| **50S ribosome** | **Macrolides** (erythromycin — reversible, blocks aminoacyl translocation) · **clindamycin** · chloramphenicol (peptidyl transferase) · streptogramins | Static |
| **50S 23S rRNA — initiation complex** | **Linezolid** (oxazolidinone) — prevents 30S–50S (70S) initiation complex | Static vs staph/enterococci · cidal vs strep |
| **DNA gyrase** (type II topoisomerase) | **Fluoroquinolones** (cipro, levo, moxi) | Bactericidal |
| DNA (metabolic by-products) | Metronidazole | Bactericidal |
| **RNA polymerase** (DNA-dependent) | **Rifampin** | Bactericidal |
| Mycolic acid | Isoniazid | — |
| **Folic acid** (PABA mimic) | **TMP/SMX**, sulfonamides | Static |

### Classification & indications

| Class | Drugs | Drug of choice / coverage | Toxicity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Natural penicillin | Penicillin G, V | **Strep pyogenes (A), Strep agalactiae (B), C. perfringens** | Hypersensitivity, haemolytic anaemia |
| Aminopenicillins | Ampicillin, amoxicillin | + **E. coli**, E. faecalis | As above |
| Penicillinase-resistant | Methicillin, **nafcillin, oxacillin**, cloxacillin | + PCNase-producing **S. aureus** | + **Interstitial nephritis** |
| Antipseudomonal penicillins | Piperacillin, ticarcillin | + **Pseudomonas** | — |
| **1st-gen cephalosporin** | **Cefazolin**, cephalexin | **S. aureus, S. epidermidis**, some GN (E. coli, Klebsiella) | Allergy · **Coombs + anaemia (3%)** |
| 2nd-gen | Cefoxitin, cefaclor, cefuroxime | + GN | Disulfiram reaction |
| 3rd-gen (4th = cefepime) | Ceftriaxone, ceftazidime | + GN, **Pseudomonas** | Disulfiram reaction |
| **Vancomycin** | — | **MRSA**, PCN/ceph allergy, S. epidermidis | **Red man syndrome**, nephro-, ototoxicity |
| Carbapenems | Imipenem (+ cilastatin), meropenem | **Broadest spectrum** (✗ Wrong options: MRSA, Mycoplasma) | — |
| Aztreonam | — | GN rods, aerobes, hospital-acquired | — |

| Class (non-β-lactam) | Drugs | Drug of choice / coverage | Toxicity |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Aminoglycosides** | Gentamicin, tobramycin, amikacin | **Aerobic GN**, Pseudomonas · with cephalosporin in **open fractures** | **Nephrotoxicity, ototoxicity** |
| Tetracyclines | Doxycycline, minocycline | Rickettsia, Mycoplasma, **Lyme** | **Tooth/bone staining** · avoid <12 yr |
| Macrolides | Erythromycin, azithro | Strep, H. influenzae, Mycoplasma | **Warfarin interaction (P450)** |
| Chloramphenicol | — | Meningitis, brain abscess | Aplastic anaemia, grey baby |
| **Clindamycin** | — | B. fragilis, S. aureus · **excellent bone penetration** | **Pseudomembranous colitis** |
| Linezolid | — | Resistant G+ (**MRSA, VRE**) | **Serotonin syndrome** (MAOI) |
| Streptogramins | Quinupristin/dalfopristin | VRE | — |
| **Fluoroquinolones** | Cipro (+ Pseudomonas), levo, moxi (+ anaerobes) | Equivalent IV/PO · osteomyelitis, PJI | Phototoxicity · **Achilles rupture** · **↓ fracture healing** |
| Metronidazole | — | **Anaerobes** | Seizures, cerebellar, disulfiram |
| Rifampin | — | **Staph** (intracellular), **TB** | Orange body fluids · hepatotoxic (with INH) |
| TMP/SMX | — | UTI, Proteus | Thrombocytopenia · avoid 3rd trimester |

### Splenectomy

**Required** — pneumococcal · **Hib** · meningococcal C · influenza vaccines · **lifelong oral penicillin V or erythromycin**

## 2 Mechanism

### Resistance (mecA, biofilm, efflux, D-zone test)

**Three basic mechanisms (OB)** — **avoidance** · decreased susceptibility · inactivation

| Mechanism | Confers resistance to | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **β-lactamase** (hydrolysis) | Penicillin | E. coli, S. epidermidis, Pseudomonas, Klebsiella → add clavulanate (Augmentin) |
| **Genetic mutation — mecA** (on SCCmec) → **PBP2a** (low β-lactam affinity) | **Methicillin, oxacillin, nafcillin**, cephalosporins | **MRSA** · SCCmec type IV = **CA-MRSA** (less multidrug-resistant) |
| Altered cell-wall permeability | Tetracyclines, quinolones, trimethoprim, β-lactams | — |
| **Biofilm** (glycocalyx) = **avoidance** | Adherence to implants, resists phagocytosis | **S. epidermidis**, Salmonella |
| **Active efflux pumps** | **Erythromycin, tetracycline** | msrA gene (Staph) |
| Altered peptidoglycan (D-Ala-D-Ala) | **Vancomycin** | **VRE** |
| **Ribosome alteration** — erm gene methylates 23S rRNA | Inducible **MLS** (macrolide–lincosamide–streptogramin) | **D-zone test** |
| Altered DNA gyrase | Quinolones | — |
| Altered RNA polymerase | Rifampin | — |
| Altered membrane-binding protein | β-lactams | — |

**Tetracycline resistance (3)** — **increased drug efflux** · ribosome protection · tetracycline modification

**Biofilm biology** — forms when bacterial mass sufficient → **quorum sensing** → stationary/reduced growth phase · **high-shear** environments stimulate it

**MRSA options** (don't bind PBPs) — **vancomycin, TMP/SMX, clindamycin, linezolid**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### D-zone test

**Indication** — **erythromycin-resistant MRSA** (or β-haemolytic strep) **before starting clindamycin**

**Basis** — plasmid alters **50S** binding site for both clindamycin + erythromycin → **inducible clindamycin resistance**

**Method** — erythromycin disc next to clindamycin disc → **D-shaped zone of inhibition = positive** → **don't use clindamycin**

![D-zone test — flattening next to the erythromycin disc](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9059-d-zone-test.jpg)

| Test | Use |
|---|---|
| **D-zone** | ✓ **Inducible clindamycin resistance** |
| Etest | MIC |
| CAMP | Group B strep |
| Ziehl–Neelsen | Acid-fast (mycobacteria) |
| Oxidase | Cytochrome c oxidase +/− |
| Phenylalanine deaminase | Proteus |
| Citrate / decarboxylase | Enterobacteriaceae |
| β-lactamase | Penicillin resistance |

## 5 Management

### Clinical use (rifampin, antibiotic cement, dental prophylaxis)

**Rifampin** — **lipophilic → high cell penetration** → effective vs **intracellular phagocytised S. aureus** (in macrophages) · always combined (synergy)

**Vancomycin indications** — serious β-lactam allergy · MRSA/MRSE infection · prosthesis prophylaxis where **MRSA/MRSE rate is high**

**Vancomycin coverage** — G+ only · no GN bacilli (too large) · time-dependent killing

**Antibiotic cement (PMMA)** — max **5% of cement weight** without ↓ mechanical strength → **2 g per 40 g** · commercial = **1 g / 40 g** · add as **powder, never liquid**

**Antibiotic nail for septic tibial nonunion** — PMMA + **vancomycin (G+) + tobramycin (GN)**

**Dental prophylaxis after TJA** — only **high-risk**: immunocompromised (transplant, AIDS, inflammatory arthritis, chemo) · **prior PJI** · **uncontrolled diabetes**

**Regimen** — **cephalexin / amoxicillin / cephradine 2 g PO within 1 h** \
✗ Wrong options: routine IV

## 6 Complications

### Adverse effects & contraindications

**Quinolones** — **inhibit early fracture healing** (toxic to **chondrocytes** → immature callus, more cartilage) · **Achilles tendinitis/rupture (RR 3.7)**

**Tetracycline** — ✓ **contraindicated in children** (stains teeth + bone)\
✗ Wrong options: penicillin, cephalexin, erythromycin, ampicillin

**Linezolid** — reversible non-selective **MAOI** → **serotonin syndrome** (esp. with SSRIs): headache, diaphoresis, tachycardia, hypertension, tremor, **clonus**, unreactive pupils → stop drug + **IV benzodiazepines** · severe → serotonin antagonist

**Vancomycin** — flushing = **red man syndrome**

## X vs Y

| | Aminoglycoside (gentamicin/tobramycin) | Tetracycline (doxycycline) | Macrolide (erythromycin) | Clindamycin | Linezolid |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Subunit | **30S** | **30S** | **50S** | **50S** | **50S (23S rRNA)** |
| Binding | **Irreversible** | Blocks tRNA | Reversible | Translocation / peptidyl transferase | Blocks **initiation complex** |
| Action | Cidal | Static | Static | Static (cidal high dose) | Variable |

| | Vancomycin | Tobramycin |
|---|---|---|
| Target | Cell wall (D-Ala-D-Ala) | 30S ribosome (irreversible) |
| Spectrum | **Gram-positive** | **Gram-negative** |

## How it's asked

**"Mechanism of vancomycin?"** → **inhibition of cell-wall synthesis**\
✗ Wrong options: permeability · ribosome · DNA · antimetabolite

**"All inhibit ribosomes EXCEPT?"** → **vancomycin**\
✗ Wrong options: gentamicin, tobramycin, erythromycin, linezolid

**"All interfere with cell-wall synthesis EXCEPT?"** → **gentamicin**\
✗ Wrong options: cefazolin, penicillin G, vancomycin, imipenem

**"Family that inhibits DNA gyrase?"** → **quinolones**\
✗ Wrong options: penicillins, aminoglycosides, macrolides, sulfonamides

**"Binds the 30S subunit?"** → **aminoglycosides**\
✗ Wrong options: macrolides (50S) · rifampin (RNA polymerase)

**"Linezolid (oxazolidinone) targets?"** → **ribosomal activity**\
✗ Wrong options: DNA-dependent RNA polymerase (rifampin) · membrane (daptomycin)

**"Cephalosporins block transpeptidase → effect?"** → **inhibit cell-wall synthesis**

**"Fluoroquinolones inhibit?"** → **DNA gyrase**\
✗ Wrong options: RNA gyrase · DNA polymerase · telomerase

**"Amphotericin acts by?"** → **binding sterols, disrupting the cell membrane**

**"Tobramycin in an antibiotic nail — mechanism?"** → **irreversibly binds 30S**\
✗ Wrong options: reversible · 50S · D-Ala-D-Ala = vancomycin

**"Correct class–mechanism pairing?"** → **fluoroquinolones – DNA gyrase**

**"Prevents 30S–50S initiation complex?"** → **linezolid**\
✗ Wrong options: gentamicin · doxycycline · chloramphenicol · erythromycin

**"Binds the 50S subunit?"** → **clindamycin**\
✗ Wrong options: cefazolin, cipro, doxycycline, vancomycin

**"Binds D-Ala-D-Ala to block transpeptidation?"** → **vancomycin**\
✗ Wrong options: daptomycin (membrane depolarisation)

**"Species producing mecA?"** → **MRSA**

**"How does S. epidermidis adhere and resist phagocytosis?"** → **biofilm**

**"MRSA resistance to penicillinase-stable antibiotics via?"** → **genetic mutation (mecA → PBP2a)**\
✗ Wrong options: efflux · biofilm · permeability · peptidoglycan

**"Tetracycline resistance: ribosome protection, modification and?"** → **increased drug efflux**

**"Biofilm resistance is an example of?"** → **avoidance**

**"Erythromycin-resistant MRSA, want clindamycin — test?"** → **D-zone test**\
✗ Wrong options: oxidase, CAMP, Ziehl–Neelsen, Etest

**"MSSA abscess → recurrence now methicillin-resistant — mechanism?"** → **mecA gene mutation**

**"Antibiotic toxic to chondrocytes, inhibits early fracture healing?"** → **quinolones**

**"Contraindicated in children?"** → **tetracycline**

**"Linezolid → clonus, tachycardia, hypertension — next step?"** → **IV benzodiazepines (serotonin syndrome)**

**"Why rifampin kills intracellular staph?"** → **high cell penetration** (lipophilic)

**"Max vancomycin in 40 g PMMA?"** → **2 g** (5% by weight)

**"TKA 8 mo ago, A1c 9.0, gingival dental procedure — prophylaxis?"** → **cephalexin 2 g PO within 1 h**\
✗ Wrong options: IV daptomycin · none · delay · repeat A1c

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Anticoagulation Medications

*Basic Science · 7 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Coagulation & Virchow's triad

**Cascade** — series of reactions → **fibrin** → platelet activation → clot · imbalance → thromboembolism / DVT

**Virchow's triad** — **venous stasis** · **endothelial damage** · **hypercoagulable state**

**Factor Ia** — = **fibrin** · broken down by **plasmin** (from plasminogen)

![Targets for anticoagulant drugs](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9054-targets-for-anticoagulant-drugs.jpg)

### Overview of anticoagulants

| Agent | Mechanism | Metabolism | Reversal | Advantage / risk |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Compression stocking | Mechanical | — | — | No bleeding · compliance |
| **Aspirin** | **Irreversible COX** acetylation in **platelets** → ↓ TXA2, PG | Renal | None (acts at platelet) | Convenient |
| **Unfractionated heparin** | **Activates ATIII** → inhibits **IIa, III, Xa** | **Hepatic** | **Protamine** | Reversible · **highest bleeding + HIT** |
| **LMWH** (enoxaparin, dalteparin) | ATIII → principally **Xa** (also IIa) | **Renal** | Protamine (**partial** — anti-Xa not normalised) | Fixed dose, no monitoring · t½ **4–5 h** |
| **Fondaparinux** (Arixtra) | **Indirect Xa** via ATIII · synthetic pentasaccharide · no IIa effect | **Renal** | — | No monitoring · ↓ DVT vs enoxaparin (hip fx, TKA) · **highest bleeding** · avoid with epidurals · t½ **17–21 h** |
| **Warfarin** | **Vitamin K 2,3-epoxide reductase (VKORC1)** → ↓ γ-carboxylation of **II, VII, IX, X, C, S** | **Hepatic** | **Vit K** (≤3 days) · **FFP** (immediate) | **Most effective** · hard to reverse/dose |
| Dextran | Dilutional · coats RBC/platelets/endothelium (↑ electronegativity) · ↓ vWF/VIII-Ag | — | — | Fluid overload |
| **Rivaroxaban, apixaban, edoxaban** | **Direct Xa** ("-xa-ban") | Liver (major) + kidney, gut | Andexanet alfa (see below) | **Oral** · peak **2–4 h** |
| **Dabigatran** (Pradaxa) | **Direct thrombin (IIa)**, reversible | **Renal** | **Idarucizumab** (FDA 2015) | GI upset, bleeding |
| Hirudin (leech), bivalirudin | Direct thrombin | — | — | Hirudin = most potent thrombin inhibitor |
| Clopidogrel | **ADP receptor** on platelets | — | — | — |

## 5 Management

### Warfarin mechanism & pharmacology

**Mechanism** — ✓ **inhibits vitamin K-dependent clotting factors** · blocks reduction of vit K epoxide → active vit K (hydroquinone) · **inhibits carboxylation**

**Factors** — **II (prothrombin), VII (first affected), IX, X** + proteins **C and S** · not ATIII, VIII, Xa directly

**Alcoholic / liver disease** — caution due to **factor VII** (narrow margin)

**Pharmacokinetics** — oral, rapid GI absorption · **affected by liver function + diet** · plasma **t½ 36–42 h** · therapeutic in **72–96 h** · INR not affected for **2–3 days**

**Target INR** — **2–3** for orthopaedic patients \
✗ Wrong options: 2.5–3.5

**Elective surgery** — stop **3–5 days** before

**Drug interactions** — rifampin · phenobarbital · diuretics · cholestyramine

### Heparin, LMWH & fondaparinux

**Activates ATIII** — ✓ **heparin**\
✗ Wrong options: warfarin, aspirin, rivaroxaban, dabigatran

**Heparin inhibits IIa (thrombin)** — ✓ heparin\
✗ Wrong options: fondaparinux (Xa only) · rivaroxaban (Xa only) · TXA

**Enoxaparin** — primarily **Xa** (antithrombin complex irreversibly inactivates Xa) · protamine less effective than for UFH

**Indirect Xa inhibitor** — ✓ **fondaparinux**\
✗ Wrong options: rivaroxaban, apixaban, edoxaban (direct) · dabigatran (direct IIa)

**Protamine reverses** — ✓ **LMWH** (partially), UFH\
✗ Wrong options: aspirin, clopidogrel (no medical reversal) · warfarin (vit K)

**Highest bleeding + thrombocytopenia** — ✓ **unfractionated heparin** (**HIT** — antibodies activate platelets → intravascular clot) · rare with LMWH\
✗ Wrong options: coumadin

### Factor Xa inhibitors

**Direct Xa** — ✓ **rivaroxaban (Xarelto)**, apixaban, edoxaban · antithrombin-independent\
✗ Wrong options: fondaparinux (indirect)

**Class facts** — **oral** · **max effect 2–4 h** · predictable → **no routine monitoring** · **low** food/drug interaction · excretion liver (major) + kidneys + intestine

**Half-life** — rivaroxaban **8 h** · apixaban **12 h** · urgent surgery delayed until a half-life has elapsed

**Reversal** — Orthobullets: **no current antidote, andexanet alfa under investigation** · OB: **FDA-approved coagulation factor Xa (recombinant) inactivated-zhzo** for life-threatening bleeding — read the options

### Tranexamic acid

**Mechanism** — synthetic **lysine** analogue · **competitively inhibits plasminogen activation** (binds lysine-binding site) · non-competitive plasmin inhibitor at high dose · ↓ **active plasmin** → ↓ fibrinolysis

**Potency** — **8–10×** ε-aminocaproic acid

![TXA blocks plasminogen → plasmin](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9054-txa-mechanism-of-action.jpg)

| Route | Fact |
|---|---|
| IV | 10–20 mg/kg bolus ± repeat every 3 h ×1–4 or infusion 1–10 mg/kg/h |
| **Topical** | **As effective as IV** · sprayed on wound at closure · **no detectable serum level** |
| **Oral** | **As effective as IV** · 1.95 g **2 h preop** · **cheaper** |

**Kinetics** — <5% metabolised · joint-fluid t½ **3 h** · in tissues up to **17 h**

**Safety** — **no ↑ thromboembolic events** (no route difference in DVT) · avoid with **cardiac stents, prior VTE/ischaemic stroke**

**Jehovah's Witness THA** — TXA ↓ active plasmin\
✗ Wrong options: epoetin alfa (↑ Hb preop) · dexamethasone (pain) · celecoxib

### Antiplatelet effects & supplements

**Aspirin's target cell** — ✓ **platelets** (irreversibly blocks TXA2)

| Supplement | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Ginkgo** (ginkgolide B displaces PAF) | ↑ bleeding — **platelets** |
| **Ginseng** (panaxynol) | ↑ bleeding — platelets · hypoglycaemia |
| **Garlic** (aged extract — ↑ cyclic nucleotides, ↓ fibrinogen binding) | ↑ bleeding — platelets |
| **Omega-3 fish oil** | ↑ INR (platelets + vit K factors) |
| **St John's wort** | ↓ INR (↑ warfarin catabolism) · CNS reuptake inhibition · **not a platelet effect** |
| Coenzyme Q10 · green tea | ↓ INR (green tea = direct warfarin antagonist) |
| Ephedra | Cardiovascular instability |
| Kava, valerian | ↑ sedative effect of anaesthetics |

### VTE prophylaxis guidelines

**CRISTAL (9,711 THA/TKA)** — symptomatic VTE **aspirin 3.45% vs LMWH 1.8%** → aspirin **failed non-inferiority** (1% margin) · **no difference in mortality**, joint readmission, major bleeding

**ACCP** — mechanical + pharmacological · minimum **10–14 days**, ideally up to **35 days** · LMWH preferred, aspirin acceptable

**Aspirin dose** — **81 mg BID** = efficacy of 325 mg BID with **less GI** · <4 weeks → ↑ mortality + bleeding

**No prophylaxis** — DVT >**50%** after TKA → mechanical alone not recommended

**Weight-based LMWH** — = therapeutic dose → ↑ bleeding/wound drainage (avoid without confirmed VTE)

| AAOS VTED guideline (elective THA/TKA) | |
|---|---|
| Postop duplex screening | **Not recommended** |
| Only risk factor clearly supported for VTE | **Previous VTE** |
| Only bleeding risk factors clearly supported | **Known bleeding disorder, active liver disease** |
| Antiplatelet agents | **Stop preop** |
| All patients | Pharmacological and/or mechanical |
| Previous VTE | Pharmacological **AND** mechanical |
| Bleeding disorder / liver disease | **Mechanical** |
| Anaesthesia | **Neuraxial** (spinal/epidural) |
| Mobilisation | Early |
| IVC filter | Not clearly supported |
| **Aspirin** | ✓ **Included** as a prevention option |

### Operative criteria — when chemoprophylaxis is not mandatory

**Below-knee injury, no risk factors** — e.g. healthy 37-yr-old, isolated rotational ankle ORIF → **not absolutely indicated** (DVT rate = placebo)

**Elective forefoot/midfoot surgery, low risk, immediate WB** — ✓ **no routine chemical prophylaxis** (ICM-VTE Foot & Ankle) · NWB = independent risk, mitigated by **>50% load-bearing**

**Risk factors that mandate it** — older age · obesity · prior DVT · **malignancy** · OCP · smoking · non-ambulatory · SCI / axial injury · hip fracture · THA

```
Patient needing VTE prophylaxis decision
├─ THA / TKA → MECHANICAL + PHARMACOLOGICAL
│      └─ Prior VTE → PHARMACOLOGICAL AND MECHANICAL
├─ Known bleeding disorder / liver disease → MECHANICAL ONLY
└─ Below-knee / forefoot, low risk, early WB → NO ROUTINE CHEMOPROPHYLAXIS
```

## X vs Y

| | Direct | Indirect (via ATIII) |
|---|---|---|
| **Xa** | **Rivaroxaban, apixaban, edoxaban** | **Fondaparinux** (Xa only), LMWH (mainly Xa) |
| **IIa** | **Dabigatran**, bivalirudin, hirudin | **UFH** (IIa + Xa) |

| | Protamine | Vitamin K / FFP | Idarucizumab | Andexanet alfa |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Reverses | UFH, LMWH (partial) | Warfarin | Dabigatran | Xa inhibitors |

## How it's asked

**"Mechanism of warfarin?"** → **inhibits vitamin K-dependent clotting factors**\
✗ Wrong options: platelets · COX · ↓ ATIII

**"Inhibits carboxylation of clotting factors?"** → **warfarin**\
✗ Wrong options: enoxaparin, dalteparin, heparin, hirudin

**"Prevents γ-carboxylation of X, IX and prothrombin?"** → **warfarin**\
✗ Wrong options: clopidogrel (ADP receptor)

**"Alcoholic, venogram DVT — warfarin caution due to factor?"** → **VII**

**"Specific target of warfarin?"** → **vitamin K 2,3-epoxide reductase**\
✗ Wrong options: ATIII · protein C · VIII · Xa

**"Plasma half-life of warfarin?"** → **36–42 h**

**"DVT agent affected by liver function and diet?"** → **warfarin**\
✗ Wrong options: LMWH, fondaparinux (renal)

**"Protamine reverses?"** → **LMWH**

**"Highest risk of bleeding and thrombocytopenia?"** → **unfractionated heparin** (HIT)

**"Activates antithrombin III?"** → **heparin**

**"Inhibits the circled factor IIa?"** → **heparin**\
✗ Wrong options: fondaparinux · rivaroxaban (Xa only)

**"Where does enoxaparin act?"** → **factor Xa**

**"Indirect inhibitor of factor X?"** → **fondaparinux**

**"Direct factor Xa inhibitor?"** → **rivaroxaban**\
✗ Wrong options: fondaparinux (indirect) · dextran · coumadin

**"True of Xarelto class?"** → **maximal effect 2–4 h**\
✗ Wrong options: food interactions · IV/SC only · monitoring · mainly renal

**"Competitive inhibition of plasminogen activation?"** → **tranexamic acid**

**"Inhibits activation of the enzyme that breaks down factor Ia?"** → **TXA**

**"TXA in TKA — true?"** → **similar blood-loss reduction IV vs oral**\
✗ Wrong options: IV better · topical better · ↑ DVT

**"Jehovah's Witness THA — drug ↓ active plasmin?"** → **TXA**

**"Supplement affecting clotting via platelets?"** → **ginkgo**\
✗ Wrong options: vitamin D, ephedra, St John's wort, selenium

**"↑ bleeding by direct platelet effect EXCEPT?"** → **St John's wort**

**"Aspirin's primary cellular target (image)?"** → **platelets**

**"LMWH vs aspirin after THA/TKA — true?"** → **no difference in mortality**\
✗ Wrong options: aspirin non-inferior · aspirin fewer DVT · LMWH more bleeding/readmission

**"AAOS VTED recommendations EXCEPT?"** → **"aspirin not approved in low-risk patients"** (false — aspirin is included)

**"Uncomplicated TKA, BMI 39 — optimal prophylaxis?"** → **aspirin 81 mg BID for 6 weeks**\
✗ Wrong options: 325 mg × 2 wk · weight-based LMWH · INR 2.5–3.5 · SCDs only

**"DVT prophylaxis NOT absolutely indicated?"** → **healthy 37-yr-old, isolated ankle ORIF**

**"Healthy 49-yr-old, elective bunion surgery, immediate heel WB — VTE strategy?"** → **no routine chemical prophylaxis**

# Articular Cartilage

*Basic Science · 8 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Forms of cartilage

| Form | Example |
|---|---|
| **Hyaline / articular** | Joint surfaces |
| **Fibroelastic** | Meniscus |
| **Fibrocartilage** | Tendon/ligament insertion into bone |
| **Elastic** | Trachea |
| **Physeal** | Growth plate |

### Composition & function (aggrecan, water, type II collagen)

**Function** — ↓ friction · distributes load · **stress-shielding of the solid matrix** (high water, incompressible water, PG–collagen organisation) · **anisotropic**\
✗ Wrong options: isotropic

**% by weight** — **water > collagen > proteoglycan > non-collagenous protein > cells**

| Component | Amount | Role |
|---|---|---|
| **Water** | **65–80%** of wet weight · **80% superficial, 65% deep** | Load-dependent deformation · ↓ with age · **↑ in OA** |
| **Collagen** | 10–20% · **type II = 90–95%** (minor V, VI, IX, X, XI) | **Tensile strength** · framework · **resists swelling** from aggrecan osmotic pressure |
| **Proteoglycans** | 10–15% | **Compressive strength** · attract water · fluid/electrolyte balance |
| **Chondrocytes** | **~2% of volume** (OB) · "<10%" / "up to 5%" (TO) | Make collagen, PG, enzymes · only cell in matrix |

**Type I collagen gives tensile strength** — **false (TO)** · it is **type II**

**Type X collagen** — **calcified cartilage** (mineralisation) · ↑ in **early OA** at subchondral interface (strain >2.5% induces Col X mRNA)

**Type IV collagen** — **basal lamina**\
✗ Wrong options: calcified cartilage

### Proteoglycans

**Aggrecan** — **most responsible for hydrophilic behaviour** · GAGs (**chondroitin sulfate, keratan sulfate**) on a core protein → aggrecan → **link protein** binds it to **hyaluronic acid** → **proteoglycan aggregate**

**Negative charge** — sulfated GAG chains → osmotic swelling pressure · holds **50× its weight** in water · swelling restrained ~20% by type II collagen · MW ~1,000,000\
✗ Wrong options: positive

![Aggrecan aggregate on hyaluronic acid](../../MCQ/attachments/q4453-illa-aggrecan-image.jpg)

| Molecule | Role |
|---|---|
| **Aggrecan** | ✓ **Hydrophilicity, swelling pressure** |
| Decorin, biglycan, fibromodulin | Small PGs · bind type II collagen · matrix stabilisation (decorin organises collagen) |
| Versican | Cell-surface PG |
| Matrilin-1 | Cartilage tissue organisation |
| Type IX collagen | Stability · links matrix molecules to type II |
| COMP | Regulates chondrocyte proliferation |
| Osteocalcin | Most prevalent non-collagenous protein of **bone** |

### Structure & zones

![Zones of articular cartilage](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9017-cartilage-layers.jpg)

| Zone | Collagen | Chondrocytes | Content |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Lamina splendens** | Cell-free, random flat fibril bundles | None | Surface layer |
| **Superficial (tangential, zone 1)** | **Parallel** to surface (resists shear) | **Flattened**, parallel to tidemark | **Highest collagen, lowest PG**, high water · thinnest · **only zone with progenitor cells** |
| **Transitional / intermediate (zone 2)** | Oblique / **random**, larger fibres | Round, active | **Thickest** · more PG |
| **Deep / radial / basal (zone 3)** | **Perpendicular**, **crosses tidemark**, largest fibrils | **Round, in columns** | **Highest PG, lowest water** (lowest collagen) |
| **Tidemark** | Basophilic line | — | Boundary **uncalcified / calcified** cartilage |
| **Calcified (zone 4)** | Type X | Small, metabolically inactive | Anchors to subchondral bone |

**Tidemark (TO)** — lies between **radial (deep) and calcified zone** · part of zone 4 \
✗ Wrong options: calcified/subchondral · superficial/transitional

**Tidemark (Orthobullets)** — separates true articular cartilage from remnant of **cartilage anlage** · divides nutritional sources · found only in joints, most prominent in adults

**Water vs depth (TO)** — as PG ↑ from superficial → deep, **water ↓**

### Nourishment, growth factors

**Avascular** — fed by **synovial fluid** (surface) + **subchondral bone** (base) · ATP by **glycolysis**

**Immature cartilage** — has stem cells · mature does not

| Growth factor | Effect |
|---|---|
| **TGF-β1** | **Most investigated** · ↑ PG + ECM synthesis · ↓ IL-1 + MMP catabolism · **synovial proliferation + fibrosis** · osteophytes |
| b-FGF (FGF-2) | ↑ chondrocyte DNA synthesis, PG · but ↑ inflammation, osteophytes · no defect healing |
| **IGF-1** | ↑ DNA + matrix synthesis (adult) · ↓ synovial inflammation · **additive with TGF-β** · ↓ in OA |
| PDGF | Laceration healing (from PRP data) · **no adverse or positive effect in vivo** |
| **IL-1** | Catabolic · ↑ **MMP**, COX-2, nitric oxide synthase |
| Glucosamine | Anabolic · prevents IL-1β-induced ↓ PG synthesis |

## 2 Mechanism

### Response to loading & mechanotransduction

| Stimulates matrix synthesis | Suppresses synthesis, ↑ chondrolysis |
|---|---|
| **Cyclic** stress **1–5 MPa** | Excess **>5 MPa** |
| Moderate frequency **0.1–1 Hz** | **Static** <0.01 Hz |
| Low rate <1000 MPa/s | High rate >1000 MPa/s |

**Mechanosensors** — **primary cilia** (chondrocytes + osteoblasts) · transduction via **integrins**

**Marathon runner, cartilage 2× thick** — ✓ **chondrocyte modulation via mechanotransduction**

**Moderate running** — ↑ thickness · ✓ **↑ GAG** production (T1 gadolinium MRI) · lateral femoral GAG > medial

**Strenuous loading** — thinning + PG loss

**Immobilisation / NWB** — thinning, softening, ↓ PG · **only in the off-loaded limb**\
✗ Wrong options: contralateral hypertrophy

### Lubrication (lubricin, boundary, fluid film)

| Type | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Elastohydrodynamic** | **Main** during dynamic function · elastic deformation + thin fluid film |
| **Boundary** | Non-deformable surfaces, lubricant partially separates · **lubricin** (superficial zone protein) + HA |
| Boosted | Fluid trapped in pools between contact regions |
| Hydrodynamic | Fluid separates sliding surfaces |
| Weeping | Fluid exuded under load → hydrostatic separation |
| Squeeze film | Fluid slowly squeezed out from between surfaces |

**Lubricin** — mucinous glycoprotein · binds **hyaluronic acid** → **boundary** layer \
✗ Wrong options: vitronectin (doesn't bind HA)

**Fluid-film in MoM THA** — ↑ by **larger head**, slight **radial clearance** (most important), low roughness · **complete congruence prevents fluid film**

**Wear mechanisms** — adhesion · abrasion · transfer · fatigue · third body

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Aging vs osteoarthritis changes

| | **Aging** | **Osteoarthritis** |
|---|---|---|
| **Water** | **↓** | **↑** (→ ↑ permeability, ↓ strength) |
| Modulus / stiffness | **↑** (less elastic, brittle) | **↓** (more elastic) · ↓ tensile + shear + compressive modulus |
| Chondrocytes | Fewer but larger (hypocellular) | Clusters / clones (stage 2) |
| GAG ratio | **↑ keratan sulfate : chondroitin** (KS ↑ until ~30 yr, CS ↓; C4S → C6S) | **↑ chondroitin 4-sulfate : keratan sulfate** |
| Proteoglycans | ↓ synthesis, ↓ size · ↑ decorin | **↑ degradation** · unbound from HA · **aggrecan ↑ early, ↓ late** |
| Collagen | **↑ cross-linking** (passive glycation) | **Disorganised** (↑ collagenase) |
| AGEs | **↑** (→ ↑ stiffness, brittleness, fatigue failure) | AGE accumulation thought to lead to knee/ankle OA |

**Common to both** — ✓ **↓ proteoglycan content**

**OA collagen** — ↓ collagen content (TO, FNB 2016) · "relative ↑ collagen concentration from PG loss" (TO second key) — read the options

**AGEs** — non-enzymatic glycation (glucose, fructose, ribose + lysine/arginine) · **pentosidine** · cross-link type II collagen · ↓ chondrocyte response to TGF-β, IGF-1, BMP-7

### OA stages

| Stage | Event |
|---|---|
| **1 (early)** | Matrix disruption → **↑ water** (hallmark), ↓ PG aggregation, ↓ aggrecan → ↓ stiffness |
| **2** | Chondrocytes detect damage → mediators → **↑ proliferation (clusters/clones)** + ↑ matrix synthesis **and** ↑ catabolism |
| **3** | ↓ proliferative + anabolic response → visible cartilage loss → clinical OA |

**Initiators** — inflammation · overload · ↓ matrix production

**Microscopy** — fibrillation of superficial + transitional zones → **tidemark breached by vessels** → subchondral remodelling

**Late / terminal** — water ↓ with degradation

**Molecular** — Wnt-induced signal protein 1 ↑ proteases · ↓ response to TGF-α, IGF-1 · ROS, senescence

## 5 Management

### Healing

**Deep laceration (through tidemark into subchondral bone)** — haematoma → marrow MSCs → **fibrocartilage (type I collagen)** · poor wear, ↓ stiffness

**Superficial laceration (above tidemark)** — chondrocyte proliferation but **no healing** (avascular)

## 6 Complications

### Post-traumatic OA & inflammatory mediators

**Initiating event** — ✓ **chondrocyte apoptosis** along superficial matrix cracks

**Direct mediator of chondrocyte death** — ✓ **caspases** (mainly **caspase-3**) · aspartate-specific cysteine proteases

**Cytokines after intra-articular fracture** — **IL-1β, TNF-α, nitric oxide, MMPs, aggrecans, DAMPs**

**Neutrophil recruitment** — ✓ **IL-8** (with IL-6) · IL-6 → MMPs, hypertrophy (STAT3/ERK)\
✗ Wrong options: IL-10 anti-inflammatory · RANKL osteoclasts

### Intervertebral disc degeneration

**DDD** — ✓ **↑ MMP-1**, **↓ aggrecan/PG**, **↓ water** → ↓ osmotic pressure → disc height loss · **↓ T2 signal** ("dark disc")

## X vs Y

| | Superficial zone | Deep zone |
|---|---|---|
| Collagen orientation | **Parallel** | **Perpendicular** |
| Chondrocytes | **Flat** | **Round, columns** |
| Collagen | **Highest** | Lowest |
| Proteoglycan | **Lowest** | **Highest** |
| Water | Highest (80%) | **Lowest** (65%) |

| | Aging | OA |
|---|---|---|
| Water | ↓ | ↑ |
| Stiffness | ↑ | ↓ |
| KS : CS | ↑ | ↓ |

## How it's asked

**"Tidemark lies between (TO)?"** → **radial and calcified zone**

**"TRUE about articular cartilage?"** → **stress-shielding of solid matrix**\
✗ Wrong options: isotropic · water deep · type IV in calcified cartilage

**"Highest PG, lowest water?"** → **deep zone**

**"Zone containing progenitor cells?"** → **superficial (A)**\
✗ Wrong options: lamina splendens · tidemark

**"Superficial zone appearance?"** → **flattened chondrocytes + collagen both parallel to tidemark**

**"Majority by weight of ECM?"** → **water**

**"Superficial-zone collagen orientation?"** → **parallel, low PG content**

**"Chondrocyte % volume?"** → **2%**

**"Arrowed superficial zone?"** → **high collagen, low PG**

**"All true EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **"type I collagen provides tensile strength"**

**"Contributes most to water attraction?"** → **aggrecan**\
✗ Wrong options: biglycan, decorin, fibromodulin, osteocalcin

**"True statement?"** → **type II collagen resists swelling from osmotic pressure**\
✗ Wrong options: collagen hydrophilic · water 25% · aggrecans form GAGs · positive charge

**"Main biologic effect of aggrecan?"** → **hydrophilic behaviour**

**"Swelling pressure mainly due to water with?"** → **aggrecan**

**"MoM feature NOT improving fluid-film lubrication?"** → **complete congruence**

**"Lubricin provides?"** → **boundary lubrication**

**"Binds HA to form boundary layer?"** → **lubricin**

**"Marathon runner, thick cartilage?"** → **mechanotransduction**

**"Non-weightbearing after ankle ORIF — knees at 2 months?"** → **thinning ipsilateral, no change contralateral**

**"Moderate running effect?"** → **↑ GAG production**

**"OA changes EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **decreased water content**

**"In OA but not aging (TO)?"** → **↑ water + ↑ PG degradation**

**"Common to aging and OA?"** → **↓ proteoglycan content**

**"Best describes OA degeneration?"** → **initiated by inflammation, overload or ↓ matrix production**\
✗ Wrong options: stage 2 ↓ catabolism · repair improves with age

**"Change with age?"** → **PG synthesis ↓**\
✗ Wrong options: cells ↑ · CS ↑ · KS ↓ · water ↑

**"Arthritic cartilage characterised by?"** → **PG loss**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ moduli · ↓ water

**"Collagen ↑ in early OA?"** → **type X**

**"First stage of OA?"** → **↓ PG/aggrecan + ↑ water**

**"↑ water is the hallmark of which stage?"** → **early**

**"AGEs characteristic of?"** → **aging cartilage → ↑ stiffness**

**"Most common change in arthritic cartilage?"** → **↑ water content**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ KS:CS · ↑ stiffness · ↑ cross-linking

**"↑ early, ↓ late in OA?"** → **aggrecan**\
✗ Wrong options: water, MMP, CS:KS (all uniformly ↑) · modulus (uniformly ↓)

**"Bone-on-bone OA — cellular environment?"** → **↓ aggrecan, ↑ water, ↑ permeability**

**"Growth factor: most studied, ↑ ECM, synovial fibrosis?"** → **TGF-β1**

**"IL-1 increases?"** → **MMP**

**"Initiating cellular event in PTOA?"** → **chondrocyte apoptosis**

**"Cytokines after intra-articular fracture → PTOA?"** → **IL-1β, TNF-α, NO, MMPs, aggrecans, DAMPs**

**"Mediator of chondrocyte death after chondral injury?"** → **caspases**

**"Recruits neutrophils after intra-articular fracture?"** → **IL-8**

**"Degenerative disc composition?"** → **↑ MMP-1, ↓ water, ↓ PG**

**"Why is L5/S1 darker than L3/4 on T2?"** → **↓ aggrecan, ↓ water**

# Axillary Nerve

*Basic Science · 9 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Course

**Origin** — **posterior cord** (with radial nerve) → just **inferior to subscapularis** → winds posteriorly through **quadrangular space** with **posterior circumflex humeral artery**

![Axillary nerve, posterior view](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10136-axillary-posterior.jpg)

### Motor & sensory distribution

| Branch | Supplies |
|---|---|
| **Anterior branch** | Winds round **surgical neck** → **anterior (half) deltoid** + overlying skin |
| **Posterior branch** | **Teres minor** + **posterior deltoid** · skin over lower ⅔ of posterior deltoid (**superior lateral cutaneous nerve of arm**) |
| Collateral branch | Long head of triceps |

**Axillary nerve muscles** — ✓ **deltoid + teres minor**\
✗ Wrong options: teres major (**lower subscapular n.**) · supraspinatus (**suprascapular n.**) · latissimus (thoracodorsal)

## 3 Presentation

**Lateral deltoid numbness + weak Hornblower's (teres minor, ER in abduction)** — ✓ **posterior branch of axillary nerve** (e.g. after greater-tuberosity fracture)

**Deltoid palsy test** — weakness of **abduction with the arm internally rotated** (supraspinatus rotated anteriorly, contributes less)

**Posterior cord injury** — would cause wider deficit (radial nerve too)

## 6 Complications

**Iatrogenic** — large **anterior shoulder exposure** (e.g. **Latarjet**) · uncommon but debilitating

**Treatment** — **Leechavengvongs radial → axillary nerve transfer**: a triceps branch of the radial nerve (medial, lateral or long head) → axillary nerve, as proximal as possible on axillary nerve, maximal excursion of donor

## X vs Y

| Muscle | Nerve |
|---|---|
| Deltoid, **teres minor** | **Axillary** |
| **Teres major** | **Lower subscapular** |
| Supraspinatus, infraspinatus | Suprascapular |
| Coracobrachialis, biceps, brachialis (medial) | Musculocutaneous |

## How it's asked

**"Healed GT fracture, lateral-deltoid numbness, weak Hornblower's — structure injured?"** → **posterior branch of axillary nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior branch · posterior cord · suprascapular · musculocutaneous

**"Axillary lesion weakens deltoid and?"** → **teres minor**\
✗ Wrong options: teres major · latissimus

**"Post-Latarjet weak abduction in IR, radial-to-axillary transfer — injured nerve supplies?"** → **deltoid + teres minor**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Biceps Brachii

*Basic Science · 10 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy & innervation

| | Biceps brachii |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | **Short head:** tip of **coracoid** · **Long head:** **supraglenoid tubercle** |
| **Insertion** | **Radial tuberosity** + forearm fascia via **bicipital aponeurosis** |
| **Action** | **Supinates** + flexes forearm |
| **Innervation** | **Musculocutaneous nerve (C5, C6)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Muscular branches of brachial artery |

![Biceps brachii](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10017-biceps-brachii.jpg)

### Long head in the rotator interval

**Rotator interval** — superior **supraspinatus** · inferior **subscapularis** · medial **glenoid**

**Sagittal MRI** — LHB seen in cross-section in the interval between subscapularis + supraspinatus → from superior labrum laterally → turns inferiorly into **bicipital groove**

**MGHL on sagittal MRI** — runs obliquely anteroinferiorly from superior labrum, **deep (posterior) to subscapularis**

![Sagittal MRI — arrow on the long head of biceps in the rotator interval](../../MCQ/attachments/q6457-a-31.jpg)

**Arthroscopy (posterosuperior portal)** — LHB tendon from supraglenoid tubercle → muscle innervated by **musculocutaneous**

## How it's asked

**"Sagittal shoulder MRI, structure between subscapularis and supraspinatus?"** → **long head of biceps**\
✗ Wrong options: MGHL (oblique, deep to subscap) · CA ligament

**"Arthroscopic tendon from supraglenoid tubercle — nerve to its muscle?"** → **musculocutaneous**\
✗ Wrong options: axillary, median, radial, ulnar

# Biceps femoris short head

*Basic Science · 11 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy & innervation

| | Biceps femoris short head |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | Lateral lip of **linea aspera**, lateral supracondylar ridge, lateral intermuscular septum |
| **Insertion** | **Fibular head** (mainly) · also LCL + lateral tibial condyle |
| **Action** | Flexes knee · externally rotates tibia (long head also extends hip) |
| **Innervation** | **Common peroneal (peroneal division of sciatic) — L5, S1, S2** |
| **Arterial supply** | Perforators of profunda femoris, inferior gluteal, superior muscular branches of popliteal |

![Biceps femoris short head](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10072-biceps-femoris-short-head.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | Long head | **Short head** |
|---|---|---|
| Nerve | **Tibial division** of sciatic (L5–S3) | **Peroneal division** of sciatic (L5–S2) |
| Crosses hip | Yes (extends hip) | No |

**Only hamstring on peroneal division** — short head of biceps femoris

## How it's asked

**"Nerve to short head of biceps femoris?"** → **peroneal division of sciatic**\
✗ Wrong options: tibial division (long head) · femoral · superior gluteal · saphenous
