# Bioabsorbable Materials

*Basic Science · 12 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Why bioabsorbable

**Problems solved** — migration · growth disturbance · rigidity · radio-opacity · infection · **need for removal**

**Indications** — paediatrics (transphyseal **SR PLGA 80/20** screws → only temporary growth arrest in rabbits) · osteomyelitis (antibiotic-eluting PLA) · **carriers for rhBMP-2/-7** · iliac-crest harvest site

### Types

| Material | Properties |
|---|---|
| **PGA** | **Hydrophilic**, crystalline · Tg **36 °C** · **early degradation + strength loss** · must be heated/cooled intraop |
| **PLA** | More **hydrophobic** than PGA |
| **PLLA** (L-isomer) | Hydrophobic, crystalline · Tg **57 °C** · **degrades over years** → **late reactions** |
| D-isomer PLA | Amorphous, less stable · for copolymers |
| **P(L/D)LA** | Adding D → less tightly packed → **less crystalline, faster** than PLLA · e.g. 70/30 (maxillofacial) |
| **PLGA** (PLA + PGA) | Low crystallinity |
| **Self-reinforced (SR)** | Oriented fibres in matrix (composite) · ↑ **rigidity + strength** · **malleable at room temp** · withstands 4× bending · minimal memory · **gamma sterilisation OK** (✗ Wrong options: non-reinforced (↓ molecular weight)) |

### Absorption time

| Material | Time |
|---|---|
| **SR PLLA** | **>5–6 yr** |
| PLLA | >5 yr |
| P(D/L)LA 70/30 | 2–3 yr |
| P(D/L)LA 96/4 | 2 yr |
| PLGA 80/20 | 1–2 yr |
| SR PGA | 0.5–1 yr |
| PDS | 2 months |
| **PGA** | **1–2 months** |

## 2 Mechanism

### Biodegradation & excretion

**Main mechanism** — **random hydrolysis of ester bonds** → ↓ molecular weight → loss of strength → **CO₂ + H₂O + Krebs (TCA) cycle** products

![Poly-hydroxy-acid degradation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9071-biodegradation.jpg)

**PLA excretion** — lactic acid → Krebs cycle → ✓ **CO₂ via lungs (respiratory)**\
✗ Wrong options: hepatic · renal · GI

**Kidney** — **PGA and PDS** (poly-p-dioxanone) products may be renally excreted

**Enzymes** — involved in PLA + PGA degradation

**↓ pH** — breakdown products lower pH → **accelerate** breakdown

**Crystallinity** — **amorphous / hydrophilic → faster** · crystalline / hydrophobic → slower

**Other variables** — composition, MW · fibre orientation (SR vs simple) · monomer concentration · stereoisomerism · porosity, surface area/volume · sterilisation · enzymatic vs hydrolytic

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Histopathology

**Granulomatous inflammation** — T lymphocytes (**CD4 > CD8**) · plasma cells · endothelial cells · **birefringent polymer debris** · thin macrophage layer · **multinucleated giant cells**

**Capsule** — **inner** 2–3-cell layer (**type III collagen**) · **outer** fibrous layer (**type I collagen**)

**Sequence** — neutrophils → CD4 T cells → **macrophages last**

## 6 Complications

### Adverse tissue reactions

**Incidence** — **3% children** · **up to 60% adults**

**Cause** — degradation outpaces debris clearance → **sterile fluid** accumulation · fluctuant papules · synovitis · sinus

**Timing** — late with slow materials (**PLLA ~5 yr**)

**Labs** — cultures **sterile** unless secondary infection

**X-ray** — **osteolysis** in up to **60%**

```
Bioabsorbable implant reaction
├─ Mild, sterile → OBSERVE
├─ Fluid / sinus → ASPIRATE ± DEBRIDE
│      └─ Sterile implant failure or 2° infection → REMOVE IMPLANT
└─ Severe OA → ARTHRODESIS
```

## X vs Y

| | PGA | PLLA |
|---|---|---|
| Water affinity | Hydrophilic | Hydrophobic |
| Tg | 36 °C | 57 °C |
| Absorption | **1–2 months** | **>5 yr** |
| Problem | Early strength loss | Late reactions |
| Excretion | Kidney possible | Lungs (CO₂) |

## How it's asked

**"PLA implants excreted after absorption by?"** → **respiratory system (CO₂)**\
✗ Wrong options: hepatic · renal (PGA/PDS) · GI

**"Bioabsorbable that degrades fastest?"** → **PGA** (1–2 months) · slowest **SR PLLA**

**"Degrade faster — amorphous or crystalline?"** → **amorphous / hydrophilic**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Bisphosphonates

*Basic Science · 13 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Overview

**Class** — stable **pyrophosphate analogues** · strong affinity for **hydroxyapatite** (calcium) → concentrate in bone → taken up by **osteoclasts** during resorption

**Net effect** — ✓ **inhibit osteoclast bone resorption** · ↓ recruitment + activity, ↑ **apoptosis** · loss of **ruffled border** (cytoskeleton/microtubules) · ↓ bone turnover · bone formed on therapy is **histologically normal**

**Excretion** — ✓ **renal** → avoid in severe renal disease

![Bisphosphonate disrupts the osteoclast ruffled border](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9058-ruffledborder.jpg)

### Mechanism (nitrogen vs non-nitrogen containing)

| | **Nitrogen-containing** | **Non-nitrogen (simple)** |
|---|---|---|
| Drugs | **Alendronate, risedronate, pamidronate, zoledronate** (IV yearly) | **Etidronate, clodronate, tiludronate** |
| Target | **Farnesyl pyrophosphate (diphosphate) synthase** — **mevalonate (cholesterol) pathway** | Metabolised to **non-functional / toxic ATP analogue** (replaces terminal pyrophosphate of ATP) |
| Result | ↓ **protein prenylation** (geranylgeranylation) of **small GTPases** → osteoclast inactivation/apoptosis | Competes with ATP → targets **mitochondria** → **osteoclast apoptosis** |
| Potency | **Much more potent** | Earlier, weaker |

![Nitrogen vs non-nitrogen bisphosphonates](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9058-types-of-bisphosphonates.jpg)

**Traps** — ✗ Wrong options: HMG-CoA reductase (statins) · caspase · MMP · reverse transcriptase · ↑ osteoblast activity · ↑ gut Ca · ↓ PTH

## 5 Management

### Clinical uses (OI, fibrous dysplasia, osteolysis, fracture prevention)

**Indications** — osteoporosis · metastatic bone disease · **multiple myeloma** · **Paget's** · **polyostotic fibrous dysplasia** · osteolysis after TJA · early AVN · **osteogenesis imperfecta** · **metastatic hypercalcaemia**

| Use | Outcome |
|---|---|
| **Osteoporotic VCF** (T-score ≤ −2.5) | 1 yr therapy → vertebral fracture risk ↓ **50–60%** |
| **Hip fracture risk** (N-BP, e.g. alendronate) | ↓ **~50%** (45–55%) |
| Non-vertebral fragility fracture | ↓ risk of multiple fractures |
| **After hip fracture fixation** | Resume at **1 week** → **no effect on outcome** (✗ Wrong options: nonunion, cutout, AVN, faster union) |
| **OI** — cyclical **IV pamidronate** | ↓ bone pain, ↓ fractures, ✓ **↑ bone density**, ↑ ambulation |
| **McCune-Albright / polyostotic FD** pain | ✓ **Bisphosphonates** (✗ Wrong options: calcitonin, PTH, vit D, MTX) |
| **Myeloma / metastases** (pamidronate, zoledronate) | ↓ skeletal events, pain, radiotherapy need · **no survival benefit** · monthly IV most effective |
| **TJA osteolysis** | ✓ ↓ periprosthetic bone loss (alendronate, 6 yr DXA) · may ↑ BMP-2 |
| **Precollapse AVN** | Investigational · RCT collapse at 2 yr **6.9% vs 76%** placebo |

### Contraindications

**Severe renal disease** — renal excretion

**After lumbar fusion** — animal models: ↑ fusion-mass size but **↓ fusion rate**

**Invasive dental work** — complete **before** starting bisphosphonates

## 6 Complications

### Osteonecrosis of the jaw & dental work

**ONJ** — ✓ chronic **alendronate** / bisphosphonates\
✗ Wrong options: warfarin, LMWH, diclofenac, ketorolac

**Risk factors** — **IV** therapy · duration **>36 months** (length of exposure most important) · elderly **myeloma** · **recent dental extraction** · incidence **4–17%**

**Teriparatide** — used to treat bisphosphonate ONJ

![Osteonecrosis of the jaw](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9058-on-of-jaw.jpg)

### Atypical femur fractures

**Site** — ✓ **subtrochanteric / proximal diaphyseal femur** · **lateral cortical thickening ("beaking")** → transverse fracture

**ASBMR features** — no/minimal trauma · **transverse lateral, oblique medial** · periosteal reaction · behaves as stress fracture

**Numbers** — **17–29%** of subtroch fractures atypical · **28% bilateral** → ✓ **contralateral films** · risk 3.2–50 / 100,000 person-yr → up to **100** with prolonged use

**Prodrome** — thigh pain (often misdiagnosed as quadriceps strain)

![Lateral cortical beaking, subtrochanteric femur](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9058-stress-fx-therapilates-files-wordpress-com.jpg)

**Incomplete AFF with "dreaded black line"** — stop bisphosphonate + **prophylactic IM fixation**

### Atypical femur fracture — drug management after nailing (TO)

**Denosumab AFF** — ✓ **stop denosumab → start teriparatide (anabolic window) → mandatory short-acting bisphosphonate after the anabolic course** **(TO)**

**Why** — denosumab **doesn't embed** in matrix → withdrawal = **rebound osteoclastogenesis**, rapid bone loss, **multiple vertebral fractures** → bisphosphonate "locks in" bone **(TO)**

**Traps (TO)** — ✗ Wrong options: drug holiday alone (rebound) · continue denosumab ± teriparatide (suppressed turnover) · switch to IV zoledronate (keeps turnover paralysed)

### AFF nailing technique (TO)

| Step | Rule (TO) |
|---|---|
| Implant | **Full-length cephalomedullary nail** (protects neck) · nail with **smaller radius of curvature (1.0–1.5 m)** vs standard 2.0–3.0 m |
| Plates | **Contraindicated** — fatigue failure before union |
| Entry | **Lateralised** (± slightly posterior) trochanteric start |
| Reaming | ✓ **Over-ream ≥1.5–2.0 mm** above nail diameter · (✗ Wrong options: line-to-line (incarceration)) · sharp reamers, slow, flexible shafts (avoid anterior blowout, thermal necrosis) |
| Nail size | Largest feasible (fatigue life) · never deliberately under-ream/downsize as routine |
| Extreme bow | Downsize slightly **or** closing-wedge osteotomy at apex |
| Biology | Pack **reamer dust** into fracture · **no distraction** (→ nonunion) — back-slap or compress |

### Paediatric effects

**Osteopetrosis-like radiographic changes in children** — ✓ **bisphosphonates** (osteoclast is the common pathway)\
✗ Wrong options: RANKL antibody, TNF inhibitor, BMP, fluoroquinolone

## X vs Y

| Drug | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Bisphosphonate** | Inhibits osteoclasts |
| **Denosumab** | Anti-RANKL antibody · no matrix binding → rebound on withdrawal |
| **Teriparatide** | PTH analogue → **activates osteoblasts** (anabolic) |
| SERM (raloxifene) | Oestrogen action on bone → ↓ vertebral fractures |
| Corticosteroid | Cause of osteonecrosis |

## How it's asked

**"Non-nitrogen BP (clodronate, etidronate) mechanism for osteoclast apoptosis?"** → **toxic ATP analogue targeting mitochondria**\
✗ Wrong options: farnesyl diphosphate synthase (N-BP)

**"Mechanism of non-nitrogen BPs?"** → **increased osteoclast apoptosis**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ turnover · FPPS

**"Binds hydroxyapatite and prevents protein prenylation?"** → **alendronate**\
✗ Wrong options: calcitonin, PTH, raloxifene

**"N-BPs target?"** → **farnesyl diphosphate synthase**\
✗ Wrong options: HMG-CoA reductase

**"Inhibits prenylation and GTPase formation?"** → **alendronate**\
✗ Wrong options: etidronate

**"Paget's patient on non-nitrogen BP — mechanism?"** → **non-functioning ATP analogue**

**"Mechanism of bisphosphonates?"** → **inhibit osteoclast resorption**

**"N-BPs work by?"** → **GTPase inhibition**

**"BP mechanism in OI?"** → **inhibits osteoclasts**

**"Osteonecrosis hip, drug inhibits FPPS in figure?"** → **nitrogen-containing BP**

**"Denosumab 7 yr, nailed incomplete AFF — drug plan (TO)?"** → **stop denosumab → teriparatide → then short-acting BP**

**"Bowed femur AFF, cephalomedullary nail — critical technique (TO)?"** → **lateralised entry + over-ream 1.5–2.0 mm**

**"Long-term BP → stress fracture where?"** → **subtrochanteric femur**\
✗ Wrong options: jaw (ONJ not stress fracture)

**"Thigh pain then low-energy transverse subtroch fracture?"** → **long-term alendronate**

**"10 yr alendronate, proximal diaphyseal fatigue fracture — aetiology?"** → **prolonged bisphosphonates**\
✗ Wrong options: HCTZ · metastasis · rickets

**"ONJ is a complication of?"** → **alendronate**

**"Finish invasive dental work before starting?"** → **bisphosphonates**\
✗ Wrong options: teriparatide (treats ONJ)

**"BP therapy in myeloma/metastases associated with?"** → **ONJ**\
✗ Wrong options: improved survival

**"Medication ↓ osteolysis after TJA?"** → **bisphosphonates**

**"OI child on cyclical IV pamidronate — associated with?"** → **↑ bone density**\
✗ Wrong options: no change in pain/fractures · osteosarcoma

**"McCune-Albright, bone pain — next treatment?"** → **bisphosphonates**

**"Restart BP 1 week after hip fracture fixation — effect?"** → **no effect on outcomes**

**"Which hip fracture needs contralateral films?"** → **atypical (subtrochanteric) femur fracture**

**"T-score −2.7 + VCF, FPPS inhibitor — hip fracture risk?"** → **↓ ~50%**

**"Osteopetrosis-like changes in children from?"** → **bisphosphonates**

**"Primary excretion of BPs?"** → **renal**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Bone Cells

*Basic Science · 14 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Lineage

| Cell | Origin |
|---|---|
| Osteoblast, osteocyte, osteoprogenitor, chondroblast | **Mesenchymal** stem cell |
| **Osteoclast** (preosteoclast) | **Myeloid haematopoietic** — **monocyte/macrophage** lineage · mononuclear cells **fuse** |

**MSCs form** — bone, cartilage, fat, muscle, tendon (fibroblasts), marrow stroma · **not osteoclasts**

### Osteoprogenitor fate

| Environment | Becomes |
|---|---|
| **Low strain, high O₂** | **Bone (osteoblast)** |
| Intermediate strain, low O₂ | **Cartilage** |
| High strain | Fibrous tissue |

| Transcription factor | Lineage |
|---|---|
| **Runx2 (Cbfa1), Osx** | ✓ **Osteoblast** |
| Sox9 | Chondroblast |
| PPARγ2, C/EBPα | Adipocyte |
| MyoD | Myoblast |
| c-Fos | Osteoclast |

![Osteoprogenitor cell differentiation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9002-osteoprogenitor-cells.jpg)

### Osteoblasts (Runx2, osteocalcin, RANKL, PTH)

**Structure** — ↑ ER, Golgi, mitochondria · **cuboidal**, line immature osteoid · most active at bone surface

**Products** — non-mineralised matrix: **type I collagen**, **alkaline phosphatase**, osteonectin, **osteocalcin** (↑ by 1,25-(OH)₂D)

**Most specific marker of mature osteoblast** — ✓ **osteocalcin** (not in immature, proliferating cells)\
✗ Wrong options: TGF-β · COL2A1 (chondrocyte) · c-Fos (osteoclast) · IL-1

**Differentiation signals** — **BMP** (MSC → osteoprogenitor) · **Runx2** · **stable β-catenin** (→ intramembranous bone) · PDGF · IGF

| Signal on osteoblast | Effect |
|---|---|
| **PTH receptor** | ↑ ALP, type I collagen · → **RANKL** + M-CSF · **intermittent rhPTH (1-34) → anabolic** · chronic ↑ PTH → resorption |
| 1,25-(OH)₂D receptor | ↑ matrix, ALP, **osteocalcin** |
| Oestrogen | ↓ resorption, ↑ formation (inhibits adenylyl cyclase) |
| Glucocorticoids | ↓ collagen + matrix |
| Prostaglandins | ↑ resorption (activate adenylyl cyclase) |

**Coupling** — osteoblast **RANKL → RANK** on osteoclast precursor → resorption · osteoblast **OPG** = decoy for RANKL → ↓ osteoclasts

**Jagged1–Notch** — ✓ **PTH** induces **Jagged1** on osteoblasts → Notch on **HSCs** → HSC proliferation (immune/haematopoietic regulation)

![Osteoblasts lining osteoid](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9002-osteoblast-moved.jpg)

### Osteoclasts (origin, cathepsin K, integrins, RGD)

**Appearance** — **multinucleated giant cell**, monocyte lineage, **ruffled border** · sits in **Howship's lacunae**

**Differentiation** — **M-CSF + RANKL** (progenitors express RANK) · also PU.1, CSF-1

**Activators** — **RANKL** (osteoblasts, tumour cells) · **IL-1** (next to loose TJA implants)

**Inhibitors** — **calcitonin** · IL-10 · OPG

**Acid phosphatase** — ✓ **osteoclast (TRAP)** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: osteoblast · osteocyte

| Step | Molecule |
|---|---|
| **Attachment (sealing zone)** | **Integrins** — **αVβ3 = vitronectin receptor** · bind **RGD (Arg-Gly-Asp)** sequence on vitronectin, fibronectin, osteopontin · **reversible** binding · anti-αVβ3/RGD block resorption |
| Polarisation | Ruffled border · functional secretory domain · basolateral membrane |
| **Mineral dissolution** | **HCl** into resorption lacuna · ATP proton pumps · H⁺ from **carbonic anhydrase II** · Cl⁻ channels · CA deficiency → no resorption |
| **Organic matrix** | **Cathepsin K** (major) · **MMP-9** · lysosomal cysteine proteinases |
| Removal | Transcytosis to FSD · **TRAP** in vesicles → reactive O₂ species destroy collagen |
| End | Apoptosis or return to non-resorbing state |

**Cathepsin K** — function = ✓ **bone resorption** · mutation → **pycnodysostosis** · inhibitors studied for osteoporosis

**Bisphosphonates** — block ruffled border + acid hydrolase production

![Osteoclast zones — sealing zone, ruffled border, resorption lacuna](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9002-osteoclast-zones.jpg)

**Disuse** — ↓ integrin expression → disuse osteoporosis

### Osteocytes

**Origin** — **former osteoblasts trapped in their own matrix** · **90%** of cells in mature skeleton

**Structure** — high nucleus:cytoplasm · long processes through **canaliculi** → **gap junctions** (connexins; cAMP, IP3 pass) · radial around Haversian canal

**Function** — maintain bone matrix · **Ca/P regulation** · no ALP

**Signals** — **stimulated by calcitonin**, **inhibited by PTH**

| Structure | Role |
|---|---|
| **Canaliculi** | ✓ **Osteocyte–osteocyte communication** |
| Haversian canal | Centre of osteon · vessels + nerves |
| Volkmann canal | Perpendicular · links Haversian canals + periosteum |
| Cement line | Boundary of osteon |

## X vs Y

| | Osteoblast | Osteoclast | Osteocyte |
|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Mesenchymal | **Myeloid (monocyte)** | Trapped osteoblast |
| Marker | **Osteocalcin**, ALP | **TRAP**, cathepsin K, calcitonin receptor | No ALP |
| PTH | Receptor ✓ (→ RANKL) | **No receptor** (indirect) | Inhibited |
| Calcitonin | — | Inhibited (receptor ✓) | Stimulated |

| Function | Molecule / cell |
|---|---|
| Osteoclast attachment | **Integrin αVβ3 / vitronectin / RGD** |
| Binds RANKL, prevents RANK activation | **OPG** |
| Chemotaxis in fracture haematoma | PDGF |
| Induces type II collagen + PG in callus | TGF-β |

## How it's asked

**"Acid phosphatase contained in (TO)?"** → **osteoclast**

**"Function of cathepsin K?"** → **bone resorption**\
✗ Wrong options: RANK activation · water absorption

**"Vitronectin receptor function?"** → **osteoclast attachment to bone**\
✗ Wrong options: RANKL interaction · chemotaxis (PDGF) · type II collagen (TGF-β)

**"MSCs can form all EXCEPT?"** → **osteoclasts**

**"Multinucleated giant cells, monocyte lineage, ruffled border?"** → **osteoclasts**

**"True about the cell in Figure A (osteoclast)?"** → **haematopoietic, macrophage lineage**\
✗ Wrong options: mesenchymal (osteoblast) · trapped osteoblast (osteocyte)

**"Integrins function?"** → **osteoclast attachment to bone**\
✗ Wrong options: binds RANKL (OPG)

**"RGD sequence allows?"** → **interaction with integrins**

**"Enzyme used by mature osteoclasts to resorb bone?"** → **cathepsin K**\
✗ Wrong options: ALP, TIMP, osteonectin, osteopontin

**"Lineage and mediator of osteoclast differentiation?"** → **myeloid / RANKL**\
✗ Wrong options: lymphoid · BMP · OPG

**"Cell of myeloid origin?"** → **preosteoclast**

**"Primary function of RGD bone protein?"** → **reversible osteoclast binding to bone**

**"True about normal remodelling (osteopetrosis patient)?"** → **Howship's lacunae form at resorption sites**\
✗ Wrong options: PTH acts directly on osteoclasts · OPG ↑ turnover · TRAP from osteocytes · lamellar → woven

**"Function of Cbfa1/Runx2?"** → **osteoblastic differentiation**

**"Releases RANKL?"** → **osteoblasts**

**"Most specific marker of mature osteoblasts?"** → **osteocalcin**

**"Intermittent rhPTH targets?"** → **osteoblasts**

**"Transcription factor committing to bone?"** → **Runx2**\
✗ Wrong options: PPARγ2, MyoD, Sox9, C/EBPα

**"Activates Jagged1–Notch pathway?"** → **PTH**

**"Osteocyte communication network?"** → **canaliculi**\
✗ Wrong options: Volkmann · cement lines · Haversian

**"Spindled cells in mature osteoid connected by canaliculi?"** → **osteocytes**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Bone Circulation

*Basic Science · 15 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Overview

**Share of cardiac output** — **5–10%** · intraosseous flow **5–20 mL/min/100 g**

**Tenuous supply** — **scaphoid** · **talus** · **femoral head** · **odontoid**

**Three sources (long bone)** — nutrient artery · metaphyseal–epiphyseal · periosteal

![Arterial vascularisation of a long bone](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9005-arterial-vascularization-of-long-bone.jpg)

### Blood supply to physis & diaphysis

| System | Pressure | Supplies |
|---|---|---|
| **Nutrient artery** | **High** | Enters via nutrient foramen → medullary canal → ascending/descending → **inner ⅔ of mature cortex** (Haversian) |
| Metaphyseal–epiphyseal | — | From periarticular plexus (e.g. geniculates) |
| **Periosteal arterioles** | **Low** | **Outer ⅓ of diaphyseal cortex** · linked by Volkmann (perpendicular) + Haversian (parallel) |
| **Perichondrial artery** (of LaCroix) | — | ✓ **Major source of nutrition of growth plate** |
| Epiphyseal artery | — | Proliferative zone of physis |

**Emissary system** — **venous** only · no "emissary artery"

### Direction of flow

| | Arterial | Venous |
|---|---|---|
| **Mature bone** | **Centrifugal** (inside → out; nutrient high-pressure dominates) | **Centripetal** (cortical capillaries → sinusoids → emissary veins) |
| **Immature bone** | **Centripetal** (periosteal dominates) | — |

**↑ Bone blood flow** — hypoxia · hypercapnia · sympathectomy

## 2 Mechanism

### After fracture

| Phase | Flow |
|---|---|
| **Immediate** | **↓ flow** · becomes **centripetal** (nutrient system disrupted, periosteal dominates) |
| Hours–days | **↑ flow** (regional acceleratory phenomenon) · **peaks 2 wk** · normal by **3–5 months** |

### Intramedullary nails

**Unreamed nail** — preserves endosteal supply

**Reaming** — devascularises **inner 50–80%** of cortex · delays endosteal revascularisation

**Loose-fitting nail** — spares cortical perfusion, faster reperfusion · **tight-fitting** → compromised, slow

## How it's asked

**"Disruption interrupts major nutrient supply to growth plate?"** → **perichondrial artery**\
✗ Wrong options: diaphyseal, metaphyseal, synovial

**"Supplies outer third of diaphysis?"** → **periosteal arterioles**\
✗ Wrong options: nutrient (inner ⅔) · emissary artery (doesn't exist) · perichondral (physis)

**"Direction of arterial flow in mature bone?"** → **centrifugal** · immature → **centripetal**

**"Immediately after fracture, flow becomes?"** → **↓ and centripetal**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Bone Grafting

*Basic Science · 16 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Overview

**Volume** — ~**1 million** grafting procedures/yr in the US · growth ~12–13%/yr

**Indications** — fractures, delayed union, **nonunion** · arthrodesis, **spinal fusion** · defects after trauma/tumour

### Graft properties (osteogenic, osteoinductive, osteoconductive)

| Property | Definition | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **Osteoconductive** | **Scaffold** — permits ingrowth of capillaries, perivascular tissue, osteoprogenitors → **creeping substitution** | Allograft, ceramics, DBM |
| **Osteoinductive** | Factors that **recruit + differentiate MSCs** into osteoblasts (bind transmembrane receptors) | **BMP** (TGF-β superfamily), DBM |
| **Osteogenic** | **Living bone-forming cells** — MSCs, osteoblasts, osteocytes | **Fresh autograft, bone marrow aspirate** only |
| Osseointegration | Direct bone–implant contact (Brånemark) · **not a graft property** (TO) | Ca-phosphate-coated stems |

**Most common / most important property (TO)** — ✓ **osteoconduction**

**Live mesenchymal precursor cells** — ✓ only **autograft / bone marrow aspirate**\
✗ Wrong options: fresh-frozen allograft · rhBMP · DBM · Ca phosphate

**Cancellous > cortical** for new bone — larger surface area

| Material | Conductive | Inductive | Osteogenic |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Autograft** (ICBG, **RIA**, local cancellous/cortical) | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| **Bone marrow aspirate** | No (no structure) | Yes (OB) | ✓ **(TO: osteogenic potential)** |
| **DBM** | Yes | Yes | No |
| **BMP** (rhBMP-2, -7) | No | ✓ **only** | No |
| **PRP** | No | "**solely osteoinductive**" (Orthobullets/OB) · TO: mitogenic + angiogenic only, **not true osteoinduction** | No |
| Allograft (freeze-dried, cortical, cancellous, fibular strut) | ✓ **only** | No (fresh/fresh-frozen retain some BMP) | No |
| Ceramics (Ca phosphate, sulfate, carbonate, TCP, HA), polymers | ✓ **only** | No | No |

**Most osteoinductive (of listed)** — **cancellous autograft**

### Diamond concept & graft biology in nonunion (TO)

**Giannoudis Diamond (4 pillars)** — **osteoconductive scaffold** · **osteoinductive mediators** · **osteogenic cells** · **mechanical stability** **(TO)**

**HA/β-TCP + rhBMP-2 + LR-PRP + locking plate** — deficient in ✓ **osteogenesis** (no viable MSCs) → add **autograft or BMAC** **(TO)**

**rhBMP-2** — serine/threonine kinase receptors → **SMAD** → MSC → osteoblast **(TO)**

### Antigenicity

**Antigens** — class I + II MHC on graft cells → host **T cells** · **marrow cells most immunogenic** · endothelium · type I collagen (humoral + cell-mediated) · non-collagenous matrix

**Hydroxyapatite** — no immune response

**Rejection** — **cell-mediated**, MHC incompatibility · greater mismatch → less new bone

**Ligament allograft antigenicity** — related to **bone** > soft tissue → **BPTB for ACL = highest** (also meniscal bone plugs)

## 2 Mechanism

### Graft healing stages

| Stage | Event |
|---|---|
| 1 Inflammation | Necrotic debris → chemotaxis |
| 2 Osteoblast differentiation | From mesenchymal precursors |
| 3 Osteoinduction | Osteoblast + osteoclast stimulation |
| 4 Osteoconduction | Bone forms on scaffold |
| 5 Remodelling | Years |

### Types of autograft

| Type | Facts |
|---|---|
| **Cancellous** | Little structure · ↑ osteoconduction · **rapid incorporation by creeping substitution** |
| **Cortical** | Slow (remodel existing Haversian canals) · structural · interstitial lamellae preserved |
| **Vascularised** | Faster union, cells preserved · **free fibula (peroneal a.)**, free iliac crest (deep circumflex iliac a.), **distal radius 1,2-ICSRA** for scaphoid |

### Structural allograft biology & outcomes

**Incorporation** — particulate > structural · massive cortical graft largely **never remodels, stays devascularised**

**Insufficiency fracture** — **15–30%** (~**25%**) of massive structural allografts

**Retrieved bulk allograft (10 yr)** — junction heals by **bridging external callus** + persistent callus **perpendicular** to junction · little penetration\
✗ Wrong options: osteonal/Haversian remodelling · complete incorporation

**Osteoarticular allograft cartilage at 5 yr** — **completely acellular** (no donor or host chondrocytes)

**Meniscal allograft** — success depends most on **accurate sizing (within 5%)** \
✗ Wrong options: donor cell viability (repopulated by host) · immunosuppression · cryopreservation

## 5 Management

### Types of allograft & processing

| Allograft | Immunogenicity | BMP | Disease transmission | Shelf life |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Fresh** | **Highest** | Preserved | **Highest** | Hours–days |
| **Fresh-frozen** | Less | Preserved (inductive) | Intermediate · **stronger** — better structural | **−20 °C: 1–2 yr** · **−70 °C: up to 5 yr** |
| **Freeze-dried** ("croutons") | **Least** | **Depleted** (purely conductive) | **Lowest** (theoretical HIV 1 : 2.6 billion) | **Indefinite** (5 yr–∞) |

**Shelf life order** — **freeze-dried > fresh-frozen −70 °C > fresh-frozen −20 °C > fresh**

**Processing** — debride soft tissue → **ethanol** wash (removes cells) → **gamma irradiation** (dose-dependent: kills pathogens but ↓ mechanical properties)

| Method | Process |
|---|---|
| **Cryopreservation** | ✓ **Chemicals (DMSO/glycerol) displace cellular water + controlled-rate freezing** → no ice crystals · keeps some chondrocytes (osteoarticular) |
| Freeze-drying | Water → alcohol to 5% moisture → vacuum (sublimation) · alters growth-factor proteins |
| Fresh-frozen | Frozen twice, packaged dry at −80 °C |
| Fresh | Not frozen · max viability, short shelf life |

**DBM vs freeze-dried allograft** — DBM additionally **osteoinductive** (factors bind transmembrane receptors on MSCs) · neither is osteogenic

**DBM** — **acid extraction** of allograft → removes mineral, keeps collagen + non-collagenous proteins (**BMPs, TGF-β, IGF, FGF**, residual Ca) · no structural support · **inter-lot variability**

![Demineralised bone matrix](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9011-dbm.jpg)

### Bone graft substitutes (calcium sulfate, calcium phosphate, HA)

**All ceramics** — osteoconductive void fillers · **scaffold for progenitor cells** · **low shear, tensile, torsional strength** · biocompatible with steel + titanium · used in spinal fusion

**Resorption (fastest → slowest)** — **calcium sulfate > tricalcium phosphate > hydroxyapatite**

| Substitute | Resorption | Strength | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Calcium sulfate** (plaster of Paris, OsteoSet) | **4–12 wk** (5–7 wk; gone by 6 wk) · **passive dissolution** · **faster than bone grows** | ≈ cancellous | **Sterile serous wound drainage** (osmotic load) **(TO)** · fails alone in nonunion |
| **β-TCP** | **6–18 months** · **cell (osteoclast)-mediated** **(TO)** · dissolution + fragmentation | ≤ cancellous · brittle | Less bone formed than resorbed → used as adjunct |
| **Calcium phosphate cement** | ~1 yr (1–10 yr) | **Highest compressive — 4–10× cancellous** | Hardens to **dahllite** · **early WB** without subsidence (plateau, calcaneus) · **osseointegration + osteoconduction** (stem coatings) · reduce + fix **before** injecting |
| **Coralline HA** (Interpore) | Very slow (decades) | Slightly > cancellous | Coral CaCO₃ → HA by hydrothermal exchange · **neovascularisation + appositional bone** (✗ Wrong options: creeping substitution · dissolution) |
| Calcium carbonate (Biocoral) | — | — | Unaltered coral · conductive only |
| Collagen matrix + HA coating | Collagen fast, HA slow | < cancellous | — |
| Aluminium oxide | — | — | Bonds to bone under stress/strain |

**Level I evidence** — Ca-phosphate fills defects, allows early rehab, **prevents articular subsidence** (distal radius, tibial plateau)

**Ca-phosphate cure (TO)** — isothermal / mildly exothermic · no PMMA-type thermal necrosis

**HO** — complication of **BMP**, not calcium sulfate **(TO)**

### Bone morphogenetic proteins

**rhBMP-2, rhBMP-7** — FDA-approved, long bones + spine · **solely osteoinductive** · perivascular MSC → osteoblast via **serine-threonine kinase receptors**

**Complications** — under/over-production of bone · inflammation · early bone resorption · near-zero disease transmission (synthetic)

### Autograft harvest (iliac crest, RIA)

**Autograft** — **gold standard** · all three properties · **least immunogenic**

**Iliac crest** — commonest site · cortical + cancellous · complications **2–36%**

**Anterior vs posterior** — **anterior = more complications** (pain, gait) · **posterior = more stem cells** (viability same), less pain

| Structure at risk | Where |
|---|---|
| **Superior cluneal nerves** — **commonest posterior complication** (buttock numbness) | Cross crest **>8 cm lateral to PSIS** → ✓ **stay within 8 cm** **(TO)** |
| **Superior gluteal artery** | Retractor/elevator slipping **caudally into sciatic notch** · brisk bleeding (✗ Wrong options: vein · inferior gluteal) |
| SI joint posterior ligaments | Full-thickness posterior graft → **SI instability/dislocation** |
| **LFCN**, ilioinguinal nerve | **Anterior** crest |
| Deep circumflex iliac artery | Anterior crest |
| Corona mortis | Anterior intrapelvic approaches |
| **External iliac artery** | ✓ **Not at risk** **(TO)** |

**Other complications** — haematoma · hernia · infection · fracture · cosmetic defect · chronic pain

**Vascularised autograft sources** — **fibula, ribs**

### Reamer–irrigator–aspirator (RIA)

| RIA vs ICBG | |
|---|---|
| Volume | **Higher** (~50–90 mL per femur) |
| Union | **Comparable (~90%)** |
| Operative time | **Shorter** · technically easier |
| Immediate complications | **Fewer** |
| Blood loss | **Higher → transfusion** (up to 44%) |
| Structural support | **Less** (fragments ≤2 mm) |
| Equipment | More expensive |
| Biology | All three properties · **>95% cell viability** · ≥ ICBG growth factors/MSCs · similar RNA transcription profile |
| Specific risks | **Femoral shaft fracture** (eccentric reaming) · insertion-site (abductor) pain |

**RIA false statement (TO)** — false: "**heat injury from reaming abolishes osteoinductive properties**" · RIA is a viable alternative to cancellous autograft, product of long-bone nailing, osteogenic **(TO)**

![RIA harvesting from the femoral canal](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9011-ria-ap-view.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Allograft disease transmission & processing

| Pathogen | Risk (fresh-frozen bone) |
|---|---|
| **HIV** | **1 in 1,000,000 – 1,670,000** (1 in 1.6 million despite screening) · last bone transmission 1985 |
| **Hepatitis C** | 1 in 100,000 · last 2000 |
| **Hepatitis B** | 1 in 63,000 |
| HTLV | One case (improper screening) |
| CJD | **Never** in bone (cornea, dura only) |
| Bacteria | Up to 12% massive allograft · 0.7% morselised · Clostridium (2001) |

**AATB required serology** — ✓ **HIV, HBV, HCV, syphilis** (+ HTLV-I/II)\
✗ Wrong options: CMV · hepatitis A

**Donor exclusions (TO)** — malignancy (except skin BCC) · collagen vascular disease · metabolic bone disease · toxins

**Positive preimplantation culture (CoNS), no clinical infection** — ✓ **observe** · 4.8–13.3% positive, none became infected · routine culture not recommended

**Calcium sulfate** — ↑ **serous wound drainage**

## X vs Y

| | DBM | Freeze-dried allograft | BMP | BMA |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Conductive | Yes | Yes | No | No |
| Inductive | Yes | No | Yes | Yes |
| Osteogenic | No | No | No | Yes |

| | Calcium sulfate | β-TCP | Calcium phosphate cement | HA |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Resorption | **Fastest (weeks)** | 6–18 mo | ~1 yr | Decades |
| Mechanism | Passive dissolution | Osteoclasts | — | Appositional |
| Strength | ≈ cancellous | ≤ cancellous | **Highest** | > cancellous |
| Signature | **Serous drainage** | Adjunct | **Early WB** | Coral-derived |

## How it's asked

**"Contains live mesenchymal precursor cells?"** → **autologous iliac crest marrow aspirate**

**"Most osteoinductive?"** → **cancellous autograft**

**"Least immune response?"** → **freeze-dried cancellous chips**\
✗ Wrong options: fresh Achilles (highest)

**"Alternative to autograft acting by osteoinduction?"** → **BMP**

**"Osteoconductive graft property?"** → **permits capillary + perivascular ingrowth**

**"Osteoinductive + osteoconductive for plateau void?"** → **DBM**\
✗ Wrong options: Ca phosphate · BMP · allograft

**"Solely osteoinductive?"** → **PRP** (OB) ·\
✗ Wrong options: DBM · freeze-dried · ICBG

**"Osteogenic + conductive + inductive adjuvant?"** → **RIA bone**\
✗ Wrong options: BMA (no conduction) · DBM · fibular strut · BMP

**"Conductive + inductive, not osteogenic?"** → **DBM**

**"DBM differs from freeze-dried allograft by?"** → **osteoinductive factors**

**"RIA autograft capacities?"** → **osteogenic, osteoconductive, osteoinductive**

**"Ceramic degradation + complication of calcium sulfate (TO)?"** → **β-TCP osteoclast-mediated 6–18 mo · CaSO₄ passive dissolution → sterile serous exudate**

**"Fastest-resorbing substitute?"** → **calcium sulfate**

**"Disappears most quickly in vivo (plateau)?"** → **calcium sulfate**

**"Resorbs faster than bone grows?"** → **calcium sulfate**

**"True of both Ca phosphate and sulfate?"** → **scaffold for progenitor cells**\
✗ Wrong options: shear/torsion resistant · contraindicated in fusion

**"CaSO₄ vs autograft in plateau — expected ↑?"** → **serous drainage complications**

**"Permits earliest WB without subsidence?"** → **calcium phosphate**

**"Porous HA incorporation?"** → **neovascularisation + appositional bone growth**

**"Highest early compressive strength?"** → **calcium phosphate**

**"Osseointegration stem coating?"** → **calcium phosphate**

**"HIV risk, fresh-frozen bone?"** → **1 in 1,000,000** · "despite screening" → **1 in 1.6 million** · "1 in 1.67 million" → **HIV**

**"AATB required serology?"** → **HBV, HCV, HIV, syphilis**

**"BPTB allograft culture + CoNS, well patient?"** → **observation**

**"Cryopreservation achieved by?"** → **chemicals remove water + controlled-rate freezing**

**"Decreasing shelf life?"** → **freeze-dried > FF −70 °C > FF −20 °C > fresh**

**"Highest theoretical disease transmission?"** → **fresh allograft**

**"Donor chondrocytes in osteoarticular allograft at 5 yr?"** → **none — acellular**

**"Structural allograft insufficiency fractures?"** → **15–30%**

**"Histology at bulk allograft junction, 10 yr?"** → **bridging external callus + perpendicular callus**

**"Highest antigenicity knee allograft?"** → **BPTB for ACL**

**"Most critical for meniscal allograft success?"** → **accurate graft size**

**"NOT true of RIA (TO)?"** → **"heat injury abolishes osteoinductive properties"**

**"Commonest posterior ICBG complication avoided by (TO)?"** → **staying within 8 cm of PSIS**

**"Not at risk in ICBG (TO)?"** → **external iliac artery**

**"Anterior vs posterior iliac crest harvest?"** → **anterior: ↑ complications**

**"Posterior marrow aspiration vs anterior?"** → **↑ number of stem cells**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ viability

**"Elevator slips caudally, brisk bleeding (posterior crest)?"** → **superior gluteal artery**

**"RIA vs ICBG — true?"** → **↑ blood loss needing transfusion** · **greater volume** · equal union · shorter time · fewer immediate complications

**"Most common property of a bone graft (TO)?"** → **osteoconduction**\
✗ Wrong options: osseointegration (not a graft property)

**"Substitute with osteogenic potential (TO)?"** → **bone marrow aspirate**\
✗ Wrong options: DBM, PRP, BMP

**"HA/β-TCP + rhBMP-2 + LR-PRP — missing Diamond pillar (TO)?"** → **osteogenesis**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Bone Growth Factors

*Basic Science · 17 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Overview of growth factors

| Factor | Source | Receptor | Function |
|---|---|---|---|
| **TGF-β** | Platelets, bone ECM, cartilage | **Serine-threonine kinase** | Pleiotropic · **undifferentiated MSC proliferation** · type II collagen + PG by MSCs · osteoblast collagen synthesis · in fracture haematoma · coats porous implants |
| **BMP** | Osteoprogenitors, osteoblasts, bone ECM | **Serine-threonine kinase** → **SMADs** | ✓ **MSC → chondrocytes + osteoblasts** · skeletal patterning |
| **FGF** (FGF-1, -2 most abundant) | Macrophages, MSCs, chondrocytes, osteoblasts | **Tyrosine kinase** | **Mitogenic** · angiogenesis · early fracture healing |
| **IGF-1** (somatomedin C) | Bone matrix, osteoblasts, chondrocytes | **Tyrosine kinase** | **Most abundant GF in bone** · GH–IGF-1 → physeal **proliferation without maturation** (linear growth) · intramembranous defects |
| **IGF-2** | — | Tyrosine kinase | **More potent than IGF-1** · type I collagen, cartilage matrix, proliferation, bone |
| **PDGF** | Platelets, osteoblasts | **Tyrosine kinase** | Mitogen · **macrophage/inflammatory-cell chemotaxis** to fracture |
| **PPAR-γ** | — | **Nuclear receptor** (binds DNA) | **Adipogenic** differentiation |
| **Wnt** | — | **LRP5/6** → β-catenin to nucleus | Osteoblast differentiation |

**Thyroid hormone** — nuclear receptor → physeal **proliferation and maturation**

**Morphogens vs mitogens (disc)** — BMP-2, BMP-7, TGF-β change phenotype (↑ chondrogenic phenotype + matrix) without proliferation · IGF-1, FGF ↑ proliferation

### BMP signalling (SMADs, serine-threonine kinase)

**Family** — TGF-β superfamily (with GDFs, AMH, activin, Nodal) · ~30 members

**Target** — undifferentiated **perivascular MSCs**

**Cascade** — BMP → heterodimer of **type I + II serine-threonine kinase receptors** → phosphorylate **SMAD 1, 5, 8** → complex with **SMAD 4** → nucleus → osteoblast genes · 8 SMADs known

![BMP receptor → SMAD signalling](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9086-bmp-and-smad.jpg)

| BMP | Fact |
|---|---|
| **BMP-2, 4, 6, 7** | **Osteoinductive** (BMP-2 potent osteogenic + osteoinductive) |
| **BMP-3** | **Not osteoinductive** · **antagonises BMP-2** · **most abundant BMP in DBM** · knockout → 2× trabecular bone · ↑ chondrogenic markers (type II collagen, aggrecan) |
| **BMP-1** | **Metalloprotease** (protease for collagen I, II, III) · not bone-inducing |
| **BMP-4** | Altered signalling → **fibrodysplasia ossificans progressiva** |

### Lineage factors

| Factor | Drives |
|---|---|
| BMP, **RUNX2**, Wnt/β-catenin, ATF4 (with Runx2) | **Osteogenic** |
| **SOX9** | Chondrogenic |
| **PPAR-γ** | ✓ **Adipogenic** (pre-adipocyte → adipocyte) · inhibited by Wnt5a |

**Thiazolidinediones** — **PPAR-γ agonists** → ↑ marrow adiposity, **↓ osteoblastogenesis** → **↓ BMD** (femoral neck, hip), ↑ fracture risk

**GSK-3 inhibition** — ↑ β-catenin → ↑ osteoblasts\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ BMD

## 5 Management

### rhBMP-2 clinical use

| Product | FDA-approved use |
|---|---|
| **rhBMP-2** | **Single-level ALIF L2–S1** for DDD with **LT-Cage** |
| **rhBMP-2** | **Open tibial shaft fracture + IM nail, within 14 days** |
| rhBMP-7 (HDE) | Recalcitrant long-bone nonunion where autograft unfeasible + alternatives failed |
| rhBMP-7 (HDE) | Revision posterolateral lumbar fusion in compromised hosts (osteoporosis, smoking, DM) |

**Not approved (rhBMP-2)** — plates (MIPO tibia) · posterior/posterolateral lumbar · multilevel · **cervical**

**PLF in >60 yr** — rhBMP-2 **cost-effective** vs ICBG and ↓ revision surgery (off-label)

**Contraindications** — pregnancy · allergy to **bovine type I collagen** or rhBMP-2 · infection · tumour · **skeletal immaturity**

**Concerns** — **uncontrolled heterotopic bone** · cost

## 6 Complications

### BMP-4 & FOP

**FOP ("stone man")** — altered **BMP-4** signalling → massive spontaneous HO · no cure · excision helps mobility/seating

| Disease | Gene |
|---|---|
| **FOP** | **BMP-4 signalling** |
| Mazabraud | **GNAS1** (polyostotic FD + intramuscular myxomas) |
| Ollier | PTHrP / Indian hedgehog |
| Osteopetrosis | **CLCN7** |
| Osteopoikilosis | **LEMD3** |

## X vs Y

| Serine-threonine kinase receptor | Tyrosine kinase receptor |
|---|---|
| **TGF-β, BMP** | **FGF, IGF-1/2, PDGF** |

## How it's asked

**"All true of BMP-3 EXCEPT?"** → **"it is osteoinductive"**

**"BMP receptor class?"** → **serine-threonine kinase**\
✗ Wrong options: tyrosine kinase, MAPK, aurora, GSK-3

**"Phosphorylated directly after BMP binding?"** → **SMADs**\
✗ Wrong options: Myc · β-catenin (Wnt) · adenylate cyclase

**"Serine-threonine receptor, MSC metaplasia into bone-forming cells?"** → **BMP**\
✗ Wrong options: IGF-2, PDGF (tyrosine kinase) · OPG · RANKL

**"Osteoinduction and osteogenesis?"** → **BMP-2**\
✗ Wrong options: BMP-1 (metalloprotease) · BMP-3 · PPAR-γ · IGF-1

**"Promotes MSC → chondrocytes + osteoblasts?"** → **BMP**\
✗ Wrong options: TGF-β (proliferation) · FGF (mitogen)

**"↑ chondrogenic phenotype of disc cells?"** → **BMPs**

**"FDA-approved rhBMP-2 scenario?"** → **L5–S1 ALIF for DDD**\
✗ Wrong options: plated open tibia · posterolateral · cervical

**"rhBMP-2 true EXCEPT?"** → **"FDA indicated for single-level posterolateral lumbar fusion"**

**"Binds LRP5/6?"** → **Wnt**

**"Drives MSCs to adipose?"** → **PPAR-γ**\
✗ Wrong options: BMP, RUNX2, SOX9, Wnt

**"Thiazolidinedione effect on BMD?"** → **activates PPAR-γ → adipocytes ↑, osteoblastogenesis ↓, BMD ↓**

**"Altered BMP-4 signalling?"** → **fibrodysplasia ossificans progressiva**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Bone Matrix

*Basic Science · 18 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Composition of bone

| Component | % dry weight | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| **Organic** | **40%** | Collagen (90% of organic) · proteoglycans · matrix proteins · cytokines/GFs |
| **Inorganic** | **60%** | **Calcium hydroxyapatite Ca₁₀(PO₄)₆(OH)₂** · osteocalcium phosphate (**brushite**) |

### Osteocalcin & matrix components

| Component | Role |
|---|---|
| **Type I collagen** | **Tensile strength** · triple helix: **two α1 + one α2** chains |
| **Proteoglycans** | ✓ **Compressive strength** (with HA) · **inhibit mineralisation** · bind growth factors · GAG–protein complexes |
| **Hydroxyapatite** | **Compressive strength** |
| **Osteocalcin** | **Most abundant non-collagenous protein** (10–20%) · by **mature osteoblasts** · promotes mineralisation · regulates bone density · attracts osteoclasts · ↑ by **1,25-(OH)₂D₃**, ↓ by **PTH** · **marker of bone formation/turnover** (serum, urine) |
| **Osteonectin** | **Glycoprotein that binds calcium** · from **platelets + osteoblasts** · regulates Ca / organises mineral |
| **Osteopontin** | **Cell-binding** protein |
| Cytokines / GFs | IL-1, IL-6, IGF, TGF-β, BMPs |

![Type I collagen triple helix](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9003-type-i-collagen-moved.jpg)

**Osteocalcin clinical** — **most specific marker of mature osteoblast** · serum levels track **BMD gain on osteoporosis treatment** and anabolic trials (teriparatide)

## X vs Y

| | Osteocalcin | Osteonectin |
|---|---|---|
| Source | **Osteoblasts** (✗ Wrong options: osteoclasts) | Platelets + osteoblasts |
| Key fact | Most abundant non-collagenous protein | **Ca-binding glycoprotein** |

| Strength | Provided by |
|---|---|
| **Tensile** | **Type I collagen** |
| **Compressive** | **Proteoglycans + hydroxyapatite** |

## How it's asked

**"Osteocalcin true EXCEPT?"** → **"glycoprotein that binds calcium"** (that is osteonectin)

**"Osteocalcin true EXCEPT (2)?"** → **"secreted by osteoclasts"**

**"Most responsible for compressive strength?"** → **proteoglycans**\
✗ Wrong options: type I collagen (tensile) · osteocalcin · osteonectin · osteopontin

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Bone Remodeling

*Basic Science · 19 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Remodelling laws & cortical vs cancellous turnover

| Law | Statement |
|---|---|
| **Wolff's law** | Bone **remodels/adapts to the loads (mechanical stress)** placed on it · ↑ load → stronger · ↓ load → osteopenia · osteocytes sense load |
| **Davis's law** | **Soft tissues** heal according to how they are mechanically stressed **(TO)** |
| **Hueter–Volkmann** | **Compression on physis slows growth**, tension/distraction accelerates (immature bone) |
| **Delpech's law** | **Tension on physis speeds growth** **(TO)** |
| **Piezoelectric** | **Compression side electronegative → osteoblasts (formation)** · tension side electropositive → osteoclasts |
| Maxwell's law | **Not a physeal law** **(TO)** |

**Law governing remodelling (TO)** — ✓ **both Wolff's and Davis's** (bone and soft tissue)

### Remodelling mechanism

**Basic multicellular unit (BMU, Frost)** — osteoclasts + osteoblasts + osteocytes in the remodelling cavity

| | **Cortical bone** | **Cancellous bone** |
|---|---|---|
| Mechanism | **Osteoclastic tunnelling — cutting cone** (head = osteoclasts → capillaries → osteoblasts fill = closing cone → cement line) | **Osteoclastic resorption on trabecular surface** + osteoblastic lamellae |
| Cycle (TO) | **Shorter — median ~120 days** | ~**200 days** |

![Cutting cone — osteoclasts lead, osteoblasts follow](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9008-cutting-cone-moved.jpg)

**Turnover extremes (TO)** — **~100 days** in thyrotoxicosis, 1° hyperparathyroidism · **>1,000 days** in myxoedema and **after bisphosphonates** (slower, not "faster")

### Sclerostin & Wnt pathway

**Sclerostin** — glycoprotein from **osteocytes** · ✓ **inhibits osteoblastogenesis → ↓ bone formation**

**Sclerostin + Dkk-1** — block Wnt binding to **LRP5/6** → ✓ inhibit **Wnt/β-catenin** pathway

**Wnt on** — β-catenin not phosphorylated → accumulates → nucleus → osteoblast genes · also ↑ **OPG**

**Knockout / inhibition of sclerostin or Dkk-1** — ↑ bone mass

### Age-related changes

**Ageing cortex** — **↓ cortical thickness/area**, **↑ medullary canal** volume (bone volume constant) → ↑ fracture risk

## 2 Mechanism

### Calcium, vitamin D & calcitonin

**Sunlight** — UV converts **7-dehydrocholesterol → cholecalciferol (D3)** in skin → liver 25-OH → kidney 1,25-(OH)₂D

**↓ Sun exposure** — ✓ **impaired GI calcium absorption** (no 1,25-D) · liver/kidney hydroxylation intact · PTH **↑**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓

**Phosphate ↓ urinary Ca** — ✓ **complexes with Ca in the intestine** (less absorbed) · also ↑ PTH, complexes in bone/soft tissue (↓ filtered load)

**Calcitonin** — ✓ **interferes with osteoclast maturation** (receptor on osteoclasts) · ↓ renal Ca + phosphate reabsorption · chondroprotective (MMP inhibition) · no osteoblast receptor · no major gut Ca effect · used in **Paget's when bisphosphonate-intolerant**

### Anabolic therapy — intermittent PTH

**Direct anabolic** — ✓ **intermittent PTH**\
✗ Wrong options: bisphosphonate, calcitonin (antiresorptive) · corticosteroid · TNF-α inhibitor

**Continuous ↑ PTH** — bone resorption

**TNF-α** — ↓ osteoblasts, ↑ osteoclastic resorption

**Excess cortisol** — ↓ gut Ca absorption, ↑ renal Ca loss, ↓ matrix formation, secondary hyperparathyroidism

### Bone healing

**Rigid compression plating (absolute stability)** — ✓ **primary healing by Haversian remodelling** (osteonal cutting cones cross fracture) · no visible callus

**Relative stability (IM nail)** — **secondary healing, callus**, endochondral ossification

## 5 Management

### Hueter–Volkmann principle

**Clinical uses** — **tension-band (8-) plate guided growth** · scoliosis progression (concave side compressed → slower growth) · **Scheuermann extension brace** (anterior bodies in tension → growth corrects wedging)

## X vs Y

| | Wolff | Hueter–Volkmann |
|---|---|---|
| Governs | **Remodelling** (mature + immature) | **Longitudinal growth** (immature physis) |
| Stimulus | Intermittent ↑ stress → apposition | Sustained compression → growth suppression |

| | Primary healing | Secondary healing |
|---|---|---|
| Stability | Absolute (compression plate) | Relative (nail, cast) |
| Mechanism | **Haversian / cutting cones** | **Callus, endochondral** |

## How it's asked

**"Direct anabolic effect on bone?"** → **intermittent PTH**\
✗ Wrong options: bisphosphonate, calcitonin, corticosteroid, TNF inhibitor

**"Inhibits osteoblastogenesis to ↓ bone formation?"** → **sclerostin**\
✗ Wrong options: RANKL, IL-1, Wnt, OPG

**"Sclerostin + Dkk-1 inhibit?"** → **Wnt/β-catenin**

**"Physeal growth modulation by compression/tension (TO)?"** → **Hueter–Volkmann**

**"Law NOT pertaining to growth plate (TO, FNB 2023)?"** → **Maxwell's law**

**"Scheuermann extension brace corrects wedging by?"** → **Hueter–Volkmann principle**\
✗ Wrong options: Hooke, Kirchhoff, Wolff

**"Tension-band plate relies on?"** → **Hueter–Volkmann**\
✗ Wrong options: cutting cones · Wolff · piezoelectric

**"Phosphate ↓ urinary Ca by?"** → **intestinal Ca–phosphate complex**

**"↓ Sun exposure harms bone by?"** → **↓ GI calcium absorption**

**"Calcitonin mechanism (Paget's)?"** → **interferes with osteoclast maturation**

**"Rigid compression plating — healing type?"** → **primary, Haversian remodelling**

**"25-yr-old vs 85-yr-old femur?"** → **thicker cortex, smaller canal**

**"Law governing remodelling (TO)?"** → **both Wolff's and Davis's**

**"True about remodelling (TO)?"** → **cortical cycle shorter than cancellous**\
✗ Wrong options: cutting cones in cancellous · bisphosphonates speed remodelling

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Bone Signaling & RANKL

*Basic Science · 20 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### RANK–RANKL–OPG axis

**Central pathway** — couples osteoblast and osteoclast activity · resorption stimulated by **RANKL (normal)** · **PTHrP (pathological — metastases)**

**RANKL** — major source = ✓ **osteoblasts** (+ marrow stromal cells, **activated T cells**, osteocytes) · binds **RANK** on osteoclast precursors + mature osteoclasts · RANKL + **M-CSF** → fusion into multinucleated osteoclasts · secreted in response to **inflammatory stimuli** · TNF superfamily

**OPG (osteoprotegerin)** — **decoy receptor** from **osteoblasts + stromal cells** · ✓ **binds and sequesters RANKL** → ↓ osteoclast differentiation, fusion, activation

**Primary stimulus for osteoclastic resorption** — ✓ **RANK–RANKL interaction**\
✗ Wrong options: PTH–osteoclast direct · OPG–RANKL

![RANK, RANKL and OPG](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9010-rankl-mechanism-moved.jpg)

**Osteoclast transcription factors** — PU.1, NF-κB, AP-1, **NFATc1**, Mitf, Myc, Src · **DC-STAMP** required for fusion

### Osteoclast activators vs inhibitors

| Stimulate resorption | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **RANKL** | Binds RANK |
| **PTH** | Receptor on **osteoblasts** → **adenylyl cyclase/cAMP/PKA** → ↑ RANKL + M-CSF, ↓ OPG |
| **IL-1** | ↑ osteoclast differentiation |
| **1,25-(OH)₂D** | ↑ RANKL expression · terminal osteoclast differentiation |
| **PGE2** | Activates adenylyl cyclase |
| **IL-6, MIP-1α** | **Myeloma** |
| TNF-α | ↑ resorption (✗ Wrong options: not an inhibitor) |
| Thyroid hormone, GH | Systemic stimulators |

| Inhibit resorption | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **OPG** | Sequesters RANKL |
| **Calcitonin** | ✓ **Binds directly to receptor on osteoclast** → loss of ruffled border, retraction, ↓ number + activity · from thyroid **parafollicular cells** · ↑ by hypercalcaemia, gastrin, pentagastrin · on osteocytes: ↓ apoptosis, ↑ sclerostin |
| **Oestrogen** | ↓ RANKL · inhibits adenylyl cyclase · also anabolic |
| **TGF-β** | ↑ OPG |
| **IL-10** | Suppresses osteoclasts |
| Androgens | Systemic |
| Bisphosphonates | Osteoclast apoptosis |
| **Denosumab** | **Anti-RANKL IgG2** (OPG-like) |

**Dexamethasone / corticosteroids** — ↓ OPG → ↑ osteoclast formation + longevity → osteoporosis

### Wnt/β-catenin signalling (TO)

**Wnt** — binds **frizzled + LRP5/6** → β-catenin accumulates → binds **lymphoid enhancer factor (LEF)** → bone-formation genes + **OPG** **(TO)**

**Wnt antagonists** — **Dkk-1**, soluble frizzled-related proteins (**sFRP**), **sclerostin (SOST)** **(TO)**

| Favour formation (TO) | Favour resorption (TO) | Coupling factors (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| **OPG, BMPs, Wnt, β-catenin–LEF complex** | RANK, RANKL, M-CSF, DC-STAMP, Dkk-1, sFRP, sclerostin | **TGF-β** (released + activated by acid during resorption) · **ephrinB2** (osteoclast) → **EphB4** (osteoblast) |

**Does NOT inhibit bone formation (TO)** — ✓ **β-catenin–LEF complex**\
✗ Wrong options: sclerostin, Dkk-1, RANKL

### Smad signalling

**BMP-2 cascade** — receptor phosphorylates **regulatory Smad 1, 5, 8** → complex with ✓ **co-Smad 4** (not phosphorylated) → nucleus

**Smad 6, 7** — **inhibitory** · Smad 2, 3 — TGF-β pathway

### Second messengers

**Adenylyl cyclase / cAMP** — ✓ **PTH** (Gαs/PKA in osteocytes) · PGE2

**Serine-threonine kinase** — BMP, TGF-β (→ SMADs) · **Tyrosine kinase** — IGF-II

### Osteoporosis drugs by target

| Drug | Target |
|---|---|
| **Calcitonin** | **Directly binds osteoclast** |
| **Denosumab** | RANKL antibody |
| **Romosozumab** | **Sclerostin antibody** → activates Wnt |
| **Teriparatide** | PTH analogue → **activates osteoblasts** |
| Vitamin D | Osteoclast differentiation + osteoid mineralisation |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Genes & lab markers

**Alkaline phosphatase** — from **bone (osteoblasts) + liver** · bone ↑: Paget's, metastases, osteomalacia · liver ↑: hepatitis, cirrhosis, **liver metastases**, biliary obstruction

**↑ ALP, negative bone scan, known cancer** — ✓ check **ALT/AST** (liver)\
✗ Wrong options: CEA · PSA · CT hip · MRI brain

**Antibodies to RANKL or sclerostin** — ↑ BMD

## 6 Complications

### Clinical implications

| Condition | RANKL-pathway link |
|---|---|
| **Osteoporosis** | **Loss of OPG function** → constitutive osteoclast activation · **OPG knockout mice → osteoporosis** |
| **Osteopetrosis** | Absent osteoclastic resorption · **RANKL knockout → osteopetrosis-like** · not OPG knockout |
| Paget's | Altered RANK cytoplasmic binding or **OPG mutations** |
| **Lytic metastases** | ✓ **Cancer cells directly produce RANKL** · OPG blockade ↓ metastasis (animals) · denosumab ↓ pathological fractures |
| **TJA osteolysis** | PE debris → macrophages → cytokines incl. **RANKL** → osteoclasts |
| **Inflammatory arthropathy** (RA pannus, gout tophi) | IL-6, IL-8, TNF-α · ↑ RANKL, ↓ OPG |

**OPG overexpression would help** — ✓ **osteoporosis** (also Paget's, metastases)\
✗ Wrong options: osteopetrosis, rickets, achondroplasia, scurvy

## X vs Y

| | RANKL | OPG | RANK |
|---|---|---|---|
| Made by | Osteoblasts, T cells, tumour | Osteoblasts, stromal cells | — (receptor) |
| Located | Ligand | Soluble decoy | Osteoclast precursors |
| Effect | ↑ resorption | ↓ resorption | Mediates differentiation |
| Knockout | **Osteopetrosis-like** | **Osteoporosis** | — |

## How it's asked

**"How OPG affects RANKL-mediated resorption?"** → **inhibits by binding RANKL directly**\
✗ Wrong options: binds RANK

**"Major source of RANKL?"** → **osteoblasts**\
✗ Wrong options: osteoclasts · integrins · macrophages

**"Lytic breast metastasis in humerus — involved molecule?"** → **RANK**\
✗ Wrong options: OPG, IL-4, TNF-α, Sox9

**"True of OPG?"** → **binds and sequesters RANKL**\
✗ Wrong options: from osteoclasts · ↑ resorption · knockout → osteopetrosis

**"Why metastases are lytic?"** → **cancer cells directly produce RANKL**\
✗ Wrong options: osteoclasts produce RANKL

**"Cells producing RANKL?"** → **osteoblasts**

**"Osteoclastogenic factor secreted by osteoblasts after inflammation?"** → **RANKL**

**"Primary stimulus for osteoclastic resorption?"** → **RANK–RANKL interaction**

**"Function of OPG?"** → **sequesters RANKL**\
✗ Wrong options: adenylyl cyclase (oestrogen) · binds osteoclasts (calcitonin) · ↑ RANKL (PTH)

**"Drug causing OPG overexpression helps?"** → **osteoporosis**

**"Mediator reducing bone resorption?"** → **calcitonin**\
✗ Wrong options: RANKL, IL-6, PTH, dexamethasone

**"Calcitonin mechanism?"** → **directly binds osteoclast receptor → ↓ activity**

**"Osteoporosis drug binding osteoclasts directly?"** → **calcitonin**\
✗ Wrong options: denosumab, romosozumab, teriparatide, vitamin D

**"All inhibit osteoclasts EXCEPT?"** → **TNF-α**

**"Uses adenylyl cyclase in osteocytes?"** → **PTH**\
✗ Wrong options: SMADs, IGF-II, BMP-2, TGF-β

**"↑ ALP, negative bone scan, metastatic lung cancer — next test?"** → **ALT/AST**

**"Complexes with Smad 1/5/8?"** → **Smad 4**\
✗ Wrong options: Smad 6/7 (inhibitory)

**"Does NOT inhibit bone formation (TO)?"** → **β-catenin–LEF complex**
