# Brachial Plexus

*Basic Science · 21 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Variants

| Type | Roots | Frequency |
|---|---|---|
| **Standard** | C5–T1 | **77%** |
| Prefixed | + C3, C4 | 22% |
| Postfixed | Below T1 | 1% |

### Anatomy — "Robert Turner Drinks Cold Beer"

**Roots (5)** — ventral rami **C5–T1** · superior + posterior to subclavian artery

**Interscalene course** — ✓ exits between **scalenus anterior and scalenus medius** (+ first rib) **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: medius/posterior · medius/levator scapulae

**Trunks (3)** — superior (C5, 6) · middle (C7) · inferior (C8, T1)

**Divisions (6)** — 3 anterior + 3 posterior

**Cords (3)** — posterior (all 3 posterior divisions) · lateral (anterior superior + middle, C5–7) · medial (anterior inferior, C8, T1)

![Brachial plexus with branches](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10052-brachial-plexus-with-sensory-566-moved.jpg)

### Branches by level

| Level | Branch | Supplies |
|---|---|---|
| Root | **Dorsal scapular (C5)** | Levator scapulae, rhomboids |
| Root | **Long thoracic (C5–7)** | Serratus anterior |
| Superior trunk | **Suprascapular (C5, 6)** | Supra- + infraspinatus, AC + GH joints (via suprascapular notch) |
| Superior trunk | Nerve to subclavius (C5, 6) | Subclavius |
| Lateral cord | Lateral pectoral (C5–7) | Pectoralis major |
| Medial cord | Medial pectoral (C8, T1) | Pierces pec minor → pec minor + major |
| Medial cord | Medial brachial + antebrachial cutaneous | Skin |
| Posterior cord | Upper subscapular (C5, 6) | Subscapularis |
| Posterior cord | **Lower subscapular (C5, 6)** | Subscapularis + **teres major** |
| Posterior cord | **Thoracodorsal (C6–8)** | Latissimus dorsi |

### Lateral cord branches — terminal branches

| Cord | Terminal branches |
|---|---|
| **Lateral** | ✓ **Musculocutaneous + median (lateral root)** |
| **Medial** | **Ulnar + median (medial root)** |
| **Posterior** | **Axillary + radial** |

**Musculocutaneous (C5–7)** — most superficial · pierces **coracobrachialis 1.5–9 cm** from origin → biceps, coracobrachialis, brachialis → **lateral antebrachial cutaneous**

**Median** — lateral + medial roots join **anterior to axillary artery**

## How it's asked

**"Terminal branches of the lateral cord?"** → **musculocutaneous + median**\
✗ Wrong options: axillary (posterior cord) · ulnar (medial cord)

**"Brachial plexus exits between (TO)?"** → **scalenus anterior and scalenus medius**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Brachialis

*Basic Science · 22 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Brachialis |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | Distal half of anterior humerus |
| **Insertion** | **Coronoid process + ulnar tuberosity** |
| **Action** | **Major forearm flexor** — flexes in all positions |
| **Innervation** | **Medial (major) part: musculocutaneous (C5, C6)** · **inferolateral part: radial** |
| **Arterial supply** | Muscular branches of brachial artery, recurrent radial artery |

![Brachialis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10018-brachialis.jpg)

**Anterior approach to humerus** — **split brachialis** using its dual innervation (internervous plane)

### Dual innervation (composite / hybrid muscles)

| Muscle | Nerves |
|---|---|
| **Brachialis** | **Musculocutaneous + radial** |
| **FDP** | Median (AIN) + ulnar |
| **Adductor magnus** | Obturator + sciatic (tibial) |
| **Pectineus** | Femoral + obturator |
| **FPB** | Median + ulnar |

**Single nerve** — **gracilis** (obturator) **(TO)** · pronator teres · FDS · coracobrachialis · latissimus dorsi\
✗ Wrong options: dual

## How it's asked

**"All have dual nerve supply EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **gracilis**\
✗ Wrong options: FDP, adductor magnus, brachialis

**"Muscle with dual innervation?"** → **brachialis**\
✗ Wrong options: pronator teres, FDS, coracobrachialis, latissimus

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cartilage

*Basic Science · 23 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Types of cartilage

| Type | Location | Collagen | Function |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Hyaline (articular)** | Articular surfaces, ribs, nasal septum | **Type II** | ↓ friction, distributes load |
| **Fibrocartilage** | Tendon/ligament–bone junction, **pubic symphysis**, **annulus fibrosus**, menisci | **Type I** (predominantly) | Compressive strength · **repair tissue** after microfracture, drilling, abrasion arthroplasty |
| Fibroelastic | Menisci | — | Fewer PGs + glycoproteins than hyaline |
| **Elastic** | **Auricle, epiglottis, auditory tube** | **Type II** + elastin network | Tolerates repetitive deformation |
| **Epiphyseal (physeal)** | Between epiphysis + metaphysis | — | **Linear growth (endochondral ossification)** · remodelling potential |

![Fibrocartilage](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9014-fibrocartilage.jpg)

**Physeal zones** — reserve → proliferative → hypertrophic → primary spongiosa → secondary spongiosa · ECM holds PGs + GFs (BMP-2)

### Cartilage biology

**Properties** — **avascular, aneural, alymphatic** · nutrients by **diffusion from synovial fluid** · pH **7.4** · metabolism regulated mechanically

**Composition (hyaline)** — water (65–80%) > collagen > PG > non-collagenous protein > cells · **stress-shielding** of solid matrix

**Loading** — physiological = chondroprotective · **underloading** → thinning, softening, ↓ PG → fibrillation, erosion · overloading → damage (**in vitro only**)

### SOX9 & chondrocyte differentiation

**SOX9** — ✓ **"master switch" transcription factor for chondrocytes** · HMG superfamily · binds type II collagen promoter/enhancer · ↑ **type II, IX, XI collagen, aggrecan, link protein** · not a tyrosine kinase receptor

### Matrix components (aggrecan, type II & IX collagen)

**Type II collagen** — ✓ **tensile strength + reinforcement of PG gel** · restrains swelling · superficial-zone collagen bears ~**20%** of load · degradation → fibrillation

**Aggrecan** — negative GAG charge (chondroitin, keratan sulfate) → ↑ osmolality → attracts water → compressive strength

**↓ Aggrecan expression** — ✓ **↓ water content** (and ↓ osmolality, ↓ compressive strength) · no direct AGE/collagen change

**Pericellular integrin anchors** — fibronectin, **type VI collagen**

### Genes & lab markers

**OA linkage in women** — ✓ **type IX collagen** (400-family study · knockout mice → early OA) · also **ADAMTS-5** (aggrecanase)

### PTH effect on cartilage

**PTH** — **chondroprotective** · ✓ **↓ cartilage degradation** · via **Indian hedgehog (IHH)** signalling · synergy with bFGF, IGF

**Traps** — ✗ Wrong options: ↑ MMP-13 (degradation) · ↑ type X collagen (end-stage hypertrophic/OA) · ↓ PG

## X vs Y

| | Hyaline | Fibrocartilage | Elastic |
|---|---|---|---|
| Collagen | **II** | **I** | **II + elastin** |
| Example | Joint surface | Annulus, symphysis, meniscus, enthesis | Ear, epiglottis |

## How it's asked

**"SOX9 drives differentiation of?"** → **chondrocytes**\
✗ Wrong options: osteoclasts, osteoblasts, fibroblasts

**"SOX9 regulates which tissue (histology)?"** → **cartilage**\
✗ Wrong options: bone, fat, muscle, nerve

**"Strong genetic linkage with OA in women?"** → **type IX collagen**

**"Downregulate aggrecan genes → effect?"** → **↓ water content**

**"Primary biomechanical function of type II collagen?"** → **tensile strength, reinforcing PG matrix**\
✗ Wrong options: osmotic swelling (PGs)

**"PTH effect on cartilage?"** → **↓ degradation (chondroprotective)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Clinical Billing

*Basic Science · 24 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Definitions

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **CPT** | 5-digit code for the **procedure/service** → reimbursement · Category I (00100–99499) · Category III = provisional/emerging |
| **RVU** | Unit value per CPT code (4 factors) |
| **ICD-10** | WHO **diagnosis** classification · **3–7 alphanumeric characters** (1st alpha, 2nd numeric) · >68,000 codes (vs ~13,000 ICD-9) |
| **E&M** | Evaluation and management (office/hospital visits) |
| **Modifier** | 2-digit code for a special situation |

### ICD-10 & modifiers

**ICD-10 describes** — chronicity · **phase of care** · effects (routine, complication) · **laterality**

**7th character** — ✓ **phase of treatment / encounter**\
✗ Wrong options: site · laterality · aetiology · category

| 7th char | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **A** | Initial, closed fracture |
| B | Initial, open type I/II |
| C | Initial, open IIIA–C |
| **D** | Subsequent, routine healing |
| G | Subsequent, delayed healing |
| **K** | Subsequent, **nonunion** |
| **P** | Subsequent, **malunion** |
| **S** | **Sequela** |
| E/F, H/J, M/N, Q/R | Open-fracture equivalents (I/II vs IIIA–C) |

| Modifier | Use |
|---|---|
| **-22** | Unusually complicated (difficulty, time, effort) |
| **-24** | **Unrelated E&M** in the postoperative period |
| **-25** | **Significant, separate E&M on the day of a (minor) procedure** |
| **-58** | ✓ **Staged / planned / more extensive procedure in global period** · **restarts global** · 100% paid (e.g. spacer removal + graft, 2nd-stage PJI) |
| **-59** | Distinct procedural service, same encounter (different site, incision, lesion) |
| -76 | Repeat procedure |
| **-78** | **Unplanned return to OR** for related condition/complication in global period |
| **-79** | **Unrelated** procedure in global period · overlapping globals · 100% paid |

## 5 Management

### New vs established patient

**New patient** — no professional services from the physician **or anyone in the same group and same specialty within 3 years**

**Established** — seen by physician or same-group same-specialty partner **within 3 years**

**Exception** — hand + sports surgeons with a **CAQ** may bill patients seen by other group orthopaedists as new

**Consultation** — requested by another physician for **advice** · request documented · level documented · **written response** to requester · no 3-yr rule · **Medicare no longer recognises consult codes**\
✗ Wrong options: transfer of care

**Coding error rate** — 32.4% of Medicare claims (Shalowitz) · consult errors common

### E&M coding & global period

**Component-based level** — history + examination + MDM · **billed at the lowest of the three**

**Time-based** — visit mostly counselling/coordination · time documented

**Global period** — 0, 10 or **90 days** · single payment

| In the 90-day global | Not in the global |
|---|---|
| Preop visits/work, marking, positioning, prep | Diagnostic tests (e.g. POD1 CBC) |
| Skin-to-skin work | **Immobilisation applied outside OR** |
| **Immobilisation/bracing applied in OR** | Visits for separate problems |
| Inpatient + postop visits (physician **or extender**) | Evaluation by a different group |
| **Closed fracture "restorative treatment" follow-up** (code 99024, $0) | **Reoperation for complications** (infection) · **staged procedures** |

**Fraud** — services not provided · not provided by billing physician · not supported by documentation · **intentional or not** · responsibility = **billing physician**

## How it's asked

**"Which scenario is a new patient (2009 guidelines)?"** → **arthroscopy by partner 4 years ago**\
✗ Wrong options: seen <3 yr by you or partner

**"Time to revert to new patient?"** → **3 years**

**"Referred with consult request, partner saw 4 yr ago, history L3/exam L4/MDM L3, letter sent?"** → **level 3 consultation**

**"Cast removal 4 wk after closed reduction of phalanx fracture?"** → **not billed** (90-day global)

**"ICD-10 seventh character?"** → **phase of treatment**

**"Planned second-stage spacer removal + graft at 6 wk — modifier?"** → **58**\
✗ Wrong options: 78 (unplanned) · 79 (unrelated) · 59 · 25

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Clinical Trial Design

*Basic Science · 25 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Observational vs experimental

```
Clinical study
├─ Observational (no allocation) → DESCRIPTIVE or ANALYTIC
│      ├─ Descriptive → CASE REPORT · CASE SERIES · CROSS-SECTIONAL
│      └─ Analytic → CASE-CONTROL · COHORT · META-ANALYSIS
└─ Experimental (researcher allocates) → RCT
       └─ Double-blind prospective RCT → GOLD STANDARD
```

**Internal validity** — methodological rigour (↓ bias, confounding) · **external validity** — generalisability · homogeneous, tightly controlled populations ↑ internal, ↓ external

### Study types (case-control, cohort, parallel, factorial, RCT)

| Design | Definition | Level | Measure |
|---|---|---|---|
| **RCT** | Random assignment to treatment/control, followed prospectively | **I** | — · **best for causality** |
| **Prospective cohort** | Groups separated **non-randomly by exposure**, exposure **after** study start | **II** | **Relative risk**, incidence |
| **Retrospective cohort** | Exposure **before** study start | **III** | Relative risk |
| **Case-control** | Groups by **presence (cases) / absence (controls) of disease** → look back for exposure · always retrospective | **III** (most) | **Odds ratio** |
| **Case series** | Retrospective, same injury/treatment, **no control** · generates hypotheses | **IV** | — |
| **Cross-sectional** | **Snapshot** at one time, no follow-up (e.g. prevalence) | — | Prevalence |
| **Meta-analysis** | Systematic review pooling studies · **↑ power of under-powered studies** | Level of included studies | — |
| Case report / expert opinion | — | **V** | — |

**RCT with 70% follow-up** — downgraded to **Level II** (<80% follow-up)

### RCT designs

| Design | Allocation |
|---|---|
| **Parallel** | Each group gets one intervention (commonest RCT) · e.g. bisphosphonate vs placebo |
| **Crossover** | Each participant gets **both therapies sequentially** in random order · needs adequate **washout** |
| **Factorial** | Groups get **A + B, A, B or neither** → tests interaction |
| **Cluster** | **Pre-existing groups** (schools, villages) randomised |

![Study types](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9080-study-types.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Randomisation & selection bias

**Purpose of computer randomisation** — ✓ **prevent selection bias** (unbiased placement into groups)\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ power · ↑ treatment effect · significance · prevent crossover

**Sealed opaque envelopes / allocation concealment** — minimise **selection bias** (recruiters can't predict next allocation)

**Randomisation also** — distributes **confounders** (known + unknown) evenly → ✓ **best reduces confounding** · facilitates blinding · allows probability theory

**Confounders** — external factors affecting both predictor and outcome · controlled by randomisation, stratification, multivariable regression

### Blinding & other biases

**Single-blind** — **patients** unaware, surgeon delivers + assesses (experimenter bias remains)

**Double-blind** — patient + doctor/assessor unaware

**Blinding the outcome assessor** — ✓ **↓ reported treatment effect** (unblinded assessors inflate effect — Poolman, 32 RCTs)

| Bias | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Selection** | Dissimilar groups / non-random enrolment · prevented by randomisation + concealment |
| **Washout period** | Crossover — first therapy's effect persists (drug t½ long) |
| **Recall** | Subjects' recollection differs by outcome or **after unblinding** · common in retrospective/survey studies |
| Observer-expectancy | Investigator finds what they expect · prevented by masking |
| **Procedure** | Groups treated differently beyond the intervention (e.g. extra PT) |
| Attrition | Patients don't complete study |
| Detection | Look harder for outcome in one group |
| Verification | Test result influences group assignment |
| Incorporation | Diagnostic test includes features of target outcome |
| Confirmation | Interpretation skewed by researcher's opinion |
| Sampling | Homogeneous population → ↓ generalisability |
| Publication | Unfavourable results unpublished (industry funding) |
| Hawthorne effect | Behaviour changes when observed |

### Intention-to-treat analysis

| Analysis | Who is analysed |
|---|---|
| **Intention-to-treat** | **As randomised**, regardless of treatment received/compliance · preserves randomisation · ↓ non-responder bias |
| **As-treated** | By treatment actually received · no longer an RCT · used with high crossover |
| **Per-protocol** | **Excludes non-compliant** patients |

**Bayesian analysis** — prior probability + new data → posterior probability

**Effect size** — (difference between groups) ÷ SD

## 5 Management

### Evidence-based care & conflict of interest

**Evidence-based health care** — EBM principles applied to **all health professions incl. purchasing + management**

| Analysis | Effects expressed as |
|---|---|
| Cost-benefit | Money |
| Cost-effectiveness | Health units (cost per event prevented) |
| Cost-utility | Utilities (cost per **QALY**) |
| Decision analysis | Explicit quantitative choice under uncertainty |

**Conflict of interest statement** — ✓ **discloses all financial interests** of each author

## X vs Y

| | Cohort | Case-control |
|---|---|---|
| Grouped by | **Exposure** | **Outcome/disease** |
| Direction | Forward (or retrospective) | Backward |
| Measure | **Relative risk** | **Odds ratio** |
| Level | II (prospective) / III | III |

## How it's asked

**"Purpose of computer random-number generator in Level I trial?"** → **prevent selection bias**\
✗ Wrong options: power · significance · as-treated

**"Intern's RCT, attending recommends computer randomisation — why?"** → **prevent selection bias**\
✗ Wrong options: blinding · prevent crossover

**"Randomisation in sealed opaque envelopes minimises?"** → **selection bias**\
✗ Wrong options: attrition · NNH

**"Reason for computer randomisation (hemi vs screws)?"** → **unbiased placement into groups**\
✗ Wrong options: confirmation bias · heterogeneity

**"Closure-technique QI study — best reduces confounding?"** → **randomisation**\
✗ Wrong options: Hawthorne · blinding · power analysis

**"Crossover PT vs drug (t½ 2 wk), immediate switch — bias?"** → **washout period bias**

**"Patients unaware, surgeon injects and scores outcomes?"** → **single-blinded randomised**

**"Effect of blinding the outcome assessor?"** → **↓ reported treatment effect**

**"Patient discovers their drug before final evaluation — bias?"** → **recall bias**\
✗ Wrong options: selection · observer-expectancy · procedure

**"50 knee ON patients vs 23 without, compare alcohol intake?"** → **case-control**

**"Two groups randomised to bisphosphonate vs placebo?"** → **parallel design**

**"Two interventions applied separately or combined?"** → **factorial**

**"Dislocated vs non-dislocated THAs, look for factors?"** → **case-control**

**"Definition of cohort study?"** → **groups separated non-randomly by exposure, before or after study start**

**"Randomised prospective drug vs standard for AVN — true?"** → **best method for causality**\
✗ Wrong options: OR (case-control) · RR (cohort) · ↑ power (meta-analysis) · hypothesis-generating (case series)

**"RCT with 70% follow-up — level?"** → **II**

**"Athletes choose cleat, followed prospectively through season — level?"** → **II (prospective cohort)**

**"30 non-completers kept in PT group?"** → **intent-to-treat**\
✗ Wrong options: per-protocol

**"Evidence use across health professions incl. purchasing?"** → **evidence-based health care**

**"Purpose of conflict-of-interest statement?"** → **disclose all financial interests**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Collagen

*Basic Science · 26 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Overview

**Most abundant protein in humans** — ~25% of body protein · >25 types · made by **fibroblasts** · synthesis **inside and outside** the cell

**Structure** — **triple helix: two α1 + one α2** chains · repeats **Gly-X-hydroxyproline**, **Gly-Pro-X** (glycine every 3rd residue)

**Type I genes** — **COL1A1** (pro-α1) + **COL1A2** (pro-α2)

### Assembly

```
Collagen synthesis
├─ Intracellular → α-CHAINS → PROCOLLAGEN (2 α1 + 1 α2, triple helix)
└─ Extracellular → TROPOCOLLAGEN (enzymatic processing after Golgi)
       └─ Lysine/hydroxylysine aldehyde cross-links → FIBRIL → FIBRE
```

![Collagen processing](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9013-collagen-processing.jpg)

### Collagen types by tissue

| Type | Tissue | Disease |
|---|---|---|
| **I** (>90% of body collagen) | **Bone, tendon, ligament, meniscus, annulus fibrosus, skin, healed cartilage/fibrocartilage, scar, nerve** (tensile strength), deep calcified cartilage | **Osteogenesis imperfecta**, Ehlers–Danlos |
| **II** (90–95% of cartilage collagen) | **Articular/hyaline cartilage**, **nucleus pulposus**, **elastic cartilage** · very long half-life | — |
| **III** | **Skin, blood vessels** · **early tendon/ligament healing** (inflammatory + early regenerative) | **Ehlers–Danlos (vascular, type IV)**, **Dupuytren's** |
| **IV** | **Basement membrane** | Goodpasture, Alport |
| V, VI, IX | Small amounts in articular cartilage · **VI only pericellular** (lacunae, mechanotransduction) · V, VI in disc | — |
| **IX** | Cartilage | Deletion → **OA in women**, knockout mice · **COL9A1–3 → MED** |
| VII, VIII | Epithelial basement membrane | — |
| **X** | **Deep calcified cartilage** · only **hypertrophic chondrocytes** in endochondral ossification (physis, callus, HO) · ↑ **early OA** | **Schmid metaphyseal chondrodysplasia** |
| **XI** | **Adhesive holding cartilage collagen lattice together** | — |
| XII | Annulus only | — |

**Disc** — **annulus = type I** · **nucleus = type II** · type VI both

### Bone collagen

**Bone weight** — inorganic **60–70%** · water **5–8%** · organic remainder · collagen = 90% of organic = **20–25% of bone weight** · collagen gives **flexibility**

### Multiple epiphyseal dysplasia

**Genes** — **COMP (70%)** · COL9A1, COL9A2, COL9A3

**Features** — fragmented ossific centres · **coxa vara** · **genu valgum** · short stubby metacarpals

![MED — coxa vara and fragmented capital epiphyses](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9013-med3.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Tendon & ligament healing

**Inflammatory + early regenerative phase** — ✓ **type III** predominates → **type I** in late regenerative phase

## How it's asked

**"Type I collagen in peripheral nerve provides?"** → **tensile strength**\
✗ Wrong options: conduction velocity/strength · compression

**"Collagen first produced in inflammatory phase of tendon/ligament healing?"** → **type III**

**"Major cartilage collagen, 90–95%?"** → **type II**

**"Collagen % of bone weight?"** → **20–25%**

**"Predominant collagen of nucleus pulposus?"** → **type II** (annulus — type I)

**"Type II most abundant in all EXCEPT?"** → **blood vessels** (type III)

**"Collagen of hypertrophic chondrocytes, ↑ in early OA, Schmid?"** → **type X**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Complex Regional Pain Syndrome (CRPS)

*Basic Science · 27 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Synonyms** — **RSD** (type I) · **causalgia** (type II)

**Incidence** — 5.5 / 100,000 person-yr (US) · 6–26 / 100,000 (OB) · ↑ with age to 70

**Sex** — **female 4:1** (Orthobullets/OB) · 2–4× (TO)

**Site** — **upper limb 60%** (Orthobullets) · **70%** (TO) · type I **88%** vs type II 12% (TO)

**Postoperative rate** — 10–37% (OB)

### Risk factors

**Listed** — trauma with exaggerated response (**commonest cause of poor outcome after foot crush**) · surgery (multiple procedures) · **prolonged immobilisation**, tight dressings/casts (early cast complaints) · **anxiety/depression** · **ACE inhibitor at time of trauma** · migraine, asthma · **smoking** · **fibromyalgia (↑ up to 2.5×)** · RA, carpal tunnel

**Not a risk factor** — **male sex**

### Pathophysiology

**Triad** — ✓ **aberrant inflammatory response** · **vasomotor dysfunction** · **maladaptive neuroplasticity** → sustained sympathetic activity in a perpetuated reflex arc

**Mediators** — IL-1β, IL-6, TNF-α, **substance P** · ↑ osteoclasts → regional migratory osteoporosis / **bone marrow oedema**

### Genes & lab markers

**Genetics** — ↑ risk in siblings of young-onset cases · **TNF-α and ACE gene polymorphisms**

**Labs** — WBC, ESR, CRP **normal** (distinguishes from infection)

## 3 Presentation

**Cardinal signs** — **pain out of proportion** · **swelling** · **stiffness** · **skin discolouration**

**Exam** — vasomotor disturbance · trophic skin changes · **hyperhidrosis** · **allodynia, hyperalgesia, hyperpathia** · cool shiny skin · **flamingo gait** (knee) · **equinovarus** (ankle) · not **crepitus**

**Not typical** — ↓ fingertip perfusion (Raynaud) · ptosis/anhidrosis (Horner, preganglionic plexus) · fingertip ulcers (Buerger) · positive Spurling

**Natural course (TO)** — warm, ↑ hair/nail growth, oedema, erythema → later slow hair growth, brittle nails, weakness, **cold limb**

### Lankford & Evans stages

| Stage | Onset | Exam | Imaging |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Acute** | 0–3 mo | Burning pain, redness, swelling, warmth, hyperhidrosis | X-ray normal · **bone scan +** |
| **Subacute (dystrophic)** | 3–12 mo | ↑ pain, cyanosis, dry skin, stiffness, skin atrophy | **Subchondral osteopenia** |
| **Chronic (atrophic)** | >12 mo | ↓ pain, glossy skin, fibrosis, contractures, hair/nail loss | **Extreme osteopenia** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### IASP classification (type I vs II)

| | **Type I** | **Type II** |
|---|---|---|
| Nerve damage | ✓ **None identifiable** | **Identifiable nerve injury** |
| Frequency | **Most common** | Less common |
| Sympathetic block | Responds | **Minimal / no response** |
| Distribution | Whole region | Single nerve territory |

**Not distinguishing** — autonomic dysfunction · trophic changes · functional impairment · positive bone scan (all in both)

### Diagnostic criteria — Budapest

| Criterion | Requirement |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Continuing pain disproportionate** to inciting event |
| 2 Symptoms | ≥1 symptom in **3 of 4** categories (**clinical**) · **4 of 4** (research) |
| 3 Signs | ≥1 sign in **≥2** categories at evaluation |
| 4 | **No other diagnosis** explains it |

| Category | Symptoms / signs |
|---|---|
| **Sensory** | Hyperaesthesia, **allodynia**, hyperalgesia (pinprick) |
| **Vasomotor** | **Temperature asymmetry**, skin colour change/asymmetry |
| **Sudomotor / oedema** | **Oedema, sweating change/asymmetry** |
| **Motor / trophic** | ↓ ROM, weakness, tremor, dystonia, hair/nail/skin changes |

**Budapest vs IASP** — IASP sensitivity 1.00, **specificity 0.41** · Budapest sensitivity 0.99, **specificity 0.68–0.79**

**Best diagnostic test** — ✓ **history + physical examination** (clinical) · confirmed by **relief with sympathetic block** · early diagnosis critical

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Finding |
|---|---|
| X-ray | Osteopenia (**patella** — commonest knee finding) · soft-tissue swelling · subperiosteal resorption · **joint spaces preserved** |
| **Three-phase bone scan** | ↑ uptake all phases · **phase III (2–3 h) most sensitive** · **high NPV** (rules out type I) · **most sensitive investigation (TO)** · TO note: high specificity, ↓ sensitivity vs Budapest, not confirmatory |
| MRI | **Diffuse bone marrow oedema** (warm phenotype) |
| Thermography | Limb temperature difference **≥1 °C** significant · questionable utility |
| EMG/NCV | Slowing in known nerve (type II, e.g. median) · not diagnostic for type I · myoclonus only 11–36% |

![Three-phase bone scan in CRPS](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6095-bone-scan-in-patient-with-rsd.jpg)

| Bone scan phase | Shows |
|---|---|
| I (2 min) | Arteriogram |
| II (5–10 min) | Cellulitis, synovitis |
| III (2–3 h) | **Bone** |
| IV (24 h) | Osteomyelitis vs cellulitis |

### Differential

**Listed** — soft-tissue infection · malingering · **clenched fist syndrome** (psychiatric) · neuropathic/chronic pain · **Raynaud** (digital vasospasm) · TOS · arterial insufficiency · erythromelalgia

**Clenched fist syndrome** — **non-organic somatoform** disorder · fixed finger flexion · **macerated foul-smelling palm**, paradoxical stiffness · psychiatric association, poor prognosis · normal imaging/EMG → therapy + psychiatry

## 5 Management

### Vitamin C prophylaxis

**Regimen** — ✓ **vitamin C 500 mg daily × 50 days** · 200 mg if renal impairment

**Evidence** — **Level I** (Zollinger, 2 RCTs) in **distal radius fractures** · Lancet: CRPS **7% vs 22%** placebo · JBJS: 200 mg 4.2%, **500 mg 1.8%**, 1500 mg 1.7% → 500 mg recommended · later studies conflicting · also ↓ **CRPS type I** after **foot & ankle surgery** (non-diabetics)

**Mechanism** — ↓ lipid peroxidation · scavenges hydroxyl radicals · protects capillary endothelium · ↓ vascular permeability

**Other prevention** — avoid tight dressings + prolonged immobilisation

### Treatment (physiotherapy, blocks, sympathectomy)

```
CRPS treatment ladder
├─ First line → PT (GENTLE / ACTIVE, desensitisation) + DRUGS
│      (avoid casting, immobilisation, aggressive PROM)
├─ Failed → SYMPATHETIC / NERVE BLOCK → CHEMICAL SYMPATHECTOMY
│      └─ Focal LE CRPS failing blocks → DRG STIMULATION (TO)
└─ Refractory → SURGICAL SYMPATHECTOMY
       └─ Type II with known nerve → DECOMPRESS NERVE (e.g. CTR)
```

**Early CRPS** — ✓ **immediate PT: desensitisation + active ROM + multidisciplinary follow-up**\
✗ Wrong options: restrict PT · LSB first · bone scan before treating

**Modalities** — **gentle physiotherapy** · contrast baths · **tactile discrimination training** · **graded motor imagery** · mirror therapy · splinting, elevation, massage · **TENS — positive only in type II** (OB)

**Graded motor imagery sequence (TO)** — ✓ **laterality restoration → explicit motor imagery → mirror therapy**

**Drugs** — NSAIDs · **α-blockers (phenoxybenzamine, prazosin)** · β-blocker (propranolol) · antidepressants · anticonvulsants · Ca-channel blockers · **gabapentin** · **bisphosphonates** · antiarrhythmics · corticosteroids · calcitonin · vitamin C

**Also used (TO)** — **ketamine** (NMDA antagonist, topical/IV) · **botulinum toxin A** · **mirror therapy** · naltrexone · plasma exchange · cannabis inhaler · **pramipexole — not used**

**Warm phenotype + bone marrow oedema on MRI (<6 mo, TO)** — ✓ **IV bisphosphonates (neridronate)** first-line pharmacological

**Post-TKA CRPS** — ✓ **α-blockers + PT + tactile discrimination + graded motor imagery**\
✗ Wrong options: explore knee · I&D/poly exchange · L-spine MRI

**Nerve stimulation** — TENS · peripheral nerve · spinal cord stimulator · indicated when symptoms mainly in **one peripheral nerve**

**Neuromodulation — focal LE CRPS after failed blocks (TO)** — ✓ **dorsal root ganglion stimulation** (ACCURATE trial: up to 80% pain ↓, fewer postural changes vs tonic SCS) · diffuse whole-limb → SCS

| Block / sympathectomy | Target |
|---|---|
| **Stellate ganglion** | **Upper limb** |
| **Lumbar sympathetic** | **Lower limb** |
| Agents (block) | Lidocaine, bupivacaine, bretylium, guanethidine |
| Agents (chemical sympathectomy) | **Phenol, alcohol** |

**Second line after failed early PT modalities (OB)** — ✓ **surgical sympathectomy** · best candidates = responded to sympathetic block\
✗ Wrong options: cast · boot NWB · exchange nail · SCS

**Best success** — find + treat an associated nerve problem (type II)

## 6 Complications

**Prognosis** — poor response to treatment · **better: upper limb, warm CRPS, children** · late CRPS refractory → permanent disability

## X vs Y

| | CRPS | Raynaud | Horner (preganglionic plexus) | Clenched fist |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hallmark | Pain out of proportion, allodynia, sweating | Digital vasospasm in cold | Ptosis, miosis, anhidrosis | Fixed clenched fist, psychiatric |
| Mechanism | Inflammatory + vasomotor | Exaggerated arterial vasoconstriction | Sympathetic chain injury | Somatoform |

## How it's asked

**"Most sensitive investigation for CRPS (TO)?"** → **bone scan**\
✗ Wrong options: CT, MRI, US

**"Clinical features of LE CRPS EXCEPT?"** → **crepitus**

**"Commonest X-ray finding in knee CRPS?"** → **patellar osteopenia**

**"Healed DRF, now hyperalgesia, allodynia, hyperhidrosis — pathophysiology?"** → **aberrant inflammatory and vasomotor response**

**"Post-Kidner, allodynia, hyperalgesia, oedema of medial foot?"** → **RSD**

**"All common in CRPS EXCEPT?"** → **↓ perfusion to fingertips**

**"Fibromyalgia patient with DRF — at ↑ risk of?"** → **hyperalgesia, allodynia, oedema (CRPS)**

**"CRPS type I vs II differs by?"** → **no identifiable peripheral nerve involvement**

**"Whole-leg pain, shiny cool cyanotic skin after tibia ORIF — diagnosis + best test?"** → **CRPS type I; history and examination**\
✗ Wrong options: type II bone scan · EMG

**"Wrist sprain, allodynia, sweating, relief with sympathetic block?"** → **CRPS type I** (type II doesn't respond)

**"Vitamin C after DRF ORIF prevents?"** → **pain out of proportion + ↑ sweating (CRPS)**

**"Adjuvant improving outcome after DRF ORIF?"** → **oral vitamin C**

**"Level I evidence for vitamin C reducing CRPS in?"** → **distal radius fractures**

**"Vitamin C after F&A surgery ↓?"** → **CRPS type I**

**"Protocol shown to prevent CRPS?"** → **vitamin C 500 mg × 50 days**

**"NOT used in CRPS treatment (TO)?"** → **pramipexole**

**"Graded motor imagery sequence (TO)?"** → **laterality → explicit imagery → mirror therapy**

**"Focal knee CRPS after ACL, failed blocks — neuromodulation (TO)?"** → **DRG stimulation**

**"Post-TKA burning pain, oedema, ↑ hair, marrow oedema — first-line drug (TO)?"** → **IV bisphosphonate (neridronate)**

**"Positive effect in early CRPS?"** → **gentle physiotherapy**\
✗ Wrong options: casting, plyometrics

**"Post-TKA burning, sweating, purple leg, aspirate negative?"** → **α-blockers + PT + tactile discrimination + GMI**

**"Failed splinting, contrast baths, TENS — second line?"** → **surgical sympathectomy**

**"Early CRPS after ankle sprain — initial step?"** → **immediate PT, desensitisation + AROM, multidisciplinary follow-up**

**"Crush foot CRPS — risk factors EXCEPT?"** → **male gender**

**"Painful clenched hand, depression, normal studies?"** → **clenched fist syndrome**

# Deep Vein Thrombosis

*Basic Science · 28 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — 80 / 100,000 / yr · LE 1 / 1,000 / yr · 2.5–5% of population · usually **>40 yr** · rare in children

**High-risk procedures** — spine fracture with paralysis · **TKA (2–3× DVT rate of THA)** · THA · hip fracture · polytrauma

**DVT rate ≠ PE or death** — AAOS review: DVT rate does **not correlate** with PE/death after THA/TKA

**Risk factors** — immobilisation · mechanical compression · trauma · **prior DVT** · surgery · polycythaemia vera · thrombocytosis · dehydration · malignancy · CHF · obstructive airway disease

**No ↑ risk** — **factor V Leiden in THA** (no ↑ DVT or PE vs controls) · **rheumatoid arthritis in TKA** (age/comorbidity-matched)

### Pathophysiology

**Virchow's triad** — hypercoagulability · venous stasis · endothelial injury

**Factor V Leiden** — factor V resistant to **protein C** inactivation → hypercoagulable (factor V = cofactor for Xa → thrombin)

### Anatomy

| | Deep veins |
|---|---|
| **Proximal LE** | **Popliteal and above** (above trifurcation) |
| Distal LE — axial | Peroneal, anterior + posterior tibial |
| Distal LE — muscular | Soleal, gastrocnemius |
| UE | Radial, ulnar, brachial, axillary, subclavian, IJ, brachiocephalic |

## 2 Mechanism

### Timing & intraoperative embolisation

**Peak marrow embolisation in cemented THA** — ✓ **femoral stem insertion** (also acetabular prep, reduction)

**Symptomatic VTE after TJA** — ✓ **1–6 weeks** (2nd–6th week) · events on day of surgery = fat embolism or cardiac · ~10% readmitted within 3 months

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **swelling (70%)** · **calf pain (50%)** · redness · warmth

**Exam** — unilateral/bilateral swelling · erythema · dilated veins · tenderness · **Homans' sign not specific** (or sensitive)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Test | Sens / spec | Role |
|---|---|---|
| **Complete duplex US** | **96% / 97%** | ✓ **First line** · non-compressible vein, loss of phasic flow with Valsalva, ↑ superficial flow · operator-dependent · **routine screening not recommended**|
| Contrast venography | — | **Historical gold standard** · invasive · if US unavailable/inconclusive |
| CT venography | 90% / 95% | Suspected PE or inconclusive US |
| MR direct thrombus imaging | 90% / 90% | Not widely used |
| Plethysmography (proximal) | 75% / 90% | — |

![Duplex ultrasound — non-compressible vein](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422816-screenshot-2026-01-09-at-2-10-46-pm.jpg)

### D-dimer

**High sensitivity, low specificity** — excludes VTE in **low clinical probability** · >500 ng/mL suggests PE · **useless after injury/surgery** (false positives)

### Differential

| Mimic | Clue |
|---|---|
| Cellulitis | Infective labs |
| Post-thrombotic syndrome | Prior DVT, chronic swelling, stasis ulcers |
| **Ruptured Baker cyst** | US: no venous thrombus |
| Superficial thrombophlebitis | Cord-like superficial vein |
| CHF, nephrotic, cirrhosis | Chronic bilateral oedema |

## 5 Management

### Anticoagulation

| Agent | Role |
|---|---|
| **DOACs** (dabigatran, rivaroxaban, apixaban, edoxaban) | ✓ **First line** for DVT + PE (ACCP 2016, ESC) |
| **LMWH** (enoxaparin) | **Early postoperative period** · high bleeding risk |
| **Warfarin** | **Bridge with LMWH until INR 2–3** · preferred with **mechanical heart valve** |

**Indication** — postoperative **above-knee DVT** · below-knee treatment controversial

| Scenario | Duration |
|---|---|
| **Isolated provoked DVT** | **3 months** |
| With PE | **>3 months** (longer if large clot, persistent risk, recurrence) |
| **>3 months** | Unprovoked · recurrent · active malignancy · paralysis/immobilisation · thrombophilia |

### Procedures

| Intervention | Indication |
|---|---|
| **IVC filter** | **DVT identified preop** in high-risk pelvic/LE trauma · contraindication to anticoagulation |
| Thrombolysis (systemic) | Life/limb-threatening PE or acute DVT · high morbidity |
| **Catheter-directed thrombolysis** | Initial treatment **only for threatened limb loss** |
| Thrombectomy | Anticoagulation ineffective, high-risk thrombus |

### Risk factors & individualised prophylaxis

**Foot & ankle (ICM-VTE)** — ✓ **patient-specific risk assessment** (age, DM, prior DVT, NWB, obesity) · **NWB = independent risk factor** but no prophylaxis rule based on NWB alone\
✗ Wrong options: routine chemoprophylaxis for all NWB · blanket "none for below-knee" · surveillance duplex · postop D-dimer

## 6 Complications

**Post-thrombotic syndrome** — chronic venous insufficiency · venous HTN · swelling, pain, pigmentation, ulceration, induration · **recurrent DVT 4–8×**

**PE** — 1 in 1,000 / yr · anticoagulation ± intervention

**Bleeding** — anticoagulants, thrombolysis

## How it's asked

**"Peak marrow embolisation in cemented THA?"** → **femoral stem insertion**

**"When do most symptomatic VTE occur after TJA?"** → **1–6 weeks**

**"Factor V Leiden and THA?"** → **no increased risk of DVT or PE**

**"RA (RF, anti-CCP, MTX) vs OA patient for TKA — VTE prophylaxis?"** → **equal risk, standard prophylaxis for both**

**"Hindfoot fusion, NWB 6 wk, BMI 31, DM, prior DVT — strategy?"** → **patient-specific risk assessment, consider chemoprophylaxis**\
✗ Wrong options: none · routine for all · screening duplex · D-dimer

**"First-line imaging for DVT?"** → **complete duplex ultrasound** · routine screening not recommended

**"First-line DVT treatment?"** → **DOAC** · warfarin if mechanical valve

# Embryology

*Basic Science · 29 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Timeline

| Event | Time |
|---|---|
| Gastrula (3 germ layers) | ~**3 weeks** **(TO)** |
| **Neural plate** | ✓ **Day 18–19** **(TO)** |
| **Limb bud** | **4 weeks** (stage 12, **day 26**) |
| Appendicular skeleton forms | **4–8 weeks** |
| Chondrification | Humerus day 36 → distal phalanges day 50 (proximal → distal) |
| Shoulder interzone / hand interzones | Day 36 / day 47 |
| Digital rays in hand paddle | Stage 17, **day 41** |
| Interdigital apoptosis | Stages 19–22, **days 47–54** |
| **Limb bud on transvaginal US** | **8 weeks** |

**Early stages (TO)** — zygote → morula (16 cells) → blastula (128) → blastocyst (trophoblast + inner cell mass) → gastrula

### Limb bud signalling (AER, ZPA, timing)

**Limb bud** — lateral plate + somatic mesoderm under ectoderm · Shh from notochord regulates formation · growth = **AER ↔ progress-zone mesoderm** interaction (FGF-driven)

**All UL bones endochondral** — except distal tufts of distal phalanges (membranous)

| Signalling centre | Order | Molecule | Axis | Defect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Apical ectodermal ridge (AER)** | **1st** | **FGF8 (obligatory, earliest)** · FGF4, 9, 17 redundant · forms under **FGF10** | **Proximal → distal** + interdigital necrosis | Removal/insult → **transverse truncation** (congenital transverse absence of forearm), central deficiency (cleft hand), radial club hand |
| **Zone of polarising activity (ZPA)** — posterior | **2nd** | **Sonic hedgehog (Shh)** — dose-dependent | **Anteroposterior (radioulnar)** | **Duplication/anterior graft → mirror hand (ulnar dimelia)** |
| **Non-AER dorsal ectoderm (Wnt centre)** | **3rd** | **WNT7a → Lmx1b** | **Dorsoventral** (all dorsal features incl. **nails**) | — |
| Ventral ectoderm | — | **Engrailed-1** (antagonises WNT7a) | Ventral patterning | — |

![Limb bud signalling centres](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9078-limb-patterning.jpg)

### Shh gradient

**Little finger** — **highest** Shh (posterior/ulnar) · **thumb** — **lowest** Shh (anterior/radial)

**↑ Shh posteriorly** — **ulnar (postaxial) polydactyly** (dose-dependent) · ↓ Shh posteriorly → loss of ulnar digits

**↑ Shh anteriorly** — **loss of thumb**

**Timing** — **posterior elements (ulna, little finger) form early**, anterior (radius, thumb) late → AP disruption loses **radius/thumb**

**Shh activates Gremlin** — inhibits BMPs that would block AER FGF

**Mesoderm markers** — anterior **ALX4** · posterior **Hox8**

![Mirror hand (ulnar dimelia)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9078-ulnar-dimelia.jpg)

### Chondrification & joints

**Chondrification factors** — **Sox-5, -6, -9** · TGF-β, BMP-2 · FGFs · retinoids · hedgehog · PTHrP · cadherins · WNT5a, WNT7a

**Joint formation** — repress chondrogenesis: **WNT4, WNT14, GDF5** (CDMP-1)

**Finger separation** — **Msx2** in interdigital mesenchyme → **BMP4**-mediated apoptosis · Hox-7

**Apert (acrocephalosyndactyly)** — **FGFR2** mutation · severe craniofacial + mild syndactyly (gain-of-function, FGF2) · mild face + severe syndactyly (binds FGF10)

### Somites & axial skeleton (Hox genes)

**Somites** — paired **paraxial mesoderm** beside notochord · **cranial → caudal** (paraxis gene) · **42 pairs (TO)**

| Somite layer | Becomes |
|---|---|
| **Sclerotome** | ✓ **Axial skeleton — vertebral bodies + annulus fibrosus** |
| Myotome | Muscle (dorsal epimere → dorsal muscles) |
| Dermatome | Dermis/skin |

**Vertebra precursor (TO)** — ✓ **somites** · each vertebra = **caudal half of one sclerotome + cranial half of the next** (two somite levels) · sclerotome cells engulf notochord in week 4

**Metameric shift** — spinal nerves, originally mid-sclerotome, exit **between** vertebral bodies

**Hox (homeobox) genes** — ✓ **regulate segmentation/identity of axial skeleton** (rostral → caudal) · with Shh, AP patterning

| Structure | Origin |
|---|---|
| **Nucleus pulposus** | **Notochord** |
| **Annulus fibrosus** | **Sclerotome** |
| Spinal cord | Neural tube |
| PNS, pia, spinal ganglia, sympathetic trunk | **Neural crest** |

**Neural tube DV patterning** — **Shh** ventral (floor plate, notochord) vs **Wnt/β-catenin** dorsal (roof plate)

**Vertebral primary ossification centres (3)** — **centrum** · **neural arch** (posterior elements, pedicles) · costal element

## X vs Y

| Gene | Function |
|---|---|
| FGF8 / FGF10 | Proximodistal growth |
| Shh | AP (radioulnar), limb bud formation |
| WNT7a | Dorsoventral |
| Hox | Axial segmentation |
| COL1A1 | Collagen cross-linked fibres |

| Hand anomaly | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Mirror hand** | **ZPA duplication** |
| Preaxial polydactyly | Sporadic · Holt-Oram, Fanconi |
| Postaxial polydactyly | AD in African-Americans |
| Syndactyly | Failed apoptosis · Poland, Apert |
| Hypoplastic thumb | VACTERL, Holt-Oram, TAR, Fanconi |

## How it's asked

**"Congenital transverse absence of forearm — vascular insult to?"** → **apical ectodermal ridge**\
✗ Wrong options: limb bud mesoderm · ectoderm · notochord

**"Duplication of ZPA causes?"** → **mirror hand**\
✗ Wrong options: preaxial/postaxial polydactyly, syndactyly, hypoplastic thumb

**"Transplant along AP axis → mirror hand — site + molecule?"** → **ZPA, Shh**\
✗ Wrong options: AER/FGF8 · WNT7a

**"Limb bud timing and US?"** → **forms 4 wk, transvaginal US 8 wk**

**"Sclerotome becomes?"** → **axial skeleton**

**"Function of homeobox genes in axial skeleton?"** → **regulate segmentation (somatisation)**\
✗ Wrong options: limb bud (Shh) · PD (FGF10) · DV (Wnt)

**"Neural plate formation by (TO)?"** → **18 days**

**"Embryological precursor of vertebrae (TO)?"** → **somites**\
✗ Wrong options: notochord (NP)

# Endochondral Bone Formation

*Basic Science · 30 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Where it occurs

**Three settings** — **longitudinal physeal growth** · **embryonic long-bone formation** · **non-rigid (secondary) fracture healing** (cast, brace, **IM nail**)

**Principle** — **cartilage model** → chondrocytes hypertrophy/calcify → osteoclasts resorb → osteoblasts lay bone **on** the cartilage framework (bone **replaces** cartilage, not converted from cartilage) · deposition on **metaphyseal side** · **type X collagen**

### Molecular regulation

**Sox-9** — master regulator of chondrogenesis · collagen II, IV, XI, aggrecan

**Low β-catenin** — ✓ mesenchyme → **cartilage** (↑ SOX9) → collagen II, IX, XI · anlage grows interstitially + appositionally **(TO)**

**High β-catenin (Wnt)** — chondrocytes stop growing, **hypertrophy** → express **RUNX2 + VEGF-A** → vessels, osteoblasts, osteoclasts → **primary ossification centre** **(TO)**

**Ihh–PTHrP loop** — prehypertrophic chondrocytes secrete **Indian hedgehog** → ↑ **PTHrP** → **delays hypertrophic differentiation**

| Mutation | Effect on hypertrophic maturation |
|---|---|
| **Deactivating PTHrP receptor** | ✓ **Accelerated** |
| Activating Ihh | Delayed (↑ PTHrP) |
| Activating TGF-β / SMAD-3 | Delayed (TGF-β inhibits hypertrophy, type X, ALP) |
| PGE2 | No physeal effect shown |

### Physeal blood supply

**Perichondrial artery** — **major nutrition of the physis**

## 2 Mechanism

### Physeal zones & pathology

![Physeal zones with associated diseases](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9006-zones-with-disease.jpg)

| Zone | Features | Diseases |
|---|---|---|
| **Reserve / resting / germinal** | Stores **lipids, glycogen, PG aggregates** · **max PG storage (TO)** · **low O₂** · supplies cells, organises plate | **Gaucher's**, diastrophic dysplasia, Kniest, pseudoachondroplasia |
| **Proliferative** | Chondrocyte division + **columns (stacking)** · ↑ O₂ inhibits calcification · **highest ECM production (TO)** | **Achondroplasia, gigantism**, MHE |
| **Hypertrophic** — maturation, degeneration (cells ↑ 5×), **provisional calcification** | Chondrocyte hypertrophy → death → Ca release → matrix calcifies · **type X collagen** + ALP · Ihh/PTHrP · **weakest zone (TO)** | **SCFE (non-renal)**, **rickets (provisional calcification)**, enchondromas, MPS, Schmid · **physeal fractures (SH I/II) through zone of provisional calcification** |
| **Primary spongiosa** (metaphysis) | Vascular invasion, transverse septa resorbed, osteoblasts on cartilage bars → woven bone | **Metaphyseal corner fracture (abuse)**, scurvy |
| **Secondary spongiosa** | Internal remodelling (woven → lamellar), funnelisation | **Renal SCFE** |

**Type X collagen** — produced by terminal **hypertrophic** chondrocytes · ✓ **abrupt appearance heralds ossification** **(TO)** · type II still most abundant in hypertrophic zone **(TO)**

**Longitudinal growth** — **chondrocyte hypertrophy** contributes most (cell volume/height) (OB8254) · proliferative zone "responsible for longitudinal growth" (OB3234) — read the options

### Physis periphery

| Structure | Function | Disease |
|---|---|---|
| **Groove (zone) of Ranvier** | Wedge of germinal cells · supplies chondrocytes to periphery · ✓ **appositional (circumferential) growth** · ↑ mechanical strength | **Osteochondroma** · SH VI injury (lawnmower, burns) |
| **Perichondrial ring of LaCroix** | Dense fibrous ring · **peripheral stability** · links Ranvier zone to metaphyseal periosteum | — |
| Perichondrial artery | Physeal nutrition | — |

![Ring of LaCroix](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9006-ring-moved.jpg)

### Growth regulation

**Hueter–Volkmann** — **sustained compression → decelerated longitudinal growth** · tension accelerates

### Embryonic long-bone formation

```
Mesenchymal anlage (6th week in utero)
├─ Cartilage model → CHONDROBLASTS (hyaline precursor)
├─ Vascular buds invade → PRIMARY OSSIFICATION CENTRE (~8 wk) + MARROW
└─ Ends keep growing → SECONDARY OSSIFICATION CENTRES (epiphyses, physis)
```

### Non-rigid fracture healing

**Soft callus** = cartilage intermediate → proliferation → **hypertrophy** → calcification → bone · IM nail → both endochondral and intramembranous

## How it's asked

**"Physeal zone with maximum PG storage (TO)?"** → **resting zone**

**"Collagen whose abrupt appearance heralds ossification (TO)?"** → **type X**

**"SH I fractures occur through?"** → **zone of provisional calcification**

**"SH II distal radius — zone?"** → **zone of provisional calcification**

**"Gigantism affects?"** → **proliferative zone (C)**

**"Type X collagen prominent in?"** → **zone of hypertrophy**

**"Accelerated hypertrophic maturation?"** → **deactivating PTHrP-receptor mutation**\
✗ Wrong options: activating TGF-β, SMAD-3, Ihh

**"Found during chondrocyte differentiation / anlage formation (TO)?"** → **low β-catenin**\
✗ Wrong options: RUNX2, VEGF-A (hypertrophy/ossification) · RANKL

**"Sustained compression on physis?"** → **decelerated longitudinal growth**

**"Groove of Ranvier responsible for?"** → **appositional growth**

**"Process contributing most to longitudinal growth?"** → **chondrocyte hypertrophy**

# Ethics in Orthopaedic Practice

*Basic Science · 31 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Ethical principles

| Pillar | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Beneficence** | Duty to do good |
| **Non-maleficence** | Duty not to harm |
| **Respect for autonomy** | Patient self-determination |
| **Justice** | **Fair, equitable, appropriate** treatment (✗ Wrong options: "everyone gets the **same** treatment") |
| Confidentiality | Patient's right + doctor's duty to protect information |

## 5 Management

### Industry relationships & conflicts of interest

| AAOS Standards of Professionalism | |
|---|---|
| **Gifts** | Market value **<$100** (except textbooks, patient-education material) · dinners **≤$100/person** with useful education |
| **Industry non-CME course** | ✓ Tuition, travel, modest hospitality acceptable (✗ Wrong options: "must decline non-CME courses") |
| **Faculty at industry meeting** | ✓ Travel + hotel paid (contribution must match expenses) |
| **CME event** | Corporate subsidy **to the sponsor** OK · not OK: direct industry reimbursement to the surgeon, spouse's travel |
| **On-site training** (company HQ workshop, new technique/implant) | ✓ **Expenses reimbursable** |
| **Disclosure** | All industry relationships → **patients, colleagues, institution** · **even without financial compensation** |
| **Royalties** | **Disclose full relationship incl. royalties to patient** before surgery · may still use own device |
| Kick-backs | No payment for using a product ($500/knee, "consultant" after 25 implants) |
| **Stark II** | No referral to centres in which physician has a **financial interest** |
| Consulting | Defined duties + work product · fair pay for time · no thinly veiled payment |
| Residency grants | **Unrestricted** for CME ✓ · (✗ Wrong options: restricted to industry course)|
| Personal deals (e.g. rep buys 90% of a boat) | **Reject — unacceptable conflict** (equal share doesn't fix it) |

**Implant selection** — ✓ surgeon responsible for knowing the **implant's clinical track record** · (✗ Wrong options: rely on sales rep) · publish negative results too

### Research conduct

**Industry-funded RCT** — ✓ paid fair market value, disclose, register (**ClinicalTrials.gov — FDA law for implanted devices**), regulatory binder, publish\
✗ Wrong options: **company statistician analysing data** (bias → use independent blinded statistician)

**Sunshine Act** — all industry payments to physicians reported to **CMS**

**Publication** — government-funded studies must publish regardless of outcome · private studies need only report serious adverse events to FDA (publication bias)

### Advertising

**Who regulates** — AMA/AAOS **cannot stop** physicians advertising (FTC Act) · **FTC, AAOS, state medical boards, consumer protection agencies** sanction false advertising

| Avoid | ✓ Acceptable |
|---|---|
| "**Cure**" (if no true cure) | "**Relatively little pain**" |
| "**Painless / pain-free**", "**bloodless**" | "**Less invasive / minimally invasive**" (no outcome claims) |
| "Board certified in joint replacement" (doesn't exist) | "Relatively safe" |
| "World renowned" · "safe surgery for all" | — |
| "Return to all sports after TJA" | — |

### Interpreters & consent

**Preferred** — ✓ **certified (professional) interpreter**, on-site or telephonic (medico-legal)

**Family members** — not as interpreters (confidentiality) unless patient offers/agrees · **minors** not as interpreters except emergencies

**Interpreter summarising (short phrases)** — consent invalid → ✓ **repeat consent with qualified interpreter**\
✗ Wrong options: another nurse · proceed

**Interpretation style** — conduit, literal, first person, no omissions

### Patient transfer — EMTALA

**Requirements** — patient **screened** (+ stabilised) at original ED · **benefit of transfer > risk** · **accepting facility knows and formally accepts** · ✓ **exiting facility lacks resources to treat** (OB key)

**Not required** — insurance discussion · full lab work-up · cost of transport

### Team physician obligations

**Team-employed physician** — ✓ **agent of the team → must disclose findings to employer** · record = employment record, **not HIPAA-protected**

**Private physician** — HIPAA applies · no obligation to disclose to prospective teams without athlete consent

## 6 Complications

### Patient complaints, communication & litigation

**Leading cause of litigation** — ✓ **poor communication** · ↓ litigation with **effective communication**, standard of care, thorough informed consent, documentation

**Orthopaedic surgeons rated** — highest for **technical skills** · lowest for **empathy**, listening, communication ("high tech, low touch") · doctors interrupt within **23 s**

**Most common patient complaint** — ✓ **lack of empathy**

**Formal written complaint about care** — written response letter to patient

**Formal medical-board complaint** — ✓ **written response to the board with all relevant records** · governed by **state boards** · (✗ Wrong options: automatic licence suspension · contact patient's attorney) · attorney not required\
✗ Wrong options: federal

### Physician burnout & suicide

**Burnout** — loss of enthusiasm, cynicism (depersonalisation), ↓ personal accomplishment · ✓ **45–55%** of US physicians · rising · **1.5–2×** general population · ortho among higher-risk specialties · Maslach Burnout Inventory

**Suicide** — ✓ orthopaedic surgeons = **highest number among surgical subspecialties (28%)** · surgeons **6%** more likely than general population

### Disparities & diversity

**Cultural competency goal** — ✓ better patient–physician relationships, **access**, quality of care\
✗ Wrong options: quotas · stereotypes · foreign languages · travel abroad

**Implicit bias** — unconscious prejudice influencing care · ✓ **determinant of healthcare disparity**

**Paediatric access** — determined by **type of insurance**

## How it's asked

**"Standards of professionalism EXCEPT?"** → **"decline industry-sponsored non-CME courses"**

**"You receive royalties on a TKA that suits your patient?"** → **disclose before surgery**

**"True of ethical implant management?"** → **surgeon must know the implant's track record**

**"Most appropriate industry relationship?"** → **travel/hotel for faculty at non-CME industry meeting**\
✗ Wrong options: spouse travel · $200 dinner · restricted resident grant

**"Rep offers 90% share of a $200,000 boat?"** → **reject — conflict of interest**

**"Acceptable way to learn the Genuflex knee?"** → **company pays expenses for HQ workshop**\
✗ Wrong options: company pays CME tuition · $500 per knee

**"Royalty-receiving designer uses product — obligation?"** → **disclose full relationship incl. royalties**

**"Violates standards of professionalism?"** → **not reporting a conflict because no compensation received**

**"Inappropriate in company-funded TKA RCT?"** → **company statistician analysing data**

**"Acceptable newspaper advert?"** → **"relatively little pain"**\
✗ Wrong options: bloodless · board certified in joint replacement · cure

**"Acceptable advert phrase?"** → **"less invasive hip and knee replacements"**

**"Non-English-speaking mother-in-law of colleague — interpreter?"** → **certified translator**

**"Nurse interpreter gives short phrases, patient offers to sign?"** → **repeat consent with qualified interpreter**

**"True about burnout?"** → **45–55%**

**"True about physician suicide?"** → **ortho = highest among surgical subspecialties (28%)**

**"Formal complaint after infected TKA?"** → **written response to medical board with records**

**"Ortho surgeons rated highest for?"** → **technical skills**

**"Commonest patient complaint about ortho surgeons?"** → **lack of empathy**

**"Associated with ↓ litigation?"** → **effective communication**

**"Benefit of diversity/cultural competency training?"** → **better relationships, access, quality of care**

**"Prisoner with hip OA — important to?"** → **understand implicit bias**

**"EMTALA requires?"** → **exiting facility lacks resources to treat**\
✗ Wrong options: insurance discussion · complete labs

**"Incorrectly paired ethical term?"** → **justice = everyone gets the same treatment**

**"Free-agent athlete's private surgeon — disclosure?"** → **team physician must disclose to own team** · private physician not obligated

# Extensor digitorum longus

*Basic Science · 32 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Extensor digitorum longus |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | Lateral tibial condyle, proximal ¾ anterior fibula, upper interosseous membrane, fascia cruris, anterior intermuscular septum |
| **Insertion** | 4 slips after inferior extensor retinaculum → dorsal middle + distal phalanges (extensor expansion) |
| **Action** | Extends toes 2–5 · dorsiflexes ankle |
| **Innervation** | **Deep peroneal nerve (L4, L5, S1)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Anterior tibial artery |

![Extensor digitorum longus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10080-extensor-digitorum-longus.jpg)

### Deep peroneal nerve function

**Supplies** — tibialis anterior · **EHL** · EDL · peroneus tertius · EDB · EHB

**Course** — posterior half of tibial shaft proximally · crosses distal tibia **40–110 mm above the ankle joint**

**Anterolateral ankle approach** — always exposes **superficial peroneal nerve** (subcutaneous)

## X vs Y

| Deficit | Nerve |
|---|---|
| **Great-toe extension** | ✓ **Deep peroneal** |
| Hindfoot eversion (peronei) | Superficial peroneal |
| Hindfoot inversion (tibialis posterior) | Tibial |
| Great-toe flexion (FHL) | Tibial |
| Ankle plantarflexion (gastrosoleus) | Tibial |
| Posterior distal leg + lateral foot skin | Sural |

## How it's asked

**"Deep peroneal nerve injury causes?"** → **weak great-toe extension**\
✗ Wrong options: eversion (SPN) · inversion/toe flexion/plantarflexion (tibial)

# Extensor Indicis Proprius

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Extensor indicis proprius |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | Posterior ulna + interosseous membrane |
| **Insertion** | Extensor expansion of index finger |
| **Action** | Extends index finger, assists wrist extension |
| **Innervation** | **Posterior interosseous nerve (C7, C8)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Posterior interosseous artery |

![Extensor indicis proprius](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10040-extensor-indicis.jpg)

### Identification in the 4th compartment

**Position** — **deep and ulnar** to the EDC tendons · same (4th) compartment · single tendon, no subcompartment

**Distinguishing feature** — ✓ **most distal muscle belly**

**Traps** — ✗ Wrong options: separate compartment · circular cross-section (both flat) · most radial/superficial · two slips

## How it's asked

**"Dorsal wrist laceration — which 4th-compartment stump is EIP?"** → **the one with the most distal muscle belly**

# Fat Embolism Syndrome

*Basic Science · 34 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **3–4%** isolated long-bone fracture · **10–15%** polytrauma · ~**90%** of long-bone fractures have some fat emboli (subclinical ↓ PaO₂ in almost all)

**Mortality** — up to **15%**

**Causes** — long-bone fractures (femur, tibia, pelvis) · **IM nailing** · **hip & knee arthroplasty** · rare: crush, burns, marrow biopsy/transplant, liposuction, CPR, haemoglobinopathy, steroids, pancreatitis, fatty liver **(TO)**

**Risk factors (TO)** — young age · polytrauma, multiple fractures · **closed** fractures · **conservative** treatment of long-bone fractures · IM nailing: over-zealous nailing, reaming, ↑ reaming speed, ↑ nail–cortex gap

### Pathophysiology

| Theory | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Mechanical** | Marrow fat droplets enter venous system (shear of intraosseous vessels, reaming) → obstruct pulmonary vessels |
| **Biochemical** | ✓ **Lipoprotein lipase → free fatty acids + glycerol** → endothelial toxicity → cytokines → **ARDS-like** picture · ↓ surfactant |

**Arthroplasty emboli** — **fat + marrow** (obstructive, act like PE) vs free fat of fractures (chemical injury)

## 2 Mechanism

### Intraoperative embolisation in arthroplasty

**Peak intramedullary pressure** — **cement pressurisation + femoral component insertion** (whole canal pressurised) > reaming/broaching

**Uncemented hemiarthroplasty stem insertion → hypotension, hypoxia** — ✓ **intramedullary fat + marrow embolisation**

**TKA** — IM alignment rods → emboli · ✓ **extramedullary guides ↓ embolisation risk** · overdrilling the femoral canal entry ↓ risk

## 3 Presentation

**Onset** — within **24 h** (Orthobullets) · **24–72 h** (OB) · **12–72 h** (TO)

**Classic triad** — **respiratory distress (first) · cerebral signs (confusion) · petechial rash (last)**

**Petechiae** — **axillae**, upper chest, shoulders, **conjunctivae**, oral mucosa · appear within 36 h, gone within a week · RBC extravasation from distended capillaries · pathognomonic **(TO)**

**Other** — tachycardia, tachypnoea, fever (non-specific but near-universal) · jaundice, oliguria **(TO)** · respiratory recovery in ~3 days if no further embolism **(TO)**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria

| Major (≥1) | Minor (≥4) |
|---|---|
| **Hypoxaemia (PaO₂ <60 mmHg)** + respiratory signs, radiographic changes | **Tachycardia >110** |
| **CNS depression** (unrelated to head injury) | **Pyrexia >38.5 °C** |
| ✓ **Petechial rash** | **Retinal** fat/petechiae |
| Pulmonary oedema (Orthobullets) | Fat in urine/sputum · renal changes · jaundice |
| — | **Thrombocytopenia** · ↓ Hct/Hb · ↑ ESR |

**Gurd & Wilson (TO)** — **1 major + 4 minor + fat macroglobulinaemia**

**Additional** — PCO₂ >55 · pH <7.3 · RR >35 · dyspnoea · anxiety

### Key radiology findings

**ABG (TO)** — early hypoxia + **hypocarbia, respiratory alkalosis** → later respiratory acidosis · ↑ A–a shunt within 24–48 h without ARDS

**CXR (TO)** — initially normal → diffuse fluffy bilateral infiltrates (**"snowstorm"**), bases + periphery

**CT angiogram** — negative for PE (distinguishes)

## 5 Management

### Prevention

**Most important** — ✓ **early fracture stabilisation (<24 h)** of long bones\
✗ Wrong options: delay fixation, TO

**Techniques** — narrower-shaft reamers · overdrill femoral entry in TKA · **external fixation** in medically unstable patients

### Treatment

**Supportive (ICU)** — O₂ → **CPAP** (↑ PaO₂ without ↑ FiO₂) → **mechanical ventilation with high PEEP** if FiO₂ >60% + CPAP >10 cm needed **(TO)**

**Fluids** — **albumin** preferred (restores volume, lipophilic) **(TO)** · CVP monitoring (pulmonary HTN, RV failure)

**Not recommended (TO)** — **heparin** (bleeding in polytrauma) · **high-dose steroids** (insufficient evidence)

## 6 Complications

### Bone cement implantation syndrome (TO)

**Definition** — **hypoxia, hypotension** ± **unexpected loss of consciousness**, arrhythmia, ↑ PVR, cardiac arrest around **cementation, prosthesis insertion, reduction or tourniquet deflation** in cemented arthroplasty **(TO)**

**Not a feature** — **hypertension** **(TO)**

**Timing (5 stages)** — femoral reaming · acetabular/femoral cement · prosthesis insertion · joint reduction · (tourniquet deflation in TKA) **(TO)**

**Mechanism** — exothermic cement (up to **96 °C**) expands, traps air + marrow under high intramedullary pressure → embolisation **(TO)**

| BCIS grade (TO) | Criteria |
|---|---|
| **1** | SpO₂ **<94%** or SBP fall **>20%** |
| **2** | SpO₂ **<88%** or SBP fall **>40%** or unexpected LOC |
| **3** | **Cardiovascular collapse → CPR** |

**Risk factors (TO)** — old age · poor reserve · cardiopulmonary disease · **pulmonary hypertension** · osteoporosis · **bony metastases** · hip fracture (**pathological/intertrochanteric**) · **previously uninstrumented canal** · **long-stem** component

**Treatment (TO)** — IV fluids, inotropes, vasopressors → **ECMO** if no recovery

## X vs Y

| | FES | PE (DVT) | BCIS |
|---|---|---|---|
| Timing | 12–72 h after fracture/nail | 1–6 wk postop | **Intraop** — cement, stem, reduction, tourniquet release |
| Rash | **Axillary petechiae** | No | No |
| CNS | Confusion | Rare | LOC |
| Test | Clinical · CTA negative | **CTA positive** | Clinical |

## How it's asked

**"Hypotension + hypoxia during uncemented hemi stem insertion?"** → **intramedullary fat and marrow embolisation**\
✗ Wrong options: DVT-PE · MI · fluid deficit

**"Benefit of extramedullary guides in TKA?"** → **↓ embolisation risk**

**"Cemented bipolar, death after hypoxia; marrow emboli in lungs — cause?"** → **femoral component insertion after cement pressurisation**\
✗ Wrong options: reaming · undersized broaches

**"Emboli during TKA composed of?"** → **fat and marrow**

**"Major criterion for fat embolism (TO)?"** → **petechial rash**\
✗ Wrong options: tachycardia, pyrexia, retinal changes (minor)

**"True regarding FES (TO)?"** → **CPAP and PEEP for hypoxia**\
✗ Wrong options: heparin · steroids · delay fixation

**"Bilateral femur + humerus fractures, tachycardia, hypoxia, confusion, axillary petechiae, CTA negative?"** → **fat embolism syndrome**

**"Day after reamed tibial nail: ↑ O₂, delirium, chest rash — mechanism?"** → **lipoprotein lipase activation → free fatty acids**\
✗ Wrong options: tissue factor/collagen (PE) · IgE · plasmin inhibition (TXA) · ↑ surfactant

**"TKR — hypotension + dyspnoea after tourniquet deflation (TO)?"** → **bone cement implantation syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: fat embolism (IM rod insertion)

**"NOT a component of BCIS (TO)?"** → **hypertension**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Femoral nerve

*Basic Science · 35 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

**Course** — runs **within the fascial sheath of iliopsoas** → vulnerable to iliopsoas haematoma

## 3 Presentation

### Iliopsoas haematoma

**Setting** — **over-anticoagulation** (warfarin), alcoholism → spontaneous bleed into iliopsoas sheath → compartment-like syndrome

**Features** — rapid **LLQ abdominal/groin pain → anterior thigh** · **"psoas sign"** (pain on resisted hip flexion) · **quadriceps weakness → knee buckling** · progressive femoral motor loss

**Management** — rapid evaluation · **release iliopsoas fascia + evacuate haematoma** to prevent permanent palsy

## X vs Y

| Cause of acute quadriceps weakness | Clue |
|---|---|
| **Iliopsoas haematoma** | Anticoagulated · **abdominal pain** · psoas sign |
| L3–4 posterolateral / L4–5 far-lateral disc | Back/leg pain, **no abdominal pain** |
| Epidural haematoma | No abdominal pain · cord/cauda signs |
| Femoral pseudoaneurysm | Palpable mass · rare cause of palsy |

## How it's asked

**"Anticoagulated alcoholic, LLQ pain to anterior thigh, knee buckling, quads 1/5, pain on resisted hip flexion?"** → **spontaneous bleed into iliopsoas sheath**\
✗ Wrong options: disc herniation · epidural haematoma · pseudoaneurysm

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Flexor Hallucis Longus

*Basic Science · 36 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Flexor hallucis longus |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | **Inferior ⅔ of posterior fibula** + lower interosseous membrane |
| **Insertion** | Plantar base of **distal phalanx of great toe** |
| **Action** | Flexes great toe · helps supinate · very weak ankle plantarflexor |
| **Innervation** | **Tibial nerve (S2, S3)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Muscular branches of peroneal + posterior tibial arteries |

![Flexor hallucis longus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10091-flexor-hallucis-longus.jpg)

### Origin — leg compartments (TO)

| Compartment | Origin rule (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Anterior** | All from **fibula** except **tibialis anterior** (tibia) |
| **Lateral** | All from fibula (peroneus longus, brevis) |
| **Posterior** | ✓ **Only FHL from fibula alone** · soleus + tibialis posterior from **both** tibia and fibula · FDL from tibia |

| Posterior muscle (TO) | Origin | Insertion |
|---|---|---|
| Gastrocnemius | Femoral condyles | Calcaneus (Achilles) |
| Plantaris | Lateral supracondylar ridge | Calcaneus |
| Soleus | Tibia + fibula | Calcaneus (Achilles) |
| Popliteus | Lateral femoral condyle | Tibial shaft |
| FDL | **Tibia** | Distal phalanges, lateral 4 toes |
| **FHL** | **Fibula** | Distal phalanx, great toe |
| Tibialis posterior | Tibia + fibula + IOM | Navicular tuberosity |

### MRI anatomy

**Axial ankle MRI** — FHL tendon runs on **posterior tibia, just anterior to Achilles** · peroneals behind fibula · tibialis posterior + FDL behind medial malleolus

## How it's asked

**"Axial ankle MRI, tendon on posterior tibia just anterior to Achilles?"** → **FHL**\
✗ Wrong options: tibialis posterior, FDL (behind medial malleolus) · peroneals (behind fibula)

**"Posterior-compartment muscle arising from fibula only (TO)?"** → **FHL**\
✗ Wrong options: tibialis posterior, soleus (both bones) · FDL (tibia)

### Anatomy

| | Flexor pollicis longus |

| **Origin** | Anterior radius + adjacent interosseous membrane |
| **Insertion** | Base of **distal phalanx of thumb** |

### AIN injury

**AIN** — branches **posteriorly from median nerve deep to pronator teres** → at risk in forearm fracture fixation
