# Fracture Healing

*Basic Science · 38 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Stem-cell sources

**Two major sources** — **periosteum** + **endosteum** · periosteal response ↑ by motion, ↓ by rigid fixation

### Primary vs secondary healing & strain theory

**Strain (definition)** — ✓ **change in length ÷ original length** (ΔL/L) · stress = force/area

**Perren's interfragmentary strain theory** — gap tissue differentiation depends on strain · **comminuted fractures tolerate more motion** (motion shared across many gaps) · simple transverse fractures in a nail → higher strain

| Strain | Outcome |
|---|---|
| **<2%** | **Primary healing** (intramembranous, **Haversian remodelling / cutting cones**) |
| **2–10%** | **Secondary healing** (endochondral, callus) |
| 10–100% | Fibrous tissue (or hypertrophic nonunion) |
| >100% | Nonunion |

**Tissue tolerance (TO)** — **cortical bone tolerates only 2%** · **lamellar bone up to 10%** (TO wording) · granulation tissue tolerates the **greatest** strain

**Osteoblasts** — stimulated at 0.3–2.8% strain · optimal interfragmentary motion **0.2–1 mm**

| Construct | Healing |
|---|---|
| **Compression plate**, **lag screw + neutralisation plate**, tension band, medial malleolar screws | **Primary — Haversian remodelling** (absolute stability, anatomic reduction, <2%) |
| **IM nail** (incl. CMN), **external fixator**, **bridge plate**, cast/brace/splint | **Secondary — endochondral** (relative stability, 2–10%) |
| **Locked compression plate (bridge mode)** | ✓ **Secondary (TO)** — "internal fixator" |

**Primary types (TO)** — **contact healing** (ends apposed → osteoclast **cutting cones** cross, osteoblasts follow) · **gap healing** (small gap → transverse woven bone → lamellar) · also unicortical/greenstick fractures

**Primary healing preferred** — periarticular · both-bone forearm · simple ankle fractures with syndesmotic disruption (callus would impair rotation/joint)

### Biomechanics of callus

**Peripheral callus** — ✓ **↑ moment of inertia → ↓ strain** · bending stiffness ∝ **diameter⁴** (2× diameter → **16×** stiffness) · ↑ torsional strength, peak torque

## 2 Mechanism

### Stages of secondary healing

| Stage | Events |
|---|---|
| **Inflammation** (haematoma) | Haematopoietic cells, macrophages, neutrophils, platelets → **PDGF, TNF-α, TGF-β, IL-1, 6, 10, 12** (from 24 h) · **BMPs ↑**, MSC migration (**SDF-1, G-CSF**) · granulation tissue · **COX-2 → Runx2/osterix** (NSAIDs act here) |
| **Repair** | **Primary callus within 2 wk** · stable → osteoblasts, unstable → chondrocytes · **type II collagen early → type I** · soft → hard callus (woven bone) · callus amount ∝ 1/immobilisation |
| **Remodelling** | Starts mid-repair, continues years · Ihh, PTHrP, FGF, BMP · **type X** collagen (hypertrophic chondrocytes, calcification) · chondrocyte apoptosis → **VEGF** → vessels · woven → **lamellar** (Wolff's law, piezoelectric) |

### Biologic stages & mediators

| Mediator | Role |
|---|---|
| **TNF-α** | Pro-healing inflammatory cytokine · **deficiency (HIV) delays** endochondral + intramembranous ossification |
| **BMP-2** | ↑ in **inflammatory phase** · recruits MSCs · differentiation |
| **SDF-1 + G-CSF** | ✓ **MSC migration to fracture** (absence → nonunion) |
| **VEGF** | Neovascularisation |
| RANKL | Osteoclasts |
| IL-10 | Anti-inflammatory |
| **COX-2** | **MSC → osteoblasts** · knockout mice heal **more slowly** (✗ Wrong options: faster) · converts arachidonic acid → PGH2 · ↑ in cancers |
| **MMP-13** | ✓ From **terminally differentiated chondrocytes** → degrades **cartilaginous** ECM (soft → woven bone) |

| Collagen by phase (OB) | Type |
|---|---|
| Inflammation/clot | I, II |
| Fibroblasts / fibrous tissue | III / V |
| Soft (chondroid) callus | **II + IX** |
| **Callus calcification** | ✓ **X** (hypertrophic chondrocytes) |
| Bone deposition | **I** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Variables that influence healing

**Blood supply — most important** — ↓ immediately → ↑ peaks **2 wk** → normal **3–5 months** · unreamed nails preserve endosteal supply · reaming devascularises inner **50–80%** · loose nails reperfuse faster

**Head injury** — may ↑ osteogenic response

**Mechanical** — soft-tissue attachments · stability/strain · location · bone loss · **segmental/butterfly** → ↑ nonunion (intercalary blood supply)

| Patient factor | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Vitamin D / Ca deficiency** | **84%** of nonunions metabolic · **>66% vitamin D deficient** |
| Protein malnutrition | ↓ callus strength · amino acids ↑ albumin, body mass, muscle protein, callus mineralisation (✗ Wrong options: **not IGF-1 mRNA**) |
| **Roux-en-Y gastric bypass** | **Duodenum bypassed** → ↓ Ca/vit D → **2° hyperparathyroidism** → nonunion (✗ Wrong options: sleeve · band · colectomy · nephrectomy) |
| **Diabetes** | ↓ callus cellularity, delayed endochondral ossification, weak callus · **1.6× longer** |
| **Nicotine** | ↓ angiogenesis, weak callus · **spine pseudarthrosis +500%** · open tibia **~70% longer** |
| **HIV** | Fragility fractures, delay — ARVs, poor circulation, **TNF-α deficiency**, nutrition |
| Bisphosphonates | Longer healing (wrist ORIF) · atypical femur fractures |
| Corticosteroids | **6.5%** higher IT nonunion · ↓ IGF-1, TGF-β |
| NSAIDs | COX inhibition |
| Quinolones | Chondrocyte toxicity |
| High-dose radiation | ↓ cellularity, long-term remodelling change |

**Detrimental (TO)** — age · malnutrition (**albumin <3.5, lymphocytes <1,500**) · smoking · **alcohol** · DM · cancer, osteomalacia, Cushing's, obesity · **anaemia** (iron for proline hydroxylation, mineralisation) · **PVD** · NSAIDs, chemo, steroids, BPs, quinolones · radiation · infection · open, intra-articular, segmental, comminuted, denervated fractures · **intact fellow bone** · soft-tissue interposition · inadequate reduction/fixation (too short, too stiff) · **nephrotic syndrome — not listed** **(TO)**

**Vicarious blood supply (TO)** — femoral neck · scaphoid waist/proximal pole · talar body · paediatric lateral condyle · 5th MT base · distal ⅓ tibia · distal ⅓ ulna

## 5 Management

### Nonunion (stability, metabolic causes)

```
Nonunion
├─ Hypertrophic (abundant callus) → ↑ MECHANICAL STABILITY
│      (cause: inadequate stability, biology intact)
├─ Atrophic / oligotrophic (poor biology) → TAKEDOWN + BONE GRAFT + STABILISE
└─ No infection signs → METABOLIC WORK-UP (25-OH vit D) → VITAMIN D + OBSERVE
```

**Oligotrophic distal tibia nonunion, no infection** — ✓ **observe + vitamin D** (keep weight-bearing)\
✗ Wrong options: NWB · suppressive antibiotics

**Lab of choice for vit D** — **25-hydroxyvitamin D**

### Bone stimulation (LIPUS, capacitive coupling)

| Modality | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **LIPUS** (30 mW/cm² pulsed) | ✓ **Nanomotion** (mechanical strain across cell wall) · ↑ vascularity, protein expression · ↑ callus torque/stiffness · delayed/nonunion healing ~**80%** |
| **Capacitive coupling** (AC) | ✓ **Ca²⁺ translocation via voltage-gated channels** → calmodulin → ↑ BMP, cAMP, TGF-β1 · collagen, cartilage calcification |
| **Direct current** | ↓ O₂, ↑ pH → ↓ osteoclasts, ↑ osteoblasts · inflammatory-like response |
| **PEMF** | Calcification of **fibrocartilage** (✗ Wrong options: fibrous tissue) |

**LIPUS Level I evidence** — ✓ radius shaft, distal radius, **casted tibia**, scaphoid\
✗ Wrong options: **tibia treated with reamed IM nail**

### Dynamisation

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Dynamisation | Gradually **destabilise** fixation to load bone |
| **Reverse dynamisation** | ✓ **Early micromotion → callus**, then convert to **rigid** fixation to accelerate union |
| Accordion method | Alternate distraction/compression of regenerate |

## How it's asked

**"Healing with locked compression plating (TO)?"** → **secondary** (bridge mode)

**"True regarding fracture healing (TO)?"** → **rigid compression fixation → healing by cutting cones**\
✗ Wrong options: cortical bone tolerates 10% · contact healing in LCP

**"Femoral nail — healing type?"** → **secondary**\
✗ Wrong options: Haversian · cutting cones · extramembranous

**"Definition of strain?"** → **ΔL / original length**\
✗ Wrong options: force/area (stress)

**"Direct (primary) healing with?"** → **lag screw + neutralisation plate**\
✗ Wrong options: nails, ex-fix, bridge plate

**"Peripheral callus biomechanics?"** → **↑ moment of inertia, ↓ strain**

**"Bridge plating of distal tibia relies on?"** → **endochondral ossification via chondrocyte proliferation + hypertrophy**

**"Construct healing by enchondral ossification?"** → **bridge plate**

**"Tibial nail healing — true?"** → **soft callus replaced by woven bone**\
✗ Wrong options: <2% strain · cutting cones

**"Comminuted femur IMN — healing?"** → **secondary, strain 2–10%**

**"Radial shaft compression plate — true?"** → **Haversian remodelling**

**"Why comminuted vs transverse nailed femurs tolerate different gap deformation?"** → **Perren's strain theory**\
✗ Wrong options: moment of inertia · Young's modulus

**"Medial malleolar screw fixation — healing?"** → **Haversian remodelling**

**"Relies on strain <2%?"** → **transverse radial shaft + compression plating**

**"NOT detrimental to healing (TO)?"** → **nephrotic syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: PVD, DM, anaemia

**"Hypertrophic humeral nonunion heals with?"** → **↑ mechanical stability**

**"Commonest cause of hypertrophic nonunion?"** → **inadequate mechanical stability**

**"Oligotrophic tibial nonunion, no infection — labs support?"** → **observation + vitamin D**

**"Surgery contributing to tibial nonunion?"** → **Roux-en-Y gastric bypass**

**"FALSE about COX-2?"** → **"knockout mice heal faster"**

**"Type X collagen characteristic of?"** → **cartilage callus formation + calcification**

**"Amino acid supplementation in malnourished rats ↑ all EXCEPT?"** → **IGF-1 mRNA expression**

**"Role of MMP-13 in early callus?"** → **from terminally differentiated chondrocytes, degrades cartilaginous ECM**

**"True about healing stages (clavicle)?"** → **BMP ↑ in inflammatory phase**\
✗ Wrong options: callus in inflammation · NSAIDs act in remodelling · TNF-α excess slows · woven bone final

**"No SDF-1 or G-CSF → affects?"** → **MSC migration to fracture**

**"LIPUS mechanism?"** → **nanomotion at fracture site**

**"Capacitive coupling mechanism?"** → **Ca²⁺ translocation via voltage-gated channels**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ O₂/↑ pH (direct current)

**"Level I LIPUS benefit in all EXCEPT?"** → **tibia shaft with reamed IM nail**

**"Reverse dynamisation?"** → **early micromotion then rigid fixation**

**"Ex-fix of comminuted MC — healing like all EXCEPT?"** → **Weber B ankle ORIF** (primary)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# General and Regional Anesthesia in Orthopaedics

*Basic Science · 39 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Components of anaesthesia

**Five A's** — amnesia · anxiolysis · analgesia · akinesia · attenuation of autonomic responses

### General anaesthesia

**Inhalational** — isoflurane, sevoflurane, desflurane · **nitrous oxide → gaseous abdominal (bowel) distension** (diffuses in, can't leave) → hinders fluoroscopy in pelvic/spine surgery

**IV non-opioids** — propofol, etomidate, benzodiazepines, dexmedetomidine, ketamine · **opioids** — fentanyl family, morphine, hydromorphone

| Neuromuscular blocker | Agents | Reversal |
|---|---|---|
| **Depolarising** (depolarises + blocks ACh-R) | **Succinylcholine** (short only) | — |
| **Non-depolarising** | Intermediate: **rocuronium, vecuronium**, atracurium, cisatracurium · long: pancuronium | **Neostigmine** · **sugammadex** (rocuronium) |

## 5 Management

### Upper-limb blocks

| Block | Site | Use | Key complication |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Interscalene** | Brachial plexus between **anterior + middle scalenes**, cricoid level | **Shoulder**, upper arm, elbow | **Phrenic block → hemidiaphragm paresis** (very common side effect) · **sensory neuropathy / temporary paraesthesia ≤6 mo (2.3%)** = commonest complication · Horner's · RLN · pneumothorax · vertebral artery · epidural/spinal spread |
| **Supraclavicular** | Above clavicle | Lower humerus → hand | **Hemidiaphragmatic paralysis > infraclavicular** · pneumothorax · subclavian artery |
| Infraclavicular | Below clavicle | Lower humerus → hand | Less phrenic involvement |
| Axillary | Axilla | Elbow, forearm, wrist, hand | — |
| **Bier block** | IV regional (lidocaine distal IV, tourniquet) | Short (<60 min) hand/forearm (CTR) | **Deflate ≥30 min** → avoid LAST |

![Interscalene block](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9075-interscalene-block.jpg)

**Interscalene contraindications** — ✓ **moderate–severe lung disease (FEV1 45%)** (diaphragm paresis ↓ lung volumes ~30%) · contralateral phrenic injury · LA allergy · infection · uncontrolled seizures · coagulopathy · pre-existing nerve injury\
✗ Wrong options: age, aspirin, obesity alone, 1st-degree heart block

**Hemidiaphragm paresis, SpO₂ 97% on 2 L** — ✓ **observe + monitor**\
✗ Wrong options: ABG, CTPA, chest tube, intubation

**Postop dyspnoea after supraclavicular block, normal CXR** — ✓ **hemidiaphragmatic paralysis** (sniff test) · phrenic nerve (C3–5) on anterior scalene

**Interscalene catheter → ptosis, miosis (TO)** — ✓ **Horner's syndrome → remove catheter** (reversible) · stellate ganglion paralysis from high LA concentration **(TO)**

### Neuraxial

| Technique | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Spinal** | Single shot, 24–27 G needle, morphine + bupivacaine · THA/TKA, hip fracture (= GA for delirium, 60-day mortality, ambulation) · spinal headache (↓ small needle), haematoma, opioid effects |
| Epidural | Indwelling catheter · hypotension, motor block |
| Combined spinal-epidural | Both |

### Lower-limb blocks

| Block | Target / landmark | Use | Issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lumbar plexus (psoas compartment) | L1–4 (obturator, LFCN, femoral) · 3–5 cm lateral to L4 spinous process | Hip, thigh, knee | Retroperitoneal haematoma, epidural/intrathecal spread, **falls** |
| Posterior quadratus lumborum | Behind QL → thoracolumbar fascia | Hip | Deep, technically hard |
| **Fascia iliaca** | Femoral, obturator, LFCN · 1 cm caudal to lateral ⅓ / medial ⅔ of pubic tubercle–ASIS line | Hip fracture, thigh, knee | ✓ **↓ postoperative delirium in geriatric hip fracture** |
| **Femoral** | Lateral to femoral artery (L2–4) | Knee | **Prolonged quadriceps weakness, falls** |
| **Adductor canal** | Saphenous + nerve to VMO (distal to quad motor branches) | **TKA** | ✓ **= pain control, better mobility** than femoral |
| **iPACK** | Between popliteal artery + posterior capsule | TKA | **Motor-sparing** · US-guided · ✓ **iPACK + ACB > ACB alone** |
| Sciatic | GT–PSIS / GT–sacral hiatus lines | Leg, ankle, foot | Heel ulcers, delayed nerve-injury diagnosis |
| Obturator | 2 cm inferior + 2 cm lateral to pubic tubercle | Adductor spasm, hip OA pain, knee adjunct | Anterior branch → hip/inner thigh · posterior → knee |
| Popliteal | Sciatic ~10 cm above popliteal crease | Foot/ankle (+ saphenous) | Foot drop, pressure sores |
| Saphenous | Behind sartorius | Medial knee/leg/ankle | — |
| Periarticular injection | — | TKA/THA | Better TKA pain control than iPACK (Orthobullets) |

**Geniculate blocks** — chronic OA pain before TKA, not perioperative

### Regional techniques — haematoma block & tourniquet (TO)

**Haematoma block** — LA **injected directly into the fracture site** · distal radius, ankle fracture-dislocation · not "**around nerves at target site**" (false) **(TO)**

| Tourniquet facts (TO) | |
|---|---|
| Cuff | **Widest possible** (arm 10 cm, thigh ≥15 cm) — effective at lower pressure |
| Pressure | Arm SBP + **100** · thigh SBP + **100–150** mmHg · best: **limb occlusion pressure (LOP)** + margin (LOP <130 → +40 · 131–190 → +60 · >190 → +80) |
| Time | **2 h** safe limit · if longer, deflate **10 min** |
| Commonest complication | **Nerve injury** · UL > LL · **radial nerve** most susceptible |
| Nerve injury cause | **Cuff pressure > duration** (✗ Wrong options: "duration more than pressure" (false)) · direct pressure > ischaemia · too low pressure (venous congestion) also |
| Post-tourniquet syndrome | **Ischaemia** · oedema, redness, pain, dysaesthesia, stiffness |
| Relative contraindications | PVD, DVT, severe infection, poor cardiac status · avoid exsanguination in infection/malignancy |
| Prep solutions | Prevent seepage under cuff (chemical burns) |

### Blood management

**Hypotensive epidural anaesthesia (HEA)** — ✓ **best ↓ blood loss + transfusion** in bilateral THA · block to **T2** (cardio-accelerator fibres) → **MAP ~50 mmHg** · bradycardia → low-dose **epinephrine** · normal HR, CVP, SV, CO · usable in HTN, poor cardiac function, CKD · beats spinal, normotensive epidural, hypotensive TIVA, GA

**Other** — preop EPO · autologous donation · acute normovolaemic haemodilution · cell saver

## 6 Complications

### Malignant hyperthermia & local anaesthetic toxicity

| | **Malignant hyperthermia** | **LAST** |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | **AD, RYR1** · triggered by **volatile agents + succinylcholine** | **Intravascular bupivacaine** (Na-channel block) |
| Features | Rigidity, **masseter spasm**, ↑ **EtCO₂**, ↑ temp, tachycardia, dark urine (myoglobin) | CNS: perioral numbness, metallic taste, seizures, coma · CVS: arrhythmia, **asystole/VF** |
| Treatment | Stop triggers, hyperventilate O₂, cool + ✓ **dantrolene** | ✓ **IV 20% lipid emulsion** + ACLS |
| Incidence | 1 : 15,000–50,000 | — |

| Antidote | For |
|---|---|
| Naloxone | Opioids |
| Flumazenil | Benzodiazepines |
| Protamine | Heparin |
| Neostigmine / sugammadex | Non-depolarising NMB (rocuronium) |
| Atropine, isoproterenol | β-blocker bradycardia |

**Bone cement implantation syndrome** — hypotension + hypoxaemia → IV fluids, vasopressors, 100% O₂

**Epidural morphine + steroid paste after laminectomy** — ✓ **surgical site infection**

## How it's asked

**"Interscalene catheter, facial discomfort, ptosis/miosis (TO)?"** → **Horner's — remove catheter**\
✗ Wrong options: facial palsy

**"Most likely complication of interscalene block?"** → **sensory neuropathy**\
✗ Wrong options: motor neuropathy, CRPS, pneumothorax

**"Relative contraindication to interscalene block?"** → **FEV1 45%**\
✗ Wrong options: age, aspirin, obesity, 1st-degree block

**"Most common complication after interscalene block?"** → **temporary paraesthesia up to 6 months**

**"Post-interscalene high-riding diaphragm, SpO₂ 97%?"** → **observe and monitor**

**"Post-supraclavicular block dyspnoea, normal CXR?"** → **hemidiaphragmatic paralysis**\
✗ Wrong options: pneumothorax · LAST

**"Best describes iPACK?"** → **ACB + iPACK > ACB alone**\
✗ Wrong options: femoral branches (ACB) · motor-sacrificing

**"Block C (adductor canal) vs A (femoral)?"** → **equivalent pain control, better mobility**

**"Cardiac arrest during TKA, resuscitated with 20% lipid?"** → **bupivacaine**

**"Family history, rigidity, ↑ EtCO₂ after succinylcholine — antidote?"** → **dantrolene**

**"Nitrous oxide effect?"** → **gaseous abdominal distension**

**"Bilateral THA — best ↓ transfusion?"** → **hypotensive epidural anaesthesia**

**"Epidural morphine + steroid paste after laminectomy — complication?"** → **surgical site infection**

**"False about haematoma block (TO)?"** → **"LA injected around nerves at target site"**

**"False about tourniquet palsy (TO)?"** → **"related more to duration than pressure"**

# Genetic Pearls

*Basic Science · 40 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Genes & lab markers — genetic conditions

| Autosomal dominant | Gene | Defect |
|---|---|---|
| **Achondroplasia** | **FGFR3** | ↓ chondrocyte proliferation |
| Apert | **FGFR2** | ↓ chondrocyte proliferation |
| **CMT (80–90%)** | **PMP22** | Demyelination |
| **Pseudoachondroplasia** | **COMP** | Abnormal cartilage |
| SED congenita, **Kniest** | **COL2A1** (type II) | Cartilage matrix |
| **MED type I** | **COMP** | — |
| MED type II | **Type IX collagen** | — |
| Jansen metaphyseal chondrodysplasia | **PTHrP** receptor | PTH function |
| **Schmid** metaphyseal chondrodysplasia | **Type X collagen** | Cartilage matrix |
| **Cleidocranial dysplasia** | **RUNX2 / CBFA1** | ↓ intramembranous ossification |
| **OI types I–V** | **COL1A1 / COL1A2** | Type I collagen |
| Milroy disease | FLT4 | Lymphatics |

| Autosomal recessive | Gene |
|---|---|
| **Diastrophic dysplasia** | **DTD** (sulfate transporter — PG sulfation) |
| Friedreich ataxia | **Frataxin** |
| OI type VI | SERPINF1 |
| McKusick metaphyseal chondrodysplasia | RMRP |

| X-linked recessive | Gene |
|---|---|
| **SED tarda** | **TRAPPC2** |
| CMT (10–20%) | **Connexin** |

### Tumour suppressors & translocations (RB, p53, Li-Fraumeni)

**Tumour suppressor** — normally inhibits proliferation · loss → unregulated growth

| Gene | Type | Facts |
|---|---|---|
| **RB-1** | ✓ **Tumour suppressor** · **recessive** (both alleles) | Abnormal = **phosphorylated** (can't bind DNA) · 100% retinoblastoma · **35% osteosarcoma** |
| **p53** | Tumour suppressor · **dominant** (one allele) | Blocks entry to **S-phase** if DNA damaged · ~50% of all tumours · **osteosarcoma 20–65%** · chondrosarcoma · **Li-Fraumeni (germline, AD)** |

**Li-Fraumeni** — breast (25%) > **osteosarcoma (20%)** · soft-tissue sarcoma, leukaemia, brain, colon · homozygous p53 → 100% malignancy

**Oncogenes** — **FAK** (focal adhesion kinase) · **Erb-2** (EGF receptor variant)

| Sarcoma | Translocation | Fusion |
|---|---|---|
| **Ewing's** | **t(11;22)** | **EWS-FLI1** |
| **Alveolar rhabdomyosarcoma** | **t(2;13)** (PAX3 70%) / t(1;13) (PAX7) | **PAX3-FKHR** |
| **Myxoid liposarcoma** | **t(12;16)** | **TLS-CHOP** |
| **Synovial sarcoma** | **t(X;18)** (>90%) | **SYT-SSX1/2/4** |
| **Clear cell sarcoma** | **t(12;22)** | **EWS-ATF1** |
| Myxoid chondrosarcoma | **t(9;22)** | EWS-CHN |
| **Osteosarcoma** | ✓ **None** (Rb/p53 mutations instead) | — |

**Translocations** — in up to 95% of sarcomas · allow expression of normally silent oncogenes

| Other gene | Disease |
|---|---|
| IDH-1/2 | Maffucci (Ollier) |
| **GNAS** (Gsα activating) | Fibrous dysplasia, **McCune-Albright** |

### Epigenetics & siRNA

**Epigenetics** — ✓ **inheritable alteration without DNA mutation** · **DNA methylation, histone modification, nucleosome location, non-coding RNA** · role in **OA** (methylation in chondrocytes/synoviocytes → proteases) · genomic imprinting (Prader-Willi, Angelman)

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Transgene / transgenesis | Gene artificially introduced into embryo / another organism |
| Enhancer | Region ↑ transcription |
| Promoter | Controls transcription start |
| Transformation | Uptake of exogenous DNA (plasmid into bacterium) |
| Hybridisation | Annealing of complementary DNA/RNA |

### Laboratory techniques & markers

| Assay | Detects |
|---|---|
| **Southern blot** | **DNA** |
| **Northern blot** | **RNA** |
| **Western blot** | **Protein** |
| Southwestern blot | DNA-binding proteins |
| **RT-PCR** | Low-copy RNA (→ cDNA) · very sensitive |
| **siRNA** | ✓ **Blocks translation of mRNA** (mRNA degraded) · loss-of-function studies |
| **Cytogenetics** | ✓ **Chromosome size/number → translocations, prenatal screening** (trisomy 21) |
| ELISA | Proteins in solution |
| Flow cytometry | Surface proteins → cell sorting |
| Gel electrophoresis | Separates DNA by size |

![Molecular biology assays](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9025-directions.jpg)

| Tumour marker | Cancer |
|---|---|
| **CEA** | **Colorectal** |
| CA 19-9 | Pancreatic |
| CA-125 | Ovarian |
| CA 15-3 | Breast |
| AFP | Hepatocellular |

## How it's asked

**"RB-1 is best characterised as?"** → **tumour suppressor**\
✗ Wrong options: oncogene, proto-oncogene

**"Translocation associated with osteosarcoma?"** → **none**\
✗ Wrong options: SYT-SSX1, CHOP-TLS, EWS-FLI1, FKHR-PAX3

**"Correctly paired sarcoma + translocation?"** → **clear cell sarcoma t(12;22)**

**"9-yr-old, destructive distal femur lesion, p53 mutation — histology?"** → **osteosarcoma**

**"Inherited p53 mutation causes?"** → **Li-Fraumeni**\
✗ Wrong options: Maffucci (IDH) · FD/McCune-Albright (GNAS)

**"Heritable change without DNA mutation (methylation, histones)?"** → **epigenetics**

**"Function of siRNA?"** → **blocks translation of mRNA**\
✗ Wrong options: Western (protein) · Southern (DNA)

**"DNA methylation in chondrocytes in OA?"** → **epigenetics**

**"Tool to detect translocations prenatally?"** → **cytogenetic analysis**

**"Serum marker for colorectal carcinoma?"** → **CEA**

# Gluteus medius

*Basic Science · 41 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Gluteus medius |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | Dorsal ilium below iliac crest |
| **Insertion** | Lateral + superior **greater trochanter** |
| **Action** | ✓ **Major hip abductor** · anterior fibres IR · posterior fibres ER |
| **Innervation** | **Superior gluteal nerve (L4, L5, S1)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Superior gluteal artery |

![Gluteus medius](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10062-gluteus-medius.jpg)

**Hip abduction (TO, image)** — ✓ **gluteus medius**\
✗ Wrong options: gluteus maximus · obturator externus/internus

## 3 Presentation

### Trendelenburg sign after proximal femoral replacement

**Mechanism** — abductors detached and reattached to megaprosthesis · **tendon won't heal to metal** → abductor weakness

**Trendelenburg** — weak abductor on **stance** side → **pelvis drops on the contralateral side** in single-leg stance · trunk shifts over stance leg · ↑ **hip instability** risk

| Test | Detects |
|---|---|
| **Trendelenburg** | ✓ **Abductor deficiency** |
| FADIR | Impingement |
| Ober | Tight ITB |
| Patrick (FABER) | Hip vs SI pathology |
| Thomas | Hip flexion contracture |

## How it's asked

**"3 months after proximal femoral replacement for sarcoma — positive exam?"** → **Trendelenburg sign**\
✗ Wrong options: FADIR, Ober, Patrick, Thomas

**"Muscle producing the hip movement shown — abduction (TO)?"** → **gluteus medius**

# Gout

*Basic Science · 42 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Demographics** — recurrent attacks in **men 40–60 yr**

**Site** — lower limb · **podagra (1st MTP)** · knee, ankle · hand/wrist possible · **tophi**: ear helix, eyelid, olecranon, Achilles

**Risk factors** — chemotherapy · alcohol

### Pathophysiology

**Primary gout** — idiopathic disorder of **nucleic acid (purine) metabolism** → **hyperuricaemia** → MSU crystal deposition

**Secondary gout** — high cell turnover: **psoriasis, haemolytic anaemia, leukaemia, chemotherapy**

**Inflammation** — crystals → proteases, prostaglandins, **leukotriene B4**, free radicals · tophi/pannus release IL-6, IL-8, TNF-α

**Associations** — **renal stones**, urate nephropathy · **septic arthritis can coexist** (~1.5%) — crystals don't exclude infection

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — acute severe joint pain, swelling, erythema · mimics septic arthritis · remits in 1–2 wk, recurs

**Tophus aspirate** — **white, toothpaste-like** · tophi often confused with infection

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria

**Definitive** — ✓ **joint aspiration + polarised microscopy** (or tophaceous deposits)

| Gout (MSU) | Pseudogout (CPPD) |
|---|---|
| **Needle-shaped**, thin, tapered, **intracellular** | **Rhomboid** |
| **Strongly negatively birefringent** | **Positively** birefringent |
| **Yellow parallel** to red compensator · blue perpendicular | Blue parallel |
| 1st MTP | Knee (large joints) |

**Serum uric acid** — not diagnostic · **80%** with hyperuricaemia never get gout · may be **normal during an attack**

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **punched-out periarticular erosions with sclerotic overhanging edges** · **soft-tissue tophi** · joint space preserved until late

![Gout — punched-out erosions and tophi](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9041-22a-moved.jpg)

| Foot X-ray | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| Periarticular erosions + tophi, 1st MTP | **Gout** |
| Pencil-in-cup, bilateral | Psoriatic arthritis |
| Flattened 2nd MT head | Freiberg's |
| Hindfoot fragmentation | Charcot |
| MTP dislocations, aspherical 1st MT head | RA |

**Biopsy** — acute + chronic inflammation, **prominent clefts** (crystals dissolved), no atypia

## 5 Management

### Treatment & distinguishing from septic arthritis

| Phase | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Acute** | **NSAID — indomethacin** 50 mg tid (inhibits phagocytosis) · **colchicine** (if **peptic ulcer history**; can be IV) · steroids (oral, IA, IV) if NSAID/colchicine contraindicated (IA only if no sepsis) |
| **Chronic** | ✓ **Allopurinol** (xanthine oxidase inhibitor) — not in acute attack |
| Prophylaxis after recurrent attacks | **Colchicine** (up to 85% effective) |

```
Hot swollen joint, aspirate
├─ MSU crystals, synovial WBC <50k, well → NSAIDs (± ALLOPURINOL LATER)
├─ Crystals + WBC >50k / fever / infection risk → EMPIRIC IV ANTIBIOTICS → I&D
│      (crystals do not exclude sepsis)
└─ CPPD crystals → NSAIDs ± IA STEROID (not allopurinol)
```

**Fever 38.3, tachycardia, recent abdominal abscess, crystals on aspirate** — ✓ **empiric IV antibiotics** (cultures taken) → likely I&D

**WBC 20k, normal serum markers, MSU crystals** — ✓ **oral NSAIDs**\
✗ Wrong options: antibiotics · allopurinol · I&D · uric acid level

**Isolated gout flare suitable for discharge** — ✓ **SFA 35k WBC + MSU**\
✗ Wrong options: 75k + MSU (superimposed sepsis) · CPPD · 200k RBC (haemarthrosis)

**Hand lytic lesions, uric acid 10.4, clefts on biopsy** — ✓ **colchicine + indomethacin**\
✗ Wrong options: radiation, chemo, amputation, antibiotics

## X vs Y

| Aspirate image | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| Negatively birefringent needles | **Gout** |
| Positively birefringent rhomboids | Pseudogout |
| Haemosiderin-laden giant cells | PVNS |
| Gram-positive cocci | Septic arthritis |
| Histiocytes, inflammatory cells | RA |

## How it's asked

**"60-yr-old, 2 yr intermittent great-toe pain, erosions + soft-tissue densities?"** → **MSU crystal deposition**\
✗ Wrong options: CPPD, renal osteodystrophy, TB, sarcoid

**"Foot radiograph most consistent with gout?"** → **periarticular erosions + tophi** (B)

**"Clinical + radiographic tophaceous lesion caused by?"** → **uric acid deposition**

**"Swollen hot 1st MTP — definitive diagnosis?"** → **aspiration of 1st MTP**\
✗ Wrong options: serum uric acid/CRP/ESR · MRI · RF

**"Gout diagnosed by tophi or?"** → **MSU crystals in synovial fluid**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ serum uric acid

**"Hand lesion, negatively birefringent intracellular rods?"** → **gout**\
✗ Wrong options: CPPD, infection, GCT

**"Alcoholic, recurrent knee swelling, colchicine — aspirate image?"** → **negatively birefringent needles**

**"Crystal analysis consistent with gout?"** → **negatively birefringent needle-shaped**

**"Line cook, tophi on fingers, acute wrist pain — SFA?"** → **negatively birefringent needles**

**"Recurrent medial forefoot pain responsive to NSAIDs, normal WBC?"** → **needle-shaped negatively birefringent crystals**

**"Febrile, recent diverticular abscess, crystals in knee aspirate — next?"** → **empiric IV antibiotics**

**"Elderly woman, finger lytic lesions, uric acid 10.4 — treatment?"** → **colchicine + indomethacin**

**"Knee aspirate WBC 20k, MSU, normal labs — next?"** → **oral NSAIDs**

**"Isolated gout flare suitable for discharge?"** → **35k WBC + MSU**
