# Hemophilia

*Basic Science · 43 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & types

| | Haemophilia A | Haemophilia B (Christmas) |
|---|---|---|
| Factor | **VIII** | **IX** |
| Incidence | **1 : 5,000** males | **1 : 30,000** males |
| Inheritance | **X-linked recessive** | **X-linked recessive** |

**Acquired haemophilia** — rare, autoimmune, not inherited

**Family history** — affected **maternal uncles**

**Pathophysiology** — VIII + IX needed for **thrombin generation in the intrinsic pathway** → unstable clot

### Genes & lab markers

**Coagulation** — ✓ **aPTT prolonged, PT normal** · LFTs normal · CBC (Hb)

**Factor assays** — plasma factor VIII/IX activity + **inhibitor assay** (diagnosis)

**Bethesda assay** — quantifies inhibitor antibody if inhibitor positive · none / **<5 BU low** / **>5 BU high**

| Severity | Factor level |
|---|---|
| Mild | 5–25% |
| Moderate | 1–5% |
| **Severe** | **0–1%** |

| Bleeding disorder | Inheritance | Feature |
|---|---|---|
| **Factor VIII / IX deficiency** | ✓ **X-linked recessive** | Spontaneous haemarthroses |
| Factor XI (haemophilia C) | Autosomal recessive | Surgical bleeding, no spontaneous haemarthrosis |
| **von Willebrand** (commonest bleeding disorder) | Autosomal dominant (chr 12) | **Mucosal** bleeding · ↓ platelet adhesion |
| Protein C / S deficiency, factor V Leiden | AD | **Thrombosis** (not bleeding) |
| Vitamin K deficiency | Acquired | Diet, malabsorption, liver disease |

### DIC (fulminant)

**Labs** — ✓ **aPTT ↑** and PT ↑ · ↓ fibrinogen, ↓ platelets · ↑ FDPs, **↑ D-dimer** · heparin not proven · causes: massive transfusion, sepsis, burns, crush, liver disease, malignancy, obstetric

## 3 Presentation

**Bleeding** — joints (**knee most common** haemarthrosis; elbow, ankle, shoulder, spine) · muscles (quadriceps, hamstrings, **iliopsoas**, biceps, triceps) · mucocutaneous

### Orthopaedic manifestations

**Haemophilic arthropathy** — synovitis → cartilage destruction → deformity

**Intramuscular haematoma (pseudotumour)** — ✓ **iliacus haematoma → femoral nerve** compression → paraesthesia in **L4 / saphenous** distribution (medial distal tibia) · groin pain after sport (forceful hip flexion)

**Other** — LLD (**epiphyseal overgrowth**) · fractures (osteopenia, **heal in normal time**) · compartment syndrome

![Dermatomes — L4/saphenous territory](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4113-dermatomes-netter2-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| X-ray (AP, lateral, oblique, skyline) | Epiphyseal overgrowth · osteopenia · **squared patella + condyles (Jordan's sign)** · ballooned distal femur · **wide intercondylar notch** · long thin patella |
| CT | Pseudotumour location (bone vs soft tissue), fracture pattern |
| **MRI** | **Best soft tissue** · haemarthrosis, synovitis, **haemosiderin** · cartilage |
| US | Effusion vs pseudotumour · follow intramuscular haematomas |

![Knee — squared condyles, widened notch](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4113-xray-knee-col-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Management (factor replacement, synovectomy, haematoma)

| Situation | Target factor level |
|---|---|
| Vigorous physiotherapy | 20% |
| **Acute haematoma** | **30%** |
| **Acute haemarthrosis, soft-tissue surgery** | **40–50%** (OB: "within 60%") |
| **Skeletal surgery** | **100% for week 1 → >50% for week 2** |

**High-titre factor VIII inhibitor** — ✓ **factor VIIa** (bypasses VIII) for acute bleed, **compartment syndrome**, surgery

**Femoral fracture + compartment syndrome + high inhibitor titre** — ✓ **factor VIIa immediately → fasciotomy + IMN**\
✗ Wrong options: VIII replacement (won't work) · operate first · plate

**Non-life-threatening bleed** — analgesia, physio · no routine immobilisation or paracentesis

**Synovectomy** — ✓ **recurrent haemarthrosis despite optimal medical management** · ↓ bleeding but doesn't restore ROM or reverse cartilage damage · not for radiographic narrowing, stiffness, prophylaxis

**Radioactive synoviorthesis** — ↓ recurrence **more than surgical synovectomy**

**End-stage** — arthroplasty

### VTE prophylaxis

**Known bleeding disorder (factor VIII deficiency)** — ✓ **pneumatic compression only, no pharmacoprophylaxis** **(TO)** · also active liver disease\
✗ Wrong options: protein C/S deficiency, factor V Leiden (thrombophilias)

## 6 Complications

**Inhibitors (IgG)** — **4–20%** · any time · **relative contraindication to surgery** · screen preop

**Blood-borne infection** — HIV ~**10–15%** (this note) · up to 90% (arthropathy note, older cohorts) · recombinant factor VIII (1992) eliminated risk

**Allergy** to blood products

## How it's asked

**"Avoid pharmacoprophylaxis in favour of pneumatic compression (TO)?"** → **factor VIII deficiency**\
✗ Wrong options: protein C, S, factor V Leiden

**"Haemophilic boy, iliacus haematoma, medial distal tibia paraesthesia — nerve?"** → **femoral**\
✗ Wrong options: obturator, genitofemoral

**"Femur fracture + compartment syndrome + high VIII inhibitor?"** → **factor VIIa now, then fasciotomy + IMN**

**"Primary indication for synovectomy?"** → **recurrent bleeding despite optimal medical management**

**"Fulminant DIC finding?"** → **↑ aPTT**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ fibrinogen · ↓ D-dimer · normal PT · heparin curative

**"Factor VIII deficiency inheritance?"** → **X-linked recessive**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hemophilic Arthropathy

*Basic Science · 44 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — ✓ **↓ significantly due to home factor treatment** (recombinant factor VIII since 1992 — also removed HIV/hepatitis risk)

**Demographics** — young males, **3–15 yr**

**Site** — **knee** (most common) · elbow, ankle, shoulder, spine

### Prevention, factor replacement & inheritance

| Cause | Inheritance | Factor |
|---|---|---|
| **Haemophilia A** | X-linked recessive | ↓ **VIII** |
| **Haemophilia B (Christmas)** | X-linked recessive | ↓ **IX** |
| von Willebrand | **Autosomal dominant** | Abnormal VIII + platelet dysfunction · mucosal bleeding, rare joint bleeds |

**X-linked recessive bleeding disorders** — ✓ **both factor VIII and IX deficiency**\
✗ Wrong options: factor XI (AR) · vWD (AD)

**Pathoanatomy** — repeated minor trauma → haemarthrosis → **synovitis (hypertrophic, hyperplastic)** → enzymatic cartilage destruction → deformity

**Associations** — iliacus haematoma → **femoral palsy (L4 paraesthesia)** · LLD (epiphyseal overgrowth) · osteopenic fractures (normal healing) · **HIV up to 90%** (older cohorts)

## 3 Presentation

| Haemarthrosis | Features |
|---|---|
| **Acute** | Painful, **tense** effusion |
| **Subacute** | After **2 prior bleeds** |
| **Chronic** | **Contractures, arthritis** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Arnold–Hilgartner staging

| Stage | Radiographic finding |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Soft-tissue swelling** |
| **2** | **Osteoporosis** (± epiphyseal overgrowth) |
| **3** | **Subchondral cysts**, joint grossly intact |
| **4** | **Cartilage loss — joint narrowing** |
| **5** | **Severe arthritis** (end-stage, fibrous ankylosis) |

![Arnold–Hilgartner stage 5](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9051-stage-5-of-the-arnold-hilgartner-classification.jpg)

### Key radiology findings

**Knee** — **squared patella + condyles (Jordan's sign)** · ballooned distal femur · **widened intercondylar notch** · narrowing · long thin patella

**MRI** — early degenerative change, haemosiderin · **US** — follow muscle haematomas

### Labs

**Factor VIII inhibitor (IgG) screen** — ✓ **before surgery** (negates factor therapy) · found in **5–25%** · relative contraindication to surgery

**Exclude septic arthritis** — exam + aspiration

## 5 Management

| Indication | Factor VIII target |
|---|---|
| Vigorous PT | **20%** |
| Acute haematoma | **30%** |
| **Acute haemarthrosis, soft-tissue surgery, synovectomy** | **40–50%** |
| **Skeletal surgery, TJA, arthrodesis** | **100% wk 1 → >50% wk 2** |

**Before TKA (X-linked bleeding disorder)** — ✓ **factor VIII replacement**\
✗ Wrong options: protein C/S · vit K · vWF

**Desmopressin** — mild–moderate haemophilia A

**Non-operative** — compressive dressing, analgesia, short immobilisation → rehab, steroids, splints, PT

**Synovectomy** — recurrent haemarthroses despite medical management · ↓ recurrence, pain, swelling

**Synoviorthesis** — intra-articular radioactive **colloidal P-32 chromic phosphate**

**Arthroplasty** — end-stage · **arthrodesis** — ankle

## How it's asked

**"Main reason for ↓ haemophilic arthropathy over 10–20 yr?"** → **home factor treatment**\
✗ Wrong options: PT, steroid injections, debridement, splints

**"X-linked clotting disorder before TKR — replace?"** → **factor VIII**

**"Bleeding disorders that are X-linked recessive?"** → **factor VIII and IX deficiency**

**"Arnold–Hilgartner stage with subchondral cysts, joint intact?"** → **stage 3**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hyperparathyroidism

*Basic Science · 45 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **0.1%** · **women** > men · with malignancy = most cases of **hypercalcaemia**

**Cause (primary)** — **single adenoma 80–90%** · hyperplasia 10%

### Pathophysiology

**PTH acts indirectly** — binds **osteoblast** receptor → RANKL + M-CSF → osteoclasts → excess resorption · **cortical > cancellous**

**PTH effects (OB)** — ↑ renal tubular Ca reabsorption · ↑ osteoclast activity · ↑ active vitamin D → ↑ gut Ca

### Classification

| Type | Cause | Ca | Phos | PTH |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Primary** | Adenoma / hyperplasia | **↑** | **↓** | **↑** |
| **Secondary** | Compensation for **↓ Ca / ↑ phosphate** — **CKD** (renal osteodystrophy), **malabsorption (coeliac)**, vit D deficiency | **↓ / N** | ↑ (renal) / ↓ (malabsorption) | **↑** |
| **Tertiary** | Autonomous glands after long secondary HPT / renal transplant | **↑** | **↑** | **↑** |
| Malignancy (PTHrP) | Ectopic | ↑ | ↓ | **↓** |

### Genes & lab markers

**Serum** — ↑ ALP · normal anion-gap metabolic acidosis (↓ HCO₃ reclamation)

**Urine (primary)** — **hypercalciuria (stones)** · ↑ cAMP

**ECG** — **short QT**

## 3 Presentation

**"Stones, bones, groans"** — renal stones · bone pain · constipation · weakness · often asymptomatic · rare 2° HTN · **fragility fracture** with abnormal labs

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Finding | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Subperiosteal resorption** | **Radial side of middle phalanges**, phalangeal tufts, distal clavicle, proximal tibia/humerus/femur |
| **"Salt and pepper" skull** | Calvarial trabecular resorption |
| **Rugger-jersey spine** | Sclerotic endplate bands (excess unmineralised osteoid) — **distinctive for HPT (TO)** |
| **Brown tumours** | Lytic, expansile → pathological fracture · **osteitis fibrosa cystica** (jaw) |
| Chondrocalcinosis | Menisci, articular cartilage |
| Looser zones | Renal osteodystrophy (osteomalacia) |

![Subperiosteal resorption of the phalanges](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9089-subperiosteal-resorption.jpg)

### Brown tumour histology

**Features** — **bland spindled fibrous stroma** + **small multinucleated giant cells** · **haemorrhage + haemosiderin** · variable lytic/dense multiple lesions

**vs GCT** — GCT: larger giant cells, round stromal nuclei identical to giant-cell nuclei

**vs myeloma/metastases** — serum markers (Ca, PTH) distinguish

## 5 Management

**Acute hypercalcaemia** — **IV fluids + loop diuretics**

**Symptomatic** — **parathyroidectomy** → watch **postop hypocalcaemia** (numbness, tingling, cramps) → **IV calcium gluconate**

## 6 Complications

**Peptic ulcer** (↑ gastrin) · **pancreatitis** (↑ lipase) · CNS (anxiety, confusion, coma) · **osteoporosis/fragility fractures**

## X vs Y

| Lab panel | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| ↑ PTH, ↑ ALP, ↑ Ca, ↓ PO₄, normal renal function | **Primary HPT** |
| ↑ PTH, ↑ ALP, ↓ Ca, ↑ PO₄ | Secondary (renal) |
| ↑ PTH, ↑ Ca, ↑ PO₄ | Tertiary |
| Normal PTH, **↓ ALP**, ↑ Ca, ↑ PO₄, ↑ urinary phosphoethanolamine | Hypophosphatasia |
| ↓ PTH, normal ALP, ↓ Ca, ↑ PO₄ | Hypoparathyroidism |

## How it's asked

**"Rugger-jersey spine X-ray characteristic of (TO)?"** → **hyperparathyroidism**

**"Dialysis patient, Looser zone, rugger jersey, salt-and-pepper skull — labs EXCEPT?"** → **hypophosphataemia** (phosphate ↑ in renal osteodystrophy)

**"25-yr-old, brown tumours, normal GFR — labs?"** → **↑ PTH, ↑ ALP, ↑ Ca, ↓ PO₄ (primary)**

**"Coeliac sprue, brown tumour fracture — expected?"** → **↑ PTH**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ Ca, ↑ 1,25-D, ↑ PO₄, ↓ ALP

**"66-yr-old, multiple variable tibial lesions, spindle stroma + small giant cells + haemosiderin?"** → **hyperparathyroidism (brown tumours)**\
✗ Wrong options: myeloma, mets, GCT

**"Fragility fracture, ↑ Ca, ↑ ALP, ↓ PO₄, ↑ PTH — aetiology?"** → **PTH adenoma**\
✗ Wrong options: age-related osteoporosis (normal labs)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hypoparathyroidism

*Basic Science · 46 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Pathophysiology

**Cause** — ↓ PTH from chief cells · **thyroidectomy** (incl. parathyroids) = **most common**

**Effects of ↓ PTH** — ↓ renal phosphate excretion → **↑ serum phosphate** · ↓ 1α-hydroxylation → **↓ 1,25-(OH)₂D** → **↓ calcium**

### Genes & lab markers

| ↓ | ↑ | Normal |
|---|---|---|
| **PTH, calcium**, 1,25-D, urinary Ca | **Serum phosphate** | **ALP** |

**Alkalosis** — ↑ albumin binding of ionised Ca → hypocalcaemia

**ECG** — **prolonged QT**

### PTH & vitamin D lab patterns

| Condition | Ca | PO₄ | PTH | Cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hyperparathyroidism | ↑ | ↓ | ↑ | Adenoma |
| **Hypoparathyroidism** | **↓** | **↑** | **↓** | **Thyroidectomy** |
| Ectopic PTH(rP) | ↑ | ↓ | ↓ | Malignancy |
| Vitamin D malabsorption | ↓ | ↓ | ↑ | Coeliac, GI disease |
| ↓ Vit D + no renal PO₄ excretion | ↓ | ↑ | ↑ | **Renal failure**, pseudohypoparathyroidism |

## 3 Presentation

**Hypocalcaemia** — neuromuscular irritability: **Chvostek's sign**, **tetany**, **seizures** · **cataracts** · nail fungal infections · hair loss · vitiligo, blotchy skin

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Skull X-ray** — **basal ganglia calcification**

![Basal ganglia calcification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9028-basal-ganglia-calcification.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Calcium + vitamin D supplementation** · monitor labs · **no hormone replacement** available (Orthobullets)

### Renal disease & PTH

**Chronic renal disease** — ✓ **↑ PTH production** (Ca wasted, PO₄ retained, ↓ 1,25-D)\
✗ Wrong options: TSH unaffected

**Renal osteodystrophy** — high-turnover (↑ PTH, gland hyperplasia → osteitis fibrosa cystica, persists after correction) vs **low-turnover (more common on dialysis)** · metaphyseal enlargement, frontal bossing, bowing, genu varum

## How it's asked

**"Chronic renal disease — endocrine abnormality?"** → **↑ PTH**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ PTH · TSH change

**"Post-thyroidectomy tetany, ↑ PO₄, ↓ Ca, ↓ PTH?"** → **hypoparathyroidism**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hypophosphatasia

*Basic Science · 47 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Severe (perinatal/infantile)** — 1 : 100,000–300,000 · **mild (adult)** — 1 : 500–3,000 · diagnostic delay common

### Genes & lab markers

**Gene** — ✓ **loss-of-function TNSALP** (tissue-nonspecific alkaline phosphatase) in osteoblasts

**Inheritance** — **severe forms autosomal recessive** · mild/adult often autosomal dominant

**Pathophysiology** — ↓ ALP → extracellular **↑ inorganic pyrophosphate (PPi)**, pyridoxal-5'-phosphate, **phosphoethanolamine** · **PPi inhibits hydroxyapatite** formation → hypertrophic-zone osteoid fails to mineralise → no zone of provisional calcification

| Lab | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Serum ALP** | ✓ **↓ (low)** |
| **Urine phosphoethanolamine** | ✓ **↑ — diagnostic** |
| Urine PPi | ↑ |
| Ca, PO₄ | Normal or ↑ (hypercalcaemia) |
| PTH | Normal |

**Low ALP in metabolic disease** — ✓ **hypophosphatasia**\
✗ Wrong options: FHH, X-linked hypophosphataemia, secondary/tertiary HPT (all ↑ ALP)

## 3 Presentation

**Rickets-like** — genu varum, short stature, ↑ fractures

| Form | Features |
|---|---|
| **Perinatal** | Hypomineralisation, long-bone (pretibial) bowing, osteochondral spurs, caput membranaceum, **respiratory failure / lung hypoplasia** |
| **Infantile** | **Craniosynostosis**, rachitic ribs, flared metaphyses |
| **Childhood** | **Premature loss of deciduous teeth**, short stature, bone pain, fractures |
| **Adult** | **Femoral pseudofractures**, **metatarsal stress fractures**, chondrocalcinosis, poor healing, osteopenia |

![Abnormal dentition in hypophosphatasia](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9083-teeth.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — "deossification" adjacent to physis (tongues of lucency) · **physeal widening** · flared metaphyses

![Abnormal bone formation near the physis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9083-abnormal-bone-formation-in-hypophosphatasia.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Therapy** — summary lists **enzyme replacement**, anti-inflammatories, multidisciplinary care · treatment section: "no approved therapies, phosphate under investigation" (Orthobullets) — read the options

### Hypophosphataemic rickets — contrast

**Child with marked genu varum + wide physes** — ✓ **first get serum phosphate, calcium, ALP**\
✗ Wrong options: osteotomy, orthosis, scanogram, reassurance

**X-linked hypophosphataemic (vit D-resistant) rickets** — **X-linked dominant** · presents 18–24 mo · renal **phosphate wasting** · **↓ PO₄, ↑ ALP**, normal Ca, normal vit D · short (<10th centile)

**Treatment** — **phosphate + calcitriol** together · calcitriol alone → ✓ **no effect on alignment or height** · phosphate alone → ↓ Ca → 2° HPT → more phosphaturia

## X vs Y

| | Hypophosphatasia | Hypophosphataemic rickets |
|---|---|---|
| ALP | **↓** | **↑** |
| PO₄ | N / ↑ | **↓** |
| Urine | **Phosphoethanolamine** | Phosphate wasting |
| Inheritance | AR (severe) | X-linked dominant |

## How it's asked

**"Hypophosphatasia lab finding?"** → **↓ serum ALP**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ urinary phosphoethanolamine · ↓ urinary PPi

**"Metabolic disorder with ↓ ALP?"** → **hypophosphatasia**

**"2-yr-old, marked genu varum, wide physes — first step?"** → **serum phosphorus, calcium, ALP**

**"Hypophosphataemic rickets treated with calcitriol only?"** → **no effect on alignment or height**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Iliacus

*Basic Science · 48 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Iliacus |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | Upper ⅔ iliac fossa, inner lip of iliac crest, lateral sacrum, ventral SI + lower iliolumbar ligaments |
| **Insertion** | **Lesser trochanter** (with psoas → iliopsoas) |
| **Action** | Flexes thigh on trunk (and trunk on thigh) |
| **Innervation** | **Muscular branch of femoral nerve (L1–L3)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Iliolumbar (iliac branch) from internal iliac |

![Iliacus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10054-iliacus.jpg)

### Cross-sectional anatomy at the femoral heads

| Structure | Position relative to iliacus |
|---|---|
| **Psoas tendon** | Immediately **medial** |
| Rectus femoris | Just anterior + slightly lateral |
| Sartorius | Superficial + anterior |
| Tensor fascia lata | Lateral + superficial |
| Obturator externus | Medial, deep to pectineus |

**Iliacus haematoma (haemophilia, anticoagulation)** — compresses **femoral nerve** (see Femoral nerve, Hemophilia chapters)

## How it's asked

**"Pelvic cross-section at femoral heads — arrowed muscle with psoas tendon just medial?"** → **iliacus**\
✗ Wrong options: rectus femoris, sartorius, obturator externus, TFL

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Immunology

*Basic Science · 49 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Innate & adaptive immunity

| | **Innate** | **Acquired (adaptive)** |
|---|---|---|
| Specificity | Non-specific (structures common to microbes) | Pathogen/antigen-specific |
| Memory | No | Yes |
| Components | Skin barrier, inflammation, **complement** | Antigen processing + presentation |
| Cells | **NK cells, mast cells, eosinophils, basophils, macrophages, neutrophils, dendritic cells** | **CD8+ T cells**, T helpers, γδ T cells, B cells, plasma cells |

**First step of acquired response** — ✓ **antigen processing + presentation to T cells** by **APCs (B cells, dendritic cells)** → peptides on **MHC** → TCR recognises MHC/antigen\
✗ Wrong options: T cells phagocytose antigen · disulfide cleavage occurs in APC

**MoM bearing reactivity** — innate (cell-mediated) per OB · metal implants → **type IV** (Orthobullets)

### Hypersensitivity

| Type | Mediator | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **I — immediate/anaphylactic** | **IgE** → mast cells, basophils | Allergy, **urticaria** (rapid hives) |
| **II — cytotoxic** | IgG, IgM | Transfusion reactions |
| **III — immune complex** | IgG, IgM complexes deposit | — |
| **IV — delayed (cell-mediated)** | **T cells**, monocytes, macrophages · 2–3 days (7–10 for dermatitis) | **TB skin test** · **metal implants (plate + screws)** · **topical antibiotic (bacitracin) contact dermatitis** |

**Bacitracin contact dermatitis** — **5–10%** · erythema, oedema, **blistering**, no pus/fluctuance → stop agent ± steroid cream

### Immunoglobulins

| Ig | Key fact |
|---|---|
| **IgG** | **Most abundant** |
| **IgM** | ✓ **First to appear** after antigen exposure |
| **IgA** | **Secretions** (gut, bronchial mucus, saliva, tears) |
| **IgE** | Parasites, allergy |
| IgD | Role unknown |

### Benign ethnic neutropenia

**Most common neutropenia worldwide** — **25–50% of people of African descent**, some Middle Eastern groups · lifelong low ANC **without ↑ infection risk**

**ANC <1.5 × 10⁹/L** — 4.5% African-Americans · 0.79% white · 0.38% Mexican-Americans

**More common in** — **males**, **athletes**, children <5 · hypothesis: malaria protection

### HLA associations & HIV arthropathy (TO)

**Coeliac disease** — ✓ **HLA-DQ2** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: DR4, B5, B47

| HIV-associated arthritis (TO) | |
|---|---|
| **Most common form** | **Asymmetric oligoarthritis** (knees, ankles, male) (✗ Wrong options: "symmetrical polyarthritis most common") |
| Symmetrical polyarthritis | Mimics RA (ulnar deviation), acute onset, **non-erosive** |
| Course | Peak 1–6 wk, short-lived · some chronic destructive |
| **Mucocutaneous / enthesopathy** | **Rare** |
| X-ray | Usually non-erosive · may mimic RA · **new bone formation** (unusual in RA) |
| Other | Jaccoud arthropathy occasionally |

## How it's asked

**"First step of TCR–MHC acquired response?"** → **antigen processing + presentation to T cells**

**"First antibody class after antigen exposure?"** → **IgM** (IgG — most abundant)

**"Type IV hypersensitivity seen with?"** → **metal plate + screw placement**\
✗ Wrong options: transfusions · red man syndrome

**"Healthy patient, baseline WBC 1.5 for years?"** → **more likely African than European descent**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ infection risk · female · non-athlete

**"NOT innate immune cells?"** → **CD8+ T lymphocytes**

**"Finger laceration + bacitracin → erythema, blistering, no pus at 1 wk?"** → **allergic contact dermatitis**\
✗ Wrong options: cellulitis · IgE urticaria

**"HIV arthropathy — all true EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **"symmetrical polyarthritis is the most common form"**

**"HLA in coeliac disease (TO)?"** → **HLA-DQ2**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Informed Consent & Time Out

*Basic Science · 50 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Elective consent

**Optimal** — ✓ **by the surgeon, in the office, a few days (within 7 days) before** surgery\
✗ Wrong options: nurse/surgeon in pre-op holding · risk manager · scheduler

**Disclose** — who will operate · risks, benefits, indications, alternatives · surgeon's **HIV/HBV/HCV status** for exposure-prone procedures (at scheduling) · **origin of implants** (bovine — Hindu · porcine — Jewish, Muslim)

**Adolescents** — AAP: **>14 yr** who **dissent** treated as having capacity, even though parental legal consent needed

### Emergency consent

**Life-threatening, no legal consent available** — proceed; **confirm + document necessity with a colleague**

**Non-life-threatening** — consent required first (language barrier, child)

**Next of kin order** — **spouse → children → parents → siblings → grandchildren**

### Research & privacy

**HIPAA** — no patient consent needed to share PHI with **other treating providers**

**IRB** — mandates clinical-trial consent process · levels: exempt (still needs chair certification), expedited, full board

**No IRB review needed** — ✓ **internal quality-improvement studies** (OR efficiency, preop delays) even with identifiers · becomes research (IRB) if published/presented nationally or applied beyond the institution

**IRB required** — any study using PHI (**medical record number is PHI**) · **vulnerable populations**: prisoners, pregnant women, fetuses, neonates, cognitively impaired, **children**, students, employees

### Surgical safety checklist

**WHO checklist (2009)** — ✓ ↓ **surgical mortality**, ↓ in-hospital complications, ↑ adherence in OR crises (haemorrhage, arrest)

![WHO surgical safety checklist](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422975-surgical-safety-checklist.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"Optimal setting for elective written consent?"** → **surgeon in office within 7 days prior**

**"Which studies need no IRB review?"** → **internal QI studies (2 and 4)**\
✗ Wrong options: MRN retrospective study · children with CP

**"Next of kin first in line?"** → **spouse**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Inheritance Patterns of Orthopaedic Syndromes

*Basic Science · 51 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Mendelian patterns & pedigrees

**Mendelian (monogenic)** — AD · AR · X-linked dominant · X-linked recessive · ~1% of humans

**Rule of thumb** — ✓ **AD → structural protein defects** (e.g. FGFR3 in achondroplasia) · **AR → enzymatic/biochemical defects**

**Pedigree key** — circle = female · square = male · filled = affected · horizontal line = mating · vertical = offspring

| Pattern | Pedigree clue |
|---|---|
| **AD** | Every generation, both sexes, ~50% of offspring (no skipping if complete penetrance) |
| **AR** | Both sexes equally, **skips generations**, affected children of unaffected (carrier) parents, ¼ of offspring |
| **X-linked recessive** | Males; affected mother → **all sons affected** |
| X-linked dominant | Affected father → all daughters |

![Autosomal dominant pedigree](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9024-autosomaldominant.jpg)

| Autosomal dominant | Autosomal recessive | X-linked recessive | X-linked dominant |
|---|---|---|---|
| Syndactyly, polydactyly | **Diastrophic dysplasia** | **Duchenne, Becker** MD | **Hypophosphataemic rickets** (PHEX → ↑ FGF23) |
| **Marfan** (fibrillin) | Friedreich ataxia | **Hunter** syndrome | **Léri-Weill** (bilateral Madelung) |
| **Cleidocranial dysplasia** (RUNX2) | **Gaucher** (β-glucocerebrosidase) | **Haemophilia A, B** | — |
| **MHE** (EXT1 ~10% sarcoma, EXT2/3 ~1%) | SMA | **SED tarda** | — |
| **Achondroplasia** | **Sickle cell** | **Lesch-Nyhan** | — |
| MED · Schmid + Jansen chondrodysplasia · **Kniest** | **Hypophosphatasia** | — | — |
| **Malignant hyperthermia** | **Osteopetrosis — infantile malignant** | — | — |
| Ehlers-Danlos · **OI I–IV** | — | — | — |
| **Osteopetrosis tarda** (Albers-Schönberg) · **osteopoikilosis** · NF1 | — | — | — |

**Multiple patterns** — CMT (AD, AR, X) · osteopetrosis (AD, AR) · OI (AD, AR) · NF (AD, AR) · SED (AD, X) · hypophosphataemic rickets (XD commonest, AD, AR)

**No inheritance** — fibular hemimelia · **McCune-Albright / fibrous dysplasia** (GNAS — sporadic **mosaicism**, non-Mendelian)

### Non-Mendelian inheritance (imprinting, anticipation, mosaicism)

| Phenomenon | Definition | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **Genomic imprinting** | Expression depends on **parent of origin** | **Prader-Willi** (paternal defect — hypotonia, obesity, hypogonadism; chr 15) · **Angelman** (maternal — epilepsy, tremor, smiling) |
| **Anticipation** | **Earlier onset + more severe** in each generation · **trinucleotide repeats** | **Huntington's** (CAG, chr 4, AD), myotonic dystrophy, Friedreich ataxia, fragile X |
| **Mosaicism** | Post-zygotic mutation → mixed cell lines | Fibrous dysplasia, McCune-Albright |
| Variable penetrance/expressivity | Same gene, varying degree | — |
| Genetic drift | Chance change in genotype frequency | — |
| Trisomy | Extra chromosome | Down (21) |

### Lesch-Nyhan syndrome

**Defect** — **X-linked recessive** · deficient **HGPRT** (purine metabolism) → ✓ **↑ serum uric acid**

**Features** — cognitive impairment · **choreoathetosis** · **self-mutilation** (biting fingertips), aggression · fractures, hip dysplasia, progressive scoliosis · surgery outcomes ≈ CP · ↑ HO, femur fixation failure

| Lab | Condition |
|---|---|
| **↑ Serum uric acid** | **Lesch-Nyhan** |
| ↑ Urine keratan sulfate | Morquio |
| ↑ Ca, ↓ PO₄ | Hyperparathyroidism |
| ↑ Glucocerebrosides | Gaucher |
| Chromosome 15 deletion | Prader-Willi |

## How it's asked

**"Prader-Willi / Angelman — parent-of-origin expression?"** → **genomic imprinting**

**"Symptoms earlier each generation?"** → **anticipation**

**"Genetic defect NOT following Mendelian inheritance?"** → **Gs-α defect (McCune-Albright)**\
✗ Wrong options: FGFR3, sulfate transporter, fibrillin, glucocerebrosidase

**"Chorea at 46, father at 55?"** → **autosomal dominant with anticipation (Huntington's)**

**"Example of anticipation?"** → **more severe and earlier in subsequent generations**

**"AR pedigree — which condition?"** → **diastrophic dysplasia**\
✗ Wrong options: DMD, Hunter, haemophilia B, SED tarda (XR)

**"X-linked recessive disorder?"** → **haemophilia A**\
✗ Wrong options: Gaucher, diastrophic (AR) · Prader-Willi (imprinting) · hypophosphataemic rickets (XD)

**"AD genes typically cause which defect?"** → **structural**

**"Pedigree for MHE?"** → **autosomal dominant**

**"Spotted juxta-articular sclerotic lesions in pregnant woman — inheritance?"** → **AD (osteopoikilosis)**

**"Radiograph suggesting X-linked dominant disease?"** → **hypophosphataemic rickets**

**"Lesch-Nyhan — lab finding?"** → **↑ serum uric acid**

**"Self-mutilating, cognitively impaired, X-linked recessive boy?"** → **Lesch-Nyhan**\
✗ Wrong options: Léri-Weill (XD) · Apert (AD)

**"Bit off his fingertip, purine metabolism disorder?"** → **Lesch-Nyhan**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Intramembranous Bone Formation

*Basic Science · 52 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Definition

**Bone formed without a cartilage model** — undifferentiated mesenchymal cells aggregate → osteoblast differentiation → organic matrix deposition

**Also called** — contact healing, Haversian remodelling (in fracture healing)

### Sites of intramembranous ossification

| ✓ Intramembranous **(TO)** | Not endochondral **(TO)** |
|---|---|
| **Embryonic flat bones** — skull, maxilla, mandible, pelvis, **clavicle**, subperiosteal surface of long bones | Longitudinal physeal growth |
| **Distraction osteogenesis** | Embryonic long bones |
| **Rigid fixation (compression plate)** | Non-rigid (secondary) fracture healing |
| Perichondrial bone formation | Epiphyseal secondary centres |
| **Blastema bone** (children's amputations) | **Fracture callus** |
| One component of IM-nail healing | Degenerating OA cartilage, calcifying cartilage tumours, **DBM-induced bone** |

### Regulation

**Signalling** — canonical **Wnt** + Hedgehog · **β-catenin** → osteoblasts

**Transcription factors** — **CBFA1 / RUNX2** · **osterix (OSX)**

### Sclerostin

| Sclerostin | Fact |
|---|---|
| Gene | **SOST** |
| Source | **Osteocytes** |
| Action | ✓ **Inhibits Wnt** (✗ Wrong options: activates) → ↓ osteoblasts |
| Overexpression | **↓ bone mass** |
| Loss | **Sclerosteosis, van Buchem disease** (high bone mass) |

## 6 Complications

### Genes & lab markers

**Cleidocranial dysplasia** — **defective intramembranous ossification** · **CBFA1 (RUNX2)** on **chromosome 6** · AD · absent clavicles, skull defects

## How it's asked

**"Sclerostin — all true EXCEPT?"** → **"activates Wnt pathway"**\
✗ Wrong options: SOST product · osteocyte-derived · sclerosteosis/van Buchem

**"Intramembranous formation does NOT occur in (TO)?"** → **callus formation during fracture healing**\
✗ Wrong options: compression plating, distraction osteogenesis, flat bones

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Juvenile Idiopathic Arthritis

*Basic Science · 53 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**JIA** — chronic autoimmune arthritis **>6 weeks** in a child **<16 yr** · **diagnosis of exclusion** (rule out infection, malignancy, rheumatic fever, IBD, SLE, Lyme)

**Sex** — **female > male**

**Joints** — **knee** > hand/wrist > ankle > hip > C-spine

### Genes & lab markers

| HLA | Subtype |
|---|---|
| **DR4** | Polyarticular |
| DR8, DR5, DR2.1 | Pauciarticular |

**RF** — ✓ **seronegative common** (RF+ <15% · 97% normal RF) · RF+ → chronic, destructive, progresses to adult RA

**ANA** — "positive is diagnostic" (Orthobullets) · "so often false-positive that alone it has no diagnostic value" (OB) · **ANA+ → ↑ uveitis risk**

**HLA-B27** — positive in only **~8%**

**ESR, CRP** — frequently elevated (OB) · Orthobullets: basic serology often normal, not diagnostic · anaemia, ↓ albumin, leukocytosis

**Serology does NOT confirm JIA** — "definitive diagnosis by serology" (false)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **morning stiffness**, limp that **improves during the day**, joint swelling, fever, rash, visual change

**Diagnostic features (one required)** — rash · RF · **iridocyclitis** · C-spine involvement · pericarditis · tenosynovitis · intermittent fever · morning stiffness

### Classification

| Type | % | Joints | Features |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Pauciarticular (oligoarticular)** | **50% — most common** | **<5**, large (knee, ankle), asymmetric | **Girls 1–4 yr (peak 2–4)** · M:F 1:4 · **affected limb LONGER** · **highest uveitis/blindness risk (TO)** · best prognosis |
| Polyarticular | 30% | **>5**, hand/wrist | Wrist ulnar deviation + flexion, MCP stiff in extension, radially deviated fingers · 60% remission |
| **Systemic (Still's)** | 20% | Multiple | **Fever, rash, splenomegaly**, anaemia, ↑ WBC, ESR, CRP, platelets · M = F · 5–10 yr · **lowest uveitis risk** · worst prognosis |

**Onset** — early (pre-teen) vs late (teens)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Uveitis & ophthalmology screening

**Iridocyclitis (anterior uveitis)** — **often asymptomatic/indolent** · rapid irreversible vision loss if missed · up to 30% lose vision

**Risk by type (TO)** — ✓ **pauciarticular > polyarticular > systemic** · pauciarticular + **ANA+** highest (~75%, OB2240 · ~20% in OB1331 — read the options)

**Screening** — ✓ **urgent slit-lamp exam** · **every 4 months if ANA+**, twice yearly if ANA− · pupil asymmetry → immediate

| Eye finding | Condition |
|---|---|
| **Anterior uveitis** | **JIA (pauci, ANA+)** — urgent, preventable |
| Superior lens dislocation | Marfan |
| Inferior lens dislocation | Homocystinuria |
| Corneal clouding | Hurler (not preventable), Hunter (mild) |
| Ptosis | Pompe |

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — often **normal** early · late juxta-articular osteopenia, destruction

**C-spine** — kyphosis, facet ankylosis, **atlantoaxial subluxation** → ✓ **flexion–extension C-spine films before intubation/surgery**

## 5 Management

**First line** — **DMARDs** (etanercept — TNF inhibitor · rituximab — anti-CD20 · azathioprine — purine synthesis inhibitor · IA methotrexate/etanercept) · **intra-articular steroids** · NSAIDs/high-dose aspirin (less now) · gold occasionally · **frequent ophthalmology exams**

**Painless elbow loss of motion + multiple joint pains (13-yr-old pitcher)** — ✓ **DMARDs** (JIA, not OCD — ✗ Wrong options: microfracture, drilling, immobilisation)

**Operative** — synovectomy (failed medical) · **epiphysiodesis** for LLD (affected leg longer) · osteotomy deferred to maturity · arthrodesis/arthroplasty for severe disease

## 6 Complications

**Prognosis** — 50% resolve · 25% mild disability · 25% crippling arthritis or blindness

### Lead toxicity (related)

**Lead** — inhibits ✓ **PTHrP** → growth inhibition · ↓ BMD (animal studies)

## X vs Y

| | Pauciarticular JIA | Lyme arthritis |
|---|---|---|
| Joints | Knee, oligo | Knee, oligo |
| Rash | No | Erythema migrans |
| Antibiotic response | No | Yes |
| Association | **Uveitis** | Bilateral facial palsy, carditis |

## How it's asked

**"JRA type with highest iritis/blindness risk (TO)?"** → **pauciarticular**\
✗ Wrong options: systemic (lowest)

**"4-yr-old girl, knee effusion, limp better later in day, ANA+ low titre — next workup?"** → **ophthalmology evaluation**\
✗ Wrong options: skeletal survey, MRI, bone scan, factor levels

**"Highest risk of anterior uveitis needing ophthalmology?"** → **pauciarticular JRA with +ANA**\
✗ Wrong options: Marfan (lens) · Hunter (cornea)

**"Urgent ophthalmology to prevent irreversible complication?"** → **6-yr-old girl, 2 mo knee swelling, fever, ↑ ESR (JIA)**

**"5-yr-old, oligoarthritis better at end of day, antibiotics failed, RF− — association?"** → **uveitis**\
✗ Wrong options: facial palsy (Lyme)

**"FALSE about JIA?"** → **"definitive diagnosis by serology"**

**"4-yr-old with presumed JIA — likely serum result?"** → **normal RF**\
✗ Wrong options: normal ESR/CRP · ASLO · HLA-B27

**"Peak onset of pauciarticular JRA?"** → **2–4 years**

**"14-yr-old with JIA before rectal surgery — required work-up?"** → **cervical (flexion–extension) radiographs**

**"Painless elbow stiffness + polyarthralgia in young pitcher — first line?"** → **DMARDs**

**"Lead toxicity inhibits?"** → **PTHrP**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Lead Toxicity

*Basic Science · 54 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### PTHrP inhibition

**Mechanism** — lead inhibits ✓ **parathyroid hormone-related peptide (PTHrP)** signalling → **growth inhibition** (PTHrP normally delays chondrocyte hypertrophy in the physis)

**Bone effect** — **↓ bone mineral density** (animal studies)

**Traps** — ✗ Wrong options: TSH · growth hormone · IGF · GnRH

## How it's asked

**"Lead toxicity affects growth by inhibiting?"** → **PTHrP**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Legal Considerations in Orthopaedic Practice

*Basic Science · 55 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Medical negligence — four elements

| Element | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Duty** | Obligation to meet the professional standard of care (same specialty) |
| **Breach of duty** | ✓ **Failure to exercise the diligence and care a reasonable, prudent person would under similar conditions** — deviation from standard of care |
| **Causation** | Breach **directly caused** the injury |
| **Damages** | Money awarded for injury |

**Standard of care (legal)** — ✓ **reasonable treatment showing knowledge, skill, diligence and care** (what a reasonable physician would do)\
✗ Wrong options: majority norm · minimum · average · "what you'd want for family"

**Corrected error, good outcome, lawsuit** — ✓ **defend: error occurred but was corrected, little/no damages**\
✗ Wrong options: settle · claim no error · contact patient directly

### Litigation statistics

**Trend** — ortho suits ↑ **13%** 2003–2008 (ageing population) · ortho **7th** most sued specialty · **~33%** of claims pay out · defence ~$47,000

**Causes** — "**improper performance**" 45% · procedures: joints (non-fusion), open/closed reductions · diagnosis: **OA (21%)**, joint disorder, **femur fracture**

**Subspecialty** — ✓ **arthroplasty most sued (29.9%)** > spine (22%) > trauma (17.7%) · most cases won by surgeon · highest plaintiff success: **fasciotomy (48%)**, infection (43%)

**Highest total indemnity by fracture** — ✓ **tibia** (> femur despite fewer cases)

**Wrong-site claims** — **84%** pay indemnity vs 30% of other ortho claims

### Legislation

| Law | Content |
|---|---|
| **Anti-Kickback Statute** (1987) | **Criminal** — no remuneration to induce referrals for federal programmes |
| **Stark law** (Social Security Act §1877; 1989/1993) | ✓ **No self-referral** to entities with physician financial relationship (PT, lab, radiology, DME) · fines to **$100,000** per scheme · "in-office" exceptions |
| Fee-splitting provisions | Sharing revenue for referrals |
| Certificate of Need | State control of new facilities |
| ACA (2010) | Insurance fairness/affordability (Sunshine Act passed within it) |
| **Physician Payments Sunshine Act** (2010) | ✓ **Industry payments >$10 reported to CMS** (Open Payments) |
| **HIPAA** (1996) | PHI protection · **no consent needed to share with other treating providers** (referral records, treating chiropractor) |

### IRB

**Origin** — **Nuremberg Code** (Nazi physicians) · dignity, autonomy, informed consent, fair selection, equal burden/benefit · mandates trial consent process

**Vulnerable populations** — **fetuses, pregnant women, children, prisoners**, cognitively impaired, students, employees\
✗ Wrong options: "prisoners are not"

**No IRB needed** — ✓ **internal QI study** (even with name, DOB, MRN) · IRB needed: MRN-based research, phase III device trial, CP children, RCT

## 5 Management

### Informed consent (emergencies, minors, interpreters, competency)

**Five elements (research + procedures)** — **voluntariness** · **provision of information** (risks, benefits, alternatives, no-treatment consequences, lay terms) · **competency** · **understanding** · **decision** \
✗ Wrong options: jurisdiction

**Late-stage dementia excluded from trial** — ✓ **competency**

| Situation | Action |
|---|---|
| **Unconscious, life/limb-threatening, no family** | ✓ **Confirm + document necessity with a colleague of similar expertise**, then operate |
| GCS 10, fiancé present, IIIC tibia | **Two surgeons of similar expertise** (fiancé has no legal standing) |
| **Intoxicated, compartment syndrome, refusing, no translator** | ✓ **Fasciotomy after documenting necessity** (lacks capacity; friend can't consent) |
| **Non-emergent (closed femur), patient lacks capacity** | ✓ **Delay; obtain next-of-kin consent** (daughter before mother) |
| 13-yr-old, urgent tibia fracture, parents abroad (German) | ✓ **Call parents with a German interpreter** (✗ Wrong options: cousin · child translating) |
| Limited English | ✓ **Certified live interpreter → certified telephone** (✗ Wrong options: family · minors · own limited Spanish) |
| Jehovah's Witness | ✓ **Crystalloid, starch (Hetastarch), colloid** · (✗ Wrong options: whole blood, RBC, platelets, plasma, autologous/donor-directed) · cell saver = individual choice |
| Consent says resident only retracts, attending leaves before cementing | ✓ **Ethically unsound — patient misled** ("ghost surgery" → battery) |

**Next of kin** — legal guardian → **spouse → children → parents → siblings → grandchildren**

**Implant origin** — **bovine → Hindu** · porcine → Jewish, Muslim

**Surgeon seropositive** — disclose **HIV, HBV, HCV** at scheduling of exposure-prone procedure · needlestick transmission: HIV ~0.3% · HCV ~1.8% · HBV 6–30% (30% if HBeAg+)

### Wrong-site surgery & Universal Protocol

**Joint Commission Universal Protocol (2004)** — **pre-procedure verification** (documents, labelled imaging displayed, blood/implants/equipment available) · **site marking** · **time-out**

**Site marking** — surgeon's **initials (or "YES")**, permanent marker, **with the patient**, in pre-op holding, **visible in the surgical field after draping** · (✗ Wrong options: "X" · "NO" on other limb · plantar foot for TKA) · may be delegated to resident/PA/APRN (LIP accountable)

**Correct sequence** — ✓ **read consent with patient → mark site → regional block → time-out → incise**

**JCAHO time-out** — correct **patient, side, site, procedure** · antibiotic not mandated · **all team members speak** · any member can lead · attending present in room\
✗ Wrong options: only surgeon

**Two teams (ortho + plastics) both present** — **single time-out** sufficient · otherwise repeat

**Late-arriving resident** — greet the team · no mandate to repeat time-out

**Wrong side about to be incised (resident)** — ✓ **ask surgeon to revisit safety checklist**

**Trend** — reported wrong-site surgeries ✓ **increased** since 1997/2003 protocols (↑ reporting) · 12.7/million · **ortho + podiatry 41%**

**WHO checklist (2009)** — sign-in (before induction: ID, site, consent, airway, **blood-loss risk**) · time-out (team introductions, **antibiotics within 60 min**, **imaging displayed**) · sign-out · "implants + rep present" not a component · ↓ mortality, complications, ↑ crisis adherence · **surgeon most effective member**

### Errors & disclosure

| Error | Response |
|---|---|
| Wrong-side incision noticed | ✓ **Immediately discuss with patient + family** (then risk management) · never alter record |
| Wrong knee incised in TKA | ✓ **Close wound, complete correct TKA, tell family after surgery** |
| Wrong spinal level (T7 not T6) | ✓ **Complete correct level**, document, apologise, inform after · (✗ Wrong options: abort)|
| Intraop MCL laceration | ✓ **Tell the patient the MCL was injured and plan adjusted** |
| Documentation error | ✓ **Addendum only** — altering record is illegal, no one can authorise it |

**Full disclosure + apology + responsibility + prevention plan** — ✓ **↑ patient satisfaction** (not ↑ litigation, not guaranteed prevention) · accept responsibility ≠ blame

**Second opinion** — **ethical (not legal)** duty to disclose error/standard-of-care breach to **patient** · no legal duty to testify (but subject to subpoena) · no duty to tell original surgeon · only patient can transfer care

**Impaired colleague (drinking attending)** — ✓ **express concern to the attending** → escalate to chief if needed\
✗ Wrong options: operate unsupervised

### Communication & patient safety

**Communication breakdown** — ✓ **most common cause of errors harming patients** · leading cause of **wrong-side surgery, medication errors, diagnostic delays, loss to follow-up** · 70% of sentinel events · ↑ costs + complications\
✗ Wrong options: SSI

**Communication affects** — patient satisfaction, adherence, physician satisfaction, malpractice \
✗ Wrong options: **Stark II litigation**

**Best ↓ sentinel events** — ✓ **culture where all team members feel empowered to speak** (flattened hierarchy)

**Retained sponge after shift change** — ✓ **OR-wide crew resource management training**\
✗ Wrong options: blame nurses · single policy

**CPOE** — ✓ **↓ medication errors** (incl. allergy errors)

**Physicians interrupt** — **23 seconds**

**Child lost to follow-up after casting (Spanish-speaking)** — ✓ **instructions not communicated** · discharge transitions need clear follow-up

### Malpractice process

**Summons received** — ✓ **notify liability carrier first** (assigns attorney)\
✗ Wrong options: call patient · discard notes

**Formal complaint** — **written response + all records to medical board** \
✗ Wrong options: automatic suspension · mandatory attorney

**Insurance** — ✓ **occurrence policy** (covers incidents during employment whenever filed) · claims-made needs **tail** · **nose** = prior acts if previous claims-made without tail

**Terminating care** — written notice + **30–45 day** grace period · without it = **abandonment**

### Workers' compensation & disability

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Maximum medical improvement** | Further restoration not anticipated → ✓ **claim can be settled** (declared by treating physician) |
| **Impairment** | ✓ **Loss of function from anatomic/physiologic derangement** (medical) |
| **Disability** | Limitation in meeting personal/social/occupational demands (legal) |
| Apportionment | Share of disability from each injury |
| FCE | Tests ability to function (work) |

**Choice of physician** — ✓ **state statute**

**Suspected malingering / fear** — elicit with ✓ **empathy** (lean forward, open questions, few notes)

### Team physician & athlete confidentiality

**Employed team physician** — inform **team and player** of athletically relevant issues\
✗ Wrong options: media

**Life-threatening risk (transient quadriplegia + stenosis)** — ✓ **ban from participation** · no constitutional right to play · **scholarship kept** · waivers don't protect

**Share without consent** — ✓ **treating chiropractor** (treatment)\
✗ Wrong options: mother, coach, reporter, fiancée

### Employment & billing

**Independent contractor** — ✓ **contractor determines means + methods**; employer determines result · employer pays no taxes, insurance, retirement, training

**Injection + separate osteoporosis management same day** — ✓ **procedure code + E&M with modifier 25**

### Access to care

**Paediatric access** — ✓ **type of insurance** (Medicaid vs private)

## How it's asked

**"Wrong-side incision recognised, correct ORIF done — next?"** → **immediately discuss with patient and family**

**"Wrong-knee ACL scope — prevented by?"** → **surgeon initials operative limb with patient in pre-op holding**

**"Before THA incision, entire staff must?"** → **time-out**

**"JCAHO time-out — all EXCEPT?"** → **correct preoperative antibiotic**

**"Wrong thoracic level resected — all EXCEPT?"** → **abort case and get more imaging**

**"Ortho + plastics both present — time-out?"** → **one is sufficient**

**"Resident sees wrong carpal tunnel about to be incised?"** → **ask surgeon to revisit safety checklist**

**"Wrong knee incised in TKA?"** → **close, do correct TKA, tell family afterwards**

**"Wrong-site surgeries since protocols?"** → **increased**

**"↓ mortality + ↑ crisis adherence?"** → **surgical checklist**

**"NOT in WHO checklist?"** → **correct implants/rep present**

**"NOT a way to prevent wrong-site surgery?"** → **marking an "X"**

**"NOT part of Universal Protocol?"** → **only staff surgeon speaks during time-out**

**"Correct sequence (shoulder scope)?"** → **consent read → mark → block → time-out → incise**

**"Late resident joins case?"** → **greet team, no repeat time-out**

**"Universal Protocol violation?"** → **TKA mark on plantar foot**

**"Unconscious IIIA tibia, no family?"** → **confirm + document necessity with a colleague**

**"Patient told resident only retracts, attending leaves before cementing?"** → **unsound — patient misled**

**"13-yr-old, parents in Germany?"** → **call parents with German interpreter**

**"Intoxicated, compartment syndrome, refusing?"** → **fasciotomy after documenting necessity**

**"Elderly Spanish speaker with granddaughter?"** → **certified live interpreter, then telephone**

**"GCS 10, fiancé present?"** → **surgeon + another physician of similar expertise**

**"Jehovah's Witness — acceptable?"** → **starch (Hetastarch)**

**"Dementia excludes trial enrolment — consent element?"** → **competency**

**"Head-injured, closed femur, daughter + mother present?"** → **delay until next-of-kin (daughter) consents**

**"Correct op-note antibiotic timing?"** → **illegal to alter; addendum**

**"Disclosure after intraop fracture?"** → **↑ patient satisfaction**

**"Second opinion finds error?"** → **ethically must disclose to patient**

**"Corrected malrotation, good outcome, sued?"** → **defend — error corrected, little damage**

**"Intraop MCL laceration — tell patient?"** → **MCL injured, plan adjusted**

**"CPOE effect?"** → **↓ medication errors**

**"Communication does NOT affect?"** → **Stark II litigation**

**"Communication breakdown leads to?"** → **delayed diagnoses + medication errors**

**"Most likely to ↓ sentinel events?"** → **empowered team culture**

**"Spanish-speaking child in cast 3 months?"** → **instructions not communicated**

**"Most common cause of harmful errors?"** → **communication breakdown**

**"Drunk attending naps after time-out?"** → **express concern to attending**

**"Physicians redirect patients after?"** → **23 seconds**

**"Communication failure in surgery →?"** → **↑ cost, ↑ complications**

**"Lost sponge after shift change — fix?"** → **OR-wide crew resource management**

**"7-yr-old casted DRF, discharged — true?"** → **poor communication harms care transitions**

**"Failure to exercise reasonable diligence?"** → **breach of duty**

**"Second-opinion obligations EXCEPT?"** → **legal duty to be plaintiff's expert witness**

**"Highest total indemnity fracture?"** → **tibia**

**"Legal standard of care?"** → **reasonable treatment with knowledge, skill, diligence, care**

**"New employed trauma surgeon — insurance?"** → **occurrence coverage**

**"Received summons — first step?"** → **notify liability carrier**

**"Most likely procedure in a claim?"** → **THA**

**"Infected TKA — why written response to board?"** → **formal complaint filed**

**"Workers' comp claim settled when?"** → **MMI declared by treating physician**

**"Loss of function from derangement?"** → **impairment**

**"No further restoration expected?"** → **MMI**

**"Choice of physician in workers' comp set by?"** → **state statute**

**"Elicit fear of confrontation?"** → **empathy**

**"Share spondylosis without consent with?"** → **treating chiropractor**

**"Scholarship athlete at life-threatening risk?"** → **ban from participation**

**"Transient quadriplegia + stenosis?"** → **do not return to football**

**"Pro ACL rupture — team physician informs?"** → **team and player**

**"IRB inaccurate statement?"** → **"prisoners not vulnerable"**

**"Reporting gifts >$10?"** → **Sunshine Act**

**"Send records to neurologist?"** → **no extra consent**

**"No IRB needed?"** → **internal QI study**

**"Seropositivity to disclose?"** → **HBV, HIV, HCV**

**"Co-worker refers to his own PT company?"** → **Stark law**

**"Injection + osteoporosis visit billing?"** → **procedure + E&M with modifier 25**

**"Independent contractor true?"** → **determines means and methods**

**"Limits paediatric ortho access?"** → **type of insurance**

**"Bovine grafts matter to?"** → **Hinduism**

# Level of Evidence

*Basic Science · 56 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Steps of evidence-based medicine

| Step | Action |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Formulate an answerable question** |
| 2 | **Gather** the evidence |
| **3** | ✓ **Appraise** the evidence (levels of evidence used here) |
| 4 | **Implement** |
| 5 | **Evaluate** the process |

### Assigning level by study design & follow-up

| Level | Therapeutic study (JBJS/Oxford) |
|---|---|
| **I** | **High-quality RCT** (randomised, blinded, **>80% follow-up**, narrow CI) · systematic review/meta-analysis of **homogeneous Level I RCTs** · all-or-none |
| **II** | **Lesser-quality RCT** (<80% follow-up, no blinding, improper randomisation, crossover) · **prospective cohort / prospective comparative** · SR of Level II or heterogeneous Level I |
| **III** | **Retrospective cohort** · **case-control** · retrospective comparative · SR/meta-analysis of Level III |
| **IV** | **Case series** (no or historical control) |
| **V** | **Expert opinion**, case report, personal observation, bench/animal work |

**Meta-analysis level** — = **lowest level of included studies** (OB, Orthobullets) · one RCT with 70% follow-up in an SR → **Level II** · meta-analysis of retrospective cohorts → **Level III** · TO table: **meta-analysis of cohort studies = Level 2** (TO) — read the options

**RCT with >20% loss to follow-up (TO)** — ✓ **downgraded to Level II** (attrition bias) even with flawless randomisation, concealment, ITT, blinded assessors **(TO)**

**Reporting guidelines** — **QUORUM / PRISMA** (meta-analyses of RCTs) · **MOOSE** (observational meta-analyses) · **CONSORT** (RCTs)

**Study types** — **therapeutic** (results of treatment) · **prognostic** (patient characteristic → outcome) · **diagnostic** (test efficacy) · economic/decision

**Most common study type in ortho literature** — ✓ **Level IV case series** (58–64%)

### AAOS guideline recommendation strength

| Strength | Evidence |
|---|---|
| **Strong** | ≥2 high-quality studies |
| Moderate | 1 high or 2 moderate |
| Weak | 1 moderate or ≥1 low |
| Consensus | Expert opinion — only for interventions preventing **loss of life or limb** |

### GRADE framework (TO)

**Baseline (TO)** — **RCTs start High** · **observational studies start Low** · separates **quality of evidence** (high → very low) from **strength of recommendation** (strong / weak)

| Downgrade (5) **(TO)** | Upgrade (3) **(TO)** |
|---|---|
| **Risk of bias** (no blinding, no concealment, attrition, early stopping) | **Large magnitude of effect** — RR >2 or <0.5 (+1) · RR >5 or <0.2 (+2) |
| **Inconsistency** (heterogeneity, high I²) | **Dose–response gradient** |
| **Indirectness** (PICO mismatch, surrogate outcome e.g. BMD vs fracture) | ✓ **All plausible confounders would reduce the observed effect** |
| **Imprecision** (small n, few events, wide CI) | — |
| **Publication bias** (funnel plot) | — |

**Not upgrade criteria (TO)** — explained heterogeneity · PROSPERO registration / PRISMA adherence · precise CI around small RR 1.2

## How it's asked

**"Level of meta-analysis of cohort studies (TO)?"** → **2**

**"RCT, blinded, ITT, flawless randomisation, 22% loss to follow-up (TO)?"** → **Level II — attrition >20%**

**"Level II study?"** → **prospective cohort**\
✗ Wrong options: case-control (III) · high-quality RCT (I)

**"Level III study?"** → **retrospective case-control**

**"Moseley sham-surgery RCT, >90% follow-up?"** → **therapeutic Level I**

**"SR of 15 homogeneous high-quality RCTs?"** → **Level I**

**"Meta-analysis of retrospective cohorts (QUORUM)?"** → **Level III**

**"Prospective non-randomised matched PRP cohort, >80% follow-up?"** → **Level II**

**"Prospective surgeon-preference IT fracture study, 85% follow-up, wide CI?"** → **Level II**

**"RCT, 20% crossover, unblinded surgeon assessor, 65% follow-up?"** → **Level II**

**"10 patients, new procedure, no control?"** → **case series**

**"Surgeon's 25-yr technique article?"** → **Level V**

**"SR of 6 RCTs, one with 70% follow-up?"** → **Level II**

**"Most common study type in ortho literature?"** → **Level IV case series**

**"Third step of EBM?"** → **appraise the evidence**

**"Upgrade Low → Moderate in GRADE (TO)?"** → **all plausible confounders would reduce the effect**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ligaments

*Basic Science · 57 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Function & composition

**Function** — restrict motion · stabilise joint · **proprioception** (mechanoreceptors, free nerve endings)

**Composition** — water · **type I collagen (70% dry weight)** · **elastin (more than tendon)** · lipids · proteoglycans · epiligament (some) · **fibroblasts** · low vascularity + cellularity

**Ligament vs tendon** — ligament: **less collagen**, **more PG + water**, less organised fibres, rounder fibroblasts

**Blood supply** — enters at **insertion** (unlike tendon) · uniform microvascularity · ACL/PCL: **middle geniculate artery**

### Insertion anatomy (direct, indirect, Sharpey fibres)

| | **Indirect (fibrous)** | **Direct (fibrocartilaginous)** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | **Most common** | — |
| Mechanism | Superficial fibres → **periosteum** · deep fibres → bone via **Sharpey fibres** (type I collagen) | 4 transition zones of ↑ stiffness (force dissipation) |
| Example | **MCL into proximal tibia** | **Supraspinatus**, native ACL |

| Direct zone | Content |
|---|---|
| **1 Ligament/tendon** | Aligned type I collagen, decorin |
| **2 Fibrocartilage** (unmineralised) | Types **II + III**, small I, IX, X · aggrecan, decorin |
| **3 Mineralised fibrocartilage** | Type II + significant **type X**, aggrecan |
| **4 Bone** | Type I, high mineral |

![Sharpey fibres — indirect insertion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9016-sharpey-fibers.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Viscoelasticity & mechanical behaviour

**Viscoelastic** — ✓ behaviour depends on **loading rate** (faster → **stiffer + stronger**) → control load rate when testing prosthetic ligaments

| Property | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Creep** | ↑ deformation over time under **constant load** |
| **Stress relaxation** | ↓ stress over time under **constant deformation** |
| **Hysteresis** | Unloading curve ≠ loading curve · area = energy dissipated |

**Load–elongation curve** — **toe region** (crimped fibres straighten, **non-linear**) → **linear region** (slope = stiffness / Young's modulus) → yield (elastic → plastic) → failure

**Ligament vs tendon curve** — ligament recruits fibres gradually → **longer toe region** · tendon → shorter toe, higher loads

**Anisotropic** — strong in **tension**, weak in compression · Young's modulus **< cancellous bone, > cartilage**

![Stress–strain curve — toe, linear, failure](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9016-stressstrain.jpg)

### Failure

**Mechanism** — sequential collagen fibre rupture · no plastic deformation

**Site** — **adults: midsubstance** · **children: bony avulsion** at junction of **mineralised + unmineralised fibrocartilage**

| Grade | Injury |
|---|---|
| I | Mild sprain |
| II | Moderate / partial tear |
| III | Complete tear |

## 5 Management

### Healing & graft incorporation

| Phase | Time | Events |
|---|---|---|
| **Inflammatory** | **1–7 d** | Neutrophils, macrophages · **type III collagen** · TGF-β1, IGF, PDGF, BMP-12/13, bFGF |
| **Proliferative** | **7–21 d** | → **type I collagen** · **weakest day 5–21** |
| Remodelling | >14 d | — |
| Maturation | ≤18 months | — |

**Healed MCL at 1 year vs normal** — ✓ **↑ number of fibres** · type III proportion back to normal · **cross-links only ~45%** · ↓ fibre mass + diameter · scar → ↓ ultimate strength, adhesions

**Impair healing** — **intra-articular** (ACL < MCL) · age · **immobilisation** · smoking · **NSAIDs** · DM · alcohol · ↓ bFGF, NGF, IGF-1 · ↓ procollagen I, COMP, tenascin-C, tenomodulin, scleraxis

**Improve (experimental)** — extra-articular · CD44 knockout · **IL-10** · IL-1 receptor antagonist · macrophage depletion · MSCs (tunnel healing) · PDGF-BB (↓ adhesions, no strength gain) · G-CSF (tunnel) · BMP-2/-12 (cuff) · collagen–PRP hydrogel for primary ACL repair · **CGRP** (MCL)

**Allograft vs autograft ACL** — allograft: ✓ **prolonged inflammation**, slower incorporation, more large-diameter fibrils · tendon grafts heal to tunnel by **fibrovascular scar → Sharpey-like fibres** · intra-articular part: necrosis → host synovial repopulation → revascularisation → **ligamentisation**\
✗ Wrong options: native 4-zone insertion

## How it's asked

**"Order of direct insertion zones?"** → **ligament → fibrocartilage → mineralised fibrocartilage → bone**

**"MCL tear — structure and insertion?"** → **ligament; Sharpey fibres (indirect)**

**"Why control loading rate testing a prosthetic ligament?"** → **ligaments are viscoelastic**

**"MCL sprain — mechanical property?"** → **non-linear stress–strain on initial loading**\
✗ Wrong options: isotropic · strongest in compression

**"Grade III MCL at 1 yr vs normal — ↑?"** → **gross number of fibres**\
✗ Wrong options: type III · cross-links · diameter

**"Allograft vs autograft ACL inflammatory phase?"** → **prolonged**

# Lumbosacral Plexus

*Basic Science · 58 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Nerve at risk — iliosacral screws

**Danger** — screw anterior/superior on the **sacral ala** (above the alar slope / iliac cortical density on lateral view) → ✓ **L5 nerve root**

**L5 deficit** — ✓ **loss of great-toe extension (EHL)** · also tibialis anterior, tibialis posterior, EDL, hamstrings, gluteus medius/maximus

| Fluoroscopic view | Checks |
|---|---|
| **Lateral** | Below **iliac cortical density / alar slope** → protects L5 |
| **Outlet** | **Above S1 foramen** |
| **Inlet** | Into **anterior** sacral body |

### Lateral femoral cutaneous nerve

**Course** — lumbar plexus → **surface of iliacus** → ✓ **under the inguinal ligament** (near ASIS) → pierces fascia lata · variable path

**Traps** — ✗ Wrong options: deep to psoas · medial to femoral vein · adjacent to femoral nerve · deep to iliopectineal fascia

## How it's asked

**"Iliosacral screw too anterior-superior on S1 ala — deficit?"** → **loss of great-toe extension (L5)**

**"Most common location of LFCN?"** → **under the inguinal ligament**

# Lumbrical Muscles

*Basic Science · 59 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy & function

| | Lumbricals (4) |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | **FDP tendons** (radial side) |
| **Insertion** | **Radial lateral band** of extensor expansion |
| **Action** | **Flex MCP** (force palmar to MCP axis) + **extend PIP/DIP** |
| **Innervation** | **1st + 2nd: median** · **3rd + 4th: ulnar** |
| **Arterial supply** | Palmar metacarpal arteries |

![Lumbricals arising from FDP](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10051-lumbricals-moved.jpg)

| Muscle | Pennation / origin |
|---|---|

## How it's asked

**"Unipennate, radial side of FDP, inserts radial lateral band, palmar to MCP axis?"** → **1st lumbrical**\
✗ Wrong options: 4th lumbrical (bipennate) · dorsal interosseous
