# Material Properties

*Basic Science · 60 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Definitions

| Term | Definition | Units |
|---|---|---|
| Load | Force on a body | N |
| **Stress** | Internal force intensity = **force / area** | Pa (N/m²) |
| **Strain** | Relative deformation = **ΔL / L** | None |
| **Elastic deformation** | Reversible — returns to shape when unloaded | — |
| **Plastic deformation** | ✓ **Irreversible** — doesn't return to original length | — |
| **Toughness** | ✓ **Energy absorbed per volume before fracture = area under stress–strain curve** | J/m³ |
| **Creep** | ✓ **↑ deformation (strain) over time under constant load** | — |
| **Load (stress) relaxation** | ✓ **↓ stress (peak load) over time at constant strain/elongation** | — |
| **Hysteresis** | Loading ≠ unloading curve (viscoelastic) · area = energy dissipated | — |
| Ductility | Ability to deform plastically under tension before rupture | — |
| Strain rate | Strain ÷ time | — |
| Finite element analysis | Complex shape split into triangles/quadrilaterals, forces balanced | — |

**Creep in polyethylene liners** — plastic deformation under load **without wear debris** · TO note: creep "at high temperature, below elastic limit"

**Creep (TO, FNB)** — answer key not provided by source · options: ↑ length under constant load, ↓ length under constant load, stiffness change from viscoelasticity, ↑ stiffness with time · Orthobullets definition = **increased deformation under constant load**

### Stress–strain curve (yield, elastic, plastic, toughness, fatigue)

**Axes (TO)** — ✓ **strain on X-axis**, stress on Y-axis

![Stress–strain curve](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9062-basic-stress-strain-curve-moved.jpg)

| Feature | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Elastic zone** | Linear — returns to shape (A → B "elastic behaviour") · **Hooke's law**: stress ∝ strain |
| **Young's modulus** | **Slope in elastic zone = stiffness** (resistance to deformation) |
| **Yield point / yield strength** | ✓ **Elastic → plastic transition** · stress producing a set permanent deformation |
| **Plastic zone** | Permanent deformation |
| **Ultimate (tensile) strength** | Highest point — **maximum stress / load to failure** · then necking (ductile) |
| **Breaking (fracture) point** | Failure |
| Toe region | Ligament/tendon — crimped fibrils straighten |

**Implant absorbing most energy before failure** — ✓ **high toughness**\
✗ Wrong options: modulus · yield stress alone · brittle

**Two plates load–displacement** — larger area = **tougher** · higher peak = stronger · earlier break = more brittle

**Hip screw returns to shape after bending** — **elastic** deformation

### Metal characteristics

**Fatigue** — ✓ **failure below ultimate tensile strength after repetitive cycles** · depends on stress magnitude + cycles · ✓ mode of failure of a nailed femur that fails to unite (**cyclic fatigue**)\
✗ Wrong options: static/catastrophic (single supraphysiological load)

**Endurance (fatigue) limit** — max cyclic stress never causing failure · arthroplasty implants designed above physiological loads · fracture fixation only until union (TO)

**Bone vs implants (TO)** — bone ultimate strength ~4× lower than steel → fails first in single load (periprosthetic fracture) · bone **resists fatigue better** (remodelling repairs microcracks)

## 2 Mechanism

### Material descriptions

| Type | Definition | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Brittle** | Linear to failure, little/no plastic deformation | **PMMA, ceramics**, hydroxyapatite |
| **Ductile** | Large plastic deformation before failure | **Metals** |
| **Viscoelastic** | Stress–strain depends on **rate + duration** of load (internal friction) | **Ligaments, bone, cartilage** |
| **Isotropic** | Same properties in all directions | **Metals (steel, titanium)**, golf ball |
| **Anisotropic** | Properties depend on load direction | **Ligaments, bone** |

### Viscoelasticity (creep, stress relaxation, anisotropy, strain rate)

**Ligaments** — viscoelastic → **strength depends on rate of load** · creep, stress relaxation, non-linear elasticity · not **isotropic** · load–elongation region B = linear elastic after fibrils straightened (A = toe, C = ultimate load, D = energy, E = ultimate elongation)

**Cortical bone — ↑ strain rate** — ✓ **↑ elastic modulus AND ↑ ultimate strength** · physiological rates (<0.1/s) elastic + ductile · high-impact rates brittle · modulus up to 2× in falls (TO)

**Bone true statement (TO)** — ✓ **time-dependent viscoelastic properties**\
✗ Wrong options: isotropic · less fatigue-resistant than steel · repetitive tension → oblique fracture (→ transverse)

**Anisotropy of cortical bone (TO)** — compressive strength **50% > tensile** · longitudinal modulus **17 GPa** vs transverse **12 GPa** · longitudinal strength **193 MPa** vs 133 MPa

**Articular cartilage** — biphasic viscoelastic (flow-dependent fluid drag + flow-independent matrix) · modulus changes with **duration + rate** of load

**Allograft ACL** — may lengthen by **creep**

### Bone failure (fracture patterns)

**Bone strength** — ✓ **strongest in compression, weakest in shear (TO)**, intermediate tension

| Load | Fracture |
|---|---|
| **Tension** | **Transverse** (muscle pull) |
| **Compression** | Crush · short oblique (fails at 45° plane of max shear) (TO) |
| **Bending** | **Butterfly fragment** (convex side fails in tension) |
| **Torsion** | **Spiral** · longer bone → ↑ outer-cortex stress |
| Bending + compression / bending + torsion | **Short oblique** (TO) |

**Bone composition** — collagen (low modulus, tensile strength) + hydroxyapatite (stiff, brittle, compressive) · remodels to ↑ **moment of inertia**

**Ligaments/tendons** — tolerate **5–10% strain** (bone 1–4%)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Modulus of elasticity of implant materials

| Rank (stiffest → least) | Material | Approx GPa (OB) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Ceramic (Al₂O₃)** | Alumina 340 · zirconia 248 |
| 2 | **Co-Cr-Mo alloy** | 240 |
| 3 | **Stainless steel** | 240 |
| — | Tantalum | 186 |
| 4 | **Titanium** | 115 — ✓ **closest to cortical bone** |
| 5 | **Cortical bone** | 18 |
| 6 | Matrix polymers | — |
| 7 | **PMMA** | 3.1 |
| 8 | **Polyethylene** | 0.8–1.5 |
| 9 | Cancellous bone | 2 |
| 10 | Tendon/ligament | — |
| 11 | Cartilage | — |

**Increasing modulus (TO)** — ✓ **polymers < bone cement < cortical bone < titanium < stainless steel < cobalt-chromium < ceramics**

**PMMA modulus** — between cortical and cancellous (OB) · 10× lower than cortical bone (Webb & Spencer quote) — read the options

**Ligament modulus** — < cancellous bone, > cartilage

![Relative Young's modulus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9062-relative-young-s-modulus-moved.jpg)

| Strength rankings | Order |
|---|---|
| **Ultimate (tensile) strength** | **Co-Cr > titanium > stainless steel > cortical bone** |
| Fatigue limit + tensile strength (TO) | Stainless steel < titanium < Co-Cr |
| Yield strength | Cast Co-Cr > titanium > wrought Co-Cr, stainless steel |

**Titanium vs cold-forged Co-Cr** — ✓ **↓ tensile strength** · ↓ fatigue + yield strength · **↑ ductility**

### Specific materials

| Material | Composition | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Titanium** (Ti-6Al-4V) | — | Most biocompatible · **self-passivation** (adherent oxide) → corrosion resistant · **low modulus** (≈ bone) | **Notch sensitivity, poor wear** → not for femoral head · more metal debris |
| **316L stainless steel** | **Iron + carbon (L = low, 0.03%) + chromium (~17–18%) + nickel (~12%) + molybdenum (~2.5–3%)** · **no cobalt (TO)** | Stiff, fracture resistant, cheap, ductile | **Pitting + crevice corrosion** · stress shielding |
| **Cobalt alloy** | **Co + Cr + Mo** | Very strong · better corrosion resistance than steel · TKA femoral components | Very stiff → stress shielding (✓ for cemented stems — protects mantle) |
| **UHMWPE** | — | Tough, ductile, resilient, wear-resistant, **high fracture toughness** | Abrasion (third body), thermoplastic, weak in tension · **gamma cross-linking ↑ wear resistance, ↓ fatigue + fracture resistance** |
| **PMMA** | Powder: polymer, **benzoyl peroxide (initiator)**, **barium sulfate (radio-opacifier)**, colour · Liquid: monomer, **DMPT (accelerator)**, hydroquinone (stabiliser) | **Interlocks with bone** (grout, not glue) · strongest in **compression** · ultimate strength at **24 h** | **Brittle**, notch-sensitive · poor **tension + shear** · **exothermic** polymerisation · hypotension on insertion · fails by microfracture |
| Silicones | — | Non-WB joint replacement | Poor strength/wear → **synovitis** |
| **Ceramics** | Alumina, zirconia | **Best wear vs PE**, high compressive strength, inert | **Brittle, low fracture toughness**, high modulus, low tensile strength, poor crack resistance |

**316L components (TO)** — chromium → oxide layer (anti-rust, passivation) · nickel → manufacturing (austenite) · molybdenum → protects oxide layer from pitting

## 5 Management

### Corrosion (galvanic, fretting, crevice, passivation)

| Type | Mechanism | Key facts |
|---|---|---|
| **Galvanic** | ✓ **Electrochemical potential between two dissimilar metals in conductive medium** (anode corrodes) | **316L + Co-Cr = highest risk** · steel very susceptible · Co-Cr "most susceptible" of listed (✗ Wrong options: Ti, ceramics, PE) · cerclage wire SS on Ti stem · mixed-metal neck/stem |
| **Crevice** | **O₂ tension difference** in cracks/crevices | **316L most prone** · **titanium least** · plate countersinks, cementless cups |
| **Fretting** | ✓ **Micromotion at contact sites under load** → passive layer scraped off → pits, grooves, oxide debris | **Head–neck taper** (trunnionosis), ↑ serum Co/Cr, synovitis, ALTR · **commonest cause of modular mid-stem junction failure** · ↑ with more interfaces · worsens over time · ceramic heads ↓ it |
| **Fretting fatigue** | Cyclic load + small oscillation → cracks | ✓ **Initiates mid-stem modular junction fracture** (completed by bending) · risk: high BMI, poor proximal bone support |
| Pitting | Local depassivation → holes | Stainless steel |
| Intergranular | Grain-boundary attack | — |
| Stress corrosion | High tensile stress + corrosive environment (manufacturing) | — |
| Degradation / oxidative | Polymers / free radical | — |

**Self-passivation** — ✓ **reason titanium is corrosion resistant** (oxide layer forms spontaneously)

![Fretting corrosion at a modular junction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9062-fretting-corrosino.jpg)

### Ceramic properties & fracture

**Ceramic disadvantage** — ✓ **low fracture toughness** (poor crack resistance)

**Most serious CoC complication** — ✓ **fracture** (fragments embed → third-body wear) · squeaking (annoying, may herald failure)

**CoC THA: new squeak + stiffness + LLD, fractured ceramic, normal CRP/ESR** — ✓ **thorough debridement + one-stage head + liner revision**\
✗ Wrong options: two-stage (no infection)

### Structural mechanics (moment of inertia, bending rigidity, tension band)

**Area moment of inertia** — ✓ ∝ **distance² of material from the centre of mass** (tube > solid rod of same area)\
✗ Wrong options: density · modulus

| Implant | Bending rigidity ∝ |
|---|---|
| **Solid pin / rod** | **radius⁴** (diameter⁴) |
| Hollow (cannulated) nail | ~radius³ |
| **Plate** | **thickness³** (2× thickness → 8× stiffness) |
| Ex-fix pin | diameter⁴ · pin stiffness ∝ 1/(bone–rod distance)³ · fragment contact most important |

**Plate on tension side** — ✓ **minimises bending stresses in the plate** (fracture closes under load, bone shares load)

**Strain sharing** — 5 mm displacement across one 5 mm gap = 100% strain · shared across multiple gaps (each 5 → 6 mm) = 20% → **80% ↓ strain** (Perren)

**Stress risers around plates/prostheses (TO)** — ✓ **overlap plate with stem**, gap <8 cm risky · interprosthetic distance <110 mm risky in weak bone · end-screw fracture 1–3% (non-locking), 2.6% (locking) · ✓ **oblique end screw** · ✓ **non-locking end screw in all-locked construct** · **unicortical end screw — NOT recommended** (weaker) · insert non-locking before locking screws

### Screw mechanics (TO)

| Term | Fact (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Core (root/minor) diameter** | = **drill size** · determines **strength, fatigue resistance, bending strength, tensile strength** |
| **Major (thread) diameter** | Thread depth (major − minor) → **pull-out strength**, stripping torque |
| **Runout** | Shaft–thread junction · **weakest part of partially threaded screw** |
| Pitch | Distance between threads · lead = advance per turn |
| **Mechanical advantage** | = **circumference / pitch** (✗ Wrong options: pitch/circumference) |
| **Pull-out strength** | ↑ outer diameter, ↑ cortical thickness · (✗ Wrong options: thread density (if 5–6 threads in cortex)) · ✓ **↓ cortex thickness ↓ pull-out** |
| **AO 3.5 mm cortical screw drill** | ✓ **2.7 mm** (TO key) |

**Screw types (TO)** — non-tapping (tap needed) · self-tapping (cutting flutes) · self-drilling · cancellous (deep threads; partially threaded = lag) · locking (threaded head) · **Herbert headless** (variable pitch → compression) · interference (no compression, presses graft)

### Plating principles (TO)

**MIPO ideal (TO)** — ✓ **comminuted metaphyseal fractures of long bones**\
✗ Wrong options: abraded skin · compound · diabetic

**DHS barrel (TO)** — standard (long) **38 mm** · short **25 mm** · lag-screw thread **22 mm**

**Bridge plating (TO)** — plate spans comminution, screws away from fracture

**Working length (TO)** — ✓ **distance between the screws/pins closest to the fracture on either side** · ↑ working length → more flexible, ↓ stress risers · unicortical screw has shorter working length than bicortical · ex-fix: pin/cluster spacing · >3 diaphyseal screws add little\
✗ Wrong options: "max distance between proximal-most and distal-most screws" = bridge span confusion

### Bioabsorbable polymers (TO)

| Polymer (TO) | Modulus | Strength retention | Full resorption |
|---|---|---|---|
| **PGA** | ~7 GPa · **highest initial strength** · hydrophilic | **2–4 wk** | 6–12 mo (TO) · 1–2 mo (Orthobullets) |
| **PLLA** | ~3 GPa · hydrophobic methyl group | 3–6 mo | **2–5 yr** |
| **PLGA 50:50** | ~2 GPa · **most amorphous** | 1–3 wk | ✓ **1–2 mo — fastest of all** |
| PCL | ~0.4 GPa · rubbery | — | 2–4 yr |
| **Magnesium** | ~45 GPa (≈ bone) · H₂ gas | 3–6 mo | 6–12 mo |
| β-TCP | Brittle | — | 6–18 mo (matches bone formation) |
| Calcium sulfate | Very low | — | 1–2 mo |

**PGA vs PLLA (TO)** — ✓ **PGA higher initial strength but degrades much faster**

## X vs Y

| | Creep | Stress relaxation | Hysteresis |
|---|---|---|---|
| Held constant | **Load** | **Strain/deformation** | — |
| Changes | ↑ Strain | ↓ Stress | Energy lost |

## How it's asked

**"On a stress–strain curve (TO)?"** → **strain on X-axis**

**"Stress at which plastic deformation begins?"** → **yield strength**

**"Plastic deformation?"** → **change in length that doesn't recover**

**"Fatigue?"** → **failure below tensile strength after many cycles**

**"Yield point on figure?"** → **C** (A fracture, B ultimate, D modulus)

**"Hip screw returns to shape after bending?"** → **elastic**

**"Plate B larger area under curve?"** → **tougher**

**"Implant absorbing most energy?"** → **high toughness**

**"A → B straight portion?"** → **elastic behaviour**

**"Point separating elastic and plastic?"** → **yield point**

**"Energy per volume before fracture on plot?"** → **area under curve (E)**

**"Nailed femur fails 8 months later?"** → **cyclic fatigue**

**"True of bone (TO)?"** → **time-dependent viscoelastic**

**"Ligament region after fibrils straightened?"** → **B (linear)**

**"Viscoelastic → tensile strength affected by?"** → **rate of load**

**"ACL properties EXCEPT?"** → **isotropism**

**"Load relaxation?"** → **↓ peak loads over time at same elongation**

**"Creep?"** → **progressive deformation under constant force**

**"Cortical bone ↑ strain rate?"** → **↑ modulus and ↑ ultimate strength**

**"Greater longitudinal than transverse compressive strength?"** → **anisotropy**

**"Melted stainless-steel cube?"** → **isotropic**

**"Allograft creep?"** → **↑ strain at constant load**

**"Viscoelasticity of cartilage?"** → **modulus changes with duration + rate of load**

**"Increasing modulus order (TO)?"** → **cortical bone < Ti < SS < Co-Cr < ceramic**

**"Stainless steel description?"** → **iron-carbon, stiffer than titanium**

**"Modulus closest to cortical bone?"** → **titanium**

**"Cr, Mo, Co components of?"** → **cobalt alloy**

**"Ti vs cold-forged Co-Cr?"** → **↓ tensile strength**

**"PMMA true?"** → **modulus between cortical and cancellous**\
✗ Wrong options: BaSO₄ initiator · ductile · shear-resistant · endothermic

**"Highest modulus?"** → **ceramic**

**"Galvanic corrosion?"** → **electrochemical potential between dissimilar metals in conductive medium**

**"Most susceptible to galvanic corrosion?"** → **Co-Cr** (of listed)

**"Cause of fretting corrosion?"** → **micromotion at head–neck junction**

**"Modular revision stem junction failure mechanism?"** → **micromotion at contact (fretting)**

**"Titanium corrosion resistance?"** → **self-passivation**

**"Initiates mid-stem modular junction failure?"** → **fretting fatigue**

**"Pits, grooves, oxide debris from micromotion?"** → **fretting**

**"True of 316L?"** → **highly susceptible to pitting/crevice corrosion**

**"Fretting corrosion true?"** → **small motion at modular junctions**

**"Black debris at Co-Cr head / Ti neck taper?"** → **fretting corrosion**

**"Low fracture toughness bearing?"** → **ceramic**

**"Most serious CoC complication?"** → **fracture**

**"Fractured CoC, normal labs?"** → **debridement + one-stage head/liner revision**

**"Bones weakest in (TO)?"** → **shear**

**"Not recommended to ↓ stress-riser fracture (TO)?"** → **unicortical end screw**

**"Bending rigidity of solid pin ∝ radius to?"** → **4th power**

**"Area moment of inertia influenced most by?"** → **distance from centre of mass**

**"Plate on tension side because?"** → **minimises bending stress in plate**

**"Strain change at Y (comminuted) vs X?"** → **80% decrease**

**"316L composition (TO)?"** → **iron, carbon, chromium, nickel, molybdenum**

**"Not a component of 316L (TO)?"** → **cobalt**

**"NOT true of cortical screw (TO)?"** → **"MA = pitch/circumference"**

**"↓ pull-out strength (TO)?"** → **thinner cortex**

**"Bending strength of cortical screw (TO)?"** → **inner core**

**"AO drill for 3.5 mm cortical screw (TO)?"** → **2.7 mm**

**"Ideal fracture for MIPO (TO)?"** → **comminuted metaphyseal**

**"Long DHS barrel (TO)?"** → **38 mm**

**"Plating principle in picture (TO)?"** → **bridge**

**"Working length — all true EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **"max distance between proximal-most and distal-most screws"**

**"PGA vs PLLA (TO)?"** → **PGA stronger initially but degrades faster**

**"PLGA ratio with fastest degradation (TO)?"** → **50:50**

# Median nerve

*Basic Science · 61 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Branches in the hand (TO)

**Carpal tunnel** — median nerve enters hand deep to flexor retinaculum → divides into **lateral** and **medial** divisions **(TO)**

| Division (TO) | Supplies |
|---|---|
| **Lateral (stouter)** | **Thenar 2½: APB, opponens pollicis, superficial head FPB** · **1st lumbrical** · sensation thumb + radial index |
| **Medial** (mainly sensory) | Rest of lateral 3½ digits · **2nd lumbrical** |

**Recurrent motor branch** — innervates **thenar muscles** → ✓ **at risk excising a thenar mass** (e.g. lipoma)\
✗ Wrong options: deep ulnar · AIN · palmar cutaneous ulnar · no "recurrent ulnar branch"

**AIN** — FPL, pronator quadratus, FDP index (± middle) · terminal branch → wrist capsule only

### Median nerve palsy — thenar function & opposition (TO)

**Loss of opposition (TO)** — ✓ **median nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar · radial · medial division

**Thumb abductors (TO)** — ✓ **median + radial nerve both** (APB — median · APL — radial/PIN)

## 3 Presentation

### High median palsy vs AIN

| Lesion | Attempted fist |
|---|---|
| **High median (arm)** | ✓ **Thumb + index can't flex** (FPL, FDP + FDS index lost) — "benediction" attitude · middle partly flexes |
| AIN syndrome | Loss of **DIP flexion thumb, index, middle** (FPL, FDP) — no sensory loss |
| PIN syndrome | Finger drop at MCPs, wrist extends in **radial deviation** |
| Radial nerve (arm) | **Wrist + finger drop** |
| Median at wrist (CTS) | Thenar weakness + sensory loss, long flexors intact |

## How it's asked

**"Excising thenar lipoma — nerve at risk?"** → **recurrent motor branch of median**

**"Can't flex thumb + index on making a fist?"** → **median nerve lesion in the arm**\
✗ Wrong options: AIN · PIN · CTS

**"Nerve supply to thumb abductors (TO)?"** → **median and radial both**

**"Nerve injury causing loss of opposition (TO)?"** → **median**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Molecular Biology Basics

*Basic Science · 62 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Nucleic acids & cell cycle

**Chromosomes** — **46** (22 autosome pairs + 1 sex pair) · **haploid** = DNA content of egg/sperm (half)

| | DNA | RNA |
|---|---|---|
| Bases | **A, T, G, C** (A–T, G–C) | **A, U, G, C** — no thymine |
| Strands | Double | Usually single (double in some viruses/interactions) |
| 2' position | **Hydrogen** (deoxyribose) | **Hydroxyl** (ribose) → less stable |
| Helix | **B-form** | **A-form** |
| Location | Nucleus | Nucleus + cytoplasm |

| RNA | Role |
|---|---|
| mRNA | Carries DNA code for protein |
| rRNA | Ribosome |
| tRNA | Brings amino acids to mRNA |

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Gene** | DNA segment encoding a protein |
| **Exon** | Coding — **ex**pressed |
| **Intron** | Non-coding |
| **Codon** | 3 nucleotides → 1 of 20 amino acids |
| **Promoter** | Controls transcription initiation |
| Enhancer | Transcription-factor binding site, regulates transcription |
| **Transcription** | ✓ **RNA polymerase reads DNA → complementary mRNA** |
| **Translation** | mRNA codons → polypeptide |
| Replication | DNA synthesis in S phase |
| Recombination | Meiotic haploid product with new allele combination |
| Translocation | Exchange between non-homologous chromosomes (Ewing, rhabdo, synovial sarcoma) |

| Cell-cycle phase | Event | Ploidy |
|---|---|---|
| G0 | Stable, not cycling | 2N |
| G1 | Growth | 2N |
| **S** | ✓ **DNA replication** | → 4N |
| G2 | Gap | 4N |
| M | Mitosis | → 2N daughters |

**No "R phase"**

![Cell cycle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9084-cell-cycle.jpg)

**Apoptosis** — programmed cell death via intracellular signalling · (✗ Wrong options: lysis (contents spilled)) · loss = hallmark of cancer · **TNF receptor 1** → ✓ **apoptosis**

### Laboratory techniques (RT-PCR, GWAS)

| Technique | Use |
|---|---|
| Agarose gel electrophoresis | Separates **DNA by size** (negative charge, small runs faster) |
| **Southern blot** | **DNA** sequence |
| **Northern blot** | **RNA** |
| **Western blot** (SDS-PAGE) | **Protein** |
| DNA ligation | **Recombinant DNA** |
| **Plasmid** | ✓ **Extrachromosomal circular DNA replicating independently** · antibiotic resistance · vector (transformation) |
| **PCR** | DNA → many DNA copies · primers + heat-stable DNA polymerase |
| **RT-PCR** | RNA → cDNA → amplified · product = ✓ **DNA** · ✓ **measures mRNA level** of a gene |
| ELISA | Antigen / antibody detection |
| Immunocytochemistry | Localise protein in cells |
| DNA microarray | Expression profiling |
| DNA profiling | Forensic identity |

| Sequencing | Scope |
|---|---|
| **Whole-genome (WGS)** | Entire genome, coding + non-coding |
| **Whole-exome (WES)** | **Protein-coding exons only** (1% of genome, 85% of disease variants, 1/6 cost) |
| **GWAS** | ✓ **SNPs across many patients with a phenotype** → mutational variability |

## 6 Complications

**Herniated nucleus pulposus pain** — ✓ **TNF-α** main inflammatory mediator (also IL-1, IL-8)

## How it's asked

**"DNA replication phase?"** → **S**

**"RNA statements EXCEPT?"** → **"RNA uses thymine, DNA uracil"**

**"Plasmid?"** → **extrachromosomal circular DNA replicating independently**

**"Transcription?"** → **RNA polymerase reads DNA → mRNA**

**"RT-PCR product?"** → **DNA**

**"Assay for mRNA level?"** → **RT-PCR**\
✗ Wrong options: Western, Southern, ELISA

**"GWAS true?"** → **uses SNPs to evaluate variability among patients**

**"TNF receptor 1 involved in?"** → **apoptosis**

**"Inflammatory modulator of disc herniation pain?"** → **TNF-α**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Multiple Sclerosis

*Basic Science · 63 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & pathophysiology

**Incidence** — 5 / 100,000 (US) · **20–40 yr** · **women > men** · **northern latitudes**

**Risk factors** — stress · smoking · **↓ sunlight / low vitamin D** · not considered hereditary

**Mechanism** — CNS **demyelination + axonal injury** · **T-cell–mediated autoimmune** · genetic + environmental + infectious factors

**Orthopaedic associations** — **↑ fracture risk** (falls + ↓ BMD) · **osteoporosis** (inactivity, ↓ vit D, immunomodulators) · gait abnormality (foot drop) · spasticity, contractures

## 3 Presentation

**Clinical definition** — **≥2 episodes of neurological dysfunction separated in time and space** (brain, cord, optic nerve)

**Symptoms** — **optic neuritis**, diplopia, nystagmus · fatigue, depression · dysarthria, dysphagia · weakness, ataxia, falls · paraesthesia, **peculiar sensations (wetness)** · bowel/bladder dysfunction · relapsing–remitting **transient limb numbness** (non-dermatomal)

**Exam** — wide-based gait, limb ataxia, slapping foot · **spasticity, hyperreflexia, Babinski, Hoffmann** · **internuclear ophthalmoplegia (MLF)** · **Lhermitte's sign**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**MRI brain + spinal cord with gadolinium** — ✓ **multiple focal demyelinating (T2) lesions** · **asymmetric periventricular plaques** · cord lesions commonest in cervical spine

![MS — periventricular plaques](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9079-ms.jpg)

### Genes & lab markers

**CSF** — **↑ IgG (80%)** · **oligoclonal bands** · mononuclear pleocytosis (25%) · routine bloods normal (exclude mimics)

**Differential** — cervical myelopathy · CNS mass · B12 deficiency · sarcoidosis · CNS infection

## 5 Management

**Young woman, transient arm numbness, UMN signs, multifocal T2 cord lesions** — ✓ **neurology referral**\
✗ Wrong options: laminoplasty · plasmapheresis/IVIG (GBS) · B12 (subacute combined degeneration) · lumbar MRI

**Acute relapse** — **corticosteroids** · **prophylaxis** — **interferon-β** (↓ relapses, slows RRMS)

**Spasticity** — baclofen, gabapentin, clonazepam, **botox** · PT: gentle stretching, progressive resistance · osteoporosis treatment

## 6 Complications

**Course** — ✓ **relapsing–remitting (commonest)** · primary progressive · secondary progressive · progressive relapsing

**Life expectancy** — 5–10 yr shorter

## How it's asked

**"29-yr-old woman, relapsing arm numbness, brisk reflexes, bilateral Hoffmann, multifocal cord lesions — next?"** → **neurology referral**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Muscle Biology & Physiology

*Basic Science · 64 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Gross anatomy

| Sheath | Surrounds |
|---|---|
| **Epimysium** | Whole muscle (bundles) |
| **Perimysium** | **Fascicles** |
| **Endomysium** | **Individual fibres** |

**Myotendinous junction** — infolded membrane ↑ attachment area · ✓ **weak link — commonest site of strains** (eccentric)

![Muscle connective-tissue layers](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9021-anatomy-epimysium-moved.jpg)

### Sarcomere

**Units** — fibre (cell, from myoblasts) → myofibril (chain of sarcomeres) · **thick = myosin**, **thin = actin**

| Band | Content | In contraction |
|---|---|---|
| **A band** | Actin + myosin | **Unchanged** |
| **I band** | Actin only | Shortens |
| **H zone** | Myosin only | Shortens |
| **Z line** | Actin attachment, sarcomere border | Move closer |

### Action potential & excitation–contraction coupling

**Nerve action potential** — ✓ **Na⁺ through voltage-gated channels** (positive-feedback depolarisation)

**Motor end plate** — ACh released → binds ACh receptor · **myasthenia gravis** = ↓ ACh receptors · **botox** = blocks ACh release

**Coupling** — depolarisation → sarcoplasmic reticulum **releases Ca²⁺** → **Ca²⁺ binds troponin** → tropomyosin shifts → myosin sites on actin exposed → cross-bridges + ATP hydrolysis → sliding · **low Ca²⁺: tropomyosin blocks** binding sites

![Excitation–contraction coupling](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9021-excitation-contraction-coupling.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Contraction types (concentric, eccentric, isotonic)

| Type | Definition | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **Isometric** | Constant **length** | **Push against wall** |
| **Isotonic** | Constant **tension** — ✓ **concentric and eccentric** | **Biceps curl up AND down** |
| **Concentric** | Shortens while contracting | ✓ **Upward biceps curl** |
| **Eccentric** | Lengthens while contracting · **highest forces** | Lowering phase · ✓ **distal biceps rupture (eccentric phase of isotonic exercise)** |
| **Isokinetic** | Constant **speed** (Cybex machine) | — |
| **Plyometric** | Rapid lengthening → contraction | Box jumps |

### Force generation & fibre types (cross-sectional area, recruitment)

**Force / maximal tension** — ✓ **most dependent on cross-sectional area** (number of contracting myofibrils)\
✗ Wrong options: fibre type · length (Blix curve) · morphology (pennation angle)

**Speed + duration of contraction** — ✓ **fibre type**

**Progressive overload (strength training)** — ✓ **muscle fibre hypertrophy** (↑ fibre size), ↑ coordination

| | **Type I (slow twitch)** — "slow red ox" | **Type II (fast twitch)** |
|---|---|---|
| Metabolism | **Aerobic, oxidative** | **Anaerobic, glycolytic** (IIa oxidative-glycolytic, IIb glycolytic) |
| Energy | Krebs / oxidative phosphorylation | ATP-CP |
| Use | **Endurance** · low strength, low speed | **Sprinting** · high strength + speed |
| Features | ↑ mitochondria, vascular | ↑ ATPase, low triglyceride · **fatigue rapidly** (IIb) |
| Atrophy | **First to atrophy with deconditioning** | — |

**Recruitment order (size principle)** — ✓ **Type I (slow, fatigue-resistant) → Type IIa (fast, fatigue-resistant) → Type IIb (fast, fatigable)** · no "slow fatigable" units

### Metabolic systems

| System | Duration | Example | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **ATP-CP (phosphagen)** | **<20 s** | 100 m sprint | Anaerobic, **no lactate** · creatine supplement (cramps) |
| **Lactic anaerobic** | **20–120 s** | 400 m | Glucose → lactic acid |
| **Aerobic** | Long, low intensity | Distance running | Krebs cycle, glucose + fatty acids |

## 6 Complications

### Muscle injury & healing

**Soreness (DOMS)** — **oedema + inflammation** of connective tissue · worse with **unaccustomed eccentric** exercise · **peaks 24–48 h** · ↑ CK

**First cells after injury (contusion)** — ✓ **neutrophils** (free radicals may ↑ damage)

**Strain** — ✓ **myotendinous junction** (eccentric) · inflammation → fibrosis · contraction bands seal membrane defects · basal lamina scaffolds preserved

**Healing phases** — necrosis → inflammation → repair → fibrosis · regeneration needs macrophage clearance (NSAIDs may impair)

**Fibrosis** — ✓ **TGF-β1** · regeneration trophic factors **IGF-1, bFGF** · **angiotensin-II receptor blockade ↓ fibrosis**

**Atrophy** — disuse/denervation → fatty infiltration, ↑ fatigability · **single-joint muscles atrophy faster** · ↓ CSA → ↓ force

## How it's asked

**"Upward biceps curl — contraction?"** → **concentric**

**"Distal biceps rupture during dumbbell curl — phase?"** → **eccentric phase of isotonic exercise**

**"Isotonic contraction?"** → **both upward and downward biceps curl**

**"True of skeletal muscle?"** → **duration + speed depend on fibre type**\
✗ Wrong options: force depends on length/fibre type

**"Progressive overload in adults →?"** → **fibre hypertrophy**

**"Cross-sectional area determines?"** → **maximal tension**

**"Force most dependent on?"** → **cross-sectional area**

**"Motor-unit recruitment order?"** → **slow fatigue-resistant → fast fatigue-resistant → fast fatigable**

**"First cells after thigh contusion?"** → **neutrophils**

**"Promotes fibrosis after muscle injury?"** → **TGF-β1**\
✗ Wrong options: IGF-1, bFGF (regeneration)

**"Calf strain — structure affected?"** → **musculotendinous junction (C)**

**"Na⁺ through voltage-gated channel →?"** → **nerve action potential**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Musculocutaneous Nerve

*Basic Science · 65 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Origin & course

**Origin** — **C5–7** · superior trunk → **lateral cord** (terminal branch) · leaves axilla at inferior border of pectoralis minor **(TO)**

**Course** — ✓ **pierces coracobrachialis** **3–8 cm** distal to coracoid (Orthobullets) · **5–8 cm** (TO) · 1.5–9 cm (brachial plexus note) → runs **between biceps (superficial) and brachialis (deep)** → pierces deep fascia **lateral to biceps tendon** above elbow → **lateral antebrachial cutaneous nerve**

| Motor | Sensory |
|---|---|
| **Coracobrachialis, biceps, brachialis** (C5–6) · articular branches to humerus, elbow **(TO)** | **Lateral antebrachial cutaneous** — lateral (+ volar) forearm |

![Musculocutaneous nerve piercing coracobrachialis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10102-msk-nerve.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Injury after coracoid transfer

**Mechanism** — iatrogenic in **deltopectoral approach** · dissection/retraction **medial to the conjoint tendon** · ✓ **vigorous retraction of conjoint tendon (short head biceps + coracobrachialis) → neurapraxia** **(TO)** · Latarjet / coracoid transfer, open shoulder reduction

**Lateral forearm numbness after coracoid transfer** — ✓ **musculocutaneous nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: axillary (lateral arm) · radial (dorsal wrist) · median

### Sensory & motor deficit (TO)

| Expected finding **(TO)** | Not expected **(TO)** |
|---|---|
| **↓ elbow flexion + supination** | Not **weak pronation** (pronators — median) |
| **Lateral (+ volar) forearm sensory loss** | Not elbow extension weakness (triceps — radial) |
| **Weak/absent biceps reflex** | — |
| **Small-amplitude LABCN SNAP**, ↓ biceps CMAP | — |
| Biceps atrophy | — |

**Pitfall (TO)** — complete division can be missed: **brachioradialis** still flexes elbow → palpate biceps during testing

## How it's asked

**"Vigorous conjoint-tendon retraction endangers (TO)?"** → **musculocutaneous nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: axillary · anterior circumflex humeral artery

**"Lateral forearm numbness 1 wk after coracoid transfer?"** → **musculocutaneous**

**"After open shoulder reduction, lateral forearm numbness — unlikely finding (TO)?"** → **weak supination AND pronation** (pronation normal)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Myasthenia Gravis

*Basic Science · 66 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & pathophysiology

**Bimodal** — **young women <40**, **older men >50**

**Risk factors** — **HLA-B8** · drugs: **penicillamine, aminoglycosides**

**Mechanism** — ✓ **autoantibodies (Ig) at the neuromuscular junction** against **nicotinic ACh receptor** (commoner) or **MuSK** · **type II hypersensitivity** · T cells stimulate B-cell antibodies

**Associations** — **thymoma**, thymic hyperplasia

### Genes & lab markers

**First test** — ✓ **anti-AChR antibodies** · anti-MuSK

**EMG** — **decremental CMAP** on repetitive stimulation

## 3 Presentation

**Hallmark** — **fluctuating weakness worse with use / end of day**, better with rest · true fatigue (↓ contractile force)

**Commonest presenting symptoms** — **ocular: ptosis, diplopia** (ptosis on sustained upgaze)

**Bulbar** — dysphagia, dysarthria, fatigable chewing · **proximal** weakness

**Bedside tests** — **ice-pack test** (cold ↓ acetylcholinesterase → ptosis improves) · **edrophonium (Tensilon)** (short-acting AChE inhibitor; only with ptosis/ophthalmoparesis)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**CT chest** — exclude **thymoma**

### Differential

| Condition | Distinguishing feature |
|---|---|
| **Myasthenia gravis** | **Worse with use** · antibodies to AChR (postsynaptic) |
| **Lambert–Eaton (LEMS)** | **Improves with use** · autonomic features |
| **Botulism** | **Poor pupillary light response** · ↓ ACh release |
| Non-depolarising NMB | ↓ ACh binding |
| **Muscular dystrophy** | **Absent dystrophin**, excess sarcolemmal Ca²⁺, young onset |
| **CMT** | **Demyelination**, distal long-axon degeneration |
| Thyroid ophthalmopathy | — |

## 5 Management

**First-line symptomatic** — **AChE inhibitor: pyridostigmine** (side effects: cramps, diarrhoea, bradycardia, sweating, secretions)

**Chronic immunotherapy** — **oral prednisone**

**Myasthenic crisis (respiratory weakness)** — **IVIG or plasmapheresis**

**Thymectomy** — any **thymoma** (with or without MG)

## 6 Complications

**Myasthenic crisis** — respiratory failure · most ocular MG progresses to generalised

## How it's asked

**"45-yr-old woman, best in morning, fatigable arms, ptosis on upgaze — pathology?"** → **Ig antibodies at NM junction**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ ACh release (botulinum) · demyelination (CMT) · absent dystrophin

**"Weakness that improves with use?"** → **Lambert–Eaton**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Nonunion and Bone Defects

*Basic Science · 67 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Definitions

**Nonunion** — arrest of fracture repair, no further capacity to heal without intervention

**FDA definition (TO)** — ✓ **≥9 months since injury + no visible progressive healing for 3 months**

**Delayed union** — not healed in expected time · **failure to unite by 6 months** (Orthobullets) · **3–6 months** (TO) · varies with site and soft-tissue injury

**Large segmental defect** — functional nonunion

### Causes

**Commonest** — **inadequate stabilisation + poor blood supply** · infection · **smoking (biggest modifiable risk)** · location (scaphoid, distal tibia, 5th MT base) · segmental/butterfly (intercalary blood supply)

**Smoking** — nicotine vasoconstriction + **CO binding Hb** · prior smokers also at risk · > NSAIDs, BMI >40, delay >24 h · **reamed nailing ↓ nonunion**\
✗ Wrong options: risk

**Spine fusion in smoker** — ✓ **maintain smoking cessation** best ↓ pseudarthrosis

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Classification & biology

| Type | Biology | Mechanics | Appearance | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Hypertrophic** | **Adequate** vascularity | **Inadequate stability** (too much motion) | **Abundant callus**, no bridging ("elephant foot") | ✓ **↑ mechanical stability only** (e.g. exchange nail, compression plate) — rarely needs graft |
| **Oligotrophic** | Usually adequate | **Malreduction/distraction** (displacement) · or **overly stiff construct** | Little/no callus | Improved fixation ± biological stimulation |
| **Atrophic** | **Inadequate blood supply** | Inadequate immobilisation (Orthobullets) · excessively stiff fixation (OB) | **No callus, sclerotic/rounded ends** | ✓ **Revision fixation + bone graft** (autologous ICBG gold standard · BMP · DBM, cancellous chips) + healthy soft tissue |
| **Septic** | Infected | — | Variable callus | Staged: debride, beads/VAC, flaps, transport · eradicate infection |
| **Synovial pseudarthrosis** | Sealed canals + fluid-filled pseudomembrane | — | False joint · **bone scan "cold cleft"** | Resect non-viable ends, stable fixation |

**Classification axes (TO)** — epiphyseal / metaphyseal / diaphyseal · septic / aseptic · hypertrophic / oligotrophic / atrophic · pseudarthrosis

![Hypertrophic nonunion — abundant callus, no bridging](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9069-hypertrophic-nonunion.jpg)

**Biopsy of aseptic nonunion after ex-fix** — ✓ **bland cartilage callus** (failed endochondral healing)\
✗ Wrong options: PMNs (infection) · Haversian/lamellar bone (healed)

**Locked plate distal femur — short working length, high screw density** — ✓ **high stiffness → low strain → oligotrophic nonunion → fatigue failure** · revised with long working length, healed **without graft**

### Work-up

**History** — mechanism, treatments, metabolic/nutritional/immune status, **NSAIDs, nicotine** · pain on axial loading

**Exam** — neurovascular, soft tissue, nonunion mobility, deformity

**Labs** — ✓ **CBC, ESR, CRP** (infection) · total protein, albumin · **vitamin D (deficient in 60–70%)**, TSH, PTH

**Most accurate test for septic nonunion** — ✓ **CRP** (rises in 6 h, peaks 2–3 days, normalises 5–21 days with treatment)\
✗ Wrong options: IL-1, IL-6, WBC, ESR

**First step when nonunion + risk of infection (open fracture, erythema)** — ✓ **labs: vitamin D, TSH, inflammatory markers** before revision

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — cornerstone · **4 views** · full-length standing films if LLD

**CT** — if union uncertain (hardware artefact)

## 5 Management

```
Nonunion (rule out infection: CRP, ESR)
├─ Hypertrophic → ↑ STABILITY (exchange nail / compression plate)
├─ Oligotrophic → IMPROVE FIXATION ± BIOLOGY
├─ Atrophic → REVISION FIXATION + AUTOGRAFT + SOFT-TISSUE ENVELOPE
└─ Septic → STAGED DEBRIDEMENT, BEADS, FLAP, TRANSPORT
```

**Atrophic ulna nonunion 6 mo after BBFA ORIF, normal CRP/ESR** — ✓ **revision ORIF with autograft**\
✗ Wrong options: observe, cast, IM nail, revision without graft

**Atrophic radius nonunion with gap** — ✓ **supportive soft-tissue envelope** essential · plating + cancellous graft treats defects **up to 6 cm** · **3.5 mm plates** adequate · poor envelope → vascularised graft + soft tissue

**Atrophic tibial nonunion 9 mo, normal labs** — cause ✓ **lack of biology** → exchange nail + graft

**Non-operative** — brace · **bone stimulators**\
✗ Wrong options: synovial pseudarthrosis · mobile nonunion · gap >1 cm

### Electrical stimulation

**Induction coupling** — ↑ **BMP-7, BMP-2, TGF-β1**, ↑ **osteoblast proliferation** · **not ↓ osteoclast differentiation**

| Mode | Effect |
|---|---|
| Direct current | ↓ O₂, ↑ pH → ↓ osteoclasts, ↑ osteoblasts |
| Capacitive coupling (AC) | cAMP, collagen, cartilage calcification |
| PEMF | Fibrocartilage calcification |
| Combined magnetic | — |

## How it's asked

**"Ulna fracture 12 mo, pain, abundant callus on X-ray (TO)?"** → **hypertrophic nonunion**

**"Nonunion after ex-fix, cultures negative — histology?"** → **bland cartilage callus**

**"Least likely to need added biology?"** → **abundant callus without bridging (hypertrophic)**

**"Tibial nail 9 mo, no bone formation, normal labs — cause?"** → **lack of biology (atrophic)**

**"Humerus ORIF, little callus, broken plate, normal labs?"** → **atrophic**

**"Distal femur locked plate nonunion healed with revision, no graft — initial problem?"** → **high stiffness**

**"Smoker having spine fusion — ↓ pseudarthrosis by?"** → **smoking cessation**

**"Obese smoker, NSAIDs, delayed nailing — highest nonunion risk?"** → **smoking**

**"Open subtroch femur, 9-mo pain, erythema, nonunion — next?"** → **vitamin D, TSH, inflammatory labs**

**"Septic nonunion — most accurate lab?"** → **CRP**

**"Atrophic ulna nonunion, normal CRP/ESR?"** → **revision ORIF with autograft**

**"Atrophic radius nonunion with gap — required besides revision + graft?"** → **supportive soft-tissue envelope**

**"Induction coupling effects EXCEPT?"** → **↓ osteoclast differentiation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Normal Bone Metabolism

*Basic Science · 68 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Regulators

**Hormones** — PTH · calcitonin · sex hormones (oestrogen, androgens) · growth hormone · thyroid hormone

**Steroids** — vitamin D · glucocorticoids

### Peak bone mass & oestrogen

**Peak bone mass** — hip **16–25 yr** · greater in **men, African-Americans** · ✓ **timing varies by anatomic site** (appendicular before axial · women earlier at hip, later at spine; spine PBM may be 3rd–4th decade) · strong genetic + environmental influence · **correlates poorly** with postmenopausal BMD

**Bone loss** — **0.3–0.5%/yr** after maturity · **2–3%/yr** in untreated women years 6–10 after menopause

**Most important sex steroid for peak bone mass (men and women)** — ✓ **oestrogen**

**Osteoporosis risk factors** — age · female · early menopause · fair skin · family history of hip fracture · low weight · smoking · glucocorticoids · alcohol · low protein · anticonvulsants, antidepressants

### Calcium & phosphate

| | Calcium | Phosphate |
|---|---|---|
| In bone | **99%** (hydroxyapatite) | **86%** |
| Intracellular | 1% | 14% |
| ECF | 0.1% | 0.08% |
| Serum forms | **45% protein-bound · 45% ionised** · 10% anion-bound | Mostly inorganic H₂PO₄⁻, unbound |
| Regulated by | Gut absorption, bone resorption, renal reabsorption | Renal reabsorption/excretion · ↑ PO₄ → ↑ PTH |
| Daily need | See below | **1000–1500 mg** |

| Group | Calcium (mg/day) |
|---|---|
| **Lactating** | **2000** |
| **Pregnant, postmenopausal, healing fracture** | **1500** |
| Adolescents / young adults | 1300 |
| Adults | 750 |
| Children | 600 |

### Hormones

| Hormone | Source | Net effect | Key mechanisms |
|---|---|---|---|
| **PTH** (84 aa) | **Chief cells** of 4 parathyroids | **↑ Ca, ↓ PO₄** | Bone: osteoblasts → IL-1, IL-6, **RANKL, M-CSF** → osteoclasts (**osteoclasts lack PTH receptor**) · kidney: ↑ **1α-hydroxylase** (25-OH → 1,25-(OH)₂D), ↑ Ca reabsorption, ↑ PO₄ excretion · gut: indirect via 1,25-D · **intermittent = anabolic** |
| **PTHrP** | Tumours | ↑ Ca | Same receptor (osteoblast, kidney) · metaphyseal dysplasia link |
| **Calcitonin** (32 aa) | Thyroid **parafollicular C cells** | Limited role · ↓ osteoclast number + activity | Osteoclasts **have** calcitonin receptor · ↑ in **medullary thyroid Ca / MEN II** · used in Paget's, osteoporosis, malignant hypercalcaemia |
| **Vitamin D** (secosteroid) | Skin UVB + diet | ↑ Ca (gut absorption, osteoclast activation) · ✓ **mineralisation of osteoid** | Liver → **25-OH-D** · kidney → **1,25-(OH)₂D** (↑ by ↑ PTH, ↓ Ca, ↓ PO₄) · or inactive **24,25-(OH)₂D** (↓ PTH, ↑ Ca, ↑ PO₄) · terminal osteoclast differentiation |
| **Oestrogen** | Ovaries | **Prevents bone loss** (↓ resorption + remodelling frequency) | ↑ femoral-neck BMD, ↓ hip fracture |
| **Growth hormone** | Pituitary | ↑ Ca (gut absorption, ↓ urinary loss) | Excess → **gigantism (proliferative zone)** |
| **Thyroid hormone** | Thyroid | Physeal growth (chondrocytes, **type X collagen**, ALP) · ↑ resorption | Excess thyroxine → **osteoporosis** |
| **Glucocorticoids** | — | ↑ bone loss | ↓ gut Ca absorption · ↓ formation (**cancellous > cortical**) — ↓ collagen, ↓ osteoblasts |

**Phenytoin** — impairs vitamin D metabolism

**Vitamin D deficiency** — osteomalacia, rickets

### Therapeutic oestrogen

**Benefit** — ↓ bone loss if started within **5–10 yr of menopause** · BMD gain **2–4%/yr** for first 2 yr

**Risks/effects** — ↑ **heart disease, breast cancer** · ↓ **hip fracture**, ↓ **endometrial cancer** (with cyclic progestin) · ↓ **urinary pyridinoline**, ↓ **serum ALP**

### Anabolic vs antiresorptive

**Anabolic** — ✓ **intermittent PTH / teriparatide** (receptor on osteoblasts, preosteoblasts) · sustained PTH → resorption

**Antiresorptive** — bisphosphonates · oestrogen · SERMs · **denosumab** (RANKL inhibitor) · **calcitonin**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Bone turnover markers (TO)

| Formation (TO) | Resorption (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Bone-specific ALP** | **Urine + serum N-telopeptides** |
| **Osteocalcin** | **Urine + serum C-telopeptides** |
| **P1CP** (carboxy-terminal procollagen-1 propeptide) | Urine **hydroxyproline** |
| Type I collagen extension peptide (P1NP) | ✓ **Urine deoxypyridinoline** · pyridinoline |
| — | Urine hydroxylysine glycosides |
| — | **Serum TRAP**, bone sialoprotein |

## How it's asked

**"Peak bone mass most dependent on which sex steroid?"** → **oestrogen**

**"True of peak bone mass?"** → **timing varies by anatomic site**\
✗ Wrong options: always end of 2nd decade · independent of environment · correlates with postmenopausal BMD

**"Hormone over-secretion affecting proliferative zone (picture)?"** → **gigantism**

**"True of anti-osteoporosis drugs?"** → **intermittent PTH is anabolic**\
✗ Wrong options: calcitonin anabolic · denosumab ↑ osteoclasts · SERMs anabolic

**"NOT a bone formation marker (TO)?"** → **urine deoxypyridinoline**

# Obturator internus

*Basic Science · 69 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Obturator internus |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | Internal surface of obturator membrane + posterior bony margin of obturator foramen |
| **Insertion** | **Medial greater trochanter** (with superior + inferior gemelli) |
| **Action** | External rotation · abducts flexed thigh |
| **Innervation** | **Nerve to obturator internus (L5, S1)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Internal pudendal, superior + inferior gluteal arteries |

![Obturator internus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10075-obturator-internus.jpg)

### Cross-section at the greater trochanters

**Obturator internus** — projects **posteriorly around the ischium** (lesser sciatic notch) → posterior proximal femur **just below piriformis**

| Structure | Position |
|---|---|
| Obturator externus | **Anterior** to ischium |
| Pectineus | Posterior, deep to femoral vessels |
| Adductor longus / brevis | Just appearing anteriorly / behind longus |
| Adductor magnus | More distal (not at this level) |

## How it's asked

**"Pelvic cross-section at greater trochanters — muscle wrapping posteriorly around ischium?"** → **obturator internus**\
✗ Wrong options: obturator externus (anterior)

# Occupational Health

*Basic Science · 70 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Radiation safety (fluoroscopy technique, lead, pregnancy, cataract dose)

| ↑ Radiation exposure | ↓ Radiation exposure |
|---|---|
| Standing **close to emitter** · on the **emitter (tube) side** | **Distance** (inverse square law) · stay >2 m (6 ft) |
| **Larger body part** / large patient (↑ kVp, mA, scatter) | **Smaller part** · **mini C-arm** |
| Limb **close to X-ray source** | Limb/patient **close to image intensifier** (detector) |
| Large C-arm, **continuous fluoro**, cine | Short **beam-on time**, pulsed shots, **surgeon controls pedal** |
| No collimation | ✓ **Collimation** (↓ scatter, better image) |
| **Geometric magnification** (moving intensifier away / tube toward patient) | Electronic magnification only when needed |
| Intraop CT (O-arm) — patient cancer risk ~2× (2.3/10,000) | **Inverted beam** (tube beneath patient, intensifier above) |
| — | Lead apron (≥0.5 mm), thyroid shield (2.5×), **lead glasses ≥0.15 mm**, **sleeves + axillary supplements** |

**Highest scatter with a lateral shot** — ✓ **3 ft from the emitter side** (patient attenuates beam toward collector)

**False statement (TO)** — false: "**image magnified by moving X-ray tube away from patient**" · (geometric magnification = intensifier away → ↑ dose) · large patients need more exposure, mini C-arm less, collimation ↓ scatter **(TO)**

**Lead aprons (TO)** — ↓ exposure 4× (lateral), 16× (PA)

**Distance (TO)** — at 18 inches from beam only **0.1%** of direct dose · scatter <1 mSv outside 2 m · in-beam **1,200–4,000 mrem/min**

### Dose limits

| Site | Annual limit |
|---|---|
| **Whole body (TO)** | **20 mSv** |
| **Thyroid / eyes (TO, ICRP)** | **150 mSv** |
| **Hands (TO)** | **500 mSv** |
| **Lens — cataract threshold (NCRP)** | ✓ **50 mGy** cumulative/yr (1 mSv = 1 mGy in diagnostic imaging) |

**Surgeon cancer risk** — ↑ **breast cancer** (**upper outer quadrant** — commonest site, poorly covered by vests → ✓ **sleeves + axillary supplements**) · lateral images expose breast more · breast as radiosensitive as marrow, colon, lung · **cataracts** (lead glasses)

![Inverted C-arm reduces surgeon exposure](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9082-carm-radiation-figure-moved.jpg)

### Bloodborne pathogen transmission

| Exposure | Risk |
|---|---|
| **HIV needlestick** | ✓ **~0.3%** (↑ with blood volume, viral titre) · PEP with NRTIs protective |
| HIV mucous membrane | ~0.09% |
| HIV frozen bone allograft | **<1 per million** · donor screening key · none since **2001** (this note) |
| HIV blood transfusion | **1 / 500,000 units** (window period) |
| **Hepatitis B needlestick** | **37–62% seroconvert** · 22–31% clinical hepatitis |
| **Hepatitis C needlestick** | **0.5–1.8%** |

### Resident duty hours (ACGME)

**Limits** — **≤80 h/week** (4-week average) · +10% if educationally justified · **1 day off in 7** · in-house call **≤ every 3rd night** · **10 h off** between shifts · includes clinical, academic, admin, call

**Concern** — **continuity of care** (handoffs) · patient safety

**Handoff failures** — omitted current condition, no anticipatory guidance, no rationale, no tasks assigned · **signing out to physician extenders** is **not** cited

### Occupational lung disease (TO)

**Miller's asthma (TO)** — **mill-workers' / grain workers' asthma** · hypersensitivity to **granary weevil** (*Sitophilus granarius*)

**Millar's asthma (TO)** — **spasmodic croup** of **infants** (<2 yr, nocturnal glottic spasm) — TO keys "seen in infants" for this spelling — read the options

## How it's asked

**"NOT true about OT radiation (TO)?"** → **"magnify by moving the X-ray tube away from patient"**

**"Annual limits body / thyroid-eyes / hands (TO)?"** → **20 / 150 / 500 mSv**

**"↑ patient exposure on C-arm?"** → **imaging a larger body part**\
✗ Wrong options: limb near intensifier · collimation · mini C-arm

**"Least surgeon exposure in hand surgery?"** → **thumb against image intensifier, mini C-arm**

**"Best ↓ exposure getting a lateral for distal locking?"** → **image intensifier as close to patient as possible**

**"↑ exposure?"** → **continuous fluoroscopy**

**"Lead garments — true?"** → **sleeves + axillary supplements ↓ breast dose**

**"Commonest breast cancer site?"** → **upper outer quadrant**

**"Pregnant resident — highest scatter on lateral?"** → **3 ft from emitter side**

**"Cataract threshold (NCRP)?"** → **50 mGy/yr**

**"HIV needlestick risk?"** → **0.3%**

**"Sign-out concern EXCEPT?"** → **signing out to physician extenders**

**"Miller's asthma due to / seen in (TO)?"** → **granary weevil / grain workers** · "Millar's asthma seen in" → **infants**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ochronosis

*Basic Science · 71 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Alkaptonuria — homogentisic acid oxidase

**Defect** — ✓ **deficiency of homogentisic acid oxidase** (rare inborn error, tyrosine/phenylalanine pathway) → **homogentisic acid** accumulates in fibrillar collagen

**Ochronosis** — **bluish-black discoloration of cartilage + connective tissue** · acid polymerises in joints → **early degenerative arthritis**

**Tendons** — deposition in **Achilles, patellar tendons** → spontaneous rupture

## 3 Presentation

**Black urine** (homogentisic acid polymerises on standing) · early large-joint OA · back pain

**Ochronotic spondylitis** — 4th decade · progressive degeneration

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Spine** — **disc calcification + disc-space narrowing** (multilevel)

**Knee** — chondrocalcinosis, early OA

![Ochronotic spondylitis — calcified, narrowed discs](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9046-ochronotic-spondylitis.jpg)

### Genes & lab markers

**Diagnosis** — **homogentisic acid in urine**

## 5 Management

**No specific medical treatment** (Orthobullets) · treat as OA: observation, NSAIDs, tramadol, steroids · **early arthroplasty** for severe disease

## X vs Y

| Finding | Disease |
|---|---|
| **Black cartilage, homogentisic acid** | **Alkaptonuria (ochronosis)** |
| Urate crystals | Gout |
| Lead | Plumbism |
| Hepcidin failure → iron overload | Haemochromatosis |
| LDLR mutation | Familial hypercholesterolaemia |
| Flowing anterior ossification | DISH |
| Syndesmophytes, SI fusion | Ankylosing spondylitis |

## How it's asked

**"Back pain, calcified discs, chondrocalcinosis, dark urine?"** → **ochronosis (alkaptonuria)**\
✗ Wrong options: DISH, AS, homocystinuria

**"Black cartilage at TKA — cause?"** → **homogentisic oxidase deficiency**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Operative Blood Loss & Transfusion

*Basic Science · 72 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Risk factors for transfusion

**Strongest predictor** — ✓ **preoperative haemoglobin**

**Others** — female · older age · larger patient · longer surgery · **lateral release in TKA**

## 5 Management

### Preoperative optimisation

| Measure | Facts |
|---|---|
| **Labs** | CBC + **MCV** (microcytic → iron; macrocytic → folate/B12), folate, B12 |
| **Iron / vitamin regimen** | **Iron 256 mg + vit C 1 g + folate 5 mg daily × 30–45 d** → ↓ transfusion (only if deficient) · iron s/e: constipation, reflux, pain |
| **Erythropoietin** | Renal pericapillary cells · > autologous donation efficacy · cost ≈ 4 units blood · high-risk patients · regimens: **600 IU/kg × 4 (days −21, −14, −7, 0)** · 300 IU/kg × 15 · 150 IU/kg × 9 |
| **Preop autologous donation** | 1–2 units **≥3 weeks** before · Hb >11, weight >50 kg (best if Hb >14) · not cost-effective, many units wasted |
| Preop embolisation | Vascular tumours (**renal cell**) within **24 h** of surgery |

### Intraoperative

| Method | Facts |
|---|---|
| **Acute normovolaemic haemodilution** | Blood drawn at surgery, replaced with colloid, reinfused · less planning · rarely used (OR time) |
| **Tourniquet** | SBP + 100–150 mmHg · **≤2 h** · Esmarch (✗ Wrong options: infection, tumour) · caution PVD: arterial insufficiency, absent pedal pulses, popliteal aneurysm, arterial calcification → vascular consult |
| **TXA** | **Lysine analogue** · blocks plasmin(ogen) binding sites on fibrin → **↓ fibrinolysis**, stabilises clot · ↓ blood loss + transfusion (TKA <3%, THA <10%) · **IV = oral = topical** · no ↑ DVT/PE · PT/PTT unchanged · s/e: **seizures** (glycine/GABA) |
| Aminocaproic acid | Lysine analogue, plasmin inhibitor · cheaper · no seizure-threshold effect |
| Collagen (microfibrillar) | Activates **intrinsic** pathway · dry powder, dry field + dry instruments |
| Fibrin sealant | Fibrin + factor XIII + thrombin + Ca²⁺ |
| PRP / platelet-poor plasma | Haemostasis, healing / autologous fibrin sealant with thrombin + Ca |
| **Gelfoam** | Animal-skin gelatin · mechanical for low-pressure bleeders · ± thrombin · **remove excess** (impairs bone healing) |
| **Gelatin–thrombin matrix** (Floseal, Surgiflo) | Porcine/bovine gelatin + human thrombin · **spine epidural bleeding** · end of cascade |
| **Cell saver** | Filter, wash, reinfuse **within 6–8 h** · complications: **coagulopathy** (FDPs, TNF-α, ILs — esp. unwashed), wound drainage, contamination, cost |

**Topical haemostats** — not cost-effective in TJA · useful in spine (near neural tissue)

### TXA dosing & contraindications (TO)

**TXA in TKR (TO)** — ✓ **oral and IV equal efficacy**\
✗ Wrong options: "topical for 2 min better than IV" · ↑ DVT · "IV best"

**Doses (TO)** — IV **10–15 mg/kg or 1 g** (20 min before tourniquet inflation ± 15 min before deflation) · topical **1.5–3 g in 100 mL** for **5 min** before tourniquet release · oral **2 g 2 h preop** + 2 doses postop (cheapest)

**Absolute contraindications (FDA)** — ✓ **current subarachnoid haemorrhage, active intravascular clotting, severe hypersensitivity** only · embolic stroke, DVT, AF, factor V Leiden are **not** absolute

**Relative (OB/TO)** — pregnancy, OCP, seizures, **coronary stent <6 mo**, prior VTE/CVA/MI (TO list) · renal dosing

**Topical TXA** — alternative route if MI, CAD, CVA history

### Postoperative transfusion

**1 unit PRBC** — ↑ Hb **~1 g/dL**

**Threshold** — consider at **Hb <8 g/dL** · no routine transfusion >8 (no ↑ mortality, even cardiac patients) · transfuse if **symptomatic** (orthostasis, fluid-resistant tachycardia)

**Allogeneic blood** — **immunomodulation** → ↓ cell-mediated immunity, ↑ infection

| Platelet count | Action |
|---|---|
| **<5,000** | Transfuse (spontaneous bleeding) |
| **<50,000** | Transfuse **before surgery** |
| <100,000 | Consider before surgery |
| >100,000 | Not needed |

**Platelets** — ✓ **blood product most likely contaminated** (stored at **room temperature**) · 10/million units · **gram-positive** commonest

**Mild postop anaemia** — oral iron

### Jehovah's Witness

**Allogeneic blood** — forbidden (some individuals consent)

**Acceptable** — ✓ **continuous closed-loop cell salvage** · colloids, coagulation factors (personal choice) · **bovine-derived haemostats** generally OK · human-plasma products individual decision\
✗ Wrong options: **batch-processed** (breaks continuity)

**Profound intraop haemorrhage** — ✓ **continuous closed-loop cell saver**\
✗ Wrong options: TXA infusion · EPO (too slow)

## How it's asked

**"True about TXA in TKR (TO)?"** → **oral and IV equal efficacy**

**"TXA true EXCEPT?"** → **"increases fibrinolysis"**

**"Absolute contraindication to TXA in THA?"** → **none of these** (embolic stroke, DVT, AF, FVL)

**"Jehovah's Witness, profound intraop bleeding?"** → **continuous closed-loop cell salvage**

**"Blood product most likely contaminated?"** → **platelets**

**"Strongest predictor of postop transfusion?"** → **preoperative haemoglobin**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Opiates & Analgesic Medications

*Basic Science · 73 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Pain physiology

**Definitions** — acute (tissue damage) · chronic (3–6 months, no damage needed) · pathological (neuropathic)

**Pathway** — nociceptors (**substance P** — depletion ↑ pain threshold; **capsaicin** depletes it) → **A and C fibres** → dorsal column + spinothalamic tract → thalamus · brainstem = endogenous opioid modulation

### Analgesic mechanisms (bupivacaine, gabapentin, acetaminophen)

| Drug | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Acetaminophen** | Central · ✓ **inhibits PGE2 production via IL-1β** · minimal anti-inflammatory · = aspirin for pain · **hepatotoxic** (avoid in liver disease) |
| NSAIDs | ↓ pain transduction (COX) |
| **Local anaesthetics (lidocaine, bupivacaine)** | ✓ **Block intracellular portion of voltage-gated Na⁺ channel** → no depolarisation |
| **Gabapentin / pregabalin** | ✓ **Bind α2δ subunit of presynaptic voltage-gated Ca²⁺ channels** → ↓ excitatory neurotransmitter release, ↑ GABA · effective: postherpetic neuralgia, diabetic neuropathy, fibromyalgia · gabapentin = pregabalin, cheaper · **taper** to stop |
| Opioids (fentanyl) | **μ** (μ1 supraspinal analgesia, μ2 respiratory depression/dependence) + **κ** (sedation, spinal analgesia) |
| **Tramadol** | μ-opioid agonist |
| Midazolam | ↑ GABA-A Cl⁻ influx → hyperpolarisation |
| Rocuronium | Competitive ACh-receptor block |
| Phentolamine | α-adrenergic blocker |
| Cyclobenzaprine | Central · ↑ noradrenaline, serotonin receptors · helps first 2 weeks · not for neuromuscular spasticity · interacts with MAOIs, alcohol |

| Local anaesthetic | Duration (± adrenaline) | Max dose (± adrenaline) |
|---|---|---|
| **Lidocaine** (amide) | 2 h (4 h) | **4.5 mg/kg (7 mg/kg)** |
| **Bupivacaine** (amide) | 4 h (8 h) | **2.5 mg/kg (3 mg/kg)** |

**Amides** — lidocaine, bupivacaine (liver metabolism, renal excretion) · **esters** — procaine, cocaine, benzocaine

### Opioid metabolism

**CYP2D6 activates** — oxycodone, hydrocodone, tramadol, **codeine** · **SSRIs/SNRIs block CYP2D6 → analgesic failure**

**Morphine milligram equivalents** — **hydrocodone 1 · oxycodone 1.5 · hydromorphone 4** (3–7× morphine) → ✓ **hydromorphone > 2× oxycodone**

**Methadone** — synthetic, 3× morphine bioavailability, cheap · CYP450 · distribution 2–3 h, **elimination 15–60 h** → accumulates · **respiratory depression, torsades** → pain specialist

## 5 Management

### Special populations

**Morbidly obese** — ✓ **IV PCA dosed on ideal body weight**\
✗ Wrong options: actual weight · IM/SC (poor fat vascularity)

**Red hair (MC1R mutation)** — ✓ **need more anaesthetic (MAC) + higher postoperative opioid doses** (esp. women)

**Elderly** — ↑ opioid sensitivity (↑ fat %, ↓ hepatic/renal function)

**Children** — AAP dosing guidelines

### Opioid prescribing & consumption

**Bony > soft-tissue procedures** — ORIF distal radius uses **more** than cubital tunnel release

**Upper limb consumption** — 75% need ≤15 pills · 45% <5 · reasonable: **15 pills + 1 refill** (3–4% use it) · ✓ **>50% of prescribed pills unused**

**Carpal tunnel release** — ✓ **ibuprofen 600 mg ≈ oxycodone 5 mg** pain scores · most use <10 tablets for ≤2 days · opioids → more adverse events · open = endoscopic · standardised protocol ↓ prescribing

| Hand prescribing guide | Tablets |
|---|---|
| Trigger finger, lumps/bumps | 0 |
| Mucous cyst, CTR, de Quervain, Dupuytren | 10 |
| Wrist ganglion, hand ORIF, LRTI, tendon transfer | 20 |
| Large trauma, wrist fusion, DRUJ reconstruction | 40 |

**Chronic opioids** — pain specialist, written contract, in-person refills · for OA → ↑ surgical complications

**Weaning opioids 50% before THA** — ✓ **better outcome than not weaning, similar to opioid-naïve**

## 6 Complications

### Pain-pump chondrolysis

**Continuous intra-articular local anaesthetic infusion** — **FDA warning** · **glenohumeral chondrolysis** (onset ≥2 months, mean diagnosis 8.5 months) · pain, stiffness, ↓ ROM after shoulder scope → ✓ **shoulder radiographs to assess chondrolysis**

## How it's asked

**"Morbidly obese postop analgesia?"** → **IV PCA by ideal body weight**

**"Distal radius ORIF vs cubital tunnel release — opioid use?"** → **ORIF patient uses more**

**"Opioid user weans 50% before THA?"** → **better than not weaning, similar to opioid-naïve**

**"True about CTR pain management?"** → **over half of prescribed pills unused**

**"CTR analgesia — most accurate?"** → **ibuprofen 600 mg ≈ oxycodone 5 mg**

**"MME comparison?"** → **hydromorphone > 2× oxycodone**

**"Binds marked site on voltage-gated Na channel?"** → **bupivacaine**

**"Binds presynaptic Ca channels?"** → **gabapentin**

**"Inhibits PGE2 via IL-1β?"** → **acetaminophen**

**"Red-haired patient — postoperative implication?"** → **higher analgesic doses**

**"Pain + stiffness 3 mo after Bankart with intra-articular pump?"** → **radiographs for chondrolysis**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Orthopaedic Implants

*Basic Science · 74 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Determinants

**Implant behaviour** — **material properties** (modulus, strength) + **structural properties** (shape, size, moment of inertia)

### Screws

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Pitch | Distance between threads |
| Lead | Distance advanced per revolution |
| **Working length (distance) of a screw** | ✓ **Length of bone traversed by the screw** |
| Root / core diameter | Narrowest diameter |
| **Bending strength** | ∝ **inner (minor) diameter³** |
| **Pull-out strength** | ∝ **outer (major) diameter²** · ↑ with large major–minor difference, **fine pitch** |

**Pull-out in osteoporotic bone ↑ by** — cortical purchase · placement **parallel to trabeculae** · **locking (fixed-angle)** construct · **PMMA augmentation** \
✗ Wrong options: **tapping** (↓ pull-out)

**Pedicle screw pull-out** — most affected by **bone quality** · pedicle screws need the **largest force** to disrupt bone–implant interface (vs sublaminar cables, laminar/pedicle hooks)

![Pull-out vs bending strength vs minor diameter](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9063-relationship-between-pullout-strength-bending-strength-and-c.jpg)

### Plate biomechanics (compression, bridging, working length, stress shielding)

**Load-bearing device** — best on **tension side** · bending rigidity ∝ **thickness³** · titanium modulus closest to cortical bone

**Plate working distance** — ✓ **distance between the two screws closest to the fracture on either side** · ↓ working distance → ↑ stiffness

| Plate function | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Compression (DCP)** | ✓ **Cortical screw placed eccentrically (away from fracture) in oval hole** → head slides to centre → compression · transverse fractures |
| **Concave pre-bend** | Compresses **near and far cortices** (transverse fracture) |
| **Neutralisation / protection** | With lag screws — resists shear, rotation (absolute stability) |
| **Buttress / antiglide** | Resists shear |
| Tension band | Converts tension to compression |
| **Bridging** | Spans comminution, screws far from fracture · **relative stability**, length + alignment · preserves blood supply · **flexible but axially stable** (long working length) · indirect reduction (distraction) · locking not required |

| Stability | Healing | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| **Absolute** | Primary (Haversian/intramembranous) | **Lag screw ± neutralisation / compression plate** · **low strain (<2%), high stiffness** · no micromotion |
| **Relative** | Secondary (endochondral) | Strain **<10%** (else fibrous union) · nail, bridge plate, ex-fix, cast |

**Strain** — residual 2 mm gap after lag screws (simple oblique) → **greater interfragmentary strain** than bridged comminuted fracture (strain shared)

**Open vs submuscular plating** — open plating ✓ **compromises periosteal and medullary blood flow more**

**LC-DCP vs DCP** — ✓ **less contact-induced osteopenia** (less plate–bone contact)

**Stress shielding** — ✓ **↓ physiological stress in bone because a stiffer structure shares load** → resorption (Wolff)

**Locked bridge plate** — biomechanically ≈ **external fixator without compression** ("internal fixator")

### Locking plates (indications, hybrid, unicortical, stiffness)

**Indications** — indirect reduction · **osteoporotic** diaphyseal/metaphyseal fractures · bridging severe comminution · **short periarticular segments** · can't plate tension side · periprosthetic · proximal humerus, distal radius, distal femur, proximal tibia

**Not indicated** — **compression plating of transverse fractures** · simple transverse **both-bone forearm** · oligotrophic diaphyseal nonunion · lateral malleolus (least evidence)

**Advantages** — less angulation in comminuted metaphyseal fractures · **axial load borne by the plate** (no plate–bone friction needed) · preserves periosteum · ↓ screw toggle

| ↑ Locked construct stiffness | ↓ Stiffness |
|---|---|
| **Bicortical locking screws** (vs unicortical — biggest difference in **torsion**) | Unicortical screws |
| **More screws** | **Far cortical locking screws** (flexible shaft, unthreaded near cortex) |
| **Longer plate** | Overdrilling near cortex |
| Screw divergence from hole **<5°** | Angular cross-threading |
| Screws **closer to fracture** (short working length) | Screws far from fracture |
| Stainless steel > titanium · supplemental medial plate | — |

**Unicortical locked vs bicortical non-locked** — ✓ **less torsional, greater axial** strength

**Locked vs non-locked rigid construct** — ✓ **↓ interfragmentary strain** (stiffer)

**Hybrid plating** — **non-locking screws first** (lag plate to bone = reduction aid) → then **locking screws** (fixed angle, resist **varus collapse**; osteoporotic diaphysis)

**Closed MIPO locked articular fracture** — risk ✓ **non-anatomic articular reduction** (articular surface must be reduced under vision) · percutaneous → less stripping, ↑ malunion

## 2 Mechanism

### Intramedullary nails (rigidity, curvature, dynamisation)

**Load-sharing** device

| Property | Proportional to |
|---|---|
| **Torsional rigidity** | **radius⁴** (shear modulus, polar moment) · ↑ by reaming (larger nail) · ↓ by **slotting** |
| **Bending rigidity — solid nail** | **radius⁴** |
| Bending rigidity — hollow nail | ~**radius³** |

**6 mm vs 12 mm solid nail** — ✓ **1/16 torsional rigidity** (½⁴)

**Radius of curvature** — ✓ **nail ROC > femur ROC** (straighter) → **anterior distal cortical perforation** risk · closed-section stiffer than slotted · start point affects varus/valgus · healing mostly endochondral

**Locking** — dynamic (axially + rotationally stable) · static (unstable) · **secondary dynamisation**: remove proximal screw or move it into the **dynamic (long) slot** — hole A

### External fixator stiffness

| ↑ Stability (unilateral) | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Fracture-end contact** | Most important overall |
| ✓ **Larger pin diameter** | **Greatest single factor** — stiffness ∝ r⁴ (2× diameter → **16×**) |
| More pins | — |
| ✓ **↓ Bone–rod distance** | Quick, safe fix (✗ Wrong options: oversizing small-bone pins (fracture risk)) |
| Pins in different planes · multiplanar | — |
| Larger / stacked rods · rods in different planes | — |
| **↑ Spacing between pins** in each fragment · near pin close to fracture | — |
| Bicortical pins · HA-coated | — |

| Circular (Ilizarov) — ↑ stability | |
|---|---|
| Larger wires · olive wires · extra wires | — |
| **Smaller ring diameter** · more rings · 2 central rings near fracture | — |
| Wires crossing at **90°** · **↑ tension** | ✓ **Tensioned wires → more axial compression, less transverse shear** than half pins |

## 5 Management

### Total hip implants

**Stem rigidity** — length + radius

**Femoral component** — neutral or slight **valgus** (↓ moment arm, ↓ cement stress)

**↑ Femoral offset** — ✓ ↑ abductor moment arm, ↓ joint reaction force, ↓ abductor force needed\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ implant strain + medial cement-mantle strain

### Additive manufacturing (TO)

| Technology **(TO)** | Material | Use |
|---|---|---|
| **FDM** | PLA, ABS, PEEK filament | Anatomical models, splints · **PLA Tg ~60 °C → warps in autoclave** |
| **SLA** | Photopolymer resin (UV) | Highest resolution · models, autoclavable PSI (HDT >135 °C resins) |
| **SLS** | Polyamide (nylon), metal powders | ✓ **Autoclavable PSI cutting guides** (HDT >135 °C) |
| **SLM / DMLS** | **Ti-6Al-4V**, Co-Cr (full melt) | Custom implants, cages, tumour prostheses · ✓ **gradient porosity → ↓ effective modulus → ↓ stress shielding** |
| **EBM** | Titanium · **vacuum, >700 °C** | ✓ **Porous acetabular cups / cones (pores 400–600 μm)** · in-situ annealing ↓ residual stress |
| Bioprinting | Cells in bioink | Experimental |

**AM limits (TO)** — doesn't eliminate fatigue (micro-voids → need HIP/polishing) · introduces build-axis anisotropy · no intrinsic anti-biofilm property

**Solid titanium modulus (TO)** — ~110 GPa vs cortical 10–20, cancellous <3 GPa

## How it's asked

**"Open vs submuscular plating — blood flow?"** → **open compromises periosteal + medullary more**

**"LC-DCP advantage?"** → **less contact-induced osteopenia**

**"Distance of bone traversed by a screw?"** → **working length**

**"How DCP compresses?"** → **eccentric cortical screw in oval hole**

**"Lag screws with 2 mm gap vs bridge plate?"** → **greater interfragmentary strain**

**"Ulna plating technique in figure?"** → **bridging**

**"Stress shielding?"** → **↓ bone stress due to stiffer structure sharing load**

**"Absolute stability construct?"** → **lag screws + neutralisation plate (clavicle)**

**"Low strain + high stiffness humerus?"** → **lag screw + locked neutralisation plate**

**"Compression at near + far cortices?"** → **concave-bent compression plate**

**"Locked bridge plate ≈?"** → **external fixator without compression**

**"Plate working distance?"** → **between screws closest to the fracture**

**"Bridge plating true?"** → **long working length → flexible, axially stable**

**"MC plate in figure acting as?"** → **bridge plate**

**"MIPO healing due to?"** → **flexible and stable construct**

**"Bridging plate image?"** → **C**

**"Fibula: compression + shear/rotation protection?"** → **lag screw + neutralisation plate (E)**

**"↑ locking construct stiffness?"** → **more screws**\
✗ Wrong options: far cortical, unicortical, overdrilling

**"Locking plate indications EXCEPT?"** → **compression plating of transverse fracture**

**"Hybrid plate — screw A vs B?"** → **A locked (fixed angle), B non-locked (reduces plate to bone)**

**"No relative indication for locking?"** → **oligotrophic diaphyseal nonunion**

**"Closed locked-only distal femur articular fracture — risk?"** → **non-anatomic articular reduction**

**"Least evidence for locking?"** → **lateral malleolus (E)**

**"Biggest unicortical vs bicortical locking difference?"** → **torsion**

**"Unicortical locked vs bicortical unlocked?"** → **less torsional, greater axial strength**

**"Locked vs non-locked?"** → **↓ interfragmentary strain**

**"Least indicated for locking?"** → **transverse both-bone forearm**

**"Hybrid plating advantage?"** → **non-locked first as reduction aid, locked resist varus**

**"Axial forces in locked construct borne by?"** → **plate**

**"Locking plates true?"** → **stiffness ↑ with plate length**

**"↓ stiffness of distal femur locked plate?"** → **far cortical locking screws**

**"Most stiffness added (periprosthetic)?"** → **bicortical locking screw at B (near fracture)**

**"↑ ex-fix strength?"** → **↓ bone–rod distance**

**"Greatest impact on unilateral frame stiffness?"** → **pin diameter**

**"Pin twice the diameter — bending stiffness?"** → **16×**

**"Strongest influence on ex-fix stiffness?"** → **pin diameter**

**"Tensioned fine wires vs half pins?"** → **↑ axial compression, ↓ transverse shear**

**"Distal radius ex-fix losing reduction — next?"** → **move rods closer to bone**\
✗ Wrong options: 6 mm MC pins

**"6 mm vs 12 mm solid nail torsional rigidity?"** → **1/16**

**"Nail planning — true?"** → **nail ROC > femur ROC**

**"Dynamise tibial nail — hole?"** → **A (dynamic slot)**

**"Pull-out in osteoporotic bone ↑ by all EXCEPT?"** → **tapping**

**"Spinal fixation needing largest force to disrupt?"** → **pedicle screws**

**"Modulus closest to cortical bone?"** → **titanium**

**"Porous Ti cup manufacture minimising thermal stress (TO)?"** → **EBM Ti-6Al-4V, 400–600 μm lattice**

**"PSI guide surviving autoclave (TO)?"** → **SLS/SLA nylon or resin with HDT >135 °C**

**"DMLS/SLM advantage over CNC (TO)?"** → **gradient porosity ↓ modulus → ↓ stress shielding**
