# Osteopenia & Osteoporosis

*Basic Science · 75 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Prevalence** — **10 million** Americans · **200 million** worldwide · 34 million US osteopenia

**Fragility fractures / yr** — **1.5 million** → 700,000 vertebral · 300,000 hip · 200,000 wrist

**Commonest fragility fracture** — ✓ **vertebral body > peritrochanteric (hip) > distal radius**\
✗ Wrong options: "distal radius more common than VCF"

**Peak age** — wrist 50–60 · vertebral 60–70 · hip 70–80

**M:F** — **1:4** · men → **secondary osteoporosis in 60%** (hypogonadism, glucocorticoid excess, alcoholism)

**Bone mass** — peak bone mass attained **before 30 yr** · failure to attain it = major cause of osteoporosis

**Ageing** — ✓ **trabecular (cancellous) bone loses strength more than cortical** · cancellous ↓ **8.5%/decade** vs cortical **5%/decade** · volume fraction (apparent density) explains most of the loss

### Pathophysiology

**Osteoporosis** — ✓ **quantitative** defect · **↓ bone quantity, normal bone quality (mineralisation)**\
✗ Wrong options: qualitative (= osteomalacia)

**Causes** — failure to build peak mass · bone loss later in life · ↓ loading (non-weight-bearing)

**Histology** — **thinned trabeculae** · **↓ osteon size** · enlarged Haversian + marrow spaces

![Osteoporotic bone — thinned trabeculae](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9032-histo-1.jpg)

| Osteoclast ruffled border | Agents |
|---|---|
| **↑ (activated)** | **PTH** · **1,25(OH)₂ vit D₃** · **prostaglandin E** |
| **Flattened** | **Bisphosphonates** · **calcitonin** |

### Disuse osteopenia — Wolff's law

**Wolff's law** — ✓ bone densifies under repetitive load, ↓ density with ↓ load → **disuse osteopenia** after non-weight-bearing (e.g. bilateral calcaneus fractures) · dense calcar = load path

| ✗ Wrong option | What it actually describes |
|---|---|
| Hydraulic stiffening | Tissue fluid stiffens skeleton → ↑ strength |
| Hooke's law | Stress–strain of a solid |
| Hueter-Volkmann law | Compression inhibits physeal growth |
| Delpech's law | Growth across physis |

### Genes & lab markers

**Gene polymorphisms** — **calcitonin receptor** · **estrogen receptor-1** · **vitamin D receptor** · **COL1A1** (+ >45 others) · not **COMP** (= multiple epiphyseal dysplasia) · no single cause

**Labs** — generally **normal** in osteoporosis · **↓ 25-OH vitamin D** = most likely abnormal lab after low-energy fracture · other labs exclude secondary causes\
✗ Wrong options: hydroxyproline, PTHrP, Sox-9, LRP5

**Vitamin D** — D₃ → 25-OH D (liver) → **1,25-(OH)₂ D** (active) or 24,25-(OH)₂ D (inactive) in kidney · deficient **<20 ng/mL** · not diagnostic of osteoporosis

**Vitamin D replacement (TO)** — ✓ **↑ 25-OH D and ↑ 1,25-(OH)₂ D, ↓ sclerostin** **(TO)** · osteocytes have 1,25-D receptors → ↓ sclerostin synthesis → ↑ bone formation **(TO)**

**Bone remodelling (TO)** — peak mass at **30 yr** · resorption ~**10 days**, formation ~**3 months** · up to **20%** of skeleton remodelled/yr **(TO)**

| Bone turnover markers (TO) | Examples |
|---|---|
| **Formation** (osteoblast) | **P1NP / P1CP** (type 1 collagen propeptides) · **ALP** (total, bone-specific) · **osteocalcin** |
| **Resorption** (osteoclast) | **CTX-1, NTX-1** (telopeptides) · hydroxyproline · pyridinoline / deoxypyridinoline · bone sialoprotein · **TRAP** · **cathepsin K** |
| Osteocyte activity | RANKL · OPG · Dickkopf-1 · sclerostin |
| **Reference markers (IOF)** | ✓ **serum CTX-1** (resorption) + **serum P1NP** (formation) **(TO)** |

**BTM uses (TO)** — fracture risk (↑ BTM ∝ risk, independent of BMD) · **monitor compliance/response** — resorption markers **fall >40% within 3 mo** of bisphosphonate, formation markers fall over 6–12 mo · denosumab → very rapid fall in resorption markers · guide restart after drug holiday · **circadian variation** — time assays **(TO)**

**Response thresholds (TO)** — **>30–40% ↓ CTX** (antiresorptive) or **>30% ↑ P1NP** (anabolic) at 3–6 mo = response **(TO)**

## 3 Presentation

**Risk factors for osteoporosis** — increasing age · postmenopausal · long-term ↓ calcium · steroids · alcohol · smoking · low BMI

| Drugs ↑ osteoporosis risk | Not associated |
|---|---|
| **Oral glucocorticoids** · androgen deprivation · aromatase inhibitors · **protease inhibitors** · **SSRIs** · prolactin-raising **antiepileptics (phenytoin)** · cytotoxics | ✓ **NSAIDs** · cephalexin · simvastatin · glipizide · allopurinol |

**Phenytoin mechanism** — **hepatic CYP450 induction → ↑ vit D catabolism** · direct osteoblast inhibition · ↓ Ca absorption · ↑ homocysteine · PTH resistance · ↓ sex hormones · ↓ vit K

**Disease associations** — hyperparathyroidism · thyrotoxicosis · type I DM · hypogonadism · malnutrition / malabsorption · immobilisation · **RA** · alcoholism · vit D deficiency · multiple myeloma

**Pelvic ring insufficiency fracture** — risk: **osteoporosis, corticosteroids, RA, THA with constrained liner** (redistributes force to pelvis) · not **ceramic bearings** · groin pain in elderly · treat bed rest + analgesia

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — thinned cortices · loss of trabecular bone · kyphosis · **codfish vertebra** · views: lateral spine, AP pelvis/hip

**X-ray sensitivity** — ✓ loss not apparent until **30–40%** (Orthobullets/OB5227) · **40%** (OB8542) — read the options · normal X-ray does not exclude osteopenia

**DEXA** — ✓ **most accurate, least radiation** · **lumbar L2–L4** + hip (neck, trochanter, intertrochanter) · measures **axial AND appendicular** skeleton\
✗ Wrong options: "axial only"

**Other densitometry** — **SPA** reliable in appendicular only (spine unreliable) · **QCT ~10× DEXA radiation** · conventional **MRI** ≈ QCT accuracy without radiation · **QCT** also used to monitor therapy (cost/radiation limit use) **(TO)**

**After low-energy fracture** — ✓ **DEXA** (✗ Wrong options: MRI pelvis, urine electrophoresis, CT, bone scan) · old multilevel VCFs on MRI, no acute pain → ✓ **DEXA** then medical Rx (✗ Wrong options: brace, cement, fusion)

![DEXA report — postmenopausal woman](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9032-dexa-scan-report-for-post-menopausal-white-female.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria (WHO 1994)

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **T-score** | BMD vs **young normal controls** (peak bone mass, 25–30-yr-old same sex/ethnicity) |
| **Z-score** | BMD vs **age-, sex-, race-matched** controls |
| **Normal** | **T ≥ −1.0** (e.g. T −0.7 = normal) |
| **Osteopenia** | **T −1.0 to −2.5** |
| **Osteoporosis** | **T ≤ −2.5** (e.g. −2.6, −3.5 = osteoporosis regardless of other factors) |

**Which score** — **T-score for postmenopausal women** (lowest of spine, femoral neck, trochanter, total hip; not peripheral sites) · **Z-score for premenopausal women, young men, children** → metabolic bone disease in young \
✗ Wrong options: osteopenia/osteoporosis "by Z-score"

**WHO limits** — no data to define criteria for men or other ethnic groups · T-score tells nothing about metastases

**Vit D level** — low but not diagnostic · DEXA remains the diagnostic test

**Biopsy** — after **tetracycline labelling** · rules out osteomalacia

### Type I vs Type II

| | **Type I (postmenopausal)** | **Type II (senile)** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | 50–70 | >70 |
| Bone | Almost exclusively **trabecular** | Trabecular > cortical |
| Fractures | **Distal radius, vertebral** | **Hip, pelvis** |
| Calcium | Net negative (↓ gut absorption, ↑ urinary loss) | Poor absorption |
| Vit D | ↓ total (not free) 1,25-D | — |

## 5 Management

### Risk factors & fracture prediction

**Strongest predictor of future fragility fracture** — ✓ **prior fragility fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: T < −2.5, low vit D, family history, prednisone

**Future vertebral fracture** — ✓ **prior vertebral fracture** (~**4×**; risk ↑ with number) > prior hip/wrist · **2 previous VCFs = strongest predictor** in postmenopausal women\
✗ Wrong options: T −2.8, hip fracture in 6 mo, age >75, bisphosphonate

**After 1 VCF** — **5× 2nd vertebral** · **5× hip** · **15% ↑ 5-yr mortality** · morbidity: back pain, height loss, balance, restrictive lung disease, pneumonia

**After hip fracture** — **20% ↑ mortality** · **men > women** · only **1/3** regain prior function · up to **10× 2nd hip fracture**

**Baseline risk** — 65-yr-old white woman, no risk factors → **10% 10-yr fragility fracture risk**

**FRAX** — ✓ **Fracture Risk Assessment tool** · WHO **2008** (Kanis, Sheffield) · **10-yr probability** of **hip** and **major osteoporotic fracture** (proximal humerus, wrist, hip, clinical vertebral) · with/without **femoral neck BMD** **(TO)**

**FRAX inputs** — age · sex · race · height · weight · BMI · prior fragility fracture · **parental hip fracture** · oral glucocorticoids · **RA** · secondary osteoporosis · current smoking · alcohol **≥3 units/day** **(TO)**

**FRAX does not include** — falls risk · **bone turnover markers** · dietary calcium · vitamin D deficiency **(TO)** · developed because **~50%** of fragility fractures occur with BMD above osteoporosis threshold **(TO)**

**Monitoring response to treatment (TO)** — ✓ **DEXA** (guideline tool) · **QCT** (reliable, costly) · **BTMs**\
✗ Wrong options: **FRAX — least useful for assessing adequacy of therapy** **(TO)**

**DXA monitoring (TO)** — every **2 yr** (1 yr on anabolics) · BMD ↓ **>5% spine or >4% hip** (beyond 1–2% precision error) = failure → look for secondary causes · **new fragility fracture on treatment = failure** regardless of BMD **(TO)**

![FRAX tool](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9032-frax-tool.jpg)

**Screening (USPSTF / OB)** — **women ≥65** + younger women with risk ≥ 65-yr-old white woman · BMD testing also postmenopausal women with ≥1 risk factor · men ≥70 (OB922) · evidence insufficient for men (USPSTF)

### Operative criteria — when to start pharmacological treatment (NOF 2008)

| Postmenopausal women / men ≥50 with ANY of | Treat? |
|---|---|
| **Hip or vertebral fracture** (even without DEXA) | Yes |
| **T ≤ −2.5** femoral neck / spine | Yes |
| **T −1.0 to −2.5** AND **10-yr hip ≥3%** | Yes |
| **T −1.0 to −2.5** AND **10-yr major ≥20%** | Yes |
| **Major ≥10%** | not a criterion **(TO)** |
| FRAX hip ≥3% **without** documented osteopenia | No |
| T −2.0, hip 1.5%, major 8.9% | **no antiresorptive** |
| T −1.5, hip **4.8%**, major 8% (smoker on phenytoin) | ✓ **Ca 1200 + vit D 800 + bisphosphonate + neuro review of phenytoin + repeat DEXA 1 yr** |

```
Fragility fracture / low BMD
├─ Hip or vertebral fragility fracture → TREAT
├─ T ≤ −2.5 → TREAT
├─ T −1.0 to −2.5
│      ├─ FRAX hip ≥3% or major ≥20% → TREAT
│      └─ below both → CALCIUM + VIT D, NO ANTIRESORPTIVE
└─ T ≥ −1.0 → NORMAL
```

### Calcium & vitamin D

| Age | Daily calcium |
|---|---|
| 1–3 yr | 500 mg |
| 4–8 yr | 800 mg |
| **9–18 yr** (e.g. 13-yr-old) | **1000–1500 mg** |
| **Adults >50 / osteoporosis** | **1200–1500 mg** |

**Vitamin D** — **800–1000 IU/day** (NOF, >50 yr) · prevention dose **400–800 IU** (OB418/OB6265) · on 1200 mg Ca alone after DRF → ✓ **add 800–1000 IU vit D**\
✗ Wrong options: 1600–2000 IU · ↑ Ca to 2000 · calcitonin

**Healthy 52–55-yr-old woman** — ✓ **1000–1500 mg Ca + 400–800 IU vit D** · ✓ 1500 mg + 1000 IU\
✗ Wrong options: semiannual BMD · estrogen (HRT not approved for treatment — OB6265)\
✗ Wrong options: 2200 mg · 5000–10,000 IU

**Premenopausal, T −2.2, DRF** — ✓ **1200 mg calcium** (prevention dose, not enough for T < −2.5)

**Effects** — Ca ↓ fracture risk **34%** · vit D ↓ hip 10%, non-vertebral 7% · high-dose (~800 IU) ↓ hip **24%**, non-vertebral **30%** · best for **type II (senile)**

### Osteoporosis drugs

| Drug | Mechanism | Key facts |
|---|---|---|
| **Non-N bisphosphonates** (etidronate, clodronate, tiludronate) | **Toxic ATP analogue** → osteoclast apoptosis | Esophagitis, ulcers, ONJ, atypical fractures |
| **N-bisphosphonates** (pamidronate, alendronate, risedronate, zoledronate, ibandronate) | **Inhibit farnesyl pyrophosphate synthase** (mevalonate) | **1st line** · renal excretion, unmetabolised · bind hydroxyapatite → detectable **years** later |
| **HRT** (estrogen-progestin) | — | Type I within 6 yr of menopause · ↓ hip fracture but ↑ breast ca, CAD/MI, stroke, PE/DVT, Alzheimer's |
| **Estrogen only** | ER on osteoblasts + osteoclasts | **Prior hysterectomy only** · unopposed → endometrial hyperplasia / uterine ca |
| **Testosterone** | — | Hypogonadal men · not FDA-approved |
| **Salmon calcitonin** | **Binds osteoclast receptors** → ↓ resorption (from thyroid **parafollicular cells**) | Women **>5 yr postmenopause** · ✓ **↓ pain of acute VCF** (neurotransmitter) · nasal/injection (gastric acid destroys) · rhinitis; nausea, flushing |
| **Raloxifene** (SERM) | **ER agonist bone, antagonist breast** (competitive partial agonist) | **Vertebral fractures only** · not anabolic · hot flashes, leg cramps · **CI: VTE** |
| **Teriparatide** | **PTH 1–34**, intermittent → osteoblasts | **Anabolic** · daily SC · transient ↑ Ca |
| **Denosumab** | **Anti-RANKL** IgG2 (like OPG) | 68/40/20% · **CI severe hypocalcaemia** |
| **Romosozumab** | **Anti-sclerostin** → Wnt ↑ | ↓ vertebral **73%** at 12 mo, **75%** at 24 mo · **CV events** |
| Strontium ranelate | Dual action (↑ formation, ↓ resorption) | not FDA-approved · ↑ MI |

**FDA-approved for active treatment (OB418)** — alendronate, risedronate, raloxifene, estrogen, calcitonin → ↓ fragility fractures up to **50%**

### Bisphosphonates (indications, atypical fractures, ONJ, drug holiday)

**Mechanism** — incorporate at remodelling sites → released in acid resorption pit → taken up by osteoclasts → **flattened ruffled border**, ↓ function + **survival** of osteoclasts\
✗ Wrong options: formation

| Agent | Fracture reduction |
|---|---|
| **Alendronate** | Hip, spine, wrist **~50%** · vertebral **48%**, non-vertebral **47%** · 70 mg weekly or 10 mg daily |
| **Risedronate** | Vertebral **65%** / non-vertebral **39%** (table) · **40%** each over 3 yr (text) |
| **Ibandronate** | Vertebral **77%**, hip 41%, non-vertebral 15% (Orthobullets) · ✓ **failed to significantly ↓ hip / non-vertebral fracture in BONE trial** **(TO)** — read the options |
| **Zoledronic acid** (IV annual) | Spine **70%**, hip **40%** over 3 yr · ✓ **only bisphosphonate ↓ all-cause mortality** — **HORIZON-RFT**, infusion within **90 days** of hip fracture repair **(TO)** |

**Fragility VCF / hip fracture** — ✓ start antiosteoporotic medication regardless of DEXA → ✓ **inhibit farnesyl pyrophosphate synthase**\
✗ Wrong options: calcitonin receptor inhibition · RANK activation · ER inhibition · continuous PTH

**Atypical femoral fracture (AFF)** — long-term BP → **extremely low turnover** (↓ urinary **NTx**) → microdamage unrepaired in lateral tension cortex

| AFF radiographic features |
|---|
| **Focal lateral cortical thickening** (beaking) |
| **Transverse** orientation |
| **Medial spike** |
| **Simple, non-comminuted** pattern |
| Subtrochanteric / diaphyseal (✗ Wrong options: distal femur) |

![Atypical subtrochanteric fracture — transverse, lateral beak, medial spike](../../MCQ/attachments/q5684-e-subtroch.jpg)

**ONJ** — **IV bisphosphonates** (✗ Wrong options: oral) · also **denosumab** · rare · stop inciting drug · long-term IV zoledronate for Paget → ✓ **osteonecrosis of mandible** (✗ Wrong options: sarcoma (teriparatide risk))

**Long-term alendronate** — ✓ **diaphyseal femoral insufficiency fractures** · ONJ only jaw\
✗ Wrong options: femoral head AVN · retinal detachment · uterine ca · scurvy

**Drug holiday** — not in **high risk / declining BMD / severe osteoporosis / chronic steroids** in first **10 yr** → continue (e.g. 8 yr BP, steroids, T −2.6) · after 10 yr → holiday **1–2 yr** or until BMD ↓ / fracture (OB)

**Drug holiday (TO)** — alendronate skeletal half-life **~10 yr** · reassess after **3–5 yr oral or 3 yr IV ZA** · moderate risk → holiday **2–3 yr** (oral) / **3 yr** (ZA) with DXA + BTM · high risk → continue or switch · common practice holiday after 3–5 yr **(TO)**

**No drug holiday (TO)** — **denosumab** (rebound) · **anabolics** (BMD lost) · **SERMs** (no residual effect) **(TO)**

### Anabolic agents (teriparatide, abaloparatide, romosozumab)

**Teriparatide** — recombinant **N-terminal PTH 1–34** (of 84 aa; chief-cell hormone) · activates **adenylate cyclase** · **20 µg/day SC** (abdomen/thigh) · max **2 yr** · ✓ **stimulates osteoblast function** · **only US anabolic** (OB, older)

**Dosing pattern** — **intermittent (daily) → anabolic** · **continuous infusion → net resorption** + hypercalcaemia (e.g. 2-wk infusion)

**Intermittent PTH effects** — ↑ osteoblast number · activates existing osteoblasts · lining cells → osteoblasts · ↓ osteoblast apoptosis · osteoblasts secrete RANKL + IL-6 (coupling)

**Anabolic mediators (TO)** — ↑ **IGF-1** · ↑ **FGF-2** · ↓ **sclerostin** (Wnt/β-catenin ↑) · ↑ **Runx2** · not **RANKL** (= catabolic pathway, with M-CSF) **(TO)**

**Signalling (TO)** — PTH1R (GPCR) on osteoblasts, osteocytes, renal tubule → Gs-cAMP-**PKA** (main) + Gq-PKC · **both anabolic and catabolic** effects **(TO)**

**FDA indications (TO)** — postmenopausal high-risk · **men with primary or hypogonadal osteoporosis** · **glucocorticoid-induced** **(TO)**

| Teriparatide contraindications (TO) |
|---|
| **Paget disease** / unexplained ↑ ALP |
| Skeletal malignancy / **bone metastases** |
| **Prior skeletal radiation** |
| **Open epiphyses** (children) |
| **Hypercalcaemia** |
| **Hypercalciuria / urolithiasis** |

**Osteosarcoma** — **rats**, dose/duration dependent (3–60× human exposure) · not shown in humans · black-box (since removed per TO; 24-mo course still standard — plateau) **(TO)**

**Adverse effects** — **transient hypercalcaemia** · hypercalciuria · nausea, headache, dizziness, orthostatic hypotension · no hyperphosphataemia · **↑ ALP** (formation marker, not ↓)

**Monitoring (TO)** — serum + urine Ca before · serum Ca at **1 mo** · hypercalcaemia → ↓ calcium supplement first, then alternate-day dosing **(TO)**

**Fracture effect** — ↓ vertebral + non-vertebral (if anything, preferential **vertebral**) · Neer: non-vertebral ↓ 35% (20 µg) / 40% (40 µg)

**With bisphosphonates** — **concomitant use beneficial** (NOT true) — BPs **blunt** anabolic effect **(TO)** · IV ZA concomitant does not blunt; **denosumab + teriparatide → enhanced BMD** **(TO)** · prior antiresorptive blunts response **(TO)** · OB213138: may be given alone or as adjunct; long-term BP users → stop BP\
✗ Wrong options: abolishes

**Refractory osteoporosis** (5 yr BP, prior cement augmentation, new VCFs) — ✓ **recombinant N-terminal PTH**

**Abaloparatide (TO)** — synthetic **PTHrP 1–34** (41% homology) · ✓ **binds more selectively to RG state** (transient cAMP → formation) · **8.5×** lower R0 affinity · → less resorption, less cortical porosity, better radius/femoral neck BMD, **less hypercalcaemia**\
✗ Wrong options: PTHR2 agonist **(TO)**

| PTH1R conformation (TO) | Signal | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| **R0** | Prolonged | Osteoclast → resorption |
| **RG** | Transient | Osteoblast → formation |

**Sclerostin** — **SOST** gene, **osteocytes** · binds **LRP5/6**, blocks Wnt → β-catenin degraded → ↓ osteoblast differentiation · loss of SOST → **↑ bone mass** (**van Buchem**, **sclerosteosis**) · (✗ Wrong options: hereditary osteoporosis · antagonises BMP) · ✓ **anti-sclerostin antibodies ↑ BMD**

**Romosozumab** — ✓ **inhibits an antagonist of Wnt/β-catenin** · humanised IgG2 · **dual action** (↑ formation, ↓ resorption via ↓ RANKL, ↑ OPG) · ✓ **most rapid + greatest ↑ lumbar BMD in 12 mo** (**13.3%** spine, 6.9% hip) **(TO)**

**Romosozumab dose** — **210 mg SC monthly** (2 × 105 mg) × **12 mo** **(TO)** · **120 mg monthly** × 12 mo (TO17130219) — read the options

**Romosozumab indication** — FDA **Apr 2019** · **treatment** of postmenopausal women at high risk · not **prevention** **(TO)** · very high risk = T < −2.5 + vertebral/hip fracture in 24 mo or >2 vertebral fractures, or FRAX major >30% / hip >4.5% **(TO)**

**Romosozumab trials (TO)** — **FRAME** ↓ vertebral **73%** at 12 mo · **ARCH** vs alendronate: 48% fewer vertebral, 38% fewer hip · **CV events 2.5% vs 1.9%** → black box: avoid if MI/stroke within 1 yr **(TO)** · must follow with antiresorptive (BMD → baseline 1 yr after stopping) **(TO)**

**Blosozumab** — investigational anti-sclerostin

![Sclerostin–Wnt pathway](../../MCQ/attachments/q211145-illa-pathway-2.jpg)

### Denosumab

**Mechanism** — fully human **IgG2 anti-RANKL** · blocks RANKL–RANK (like **OPG**) · ✓ **inhibits bone resorption** · ↓ osteoclast **formation, function, survival** (BPs do not affect formation) **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: RANK · OPG · TNF

**Pharmacology** — does not bind hydroxyapatite · cleared within **6 mo**, undetectable at **1 yr** · **fully reversible** · usable with **bone metastases** · also **GCT of bone**

**Dose** — **60 mg SC every 6 mo** (100% compliance) **(TO)**

**FREEDOM (TO)** — ↓ vertebral **68%**, hip **40%**, non-vertebral **20%** over 3 yr · 10-yr extension: spine BMD **+21.7%**, **no plateau** (BPs plateau 3–5 yr) **(TO)**

**Indications (TO)** — BP alternative (**eGFR <30–35**, oesophageal disease, intolerance) · second line **(TO)**

**Adverse** — arthralgia · nasopharyngitis · back pain · **ONJ** (✓ antibody-based therapy linked to ONJ; ~0.04%) · cellulitis · hypocalcaemia (renal impairment, vit D deficiency) · CI **severe hypocalcaemia**

**Abrupt discontinuation** — ✓ **rebound turnover → multiple vertebral fractures** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: hypercalcaemia · ONJ · AFF

### Sequential therapy & treatment transitions (TO)

**Anabolic first** — ✓ **anabolic → antiresorptive** (DATA / DATA-Switch) · antiresorptive first blunts later anabolic response **(TO)**

| Sequence (decreasing BMD gain) (TO) |
|---|
| **Romosozumab 12 mo → denosumab** (greatest) |
| Teriparatide/abaloparatide 24 mo → denosumab |
| Romosozumab 12 mo → bisphosphonate |
| Denosumab alone |
| Bisphosphonate 3–5 yr then holiday (moderate risk) |

| Situation (TO) | ✓ Correct | ✗ Wrong option |
|---|---|---|
| **After 24 mo teriparatide** | ✓ **Immediately (1–3 mo) → denosumab** (continued ↑ spine + hip BMD) or BP (ZA 1–2 yr / alendronate 3–5 yr) | drug holiday (lose almost all gain within 12 mo) · "effects long-lasting, no antiresorptive" |
| **Stopping denosumab** | ✓ **Bisphosphonate** — **ZA exactly 6 mo** after last dose (≤8 mo if CTX-guided) · >2.5 yr use → repeated ZA / 1–2 yr alendronate, monitor CTX at 3 + 6 mo | ZA at 12 mo · **teriparatide after denosumab** (transient hip/radius bone loss, no rebound protection) |
| **Stress reaction on ZA (AFF precursor)** | ✓ **Stop ZA, consider teriparatide** · protected WB · **contralateral femur imaging** (bilateral **20–50%**) · correct vit D >30 ng/mL | switch to **denosumab** (absolutely avoid) · oral alendronate · continue ZA |

| Incomplete AFF (TO) | Management |
|---|---|
| **Prodromal thigh/groin pain OR lucent lateral cortical line** ("dreaded black line") | ✓ **Prophylactic full-length cephalomedullary nail** |
| Asymptomatic, beaking only, no lucent line | Protected WB **2–3 mo** + serial X-rays |

**Thigh pain + lateral cortical thickening on alendronate** — ✓ **cephalomedullary nailing** (protects femoral neck) · (✗ Wrong options: standard antegrade nail · CT staging / oncology referral · observation) · nail pearl: exaggerated anterior bow → avoid anterior cortical perforation **(TO)**

![Impending atypical femoral fracture](../../MCQ/attachments/q213039-illa-impending-arrow.jpg)

### Fracture liaison & post-fracture management

**Fragility fracture work-up** — hip + spine densitometry · labs for secondary causes · Ca **1500 mg** + vit D **400–800 IU** · **protein-enriched diet**\
✗ Wrong options: **low protein diet**

**Inpatient hip ORIF with new osteoporosis** — ✓ **metabolic work-up as inpatient + osteoporosis clinic appointment** · treatment rates after hip fracture surprisingly low\
✗ Wrong options: PCP follow-up · PT · nutritionist · orthopaedist starting BP and sending to PCP

**Fracture liaison service** — ✓ **more likely to receive DEXA** + referral to metabolic bone clinic\
✗ Wrong options: fewer labs / PCP / endocrine visits · changes fracture care

### Vertebral compression fracture & vertebroplasty

**Osteoporotic VCF** — commonest fragility fracture · T likely < −2.5 · no CT if neuro intact with adequate films · neoplasm unlikely in typical demographic

**Vertebroplasty** — ✓ **no benefit in RCTs** · kyphoplasty for pain persisting **>6 wk** nonop (limited AAOS strength)\
✗ Wrong options: kyphoplasty in first week for narcotic-level pain

### Bone density for implant fixation (TO)

| Location | Minimum BMD for implant stability (mg/cm³) (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Proximal femur** | ✓ **250** |
| Proximal humerus | 95 |
| Vertebral body (pedicle) | 95–150 |
| Vertebral body | 220 |

## 6 Complications

**Bisphosphonates** — esophagitis · dysphagia · gastric ulcer · **ONJ (IV)** · **atypical subtrochanteric fracture**

**Teriparatide** — transient hypercalcaemia · rat osteosarcoma · Paget sarcoma risk (contraindicated)

**Denosumab** — ONJ · rebound vertebral fractures on stopping · hypocalcaemia

**Romosozumab** — **CV events** · hyperostosis · OA · cancer · ONJ · AFF

**Raloxifene** — VTE · hot flashes · leg cramps

**HRT** — breast ca · CAD/MI · stroke · PE/DVT · Alzheimer's

## Osteoporosis vs Osteomalacia

| | **Osteoporosis** | **Osteomalacia** |
|---|---|---|
| Defect | **↓ mass, normal mineralisation** (quantitative) | Variable mass, **↓ mineralisation** (qualitative) |
| Age | Postmenopausal / elderly | Any |
| Cause | Endocrine, age, inactivity, alcohol, ↓ Ca | Vit D deficiency/pathway, ↓ PO₄, hypophosphatasia, RTA |
| Symptoms | Pain at fracture site | Generalised bone pain |
| X-ray | **Axial** fractures | **Appendicular**, symmetric, **Looser zones** |
| Serum Ca / PO₄ | Normal | Low or normal |
| ALP | **Normal** | **↑** (except hypophosphatasia) |
| Urinary Ca | High or normal | Normal or low |
| Tetracycline biopsy | Normal | **Abnormal** |

## Antiresorptive vs Anabolic

| | **Bisphosphonate** | **Denosumab** | **Teriparatide** | **Romosozumab** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Target | FPP synthase / ATP analogue | RANKL | PTH1R | Sclerostin |
| Action | Antiresorptive | Antiresorptive | **Anabolic** | **Anabolic + antiresorptive** |
| Dosing | Oral weekly / IV yearly | 60 mg SC 6-monthly | 20 µg SC daily, 2 yr | SC monthly, 12 mo |
| Persistence | Years in bone | Gone by 6–12 mo | BMD lost without relay | BMD lost without relay |
| Drug holiday | Yes (moderate risk) | No (rebound) | No | No |
| Signature risk | ONJ, AFF | Rebound VCFs, ONJ | Osteosarcoma (rats), ↑ Ca | CV events |

## How it's asked

**"T-score −3.5 / −2.6?"** → **osteoporosis**\
✗ Wrong options: needs more information · normal for elderly

**"Osteoporosis T-score?"** → **−2.5 or below**\
✗ Wrong options: −3.0 · +2.5

**"Postmenopausal, T −0.7, Z −0.4?"** → **normal**\
✗ Wrong options: osteopenia by Z-score

**"Osteopenia definition?"** → **T −1 to −2.5**\
✗ Wrong options: Z-score definitions · T vs age-matched

**"X-ray shows loss after what %?"** → **40%**\
✗ Wrong options: 5%, 20%, 90%

**"BMD assessment EXCEPT?"** → **DEXA measures axial skeleton only** (false)

**"Next investigation after fixation of low-energy hip / old VCFs on MRI?"** → **DEXA scan**\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan · cement augmentation

**"Strongest predictor of future low-energy fracture?"** → **prior fragility fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: T < −2.5

**"Greatest predictor of future vertebral fracture?"** → **2 previous VCFs**\
✗ Wrong options: T −2.8 · recent hip fracture

**"Which elderly woman at greatest VCF risk?"** → **prior T6 compression fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: prior hip · T −3.0

**"Bone that loses more strength with age?"** → **trabecular more than cortical**

**"Drug NOT associated with osteoporosis?"** → **NSAIDs**\
✗ Wrong options: SSRI, steroids, phenytoin, protease inhibitors

**"Medication ↑ non-traumatic hip fracture?"** → **phenytoin**\
✗ Wrong options: simvastatin, glipizide, allopurinol

**"Pelvic insufficiency fracture risk EXCEPT?"** → **THA with ceramic bearings**\
✗ Wrong options: constrained liner

**"Gene polymorphism NOT linked to osteoporosis?"** → **COMP**\
✗ Wrong options: calcitonin receptor, ER-1, VDR, COL1A1

**"Lab most likely abnormal after low-energy DRF?"** → **25-OH vitamin D**

**"Vit D replacement — expected change?"** → **↑ 25-OH D and 1,25-D, ↓ sclerostin** **(TO)**

**"Calcium for a 13-yr-old?"** → **1000–1500 mg**

**"Calcium for 52-yr-old with T −3.0?"** → **1000–1500 mg**

**"On 1200 mg Ca alone after DRF — adjust?"** → **add 800–1000 IU vitamin D**

**"Premenopausal DRF, T −2.2 — treatment?"** → **1200 mg calcium**

**"Healthy 52-yr-old, no risk factors?"** → **1000–1500 mg Ca + 400–800 IU vit D**\
✗ Wrong options: estrogen · semiannual DEXA

**"FRAX stands for?"** → **Fracture risk assessment tool** **(TO)**

**"Least useful tool to assess response to bisphosphonate?"** → **FRAX** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: BTMs, DEXA, QCT

**"NOT an indication to treat?"** → **10-yr major fracture risk ≥10%** **(TO)**

**"T −2.0, hip 1.5%, major 8.9%?"** → **do not start antiresorptive**

**"T −1.5, hip 4.8%, on phenytoin?"** → **Ca + vit D + bisphosphonate + replace phenytoin + DEXA in 1 yr**

**"NOF 2008 bisphosphonate indication?"** → **T −1.0 to −2.5 AND hip risk >3%**\
✗ Wrong options: major >10%

**"Teriparatide — NOT true?"** → **concomitant bisphosphonate beneficial** **(TO)**

**"Teriparatide anabolic effect NOT mediated by?"** → **RANKL** **(TO)**

**"Intermittent PTH effect?"** → **anabolic**

**"Recombinant PTH that builds bone?"** → **teriparatide (Forteo)** — **hormone of chief cells**\
✗ Wrong options: SOST protein · P-C-P backbone · parafollicular hormone

**"Net bone resorption?"** → **teriparatide continuous infusion**\
✗ Wrong options: daily injection

**"Osteoporosis drug with osteosarcoma risk?"** → **recombinant PTH 1–34**

**"Teriparatide — true?"** → **stimulates osteoblast function**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ ALP · hyperphosphataemia · inhibits osteoclastogenesis

**"Refractory osteoporosis after 5 yr BP?"** → **recombinant N-terminal PTH**

**"Abaloparatide vs teriparatide?"** → **binds more selectively to RG state** **(TO)**

**"Fastest, greatest ↑ lumbar BMD in 12 mo?"** → **romosozumab** **(TO)**

**"Romosozumab — NOT true?"** → **approved for prevention** **(TO)**

**"Anti-sclerostin mechanism?"** → **inhibits an antagonist of Wnt/β-catenin**\
✗ Wrong options: RANKL · FPP synthase

**"Sclerostin — accurate?"** → **anti-sclerostin antibodies ↑ bone density**\
✗ Wrong options: SOST → hereditary osteoporosis

**"Direct RANKL inhibitor?"** → **denosumab**\
✗ Wrong options: romosozumab, blosozumab

**"Antiresorptive, no hydroxyapatite binding, undetectable at 1 yr, OK with metastases?"** → **denosumab**

**"Denosumab interferes with RANKL → ?"** → **inhibits bone resorption** **(TO)**

**"Antibody therapy with ONJ?"** → **denosumab**\
✗ Wrong options: pamidronate (not antibody)

**"Abrupt denosumab cessation risk?"** → **multiple vertebral fractures** **(TO)**

**"True about anti-osteoporotic treatment?"** → **denosumab effect short-lived — follow with bisphosphonate** **(TO)**

**"Very high risk — best sequence?"** → **anabolic then antiresorptive** **(TO)**

**"After 24 mo teriparatide?"** → **switch immediately to denosumab** **(TO)**

**"Femoral stress reaction on ZA?"** → **stop ZA, consider teriparatide** **(TO)**

**"Bisphosphonate failing to ↓ hip fracture?"** → **ibandronate** **(TO)**

**"Therapy ↓ all-cause mortality after hip fracture?"** → **zoledronic acid** **(TO)**

**"Fragility VCF — mechanism of indicated drug?"** → **inhibits farnesyl pyrophosphate synthase**

**"Fracture of long-term bisphosphonate?"** → **transverse subtrochanteric, lateral beak, medial spike**

**"Long-term alendronate associated with?"** → **diaphyseal femoral insufficiency fracture**

**"Long-term IV zoledronate for Paget — complication?"** → **osteonecrosis of mandible**\
✗ Wrong options: sarcoma

**"Thigh pain, lateral cortical thickening, on alendronate?"** → **cephalomedullary nailing**

**"8 yr BP + chronic steroids, T −2.6?"** → **continue, no drug holiday**

**"Fragility fracture management NOT included?"** → **low protein diet**

**"Best osteoporosis care after hip ORIF?"** → **inpatient metabolic work-up + osteoporosis clinic**

**"Benefit of fracture liaison service?"** → **more likely to get DEXA**

**"L1 osteoporotic VCF — true?"** → **↓ bone quantity, normal quality**\
✗ Wrong options: kyphoplasty in week 1 · T −2.1 diagnostic

**"Vertebroplasty for VCF?"** → **no benefit in RCTs**

**"Disuse osteopenia principle?"** → **Wolff's law**\
✗ Wrong options: Hueter-Volkmann · Hooke

**"Minimum BMD for proximal femoral implant fixation?"** → **250 mg/cm³** **(TO)**

# Outcome Measure Tools

*Basic Science · 76 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### SF-36 & patient-reported outcomes

**SF-36** — **generic** short-form health survey, **36 questions** · most widely applied general health instrument · **self-administered** (office, mail, phone) · validated with **normative population data** + multilingual translations

**SF-36 structure** — **8 scaled scores** (each 0–100, equal item weight) · physical functioning · role physical · bodily pain · general health · vitality · social functioning · role emotional · mental health · measures functional ability, well-being, overall health

**SF-36 limitation** — ✓ **ceiling and floor effects** · bias **lower > upper extremity**\
✗ Wrong options: "no normative data" · ignores patient perspective · must be on-site

**SF-36 use** — compare disease burden · **polytrauma with foot injury → lower SF-36** than polytrauma without foot injury (≥12 mo)

**SF-12** — 12-item short version · physical + mental health · pre-injury pain-related disability predicts pain at 6 mo

**VR-12** — Veterans Rand 12-item, derived from SF-36 · physical + mental summary scores · HRQOL, disease burden

**EQ-5D** — generic QOL · **5 dimensions** (mobility, self-care, usual activities, pain/discomfort, anxiety/depression) + **VAS** · **243 health states** · index **0 = death, 1 = perfect health** · 170+ languages

**PROMs vs physician-reported** — ✓ **better assessment of patient satisfaction**\
✗ Wrong options: cost-effective care · ambulation · disposition · analgesia needs

### PROMIS

**PROMIS** — NIH (2004 Roadmap) · **general + disease-specific** · adult + paediatric · domains **physical, mental, social**

**Method** — **computer adaptive testing (CAT)** + **item response theory (IRT)** → next item chosen from previous answers → **fewer questions**, high precision

**Scoring** — **T-scores**, population mean **50, SD 10**

**Advantage** — ✓ **reduced floor and ceiling effects** · quickest, broadest population, highly reliable · high correlation with ODI + SF-36\
✗ Wrong options: more items · low reliability/validity

**Validated in** — ✓ **spine · foot & ankle · upper extremity**

**Foot & ankle** — PROMIS ✓ **superior** to FAOS / OMAS at detecting clinically significant change at **6 and 12 mo** after ankle fracture\
✗ Wrong options: needs 24 mo follow-up

**Legacy measures** — SF-36, EQ-5D, HHS, KOOS, DASH, QuickDASH, FAAM, ODI · not IRT-based · validated only in specific populations

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Joint-specific scores

| Region | Scores |
|---|---|
| **Knee** | **KSS** · IKDC · **KOOS** · **WOMAC** · Lysholm · Tegner · Oxford knee **(TO)** |
| **Hip** | Merle d'Aubigné–Postel · **Harris Hip Score** · modified HHS · HOOS · Oxford hip · Lequesne · WOMAC **(TO)** |
| **Shoulder** | **DASH** · Constant · Oxford shoulder · WOSI / WORC / WOOS · Simple shoulder test · UCLA · SPADI · ASES **(TO)** |

**"Not a knee score"** — ✓ **DASH** (upper limb) **(TO)**

**WOMAC** — hip + knee **OA** · **24 items**: **pain 5 · stiffness 2 · function 17** · sum 0–20 / 0–8 / 0–68 · **higher = worse** · TO key: **knee** (OA commoner in knee than hip) **(TO)** · also back pain, RA, JRA, SLE, fibromyalgia

**KOOS** — young/middle-aged, post-traumatic OA (also TKA) · **5 domains** (pain, symptoms, ADL, sport/recreation, QOL) · 42 items 0–4 → 0–100 (**100 = no problems**)

| Harris Hip Score (THA) | Points |
|---|---|
| **Pain** (none) | **44** |
| **Function** (gait: no limp, no aid, >6 blocks) | **33** |
| Functional activities | 14 |
| Physical exam (ROM) | 9 |
| **Rating** | **90–100 excellent · 80–90 good · 70–79 fair · 60–69 poor · <60 failed** |

![Harris Hip Score](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9087-hhs.jpg)

**FAOS** — lateral ankle instability, Achilles tendinosis, plantar fasciitis · **5 subscales** (pain, symptoms, ADL, sport, QOL) · last week · 0–4 → normalised **100 = no symptoms**

| Constant shoulder score (0–100) | Max |
|---|---|
| Pain (subjective) | **15** |
| Function / ADL (subjective) | **20** |
| **Range of motion** (objective) | **40** |
| **Strength** (objective) | **25** |

![Constant score](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9087-constant-score.jpg)

**UCLA shoulder** — **5 sections**: pain · function · active forward flexion · strength of forward flexion · **patient satisfaction** · max **35** · **>27 = good/excellent**

**DASH** — **30-item** self-report upper limb · disability/symptom (30 items, 1–5) + optional sport/music/work (4 items) · score = **[(sum/n) − 1] × 25** · not calculable if **>3 missing** · optional module not scored if any missing

**QuickDASH** — **11 items**, 5-point Likert · not scored if **>1 missing** · 0–100, higher = more disability

**Oxford Shoulder Instability Score** — 12 questions · **12 (best) to 60 (worst)** · uni- + multidirectional instability

### Spine scores

**ODI** — **gold standard** low back functional outcome · **10 sections × 5** · % = scored / possible × 100 (16/50 = **32%**; one section missed → 16/45 = **35.5%**)

**NDI** — modified ODI · 10 items · commonest neck pain measure (chronic neck pain, whiplash, radiculopathy) · sum / 50 × 100 · 0% none → 100% complete disability

**ASIA** — spinal cord injury

| Cervical myelopathy | Based on |
|---|---|
| **mJOA** (17 points) | UE motor · LE motor · sensory · **bladder** |
| **Nurick** | **Gait** · employment · cord symptoms |

![mJOA classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9087-mjoa-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

### MCID — statistical vs clinical significance

**p-value** — chance of the observed difference if no true difference · **p < 0.05** = statistically significant · says nothing about clinical relevance

**MCID** — ✓ must be identified to judge **clinical significance** (e.g. KOOS difference, p = 0.04)\
✗ Wrong options: "significant → better outcomes" · larger sample needed

**ODI 38.4 vs 34.1, p = 0.002** — ✓ **statistically significant but likely below MCID** (4.3 points) · (✗ Wrong options: NNT (applies to risk)) · 80% power / β 0.2 = standard · non-randomised → **confounding**\
✗ Wrong options: Hawthorne = behaviour change when observed

### Predictors of pain & patient satisfaction

| Predicts moderate/severe pain at 6 mo after MSK injury | Not a predictor |
|---|---|
| **Failure to complete high school** · **pre-injury pain-related disability** · **disability compensation** · **moderate/severe pain at discharge** | ✓ **Age, gender (male)** |

**↑ Satisfaction AND quality** — ✓ **interpreter for limited English proficiency**\
✗ Wrong options: requested hydrocodone · early MRI · waiting-room amenities · early pain referral

**HCAHPS** — inpatient satisfaction survey · concern it may not reflect quality

## Generic vs Specific

| | **Generic** | **Disease/joint-specific** |
|---|---|---|
| Examples | SF-36, SF-12, VR-12, EQ-5D, PROMIS | WOMAC, KOOS, HHS, DASH, Constant, ODI, NDI |
| Strength | Compare burden across diseases | Sensitive to joint change |
| Weakness | Ceiling/floor effects (SF-36) | Population-limited |

## How it's asked

**"Instrument with general + disease-specific measures, validated in spine, foot & ankle, upper extremity?"** → **PROMIS**\
✗ Wrong options: SRS-22, ODI, SF-12, PHQ-9

**"PROMIS vs other tools in spine?"** → **reduced floor and ceiling effect**\
✗ Wrong options: more items · poor correlation

**"IRT-based, quickest, broadest, reliable PROM?"** → **PROMIS**\
✗ Wrong options: DASH, QuickDASH, FAAM, SF-36

**"PROMIS vs legacy in foot & ankle?"** → **superior at detecting change at 6 and 12 mo**

**"ODI difference 4.3, p = 0.002?"** → **statistically significant, likely below MCID**

**"KOOS improvement, p = 0.04 — next?"** → **identify MCID to judge clinical significance**

**"Polytrauma with Lisfranc injury vs without at 12 mo?"** → **lower SF-36**

**"Limitation of SF-36?"** → **ceiling and floor effects**

**"Benefit of PROMs (WOMAC) over physician-reported?"** → **better assessment of patient satisfaction**

**"NOT a predictor of pain 6 mo after MSK injury?"** → **male gender**

**"↑ Both satisfaction and quality?"** → **interpreter**

**"NOT a knee functional score?"** → **DASH** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: WOMAC, KSS, KOOS

**"WOMAC is used for which joint?"** → **knee** (TO key; hip + knee OA) **(TO)**

# Pectoralis Major

*Basic Science · 77 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Pectoralis major |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | **Clavicular head** — anterior medial half of clavicle · **sternocostal head** — sternum, superior 6 costal cartilages, external oblique aponeurosis |
| **Insertion** | **Lateral lip of intertubercular (bicipital) groove** |
| **Action** | **Adducts + medially rotates** humerus · draws scapula anteriorly/inferiorly · clavicular head flexes, sternocostal head extends |
| **Innervation** | **Lateral + medial pectoral nerves (C5–T1)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Pectoral branch of **thoracoacromial trunk** |

![Pectoralis major](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10008-pectoralis-major.jpg)

### Humeral insertion

| Insertion relative to long head of biceps | Muscle |
|---|---|
| **Just lateral** | ✓ **Pectoralis major** |
| Medial (floor / medial lip) | **Teres major, latissimus dorsi** |
| Lesser tuberosity, medial | **Subscapularis** |
| ✗ Wrong option: humerus | Short head of biceps — **originates on coracoid** |

## How it's asked

**"Tendon inserting just lateral to long head of biceps?"** → **pectoralis major**\
✗ Wrong options: teres major, latissimus dorsi (medial) · subscapularis (lesser tuberosity)

# Peroneus tertius

*Basic Science · 78 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Peroneus tertius |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | With EDL from **distal third of extensor (medial) fibular surface** + anterior intermuscular septum |
| **Insertion** | **Dorsal base of 5th metatarsal** (± fascia over 4th interosseous space) |
| **Action** | ✓ **Dorsiflexion + eversion** (with EDL; also abducts foot) |
| **Innervation** | **Deep peroneal nerve (L5, S1)** · anterior compartment |
| **Arterial supply** | Anterior tibial artery |

![Peroneus tertius](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10082-peroneus-tertius.jpg)

### Function

**Absent** — in **10%** of population · unique to humans · proximally migrated deep extensor of 5th toe

**Clinical use** — **arthroscopic portal landmark** · insertion point for tendon transfers to assist dorsiflexion

## How it's asked

**"Function of peroneus tertius (portal landmark)?"** → **dorsiflexion and eversion**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsiflexion alone · eversion alone · 5th / lesser toe extension

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Piriformis

*Basic Science · 79 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy & gluteal nerves

| | Piriformis |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | **Anterior (ventral) surface of sacrum** (3 digitations) + margin of greater sciatic notch · lesser: SI capsule, sacrotuberous ligament |
| **Insertion** | **Superior border / apex of greater trochanter** (piriformis fossa) |
| **Action** | **External rotator** · abducts flexed hip |
| **Innervation** | **Nerve to piriformis (L5, S1, S2)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Superior + inferior gluteal, internal pudendal arteries |

**Only short rotator from ventral sacrum** — ✓ piriformis\
✗ Wrong options: obturator internus/externus (pubic ramus, obturator membrane) · quadratus femoris (near ischial tuberosity) · superior gemellus (near acetabulum)

**Sacral plexus** — forms on its **anterior surface**

![Posterior hip muscles — piriformis](../../MCQ/attachments/q3681-illb-posterior-hip-muscles-1.jpg)

**Gluteal nerves named by relation to** — ✓ **piriformis**\
✗ Wrong options: gluteus maximus · sciatic nerve · sacrospinous ligament

| Nerve | Roots | Exit | Supplies |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Superior gluteal** | L4, L5, S1 | **Above piriformis** | Gluteus medius, minimus, **TFL** (runs between medius + minimus) |
| **Inferior gluteal** | L5, S1, S2 | **Below piriformis** | **Gluteus maximus** (sole motor) |

![Gluteal nerves and piriformis](../../MCQ/attachments/q677-illa-screen-shot-2018-09-16-at-6-31-27-pm.jpg)

### Greater sciatic foramen contents (TO)

**Greater sciatic foramen** — notch closed by **sacrotuberous + sacrospinous** ligaments · piriformis fills upper part, divides into two **(TO)**

| Compartment (TO) | Vessels | Nerves |
|---|---|---|
| **Above piriformis** | **Superior gluteal vessels** | ✓ **Superior gluteal nerve** |
| **Below piriformis** | Inferior gluteal · **internal pudendal** | Inferior gluteal · **pudendal** · **sciatic** · **posterior femoral cutaneous** · nerve to obturator internus · nerve to quadratus femoris |

**Lesser sciatic foramen (TO)** — **obturator internus tendon** · nerve to obturator internus · internal pudendal vessels · pudendal nerve **(TO)**

✗ Wrong options: **Through sciatic notch** — ✓ **obturator vessels** (obturator canal) **(TO)**

## How it's asked

**"Superior and inferior gluteal nerves named for relation to?"** → **piriformis**

**"Muscle originating from ventral sacrum?"** → **piriformis**\
✗ Wrong options: obturator internus/externus, quadratus femoris, superior gemellus

**"Through greater sciatic foramen above piriformis?"** → **superior gluteal nerve** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: internal pudendal, posterior femoral cutaneous, nerve to obturator internus

**"Structure NOT passing through sciatic notch?"** → **obturator vessels** **(TO)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Platelet-Rich Plasma

*Basic Science · 80 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & biology

**PRP** — plasma from own blood enriched with **autologous platelets** · controversial use to stimulate bone + soft-tissue healing

**Optimal concentration** — **3–5× whole blood** · **>5× inhibits healing** · not 10–15× (OB) · therapeutic working definition **≥1 million platelets/mL** **(TO)**

**Yield (TO)** — **30 mL** venous blood → **3–5 mL** PRP **(TO)**

**Proposed actions** — ↑ ECM deposition · ↓ pro-apoptotic signals · ↓ joint inflammation · acts via **growth factors**\
✗ Wrong options: aggrecan / structural polypeptide production

**Knee OA mechanism** — ✓ **↓ COX-2 and NF-κB** + **↑ hyaluronic acid by synoviocytes** · ↓ cartilage degradation · ↑ chondrocyte proliferation\
✗ Wrong options: NSAIDs (no HA ↑) · steroid (nuclear receptor) · HA injection (rooster comb/bacterial lubricant)

### Genes & lab markers

**Growth factors released** — **PDGF** · **TGF-β** · **VEGF** · **IGF-1** · **EGF** · **CTGF** · **FGF-2** · >30 bioactive proteins; 7 fundamental GFs **(TO)**

**Cell adhesion molecules (TO)** — **fibrin, fibronectin, vitronectin** **(TO)**

| Platelet granule (TO) | Contents |
|---|---|
| **α-granules** (unique to platelets, most abundant, **50–80/platelet**) | ✓ **Growth factors** (VEGF, FGF) · αIIbβ3, P-selectin · PF4, fibrinogen · vWF, factor V · chemokines (CXCL1, IL-8) · >300 proteins |
| **Dense (δ) granules** (2nd) | **Serotonin, histamine** · adenine nucleotides · polyphosphates · **calcium** |

**Degranulation (TO)** — secretory proteins activated (histones, carbohydrate side chains) → bind receptors on MSCs, osteoblasts, fibroblasts, endothelial + epidermal cells **(TO)**

**pH dependence (TO)** — ✓ GF effect **IS pH dependent** · PDGF highest at **pH 5.1** · TGF-β lower at pH 5.0 than 7.1/7.6 (Liu) **(TO)**

**Release kinetics (TO)** — **~70% of GFs in first 10 min** after activation · **~100% within 1 h** · small amounts over platelet lifespan (**8–10 days**) **(TO)**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Types of PRP (TO)

| Type (TO) | Platelets / leukocytes | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Pure PRP / leukocyte-poor (LP-PRP)** | High / minimal | Tendon, cartilage — less inflammation · neutrophils ~**100 cells/mL** |
| **Leukocyte-rich (LR-PRP / L-PRP)** | High / high | Acute inflammatory response · **↑ IL-1β, TNF-α, MMPs** antagonise anabolic effect · neutrophils ~**5000 cells/mL** · most commercial products |
| **Platelet-rich fibrin (PRF)** | — | **No anticoagulant** → fibrin scaffold → slow sustained GF release |
| **Autologous conditioned plasma (ACP)** | ✓ **High platelet, low leukocyte** | Leukocyte-poor form · tendon, cartilage, OA |
| **PRGF** | Balanced GFs, minimal leukocytes | Controlled healing |

### Preparation (TO)

**Anticoagulant** — ✓ **citrate-based**: **acid citrate dextrose (ACD) A/B** · CTAD · sodium citrate → prevent platelet activation, preserve function\
✗ Wrong options: **EDTA damages platelet membrane** (EDTA still listed as used by one TO source) · heparin · hirudin **(TO)**

**Differential centrifugation** — sediments constituents by specific gravity · **soft spin 1500 rpm × 5 min → hard spin 6000 rpm × 15 min** **(TO)**

| Method (TO) | Steps | PRP location |
|---|---|---|
| **PRP method** | ACD tubes · **don't chill** · soft spin → transfer supernatant → hard spin | **Lower 1/3 = PRP**, upper 2/3 = PPP · platelet pellet at bottom → resuspend in 2–4 mL plasma |
| **Buffy coat method** | Store **20–24 °C** · **high-speed** spin first · remove supernatant · low spin / leucocyte filter to remove WBC | Bottom **RBC** · ✓ **middle = platelets + WBC (buffy coat)** · top **PPP** |

**Activation** — ✓ **calcium chloride** (Orthobullets/OB3629 key) · **thrombin + calcium chloride** both initiate activation (OB, TO)\
✗ Wrong options: citrate dextrose (= anticoagulant) · TGF-β, IL-8, PDGF (= released products)

**Unanswered TO item (TO24050608)** — "agent commonly used to activate platelets?" · options TGF-α / IL-2 / **calcium chloride** / **thrombin** · **answer key not provided by source** · sources support both CaCl₂ and thrombin — read the options

**Point of care** — in-office / in-OR devices, **minutes**\
✗ Wrong options: 24-h lab processing

**Dosing (TO)** — up to **3 injections, 3–6 wk apart**, **5–10 mL** **(TO)**

### Regulation

**FDA** — PRP + preparation devices **"cleared" (510(k)), not "approved"** · not a drug · **not FDA-approved for OA** **(TO)** · blood products exempt from HCT/P pathway (21 CFR 1271) → not regulated like other cellular/tissue products (OB) · one TO table labels PRP "FDA approved" vs ACS not — read the options

## 5 Management

### Clinical evidence

| Indication | Evidence |
|---|---|
| **Lateral epicondylosis (tennis elbow)** | ✓ **Potential benefit** — PROs + pain |
| **BPTB donor sites** (patellar + tibial plug) | ✓ **Improved PROs of knee function**, ↓ patellar tendon gap |
| ACL graft ligamentisation / maturation | Not supported |
| **Rotator cuff repair augmentation** | **No added benefit** (possibly detrimental) · safe |
| Meniscal repair | No clear evidence |
| **Achilles tendinopathy** (midsubstance/insertional) | Not supported |
| Acute ligament/tendon/muscle injury, chronic tendinopathy | No consensus |
| **Osteoarthritis** | No consensus, lack long-term follow-up |
| Fracture healing / fusion | Limited evidence, some detrimental |

**TO indication list** — knee OA · partial tears (RC, hamstring, quadriceps, common extensor/flexor origin, triceps) · Achilles + chronic tendinosis · meniscus grade 1–2 ± parameniscal cyst · non-union / delayed union · partial ACL / ATFL tears **(TO)**

### Orthokine — autologous conditioned serum (ACS) (TO)

**Orthokine** — blood incubated with **pyrogen-free glass spheres** at **37 °C × 24 h** → leukocytes stimulated → ✓ **↑ IL-1 receptor antagonist (IL-1Ra)** + IGF-1, PDGF, TGF-β1 → cell-free serum injected\
✗ Wrong options: platelet concentration · stem cells · steroid **(TO)**

**Process (TO)** — 10–60 mL blood · spin 3000–3500 rpm · serum stored **−20 °C** · injected once–twice weekly · hypoxia-like (HIF-1α) response, not EPO **(TO)**

**WADA (TO)** — not banned; may need **TUE** (M1/M2 blood manipulation categories) **(TO)**

## ACS (Orthokine) vs PRP (TO)

| | **ACS** | **PRP** |
|---|---|---|
| Source | Whole blood + glass spheres, incubated | Centrifuged whole blood |
| Mechanism | **↑ IL-1Ra** (anti-inflammatory) | Platelet GFs (PDGF, TGF-β, VEGF) |
| Best for | OA, lumbar stenosis, disc prolapse, muscle injury | Tendon, ligament, cartilage, post-surgical |
| Processing | **24 h** incubation | **15–30 min** |
| Injections | Fewer | Multiple over weeks |
| FDA (per TO table) | Not approved | "Approved" (✗ Wrong options: per other TO key) |

## How it's asked

**"Ideal anticoagulant for PRP?"** → **acid citrate dextrose** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: EDTA, heparin, hirudin

**"PRP — all true EXCEPT?"** → **use in OA is FDA approved** (✓ α-granules, citrate, 1 million/mL) **(TO)**

**"Buffy coat method — layer with PRP?"** → **middle layer** **(TO)**

**"PRP — all true EXCEPT?"** → **GF effect NOT pH dependent** (false — it is pH dependent) **(TO)**

**"Autologous conditioned plasma has?"** → **high platelet, low leukocyte** **(TO)**

**"Orthokine therapy — true?"** → **↑ IL-1Ra to block inflammation** **(TO)**

**"Agent used to activate platelets in prepared PRP?"** → **calcium chloride**\
✗ Wrong options: citrate dextrose, TGF-β, IL-8, PDGF

**"PRP in acute rotator cuff repair — true?"** → **may give no added benefit**\
✗ Wrong options: 10–15× concentration · 24-h processing · regulated like HCT/Ps

**"Attenuates COX-2 + NF-κB, ↑ HA production in knee OA?"** → **PRP**\
✗ Wrong options: NSAIDs, steroids, HA

**"PRP potential benefit?"** → **tennis elbow, BPTB donor sites**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Popliteus

*Basic Science · 81 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Popliteus anatomy

| | Popliteus |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | **Lateral femoral condyle** — anterior popliteal groove (main) |
| **Insertion** | **Posterior (posteromedial) tibia**, fan-like, just above popliteal (soleal) line |
| **Action** | **Internally rotates tibia on femur** → **unlocks knee** at start of flexion |
| **Innervation** | **Tibial nerve (L4, L5, S1)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Medial inferior genicular (popliteal) + muscular branch of posterior tibial artery |

![Popliteus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10088-popliteus.jpg)

**Tripartite proximal tendon (TO)** — ✓ **tripartite** · main attachment **lateral femoral condyle** · + **head of fibula** · + **posterior lateral meniscus** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: bipartite · medial femoral condyle

| Popliteus action (TO) | Foot position |
|---|---|
| **External rotation of femur** | Foot plantigrade (weight-bearing) |
| **Internal rotation of tibia** | Foot free |
| **"Unlocking of the knee"** | Both |

## 5 Management

### Posteromedial exposure

**Extended posteromedial knee approach** — interval **medial gastrocnemius ↔ posterior semimembranosus** → posteromedial capsule (inside-out meniscal repair)

**Deeper exposure** — posteromedial tibia / **PCL fossa** → ✓ **subperiosteal elevation of popliteus** off posterior tibia\
✗ Wrong options: plantaris, semitendinosus, gracilis (superficial) · soleus (distal)

**Uses** — meniscal repair · tibial plateau ORIF · **PCL tibial avulsion** fixation · PCL tibial inlay · Baker's cyst · posterior capsular release

**At risk** — superficial **saphenous nerve + vein** · popliteal artery branches (inferior medial genicular often spared)

## How it's asked

**"True about popliteus?"** → **tripartite, main proximal attachment lateral femoral condyle** **(TO)**

**"Extended posteromedial approach — dissect what for PCL fossa / posterior tibia?"** → **popliteus**\
✗ Wrong options: plantaris, soleus

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Posterior Interosseous Nerve

*Basic Science · 82 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy & injury

**Course** — middle + lower trunks → **posterior cord** → radial nerve → divides at elbow into **superficial radial nerve (SBRN, sensory)** + **PIN (motor)**

| PIN motor supply | Muscles |
|---|---|
| **Superficial extensors (4)** | **EDM · ECU · EDC · ECRB** |
| **Deep extensors (5)** | **Extensor indicis · EPB · EPL · APL · supinator** |
| ✗ Wrong option: PIN (radial nerve proper) | **ECRL** |

**Sensory** — **dorsal wrist capsule** only (no skin)

![PIN innervation — superficial and deep extensors](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10104-pin.jpg)

### Wrist — 4th dorsal compartment

| Dorsal compartment | Contents |
|---|---|
| 1 | APL, EPB |
| 2 | ECRL, ECRB |
| 3 | EPL |
| **4** | **EDC, EIP, terminal PIN** |
| 5 | EDM |
| 6 | ECU |

## 3 Presentation

**PIN palsy pattern** — (✗ Wrong options: MCP extension of thumb + fingers) · ✓ **wrist extension with radial deviation** (ECRL intact, ECRB/ECU out) · ✓ sensation radial forearm + dorsal thumb (SBRN intact) → lesion **distal to SBRN/PIN split**

**Tenodesis intact** — passive wrist flexion → digits extend → ✓ **tendons in continuity** → nerve lesion, not tendon

## 5 Management

**Proximal forearm stab, can't extend fingers/thumb, tenodesis intact** — ✓ **explore + repair PIN**\
✗ Wrong options: NCS/EMG first · splint · tendon repair · observation

**Dorsal Thompson approach** — identify / protect PIN · **pronation** moves PIN away · radial nerve splits proximal to the approach · median / AIN / musculocutaneous not encountered

## Radial nerve vs PIN palsy

| | **Radial nerve (proximal)** | **PIN** |
|---|---|---|
| Wrist extension | No (wrist drop) | ✓ **with radial deviation** |
| Finger/thumb MCP extension | No | No |
| Dorsal radial sensation | No | ✓ intact |

## How it's asked

**"Tendon in same dorsal compartment as PIN?"** → **extensor indicis proprius**\
✗ Wrong options: EDM 5th, ECRB 2nd, EPL 3rd, APL 1st

**"Stab to proximal forearm, can't extend digits, tenodesis intact — management?"** → **explore and repair PIN**

**"After dorsal Thompson approach — no thumb/index MCP extension, wrist extends radially, sensation intact?"** → **posterior interosseous nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: radial · AIN

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Prophylactic Antibiotics

*Basic Science · 83 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Overview

**Share of antibiotic use** — **25–50%** of all antibiotics used are prophylactic

**Indications** — **implanted foreign body** (e.g. IM nail — not "no antibiotics needed") · bone grafting · large dissection / dead space / haematoma · significant blood loss

**Not needed** — **carpal tunnel release** · diagnostic arthroscopy controversial

**Commonest pathogens** — **S. aureus** · **S. epidermidis** · aerobic streptococci · anaerobic cocci · gram-positive skin flora

**Most effective SSI measure** — ✓ **IV antibiotics within 1 h before incision** · lumbar spine: no iodine vs chlorhexidine difference, no benefit from >10 min scrub, laminar flow helps TJA but not shown in spine\
✗ Wrong options: MRSA screening/decolonisation · horizontal laminar flow · body exhaust suits

## 5 Management

### Timing before incision

| Agent | Timing |
|---|---|
| **Cefazolin** | ✓ **Within 1 h before incision** · (✗ Wrong options: after incision) · **before tourniquet inflation** |
| **Vancomycin** | **Within 2 h** before incision |
| Redosing | **3–4 h** after first dose for long cases (OB8335) · 2017 CDC: insufficient evidence on intra-op redosing |

**WHO Surgical Safety Checklist** — 3 checkpoints: **before induction · before skin incision · before leaving OR** · ✓ **antibiotics given within 60 min before incision**\
✗ Wrong options: blood products on standby · VTE prophylaxis ordered · implant rep · credentialing

![WHO Surgical Safety Checklist](../../MCQ/attachments/q4858-illa-checklist.jpg)

### Agent selection & allergy (TO)

| Situation | Agent |
|---|---|
| **No β-lactam allergy** | ✓ **Cefazolin** (or cefuroxime) — **1st-generation cephalosporin** (S. aureus cover, low toxicity, penetration, half-life, cost) **(TO)** |
| **Dose** | **Double cefazolin if >80 kg** |
| **Penicillin / β-lactam allergy** | ✓ **Vancomycin** (TO key) or clindamycin **(TO)** |
| **MRSA history / high-prevalence area** | **Vancomycin + cefazolin** · judicious — ↑ **VRE** risk |
| β-lactam allergy + MRSA risk | Vancomycin ± **gentamicin or aztreonam** |
| Trauma IM nail, no allergy, no MRSA | ✓ **Cefazolin** (MRSA colonisation in trauma only **1–4%**) · (✗ Wrong options: vancomycin · cipro · metronidazole)|

| Antibiotic class | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Vancomycin** (glycopeptide) | ✓ **Bactericidal, blocks peptidoglycan cross-linkage · red man syndrome** |
| Cephalosporin | Bactericidal, peptidoglycan cross-linkage, no red man |
| Aminoglycoside | Bactericidal, irreversible **30S** |
| Macrolide / clindamycin | Bacteriostatic, reversible **50S** |
| Fluoroquinolone | Bactericidal, **DNA gyrase** |

**Other prophylaxis** — ciprofloxacin: GU surgery, brackish/salt/fresh water wounds · metronidazole: GI surgery, soil/faecal contamination

### CDC guidelines — duration

**AAOS (TJA)** — prophylaxis **should NOT exceed 24 h** after surgery

**CDC 2017 (Category IA)** — ✓ **clean + clean-contaminated: no additional doses after closure in OR, even with a drain** → single perioperative dose sufficient\
✗ Wrong options: continue until drains out · 24 h for drains · duration by length of surgery

**CDC 2002** — start within 1 h · appropriate agent · stop within **24 h**

### Dental prophylaxis in TJA patients (AAOS & ADA)

| At-risk TJA patients |
|---|
| **All patients for first 2 yr after TJA** |
| **Immunocompromised** — drug- or radiation-induced |
| **Inflammatory arthropathy** (RA, SLE) |
| Previous PJI · **type I DM** · malnourishment · haemophilia · HIV · malignancy |

**Evidence** — **limited / inconclusive** → clinical judgment

| Dental regimen (1 h before) | Drug |
|---|---|
| No penicillin allergy | **Amoxicillin or cephalexin 2 g PO** · IV/IM: cefazolin 1 g or ampicillin 2 g |
| Penicillin allergy | **Clindamycin 600 mg** PO (or IV) |

### Splenectomy prophylaxis

**Vaccines** — **pneumococcal** (all, incl. functional hyposplenism) · **Hib** + **meningococcal C conjugate** (if not immunised) · **influenza annually**

**Antibiotics** — **lifelong** oral phenoxymethylpenicillin or erythromycin

### Sterilisation & disinfection (TO)

**Sterilisation vs disinfection** — ✓ **endospores**: disinfection leaves spores · sterilisation destroys spores + pathogens **(TO)**

**Arthroscopic equipment** — ✓ **plasma sterilisation** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: autoclave (damages delicate scopes) · ETO (**12-h cycle**, can't turn around same day) · formaldehyde dip

**Plasma systems (TO)** — **Sterrad-100**: vaporised **H₂O₂** (45 min) + **radiofrequency** (13.56 MHz, 300 W, 0.5 Torr) · **Plazlyte**: **peracetic acid** vapour + microwave plasma (O₂/H₂/Ar) **(TO)**

**Spaulding classification (1957)** — ✓ **principles of disinfection and sterilisation** of reusable devices by risk **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: skull, pelvic fractures, DDH

| Spaulding (TO) | Definition | Examples | Reprocessing |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Non-critical** | Intact skin only | BP cuffs, stethoscopes, toys, walking aids | Clean ± **low-level disinfection** |
| **Semi-critical** | Non-intact skin / intact mucosa, no penetration | Trans-rectal probes, specula | **High-level disinfection** minimum, sterilisation preferred |
| **Critical** | Penetrate tissue / bloodstream / sterile area | **Surgical + dental instruments** | **Sterilisation** after every use |

**Semi-critical touching open lesions** — treat as **critical** **(TO)**

| Methods (TO) | Agents |
|---|---|
| Disinfection — oxidising | Sodium hypochlorite · povidone iodine · H₂O₂ · peracetic acid |
| Disinfection — non-oxidising | Chlorhexidine · **70% alcohol** · quaternary ammonium · **glutaraldehyde**, OPA |
| **Sterilisation** | **Autoclave** · **ETO** · H₂O₂ / peracetic acid vapour or **plasma** |

## How it's asked

**"On WHO Safe Surgery Checklist?"** → **antibiotics within 60 min before incision**

**"Most reduces infection after lumbar spine surgery?"** → **IV antibiotics before incision**\
✗ Wrong options: chlorhexidine vs iodine · laminar flow

**"Optimal antibiotic timing for TJA?"** → **within 1 h before incision**\
✗ Wrong options: within 2 h · after incision

**"Most notable impact on SSI?"** → **IV antibiotics within 1 h of incision**\
✗ Wrong options: MRSA decolonisation · laminar flow · exhaust suits

**"Cephalosporin generation used before THR?"** → **1st** **(TO)**

**"Penicillin allergy — preferred prophylaxis?"** → **vancomycin** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: cefazolin

**"Cefazolin anaphylaxis + prior MRSA, THA — antibiotic?"** → **bactericidal, peptidoglycan cross-link, red man syndrome** (vancomycin)

**"Femur IMN, no allergy, no MRSA?"** → **cefazolin**\
✗ Wrong options: none · vancomycin

**"2017 CDC strong recommendation?"** → **clean-contaminated: single perioperative dose sufficient**\
✗ Wrong options: continue with drains

**"Most effective sterilisation of arthroscopic equipment?"** → **plasma sterilisation** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: autoclave · ETO

**"Spaulding classification deals with?"** → **disinfection and sterilisation** **(TO)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pseudogout (CPPD)

*Basic Science · 84 / 108*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — inflammatory arthritis from intra-articular **calcium pyrophosphate dihydrate (CPPD)** crystal deposition

**Demographics** — **elderly (>60)** · rare in young unless associated disease

**vs gout** — **older** · **more proximal joints** · **positively** birefringent crystals

### Associated conditions

| Associations |
|---|
| **Hyperparathyroidism** |
| **Haemochromatosis** |
| Wilson's disease |
| Gout · RA · SLE |
| Haemophilia |
| Long-term **haemodialysis** (pyrophosphate-like deposition) |

**Chondrocalcinosis** — present in **7%** · high association with knee **OA** (relationship unclear)

## 3 Presentation

**Acute attack** — warm, erythematous, tender, **monoarticular** · mimics gout + septic arthritis · ± superficial mineral deposits

| Joint frequency | |
|---|---|
| **1st** | ✓ **Knee** |
| **2nd** | **Wrist** |
| 3rd | 2nd + 3rd MCP joints |

**Course** — can cause rapidly progressive joint destruction · inflammatory markers normal / minimally ↑

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Chondrocalcinosis** — ✓ **linear calcification of fibrocartilage + articular cartilage** · **menisci (knee)** · **TFCC (wrist)** · distinguishes CPPD from other secondary OA (gout, haemochromatosis, RA, ochronosis)

![Chondrocalcinosis of the knee menisci](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7040-chondrocalcinosis-knee-moved.jpg)

![TFCC chondrocalcinosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7040-screen-shot-2017-04-10-at-8-05-15-pm.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria — aspiration

**Crystals** — ✓ **weakly positively birefringent, rhomboid-shaped** · WBC moderate (**<50,000**) · culture negative

![Weakly positive birefringent rhomboid crystals](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7040-crystal-analysis-showing-weakly-positive-birefringent-rhombo.jpg)

## 5 Management

| Phase | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Acute** | **NSAIDs** · splint (comfort) · **intra-articular steroid** · rest |
| **Chronic / recurrent** | **Colchicine 0.6 mg PO bid** (prophylaxis ↓ recurrence) · **intra-articular yttrium-90** |

## 6 Complications

**Complications** — permanent joint damage · renal disease

## CPPD vs differentials

| Finding | Disease |
|---|---|
| **CPPD crystals, chondrocalcinosis** | ✓ **Pseudogout** |
| Monosodium urate, **strongly negative needle** crystals, punched-out erosions | Gout |
| Destructive **pannus**, symmetric narrowing, osteopenia | RA |
| Empty osteocyte lacunae | Osteonecrosis |
| Recurrent haemarthroses, wide notch, big condyles | Haemophilia |
| Iron deposition (cirrhosis, pigmentation, DM, cardiac) | Haemochromatosis |
| Homogentisic acid (homogentisic acid oxidase deficiency) | Ochronosis |

## How it's asked

**"Knee pain, hyperparathyroidism, meniscal calcification — finding?"** → **CPPD crystals**\
✗ Wrong options: urate · pannus · empty lacunae · haemarthroses

**"Linear cartilage calcification on knee AP?"** → **chondrocalcinosis (pseudogout)**\
✗ Wrong options: gout, haemochromatosis, ochronosis

**"TFCC calcification — other joint most involved?"** → **knee**\
✗ Wrong options: 1st MTP

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Psoas

*Basic Science · 85 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Psoas |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | Transverse processes **L1–L5** + bodies and discs **T12–L5** |
| **Insertion** | **Lesser trochanter** |
| **Action** | Flexes torso and thigh on each other |
| **Innervation** | **Direct fibres of lumbar plexus (L1–L3)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Lumbar branch of iliopsoas branch of internal iliac artery |

![Iliopsoas](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10053-iliacus.jpg)

### Nerves at risk in lumbar approaches

| Nerve | Relation to psoas |
|---|---|
| **Genitofemoral (L1–L2)** | ✓ **Emerges through psoas at L3–L4 → runs on anteromedial surface** — met in anterior retroperitoneal approach |
| Ilioinguinal, iliohypogastric, LFCN | **Posterior** to psoas → on quadratus lumborum / iliacus |
| Femoral | **Posterior** to psoas → wraps laterally onto iliopsoas distally |

**Lumbar plexus in transpsoas (lateral) approach** — ✓ **more dorsal at L1–2, more ventral at L4–5**\
✗ Wrong options: constant position

## How it's asked

**"Anterior retroperitoneal approach — nerve on anteromedial psoas?"** → **genitofemoral**\
✗ Wrong options: ilioinguinal, femoral, LFCN, iliohypogastric

**"Lumbar plexus position in psoas?"** → **dorsal in upper lumbar, ventral in lower lumbar**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Psoriatic Arthritis

*Basic Science · 86 / 108*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **seronegative spondyloarthropathy** · DIP-predominant hand arthritis · silvery plaques · uveitis · nail dystrophy

**Incidence** — **5–20%** of psoriasis patients (Orthobullets, OB210216) · 5–10% (OB3340) · ~20% (OB3581) — read the options \
✗ Wrong options: "most psoriasis patients develop arthritis"

**Sex** — M = F

**Skin first** — plaques precede arthritis by years in **80–85%** · arthritis first in **~20%** **(TO)**

### Genes & lab markers

**HLA-B27** — positive in **~50%**\
✗ Wrong options: all patients

**RF and ANA** — usually **negative**

### Patterns (Wright & Moll) (TO)

| Pattern (TO) | Features |
|---|---|
| **Symmetrical polyarthritis** | ✓ **Most common** · resembles RA but milder · RF-negative **(TO)** |
| **Asymmetrical oligoarthritis** | **<5 joints**, small/medium · **2nd commonest** (DIP, PIP, MCP) **(TO)** |
| **Distal (DIP-predominant)** | DIP + PIP · **acro-osteolysis** of tufts + nail changes |
| **Arthritis mutilans** | Rare · extreme phalangeal + MCP destruction → telescoping · **opera-glass hand (doigt / main en lorgnette)** · pencil-in-cup |
| **Spondyloarthropathy** | ~1/3 · SI (usually **unilateral**) + spine · **cervical > thoracolumbar** (distinguishes from AS) **(TO)** |

## 3 Presentation

**Skin** — **silvery plaques** on extensor surfaces (elbows, knees) · scalp

**Hands** — **dactylitis (sausage digit)** — up to **50%**, toe > finger **(TO)** · **nail pitting (onychodystrophy)** · **onycholysis** (distal nail-plate lifting) · opera-glass hands

**Opera-glass hand** — shortened phalanges → excess skin folds transversely, digits retract like telescoping binoculars

**Enthesitis** — up to **40%** **(TO)** · **Achilles**, **posterior tibial tendonitis**, **plantar fasciitis** · heel, foot, hip pain

**Other** — chronic **uveitis** · ankylosis of small hand/foot joints more than RA **(TO)**

![Dactylitis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9050-dactylitis.jpg)

![Nail pitting](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9050-pitting.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Pencil-in-cup** — ✓ **DIP**: destruction of middle phalanx head + expansion of distal phalanx base · **centripetal erosions → joint-space widening**\
✗ Wrong options: DJD

![Pencil-in-cup deformity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9050-radiograph-of-pencil-in-cup-deformity.jpg)

**Other hand signs** — **acro-osteolysis** (distal tuft resorption) · **fluffy periostitis** (periosteal ossification) · small-joint erosions or **fusions** (PIP, MCP, wrist) · flail digits

**Spine** — sacroiliitis · syndesmophytes · paravertebral ossification · destructive discovertebral lesions

### Diagnostic criteria — CASPAR

**Requirement** — inflammatory articular disease (joint, spine, entheseal) + **≥3 points**

| CASPAR criterion | Points |
|---|---|
| **Current psoriasis** | **2** |
| Personal or family history of psoriasis | 1 |
| **Psoriatic nail dystrophy** (onycholysis, pitting, hyperkeratosis) | 1 |
| **Negative RF** | 1 |
| **Dactylitis** (current or recorded) | 1 |
| **Juxta-articular new bone formation** on hand/foot X-ray (✗ Wrong options: osteophytes) | 1 |

**Unanswered TO item (TO24057301)** — clinical photo: nail dystrophy, DIP swelling, skin plaques · options psoriatic arthropathy / RA / OA / reactive arthritis · **answer key not provided by source**

## 5 Management

**Medical (mainstay, like RA)** — NSAIDs · methotrexate · sulfasalazine · cyclosporine · **TNF-α inhibitors** · rheumatology referral

### Treatment — TNF inhibitors

**Failed NSAIDs, active disease** — ✓ **biologic DMARD — TNF inhibitor** (or IL-17, IL-12/23) = ACR first line → ↓ joint destruction + surgery

| ✗ Wrong option | Why not |
|---|---|
| NSAIDs | Symptomatic only, not disease-modifying |
| Weight loss / bariatric | does not treat inflammation |
| **Hydroxychloroquine** | not approved · **precipitates / worsens psoriasis** |
| **Oral prednisone** | Weak disease modification · **pustular flare on withdrawal** |
| csDMARDs (MTX, leflunomide, SSZ) | Limited effect on joint damage + skin |

**Operative** — advanced disease → **digit fusion vs resection arthroplasty** · osteotomy, arthroplasty · **high infection rates** reported

## Psoriatic vs other hand arthritis

| | **Psoriatic** | **RA** | **Erosive OA** | **Gout** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Joints | **DIP** | PIP, **MCP (2nd–3rd)**, ulnar styloid, triquetrum · no DIP | DIP, PIP, 1st CMC | Any |
| Erosions | **Centripetal**, pencil-in-cup | Marginal, subchondral cysts | **Central "gull wing"**, not marginal | **Punched-out "rat-bite"**, overhanging edges, sclerotic margins |
| Other | Periostitis, acro-osteolysis, telescoping | Ulnar drift, swan-neck, boutonnière | Osteopenia, fusiform swelling | Tophi |
| Serology | RF− · HLA-B27 ~50% | RF+ | — | — |

## How it's asked

**"Most common form of psoriatic arthritis?"** → **symmetrical polyarthritis** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: asymmetric oligoarthritis (2nd)

**"Sausage digits + pencil-in-cup?"** → **psoriatic arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: gout · RA

**"Toe dactylitis, posterior tibial tendonitis, nail pitting?"** → **psoriatic arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: scleroderma · pseudogout

**"Nail pitting + onycholysis — matching radiograph?"** → **telescoping / opera-glass destruction**\
✗ Wrong options: RA MCP ulnar drift · erosive OA gull-wing

**"Psoriatic arthritis — true?"** → **DIP erosions often centripetal**\
✗ Wrong options: most psoriasis patients get arthritis · all HLA-B27+ · RF/ANA positive · antifungal

**"Severe PsA after NSAID failure — what would have prevented TKA?"** → **TNF inhibitor**\
✗ Wrong options: hydroxychloroquine · prednisone
