# PTH & Vit D Physiology

*Basic Science · 87 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Overview

**Organs in Ca homeostasis** — skin · liver · parathyroid · kidney · bone · small intestine · ↑ PTH + ↑ vit D → ↑ serum Ca

### Vitamin D metabolism & serum markers

| Step | Site | Product |
|---|---|---|
| **7-dehydrocholesterol** + **UV-B** | **Skin** | Previtamin D₃ → **cholecalciferol (D₃)** |
| Diet / supplements | Gut | D₂ (**ergocalciferol** — vegetables, supplements) · D₃ (oily fish, fortified foods) — both inert |
| Carried on **vitamin D-binding protein** → **25-hydroxylase** | **Liver** | **25-(OH) D₃ = calcidiol** |
| **1α-hydroxylase** (↑ by PTH, ↓ Ca, ↓ PO₄) | **Kidney** | ✓ **1,25-(OH)₂ D₃ = calcitriol — active** **(TO)** |
| **24-hydroxylase** | Kidney | **24,25-(OH)₂ D₃ — inactive** · 1,24,25-(OH)₃ D₃ |

![Vitamin D pathway — A D₃, B 25-OH D, C 1,25-(OH)₂ D, D 24,25-(OH)₂ D, E 1,24,25-(OH)₃ D](../../MCQ/attachments/q210168-a-path.jpg)

**Self-regulation** — high 1,25-D → ↑ **24,25-D** (inactive) · negative feedback on itself

**Skin conversion** — ↓ with age · nearly absent in darkly pigmented individuals

**Vitamin D actions** — ↑ intestinal Ca + PO₄ absorption · ↑ bone resorption (Ca + PO₄ release; osteoclastic, TO) → **↑ serum Ca AND ↑ PO₄** · OB219175: vit D (with **FGF23**) **inhibits osteoclast activity** → ↑ bone mass — read the options

### PTH functions (TO)

**Source** — **chief cells** of parathyroid · trigger **hypocalcaemia / hypomagnesaemia**

| PTH action | Effect |
|---|---|
| Bone | ↑ **osteoclastic** resorption — receptor on **osteoblasts** → **IL-1** → osteoclasts |
| Kidney | ↑ **Ca reabsorption (DCT)** · **↓ phosphate reabsorption** |
| Kidney | ↑ **1α-hydroxylation** of 25-OH D → ↑ 1,25-D |
| Net | **↑ serum Ca, ↓ serum PO₄** |

**PTH function — EXCEPT answer (TO)** — **"↑ osteoblastic resorption of bone"** — no such thing; resorption is osteoclastic **(TO)**

### Genes & lab markers

**Best test for deficiency** — ✓ **25-OH vitamin D** — long half-life, circulating level **~1000×** 1,25-D\
✗ Wrong options: 1,25-D (estimates stores poorly; only a fraction converted)

| Half-life | 25-OH D | 1,25-(OH)₂ D |
|---|---|---|
| OB4653 | **2–3 wk** | **4–6 h** |
| OB210168 | **15 days** | **15 h** |

**Unit conversion** — 1 nmol/L = 0.4 ng/mL · 1 ng/mL = 2.5 nmol/L

| 25-OH D level | Status |
|---|---|
| **<30 nmol/L (<12 ng/mL)** | At risk of **deficiency** (NIH, OB217497) |
| 30–50 nmol/L (12–20 ng/mL) | Possible inadequacy |
| **≥50 nmol/L (≥20 ng/mL)** | Sufficient for most |
| **75 nmol/L (30 ng/mL)** | ✓ **Normal** (OB217497) · Endocrine Society: **>75 nmol/L (>30 ng/mL)** needed for max bone/muscle effect |
| **<20 ng/mL** | **Deficient** (OB219590) |
| **20–30 ng/mL** | ✓ **Insufficient** (OB219590) |
| 30–80 ng/mL | Normal range (OB7541) |

**RDA vitamin D₃** — **400 IU** infants <1 yr · **600 IU** age 1–70 (incl. pregnancy/lactation) · **800 IU** adults >70

## 5 Management

### Supplementation & fracture healing

**Vitamin D in bone healing** — ✓ **regulates inflammatory response** at injury site (macrophages, neutrophils, T cells express **VDR**, convert vit D locally) · ↑ **VEGF**-mediated vasculogenesis · ↑ **MSC commitment to osteoblasts** (matrix adhesion, integrins) · ↑ ECM: **collagen I, osteocalcin, osteopontin** · prevents infection, ↑ muscle/soft tissue

**Deficiency** — ↑ fracture + arthrodesis **nonunion** · ↑ falls in elderly (supplement ↓ falls) · atrophic nonunion work-up → **25-OH D**

**Insufficiency fracture + 25-OH D 14 ng/mL, normal Ca** — ✓ **high-dose vitamin D**\
✗ Wrong options: observation (at risk elsewhere) · calcium alone · bisphosphonate before correcting mineral imbalance · surgery

**Supplement form** — **D₃ preferred** · D₂ most available by prescription (US) · hypervitaminosis D rare, high doses tolerated

## Vitamin D vs PTH

| | **1,25-(OH)₂ vit D** | **PTH** |
|---|---|---|
| Serum Ca | ↑ | ↑ |
| **Serum PO₄** | **↑** | **↓** |
| Gut | ↑ Ca + PO₄ absorption | Indirect (via 1,25-D) |
| Kidney | — | ↑ Ca reabsorption, ↓ PO₄ reabsorption, ↑ 1α-hydroxylase |
| Disorders | Rickets / osteomalacia | Hypoparathyroidism · pseudohypoparathyroidism · renal osteodystrophy |

## How it's asked

**"Most active vit D metabolite?"** → **1,25-dihydroxycholecalciferol** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: 25-OH · 24,25

**"Correct regarding vit D?"** → **24,25-dihydroxycholecalciferol is inactive**\
✗ Wrong options: 1,25-D best lab test · 1,25-D longer half-life

**"Serum marker after vit D repletion (pathway letter)?"** → **B — 25-hydroxyvitamin D**

**"Post-hip fracture 25-OH D 75 nmol/L (30 ng/mL)?"** → **normal level**

**"Atrophic humeral nonunion — value showing insufficiency?"** → **25-OH D 25 ng/mL**\
✗ Wrong options: 35 / 45 ng/mL · any 1,25-D value

**"Sacrococcygeal insufficiency fracture, 25-OH D 14 ng/mL?"** → **high-dose vitamin D**

**"Primary function of vit D in osteoporotic fracture healing?"** → **regulates inflammatory response at injury site**\
✗ Wrong options: activates RANKL · ↓ VEGF · inhibits osteocalcin

**"NOT a function of PTH?"** → **↑ osteoblastic resorption of bone** **(TO)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Quadrangular Space, Triangular Space, Triangular Interval

*Basic Science · 88 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Contents of shoulder spaces

| Space | Borders | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| **Quadrangular (quadrilateral) space** | Medial **long head triceps** · lateral **humeral shaft** · superior **teres minor** · inferior **teres major** | ✓ **Axillary nerve + posterior humeral circumflex artery** |
| **Triangular space** | Lateral **long head triceps** · superior teres minor · inferior teres major | **Scapular circumflex artery** (no major nerve) |
| **Triangular interval** | Medial long head triceps · lateral **lateral head triceps / humerus** · superior **teres major** | **Radial nerve + profunda brachii artery** |

![Quadrangular space, triangular space and triangular interval](../../MCQ/attachments/q213128-illa-shulder.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"Correct pairing of shoulder space and contents?"** → **quadrangular space — axillary nerve + posterior humeral circumflex artery**\
✗ Wrong options: quadrangular (radial nerve/profunda) · quadrangular (scapular circumflex) · triangular space (axillary nerve)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Radial nerve

*Basic Science · 89 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

**Course** — **posterior cord** → exits **triangular interval** (long head triceps ↔ humerus) with **profunda brachii artery** → **spiral groove** → pierces lateral intermuscular septum **7.5 cm proximal to elbow** → at lateral humeral condyle gives cutaneous branches (superficial radial, posterior cutaneous) → **PIN** through **supinator**, winds around **radial neck**

**Accompanying artery** — ✓ **profunda brachii**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior humeral circumflex (axillary nerve/quadrangular space) · scapular circumflex (triangular space) · brachial (medial with median/ulnar) · lateral thoracic (serratus anterior)

![Radial nerve motor branch order](../../MCQ/attachments/q3570-illa-radial-nerve-2.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

### Injury level

| Deficit | Level |
|---|---|
| ✗ Wrong option: wrist, thumb, finger extension (wrist drop) | **Radial nerve** — spiral groove / distal-third humeral shaft |
| ✗ Wrong option: digit + thumb extension · ✓ **wrist extension with radial deviation** | **PIN at radial neck** (✗ Wrong options: spiral groove · midshaft radius · humeral necks) |

**Holstein-Lewis fracture** — **distal-third humeral shaft** fracture + **radial nerve neuropraxia** · **80–90% recover** with observation → not an indication for surgery

![Holstein-Lewis fracture](../../MCQ/attachments/q212964-a-ap-holstein-lewis.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Reinnervation order (TO)

**Recovery direction** — **proximal → distal** **(TO)**

| Recovery (TO) | Muscle / function |
|---|---|
| **First muscle** | **Brachioradialis** |
| **First function (wrist/finger drop after humeral shaft fracture)** | ✓ **Wrist extension (radial deviation)** (✗ Wrong options: supination · elbow extension · MCP extension) |
| **Last function** | **MCP joint extension** |
| **Last muscle** | ✓ **Extensor indicis proprius** (✗ Wrong options: brachioradialis, ECRB, APL, EPL) |

### Critical period of delay (TO)

**Critical period** — time beyond which motor return isn't expected if repair delayed **(TO)**

| Nerve (TO) | Critical period |
|---|---|
| **Radial** | ✓ **15 months** |
| PIN | 9 months |
| Ulnar — high | 9 months |
| Ulnar — low | 15 months |
| Median — high | 9 months |
| Median — low | 12 months |
| Sciatic | 12–15 months |
| CPN | 12 months |

## How it's asked

**"Radial palsy after humeral shaft ORIF, nerve intact — first function to recover?"** → **wrist extension** **(TO)**

**"Critical period of delay in radial nerve palsy?"** → **15 months** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: 9, 10, 12

**"Last muscle reinnervated in radial palsy?"** → **extensor indicis proprius**

**"GSW — can't extend digits/thumb, wrist extends with radial deviation — level?"** → **radial neck** (PIN)

**"Holstein-Lewis fracture, wrist drop — artery with injured nerve?"** → **profunda brachii**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Rehab & Prosthetics

*Basic Science · 90 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Principles

**Goal** — restore function close to original · multidisciplinary team

**Upper limb** — **limb salvage ideal** (prosthesis lacks sensation) · residual length matters for socket suspension

**Lower limb goals** — comfortable · easy on/off · light · durable · cosmetic · functional

**Most important factor choosing LL prosthesis** — ✓ **patient functional status** (current + potential)\
✗ Wrong options: cost, comorbidities, age, gender

### Metabolic cost of amputation

| Amputation | ↑ Metabolic cost of walking |
|---|---|
| **Syme's** | **15%** |
| **Traumatic transtibial** | ✓ **25%** — least of the options |
| Vascular transtibial | 40% |
| Traumatic transfemoral | 68% |
| Vascular transfemoral | 100% |

**Rule** — traumatic < vascular (ongoing vascular disease above level) · more proximal → higher cost

**Other costs** — **wheelchair propulsion +9%** vs normal walking · **crutch walking > walking with prosthesis**

### Gait aids & crutch gait (TO)

**Antalgic gait** — any pain-related gait · **shortened stance** on affected limb

| Gait | Pattern |
|---|---|
| **3-point** | Crutches + injured limb advance together (weight on crutches) → weight onto uninjured limb |
| **4-point** | One crutch → opposite leg → other crutch → other leg |
| **Swing-to** | Crutches advanced, legs swung **to** crutches · slower, ↓ fall risk |
| **Swing-through** | Crutches advanced, legs swung **past** them · faster, ↑ energy, ↑ fall risk · ✓ **paraplegia / SCI with paraparesis** **(TO)** |

![Gait patterns with assistive devices](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9072-gait-patterns.jpg)

**Crutch-walking muscles** — latissimus dorsi · triceps + biceps · quads · hip extensors · hip abductors

**Cane** — held **contralateral** → shifts centre of gravity toward affected side

**TTWB / NWB one leg** — ✓ **2 axillary crutches**\
✗ Wrong options: single forearm crutch · double cane · single platform crutch

## 5 Management

### Upper limb prostheses (myoelectric vs body-powered)

**Timing** — fit **ASAP**, even before full wound healing · better if **within 30 days**

| | **Myoelectric** | **Body-powered** |
|---|---|---|
| Best candidate | ✓ **Midlength transradial** (only terminal device to drive) | **Heavy labour** |
| Mechanism | Surface electrodes on residual muscles → motor | Harness: **shoulder flexion + abduction** · ring just off **C7 midline toward non-amputated side** |
| Pros | **Cosmesis** · **more proximal function/coverage** · any position incl. **overhead** · **less gross limb movement** · sedentary use | **Most durable** · moderate cost · **lighter** · **more sensory feedback** · less maintenance · less training |
| Cons | **Heavier, expensive, more maintenance**, **less sensory feedback** · clean environment only | Poor cosmesis · **more harnessing** · more gross movement |
| Rejection (Silcox) | 50% rejected myoelectric | 32% rejected conventional (of those with both) |

**Elbow disarticulation / transhumeral** — two joints → heavy · best = **hybrid** myoelectric + body-powered + switch components

**Proximal transhumeral / shoulder disarticulation** — no lever arm → **universal shoulder joint** positioned with contralateral arm + lightweight hybrids · many choose **cosmetic only**

| Component | Detail |
|---|---|
| Passive terminal device | More cosmetic, less functional |
| Active terminal device | More functional · hooks / hands with cables or myoelectric |
| **Hand-like device** | Thumb, index, long finger · gloved · **office worker** |
| **Non-hand (hook, pincer)** | Task tools, quick release · **physical labour** |
| **Voluntary opening** | Closed at rest, opens with proximal contraction · **more common** |
| Voluntary closing | Open at rest · **heavier, less durable** |
| Quick-disconnect wrist | Swap specialised devices |
| Locking wrist | Prevents rotation during lift |
| **Wrist flexion unit** | **Bilateral amputees**, on **longer** limb → midline tasks (shaving, buttoning) |
| **Rigid elbow hinge** | **Short transradial**, no pronation/supination, elbow flexion kept |
| **Flexible elbow hinge** | Wrist disarticulation / long transradial with pronation/supination |

**Grips** — precision (pincer) · tripod (3-jaw chuck) · lateral (key) · hook power · spherical

### Prosthetic foot selection

| Foot | Features |
|---|---|
| **Single axis** | Ankle hinge → **DF + PF only** · poor durability, cosmesis |
| **SACH** (solid ankle cushioned heel) | Rigid wood keel, foam · **low activity / transfers** · being phased out · **overloads contralateral foot** |
| **Dynamic response (energy storing)** | Keel = spring → DF, ↓ contralateral load, push-off · standard for most |
| **Articulating dynamic response** | ✓ **Inversion, eversion, rotation** · absorbs shear · **uneven terrain** (multi-axis — BK amputee walking uneven ground) |
| Non-articulating dynamic response | Spring but no inversion/eversion |
| **Short keel** | Less responsive · **moderate activity, hiking** |
| **Long keel** | **High-demand athletes** |
| Shank | Structural support · endo/exoskeletal · **lever arm after transmetatarsal amputation** |

**SACH vs dynamic response** — SACH: **less dorsiflexion** · ↑ **contralateral GRF** · ↓ **max gait velocity** · **less frequent replacement** · not longer midstance (dynamic foot has **longer midstance**)

![SACH foot](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9072-sach.jpg)

**Hiker on SACH** — ✓ **short-keel articulated dynamic-response foot**\
✗ Wrong options: single axis · non-articulated · long keel

**Partial foot / toe amputation, short stance, poor toe-off** — ✓ **full-length steel shank + rocker sole** (shank restores lever arm, rocker → heel–toe transfer) · (✗ Wrong options: solid AFO, HKAFO, UCBL) · rocker may destabilise knee

### Prosthetic knees

| Knee | Indication | Features |
|---|---|---|
| **Polycentric (4-bar)** | Transfemoral, **knee disarticulation**, **bilateral** | **Variable centre of rotation** · controlled flexion · moderately fast pace · supports more weight · shortens limb at step initiation (↓ stumble) · **heavier, costly, more maintenance** |
| **Stance-phase control (weight-activated)** | **Older patients**, proximal amputation, uneven terrain | Constant friction in swing · locks with weight-bearing |
| **Fluid (hydraulic / pneumatic)** | **Active** patients accepting weight | **Variable cadence** (piston) · ↓ excess flexion · extends earlier |
| **Constant friction (single axis)** | General · uneven terrain · **commonest paediatric** · not old/weak | Hinge + friction · **light, durable, cheap** · **single speed** · stance stability from alignment |
| Variable friction (cadence control) | — | Multiple pads · variable speed · not durable |
| Manual locking | — | Constant friction + extension lock |

**Polycentric centre of rotation** — posterior to weight line → stance control, harder flexion · anterior → easier flexion, less control \
✗ Wrong options: **"fixed anterior to weight-bearing line"**

![Polycentric knee](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9072-polycentric.jpg)

### Transfemoral sockets & osseointegration (OPRA) (TO)

**Socket** — stump–prosthesis interface · preparatory socket re-adjusted as oedema resolves · **PTB socket commonest for BKA** · transfemoral/quadrilateral → **hard to keep femur adducted** (allows 10° adduction, 5° flexion)

| Transfemoral socket (TO) | Weight bearing | Suspension |
|---|---|---|
| **Quadrilateral** | **Ischial tuberosity** on posterior shelf | Suction ± belts |
| **Ischial containment** | Whole stump surface · medial wall contains **ischial ramus** · more comfort/control | Suction |

**Quadrilateral socket used in** — ✓ **transfemoral amputation** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: Syme's, BKA, transhumeral

**Suspension** — belts, wedges, straps, suction · **silicone liner = airtight seal**

**Pylon** — tube linking socket and terminal device · exoskeleton (hard shell) / endoskeleton (metal frame, soft cover)

**Osseointegration (OPRA)** — stem in residual bone + percutaneous post → direct skeletal attachment **(TO)**

**OI advantages (TO)** — no socket problems (skin breakdown, fit, pain) · may ↓ disuse **osteoporosis** + contralateral arthritis · **short residual limb**, HO, scar, multiple amputations · **osseoperception** (terrain sensing) · ↑ wear time · **easier** donning/doffing **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: "no donning/doffing needed"

| OPRA contraindications (FDA) (TO) |
|---|
| **Incomplete skeletal growth** · atypical anatomy · <2 mm cortex around implant |
| **Osteoporosis** |
| **Age >65 or <22** · **weight >220 lb** (with prosthesis) · pregnancy |
| Non-compliance · severe PVD · complicated DM · residual-limb skin disease |
| Neuropathy, severe phantom pain · active infection / dormant bacteria |
| ✓ **NOT a contraindication: malignancy as cause of amputation** |

### Orthoses & splints (TO)

**GRAFO** — ✓ **all**: **Newton's third law** · **accelerates knee extension in midstance** (weak quadriceps) · **compensates weak gastrocnemius–soleus** **(TO)**

**Turnbuckle splint** — gradual stretch of contracted joint · **Volkmann's ischaemic contracture, stiff elbow** · ↓ rehab time, good compliance **(TO)**

### Physical modalities — TENS (TO)

**TENS frequencies** — **<10 Hz, high intensity → muscle contraction** · **>50 Hz, low intensity → paraesthesia** without contraction **(TO)**

**Indications** — ✓ **generalised muscle pain**, LBP, spasm, myofascial, arthritic, neurogenic, post-surgical, diabetic neuropathy, dysmenorrhoea · not acute **fracture pain** (no proven benefit) **(TO)**

**Contraindications** — **pregnancy, epilepsy, pacemaker** · pads not over eyes, trans-cerebral, anterior neck, ant + post chest together, broken skin, tumour, spine · ✓ **metal implants are NOT a contraindication** (insufficient heating) **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: "relative"

## 6 Complications

**Choke syndrome** — socket too tight → venous obstruction · acute: red, indurated, **orange-peel** skin · chronic: **haemosiderin**, venous stasis ulcers

**Skin** — **contact dermatitis** (liner, socks, suspension → remove item) · cysts + sweating = **excess shear / poor fit** · scar massage

**Painful residual limb** — HO · bony prominence · poor fit · **neuroma** · thin soft tissue

### Transtibial prosthesis alignment & gait problems

| Problem | Cause / effect |
|---|---|
| **Swing-phase pistoning** | **Ineffective suspension** |
| **Stance-phase pistoning** | **Poor socket fit** / volume change (change stump sock) |
| **Inset (medialised) foot** | **Varus strain** · **circumduction** · pain **medial-proximal + lateral-distal** |
| **Outset (lateralised) foot** | **Valgus strain** · **broad-based gait** · pain **lateral-proximal + medial-distal** (e.g. lateral knee + medial distal stump → ✓ **reduce lateral offset**) |
| **Anterior foot** | Stable ↑ **knee extension** + patellar pain |
| **Posterior foot** | Unstable ↑ knee flexion · **drop-off / buckling** |
| Dorsiflexed foot | ↑ patellar pressure · drop-off |
| Plantar-flexed foot | Drop-off + ↑ patellar pressure (per Orthobullets) |
| **Heel too soft** | ↑ **knee extension** |
| **Heel too hard** | ↑ **knee flexion** + toe external rotation |

![Inset vs outset foot](../../MCQ/attachments/q23-illa-prosthesis-inset-outset-1.jpg)

**Drop-off (knee buckling in terminal stance)** — body weight over MTPs too early · caused by ↓ anterior support: DF foot, **posterior foot**, soft keel, **posterior toe break** → ✓ **move foot anterior**

## How it's asked

**"Inset BKA foot — EXCEPT?"** → **increased knee extension in stance**\
✗ Wrong options: varus strain · circumduction · medial-proximal pain

**"Drop-off in terminal stance — fix?"** → **move foot more anterior**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsiflex · move posterior · softer keel

**"Toe amputation, short stance, irregular toe-off?"** → **full-length steel shank + rocker sole**

**"Lateral knee + medial distal stump pain after BKA?"** → **reduce lateral offset of foot**

**"Hiking BKA amputee struggling on SACH?"** → **short-keel articulated dynamic-response foot**

**"SACH vs dynamic response — EXCEPT?"** → **longer midstance**\
✗ Wrong options: less replacement · less DF · ↑ contralateral GRF

**"Multi-axis articulated foot recommended for?"** → **BKA walking on uneven ground**

**"Most important factor choosing LL prosthesis?"** → **functional status**

**"Polycentric fluid knee vs constant friction — EXCEPT?"** → **lighter weight**

**"Polycentric hydraulic knee — EXCEPT?"** → **centre of rotation fixed anterior to weight line**

**"Body-powered vs myoelectric — EXCEPT?"** → **decreased harnessing**\
✗ Wrong options: heavy labour, lighter, less therapy, less maintenance

**"Myoelectric advantage — EXCEPT?"** → **more sensory feedback**

**"Least ↑ metabolic demand?"** → **traumatic transtibial**

**"Swing-through gait used by?"** → **SCI with paraparesis** **(TO)**

**"Crutch gait in video (legs swung to crutches)?"** → **swing-to gait**

**"TTWB after pilon ORIF — device?"** → **double axillary crutches**

**"GRAFO?"** → **all of the above** (Newton's 3rd law, knee extension, weak calf) **(TO)**

**"Identify splint (gradual stretch of contracture)?"** → **turnbuckle splint** **(TO)**

**"Quadrilateral socket used in?"** → **transfemoral amputation** **(TO)**

**"NOT a contraindication to OPRA?"** → **malignancy as cause of amputation** **(TO)**

**"NOT a feature of bone-anchored prosthesis?"** → **no donning/doffing required** **(TO)**

**"True about TENS?"** → **used for generalised muscle pain** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: CI with metal · fracture pain · pacemaker relative

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Renal Osteodystrophy

*Basic Science · 91 / 108*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — metabolic bone disease of **chronic renal insufficiency** · deficient mineralisation from electrolyte + endocrine abnormalities → osteomalacia, osteonecrosis, pathological fracture

### Labs & pathophysiology (TO)

**Key step** — ✓ **uraemia-related phosphate retention**\
✗ Wrong options: PTH receptor sensitivity · metabolic alkalosis · dialysis electrolyte shifts

```
Chronic renal failure
├─ ↓ PO₄ excretion → hyperphosphataemia → Ca-PO₄ precipitates → ↓ Ca
├─ ↓ 1α-hydroxylase (PO₄ inhibits it) → ↓ 1,25-D → ↓ Ca → OSTEOMALACIA
└─ ↓ Ca + ↑ PO₄ → chief cell hyperplasia → SECONDARY HYPERPARATHYROIDISM
       └─ autonomous gland → hypercalcaemia → TERTIARY HYPERPARATHYROIDISM
```

**Tertiary hyperparathyroidism (TO)** — prolonged stimulation → gland autonomous, insensitive to Ca/PO₄/vit D → **hypercalcaemia** **(TO)**

### Classification

| | **High turnover (high PTH)** | **Low turnover (normal PTH)** |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | Chronic ↑ PO₄ → **secondary hyperparathyroidism** (PO₄ ↓ Ca, ↓ 1α-hydroxylase, directly ↑ PTH) | **Aluminium deposition** in bone |
| Mechanism | Chief cell hyperplasia | ↓ osteoblast differentiation/proliferation · **↓ PTH release** · disrupted mineralisation |
| **Ca** | **↓** | ↑ |
| **PO₄** | **↑** | Normal |
| **ALP** | **↑** | ↑ |
| **PTH** | **↑** | **Normal** |

### Genes & lab markers

**Newly diagnosed** — ✓ **↑ PTH, ↑ PO₄ (hyperphosphataemia), ↓ Ca** · ↓ active vit D · low urinary Ca + PO₄ **(TO)**

**ALP ↑ in (TO)** — ✓ **renal osteodystrophy** · rickets (all) · osteomalacia · Paget's · primary hyperparathyroidism · CRF with 2° HPT · lytic neoplasms **(TO)**

**ALP normal (TO)** — osteoporosis · **osteopetrosis** · fibrous dysplasia · **multiple myeloma** · hypoparathyroidism **(TO)**

**ALP ↓ (TO)** — achondroplasia · **hypophosphatasia** · cretinism **(TO)**

| Lab pattern (Ca / PO₄ / ALP / PTH) | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| ↓ / ↑ / ↑ / ↑ | ✓ **High-turnover renal osteodystrophy** |
| ↑ / N / ↑ / N | Low-turnover renal osteodystrophy |
| ↓ / ↓ / ↑ / ↑ | Nutritional (vit D deficiency) rickets |
| ↓ / ↑ / N / ↓ | Hypoparathyroidism |
| ↑ / N / N–↑ / ↑ | Primary hyperparathyroidism |

**Histology** — thinned trabeculae · extreme bone turnover · **amyloid** pink on **Congo red**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — weakness · bone pain · **pathological fracture (commonest complication)** · deformity · growth retardation (children)

**Hypocalcaemia** — abdominal pain · cramps · dyspnoea · seizures · mental status change · **Trousseau** (carpopedal spasm with BP cuff) · **Chvostek** (facial twitch on tapping facial nerve)

| Orthopaedic manifestations |
|---|
| **Osteomalacia** (adults) · growth retardation (children) |
| **AVN** |
| Tendinitis, **tendon rupture** |
| **Carpal tunnel** — **β2-microglobulin amyloid** |
| **Pathological fracture** — **brown tumours** (HPT) or amyloid |
| Osteomyelitis, septic arthritis |

**Deformity in children (TO)** — ✓ **genu valgum** commonest (TO; ROD occurs later than nutritional rickets' physiological-varus age) · Orthobullets lists **femoral varus** — read the options · others: genu varum, **SCFE**, ankle valgus/varus, coxa vara, bowing, scoliosis, osteonecrosis, OCD, brown tumour **(TO)**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **Looser zones** · **brown tumour** · **osteosclerosis** (mineralised osteomalacic bone) → **rugger jersey spine** (alternating sclerotic + lytic bands) · soft-tissue / **tumoral calcinosis** · chondrocalcinosis · osteopenia · fracture

![Rugger jersey spine](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9030-rugger-jersey-spine.jpg)

**Children** — **widened physis + zone of provisional calcification** · physeal **widening, bowing, cupping** · varus femurs (e.g. 9-yr-old, <5th centile height, femoral bowing → ✓ **renal osteodystrophy**; ✗ Wrong options: XLH less likely at that onset · fibrous dysplasia — no ground glass, OI — no fractures)

**CT** — osseous resorption · may **mimic bone tumour**

![Brown tumour](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9030-brown.jpg)

**Osteitis fibrosa cystica** — multiple cysts from PTH resorption **(TO)**

### Metaphyseal bands (TO)

| Dense metaphyseal bands — causes (TO) |
|---|
| **Chronic anaemia** — sickle cell, thalassaemia |
| **Chemotherapy** — methotrexate · treated leukaemia |
| Growth arrest/recovery lines — asthma, DM, CF, JIA, malnutrition |
| **Heavy metals** — **lead (commonest)**, bismuth, arsenic, phosphorus, fluoride, mercury |
| Healing **renal osteodystrophy** · healing rickets · **bisphosphonates** · neonatal normal variant |
| ✓ **Hypervitaminosis D** — rarest → "not seen" answer |

## 5 Management

**Treatment** — treat underlying renal disease / relieve urological obstruction

## How it's asked

**"Metaphyseal bands NOT seen in?"** → **hypervitaminosis D** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: lead, methotrexate, sickle cell

**"Serum ALP increases in?"** → **renal osteodystrophy** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: osteopetrosis, myeloma, hypophosphatasia

**"Labs in new renal osteodystrophy?"** → **↑ PTH, hyperphosphataemia, hypocalcaemia**

**"Key step causing osteomalacia in renal osteodystrophy?"** → **uraemia-related phosphate retention**

**"High-turnover renal osteodystrophy labs?"** → **↓ Ca, ↑ PO₄, ↑ ALP, ↑ PTH**

**"Commonest deformity in renal osteodystrophy?"** → **genu valgum** **(TO)**

**"Dialysis patient, bilateral hip fractures, ↑ ALP + PTH, thin trabeculae?"** → **renal osteodystrophy**\
✗ Wrong options: metastasis, myeloma

**"9-yr-old, short, bilateral femoral bowing, cupped wide physes?"** → **renal osteodystrophy**\
✗ Wrong options: diastrophic dysplasia, OI, fibrous dysplasia

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Rheumatoid Arthritis

*Basic Science · 92 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest inflammatory arthritis** — **3% women, 1% men**

**Genetics** — **HLA-DR4**, HLA-DW4 · ~**15%** monozygotic twin concordance · trigger: infection or HLA locus

### Pathophysiology & serology (RF, anti-CCP, synovium)

**Immunology** — **IgM cell-mediated (T cell–MHC class II)** response → soft tissue (early) → cartilage, bone (later) \
✗ Wrong options: IgA/IgD/IgE/IgG-mediated

**Primary destructive cell** — **mononuclear cells** · cytokines **IL-1, TNF-α** (+ IL-6)

**Cascade** — Ag–Ab reactions → microvascular proliferation/obstruction → **pannus** (prominent **intimal hyperplasia**) → subluxation, chondrocyte death → **tenosynovitis + tendon rupture**

| RA synovium | |
|---|---|
| ✓ **Intimal hyperplasia** | 2 → **10–20 cell layers** |
| ✓ **↓ Apoptosis** of lining | |
| ✓ **↑ Angiogenesis** | |
| ✓ **Abundant lymphocytes** | Perivascular lymphoid follicles |
| ✗ Wrong option: **basement membrane disruption** | Synovium **has no true basement membrane** |

**Pannus** — invasive granulation tissue · fibroblast-like synoviocytes, few inflammatory cells · anchorage-independent growth → directly destroys cartilage

**Bone erosion** — ✓ **TNF + IL-6 (+ IL-1) blockade slows erosion** · TNF mobilises osteoclast precursors · **M-CSF + RANKL** drive **osteoclast** differentiation · **denosumab** slows erosion, not inflammation · TNF ↑ **Dkk-1** → blocks **Wnt** → ↓ repair (Wnt interference ↑ erosion) · erosion ∝ synovitis · steroids, MTX, leflunomide bone-sparing via ↓ synovitis\
✗ Wrong options: "synovial fibroblasts resorb"

### Genes & lab markers

**Rheumatoid factor** — ✓ **IgM antibody against Fc of native IgG** · immune complexes deposit (kidney)\
✗ Wrong options: IgG vs IgM · IgA vs IgG

**RF positivity** — **75–80%** (Orthobullets) · **80–90%** (OB6543) — read the options · sensitive, not specific · also non-RA autoimmunity

**Anti-CCP** — ✓ **most sensitive + specific** · sensitivity **50–75%**, specificity **90–95%** · as sensitive as, more specific than RF · **high levels → erosive disease** · may precede arthritis · anti-CCP ⊂ **ACPA** · ACPA bind citrullinated vimentin on osteoclasts → TNF → osteoclastogenesis · **independently predicts erosion**\
✗ Wrong options: "synovitis but not erosion"

**Other** — **anti-MCV** · ↑ ESR · ↑ CRP · **ANA in 30–40%** · joint fluid: **↓ complement**, ± RF

| Autoantibody | Disease |
|---|---|
| ✓ **Anti-IgG (RF)** | **RA** |
| Anti-dsDNA | SLE |
| Anti-ACh receptor | Myasthenia gravis |
| **Anti-topoisomerase I** | **Scleroderma** |
| Anti-GAD | Type 1 DM |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — insidious **morning stiffness** · symmetric **polyarthropathy** of hands + feet · **DIP spared** · also knees, C-spine, elbows, ankles, shoulders

**Rheumatoid nodules** — **commonest extra-articular manifestation** · **20%** (Orthobullets presentation) / **25%** (finger section) · **strongly RF+**, **aggressive disease** · over IP joints, **olecranon**, ulnar forearm · may form sinus · treat steroid injection / excision (cosmesis, pain, biopsy)

**Systemic** — rheumatoid vasculitis · pericarditis · pulmonary disease

| Syndrome | Features |
|---|---|
| **Felty** | RA + **splenomegaly + leukopenia** |
| **Still's** | Acute RA + **fever, rash, splenomegaly** |
| **Sjögren** | Exocrine glands → ↓ saliva + tears · lymphoid proliferation |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **periarticular erosions** + **osteopenia** · symmetric joint-space narrowing · **central glenoid erosion** · **protrusio acetabuli**

**Protrusio acetabuli** — femoral head medial to **teardrop** · RA (bilateral + multijoint on bone scan → ✓ RA) · Marfan · Paget · Otto pelvis (**arthrokatadysis** = primary protrusio, with osteomalacia, no other joints)\
✗ Wrong options: DDH

![RA hand — periarticular erosions, MCP subluxation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9085-ra-hand-xray-ap-left-moved.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria — 2010 ACR/EULAR (TO)

**Target** — ≥1 joint with definite synovitis not better explained · ✓ **score ≥6/10 = RA** · <6 → reassess, may accumulate **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: ">8"

| Domain (TO) | Points |
|---|---|
| **Joints** — 1 large | 0 |
| 2–10 large | 1 |
| 1–3 small | 2 |
| 4–10 small | 3 |
| **>10 joints (≥1 small)** | **5** — greatest weight |
| **Serology** — RF− and anti-CCP− | 0 |
| Low +ve RF or anti-CCP (1–3× ULN) | 2 |
| High +ve (≥3× ULN) | 3 |
| **Acute phase** — abnormal CRP or ESR | 1 |
| **Duration ≥6 wk** | 1 |

**Excluded joints (TO)** — **hand DIP**, **1st CMC**, **1st MTP** **(TO)**

**1987 ARA criteria** — ≥4 of 7 for **6 wk**: morning stiffness **≥1 h** · swelling **≥3 joints** · nodules · hand X-ray erosions/decalcification · **symmetric** arthritis · serum RF · hand (MCP, PIP) + wrist arthritis

### Post-viral (chikungunya) arthritis (TO)

**Phases (TO)** — acute <3 mo (viraemic 5–10 d, sub-acute 6–21 d) · **chronic >3 mo** — RA-like, SpA, fibromyalgia patterns · chronic in **14–25%** (age >45, viral load >10⁹/mL) · neuropathic pain ~20% **(TO)**

**Tests (TO)** — RT-PCR days 0–7 · IgM from 5–10 d (peak 3 wk, lasts 2–3 mo) · IgG after 1 wk, lasts years · chronic → check **RF, anti-CCP, HLA-B27** **(TO)**

| Phase (TO) | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Acute | Rest, fluids, **paracetamol** · avoid NSAIDs until **dengue excluded**, aspirin, steroids <3 wk |
| Sub-acute | Low-dose prednisolone (10 mg × 5 d, taper) for synovitis · HCQ for persistent symptoms |
| ✓ **Chronic (>3 mo, e.g. 18 mo)** | ✓ **MTX + sulfasalazine + HCQ** (combination > HCQ alone) · TNFi if resistant |

## 5 Management

### DMARDs & biologics (TNF, IL-1, CTLA4, CD20)

**Approach** — aggressive early DMARDs > pyramid · advances → ↓ surgery

| Drug | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Methotrexate** | **Folate analogue — inhibits DHFR** · ↓ neovascularisation · adenosine release · ✓ **+ tetracycline (doxycycline) more effective** (anti-collagenase) |
| **Leflunomide** | **Pyrimidine synthesis inhibitor** |
| Sulfasalazine | Unknown · ↓ ESR/CRP · ↓ prostaglandins, leukotrienes |
| Hydroxychloroquine | Blocks **toll-like receptors** → ↓ dendritic cells |
| Gold | Inhibits monocytes |
| D-penicillamine | — |
| **Etanercept** | **TNF receptor (p75/type II)–IgG1 Fc fusion protein** binding TNF-α · SC · RA, JRA, PsA, psoriasis, AS |
| **Infliximab** | **Human–mouse chimeric anti-TNF-α mAb** · opportunistic infection, malignancy |
| Adalimumab · golimumab | Human anti-TNF-α mAb |
| Certolizumab | Pegylated anti-TNF-α |
| **Anakinra** | **Recombinant IL-1 receptor antagonist** (IL-1RI) · refractory / 2nd–4th line |
| **Rituximab** | **Anti-CD20 → B cells** · not TNF · with MTX after TNF failure |
| **Abatacept** | **Binds CD80/CD86 (co-stimulation) → T cells** |
| Ustekinumab | Anti-**IL-12/IL-23** |
| **Tocilizumab** | **IL-6 receptor inhibitor** · 2nd line after TNF failure |
| Cyclosporine | Calcineurin inhibitor |

**TNF blockers** — revolutionised RA (also JRA, SpA, CRMO, histiocytosis X) · ↓ pannus, cartilage loss, osteoclastic resorption · risks: **latent TB reactivation**, infection, **lymphoma**

![Ulnar drift at the MCP joints](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9085-ulnar-drift.jpg)

### Perioperative medication management & infection risk

| Medication | Perioperative rule |
|---|---|
| NSAIDs | Stop **5 half-lives** before (aspirin 7–10 d; naproxen 3 d) |
| Steroids | Stress dose by surgical stress |
| **Methotrexate · sulfasalazine · HCQ · penicillamine** | ✓ **Continue** |
| Leflunomide | Continue (minor) · stop 1–2 d before major, restart 1–2 wk |
| **Etanercept** | Hold **1 wk** (OB3092 key) · **2 wk** before THA (2017 ACR/AAHKS, OB212995; Orthobullets complications) · 4 wk (Keith) · plan at end of dosing interval +1 wk — read the options |
| Adalimumab | Stop **10 days** before |
| Infliximab | Lowest level **2 wk before next infusion** |
| **Rituximab** | Stop **7 months** before major surgery |
| **Ustekinumab** | Skip next dose + 1 wk (~**13 wk** from last) · restart **>14 d** → ↓ **wound complications** |
| Anakinra | Continue minor · stop 1–2 d major · restart 10 d |

**Infection** — ✓ **prior SSI = most significant risk factor** (✗ Wrong options: age, hardware, sulfasalazine, infliximab) · ✓ **etanercept** shown to ↑ post-op infection (✗ Wrong options: naproxen, leflunomide, sulfasalazine, aspirin) · immunosuppression effect overall controversial

### Cervical spine instability & preoperative screening

**C-spine** — involved in **90%**, often missed · ✓ **pre-op C-spine X-rays with flexion–extension** before any arthroplasty (anaesthetic death risk > shoulder MRI) · JRA (polyarticular/systemic) → **C-spine assessment most important**

| Pattern | |
|---|---|
| **Atlantoaxial subluxation** | **Commonest** |
| **Basilar invagination** | Cranial migration of dens |
| **Subaxial subluxation** | Multilevel listhesis (staircase), midcervical kyphosis |

**PADI (posterior atlanto-dens interval)** — ✓ **<14 mm → fusion** even if asymptomatic (predicts neuro progression)

![Atlantoaxial instability — PADI](../../MCQ/attachments/q6220-a-35.jpg)

### Hand & wrist (extensor tendon rupture, tendon transfers, wrist fusion) (TO)

**Upper-limb reconstruction order (TO)** — ✓ **hand/wrist > shoulder > elbow** · lower limb before upper · LL order **forefoot > hip > knee > hindfoot > ankle** **(TO)**

**Ulnar drift (wind-swept) (TO)** — ✓ **ulnar displacement of extensors + radial collateral ligament attenuation** · radial sagittal band fails → EDC into ulnar valleys · wrist radial deviation → ulnar bowstring (zigzag) · ECU subluxes volar-ulnar **(TO)**

```
Ulnar drift at MCP
├─ Early, flexible → SYNOVECTOMY + EXTENSOR CENTRALISATION + INTRINSIC RELEASE (cross-intrinsic)
├─ Late / fixed → SILICONE (SWANSON) MCP ARTHROPLASTY
│      (correct radially deviated wrist at same time · satisfaction ~70%)
└─ Thumb MCP without IP disease → MCP FUSION
```

| Finger deformity | Pathoanatomy | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Boutonnière** | PIP synovitis → **central slip** attenuation · lateral bands **volar** · **oblique** retinacular contracture → DIP hyperextension | Splint (flexible) · central slip imbrication / **Fowler distal tenotomy** (moderate) · PIP fusion / arthroplasty (rigid) |
| **Swan neck** | **Terminal tendon** rupture (DIP) · **FDS, volar plate, collaterals** attenuate · lateral bands **dorsal** · **transverse** retinacular attenuation, triangular ligament contracture | Splint · **FDS tenodesis** / proximal Fowler (flexible) · releases (rigid) |
| **Arthritis mutilans** | RA or PsA · pencil-in-cup, wind-chime fingers | Interposition graft + fusion |

| Nalebuff thumb | Deformity | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I — Boutonnière** | ✓ **Most common** · **MCP flexion + IP (hyper)extension** · EPB attenuation, EPL subluxes volar **(TO)** | ✓ **Restore MCP extension + stabilise IP** **(TO)** · synovectomy + EPL rerouting (early) · **MCP fusion 10–15°** (late, most reliable) · IP fusion if fixed |
| II — Boutonnière + CMC | Uncommon / very rare | As I and III |
| **III — Swan neck** | **CMC adduction/subluxation + MCP hyperextension + IP flexion** | **Address CMC first** — trapeziectomy/LRTI · MCP >30° → volar capsulodesis or fusion · first web release |
| **IV — Gamekeeper** | Adductor contracture + **UCL laxity** · P1 radial deviation | Flexible: synovectomy, UCL reconstruction, adductor release · fixed: MCP arthroplasty / fusion |
| V | MCP hyperextension — **volar plate laxity** (swan neck with MCP disease) | Volar capsulodesis (MCP in flexion) |
| VI | **Skeletal collapse (mutilans)** | Arthrodeses |

**Type I vs III (TO)** — CMC normal → I · CMC subluxed + adducted metacarpal → III **(TO)**

| Flexor problem | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Triggering | Synovectomy + **FDS resection** |
| **Mannerfelt** — **FPL** rupture (commonest flexor) on **scaphoid spur** in carpal tunnel | **FDS4 → FPL** transfer + spur excision · graft · IP fusion |
| FDP rupture | Synovectomy + DIP fusion |
| FDS rupture | Observe |

**Extensor rupture frequency** — **EDM > EDC ring > EDC small > EPL** · ✓ **extensor digiti quinti most at risk** (silent — EDC still extends small finger)

**Vaughan-Jackson** — ✓ **rupture of digital extensors ulnar → radial** · **DRUJ instability + volar carpal subluxation → dorsal ulnar head** → attrition · **EDM first** · **painless**, no warning · treat **EIP → EDC transfer + distal ulna resection**

| Ruptured | Transfer |
|---|---|
| EPL | **EIP → EPL** |
| EDQM alone | Leave |
| EDQM + EDC5 | **EIP → EDC5** or EDC piggyback |
| EDQM + EDC5 + EDC4 | EIP → EDQM + EDC4 side-to-side to EDC3 |
| Multiple | Palmaris graft, **FDS** |

**EIP transfer** — ✓ **no index extension deficit** (cut proximal to sagittal band)

**FDS (middle/ring) → EDC (Nalebuff–Patel)** — ✓ **radially around radius, dorsally** (easy, avoids dorsal synovitis, corrects ulnar deviation)\
✗ Wrong options: through interosseous membrane

**Radial sagittal band failure** — extensor slips ulnar + volar to MCP axis\
✗ Wrong options: active extension but ✓ **holds if passively extended** → ✓ **sagittal band (extensor hood) reconstruction** (Watson — distally based central-third slip)

| Loss of MCP extension | Clue |
|---|---|
| **Extensor rupture** | not tenodesis · can't hold passive extension |
| **Sagittal band rupture** | Holds passive extension |
| **PIN palsy** (elbow/radiocapitellar synovitis) | ✓ **tenodesis intact**, can't hold, wrist extends radially, no ulnar drift → ✓ **EMG/NCS confirms** (✗ Wrong options: exploration · elbow MRI) |
| MCP volar subluxation | Ulnar deviation, X-ray |
| Trigger finger | Catching |

**Caput ulnae** — **DRUJ synovitis** → ECU subsheath stretch → ECU subluxes → carpus supinates away from ulna → **volar carpal subluxation** → extensor rupture · treat **Darrach** (+ ECU stabilisation / retinacular flap) · hemiresection · **Sauvé-Kapandji** (preserves TFCC, younger)

**Radiocarpal destruction** — **supination**, radial deviation, **ulnar + volar translocation** of carpus · zigzag with MCP ulnar drift

| Wrist | Indication |
|---|---|
| Synovectomy ± **ECRL → ECU (Clayton)** | Early |
| ✓ **Radiolunate (Chamay) / RSL fusion** | **Radiocarpal (radioscaphoid) arthritis, preserved midcarpal, good arc (e.g. 80°)** |
| Four-corner fusion | Isolated midcarpal |
| **Total wrist fusion** | Pancarpal, limited arc (20°), poor bone · **gold standard** · often + Darrach |
| Total wrist arthroplasty | Sedentary, good bone, reasonable motion |

### Elbow (synovectomy, total elbow arthroplasty)

| Elbow procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| Medical + **steroid injection** | First line |
| **Arthroscopic / open synovectomy** | Pain **without instability**, ROM preserved (arc >90°) · **early** (Larsen 3 → ↑ reoperation; 5-yr reoperation-free 77%) |
| **Synovectomy + radial head excision** (lateral approach) | **Radiocapitellar** focus · **PIN compression from radial head synovitis** (wrist/thumb extension weakness) |
| Interposition arthroplasty (Achilles, fascia, dermis) | **Young active**, not TEA candidates |
| ✓ **Semiconstrained TEA** | **End-stage**, pain, loss of motion, instability · elderly · **5 lb lifting limit** · (✗ Wrong options: unconstrained in inflammatory arthritis)|
| Arthrodesis / resection | Salvage only |

**Synovectomy indications** — preserved joint space · young on DMARD · early disease · heavy work (rules out TEA) · **female gender** is not an indication

### Shoulder · hip · knee

**Shoulder** — **>90%** develop symptoms · **rotator cuff tears** (large, irreparable → poorer arthroplasty, glenoid loosening) · central glenoid wear, osteopenia, cysts

**Hip** — **protrusio acetabuli**

**Knee synovectomy** — ↓ pain + swelling · does not halt radiographic progression, prevent TKA or ↑ ROM · synovium re-forms then becomes rheumatoid

**TKA** — ✓ **resurface patella** in RA · unresurfaced → ↑ **anterior knee pain** · resurfaced → ↑ extensor rupture, patellar clunk

### Forefoot & midfoot reconstruction

**Rheumatoid foot** — bilateral, symmetric · **forefoot first** · HLA-DR4 · ~90% develop forefoot deformity (hallux valgus, hammer toes, lesser MTP dislocation)

**Earliest forefoot sign** — **MTP synovitis** → MTP hyperextension · distal fat-pad migration · plantar callosities · ulcers

**Gold standard** — ✓ **1st MTP arthrodesis + lesser metatarsal head resections** (+ hammertoe correction — **Hoffman-Clayton**) · Coughlin & Mann **90%** good/excellent

| ✗ Wrong option | Why |
|---|---|
| **Keller** arthroplasty | Unstable platform, recurrence, poorer function |
| 1st MTP replacement | Unreliable long-term |
| Bunionectomy / chevron / realignment | Doesn't stabilise ray or address arthritis · osteotomies only if no erosions |
| **Lapidus** | TMT fusion — does not address MTP |
| Weil osteotomies | do not address erosive lesser MTPs |
| **Lesser MTP fusion** | Lateral foot needs motion |
| Lesser MTP arthroplasty | Resection preferred |

![1st MTP fusion + lesser metatarsal head resections](../../MCQ/attachments/q1029-illa-first-mtp-fusion-with-lesser.jpg)

**Talonavicular arthritis** — isolated **symmetric** TN degeneration, no trauma → ✓ **RA** · treat **fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: Köhler, OA, OCD

## How it's asked

**"RA synovium EXCEPT?"** → **basement membrane disruption**\
✗ Wrong options: intimal hyperplasia, ↓ apoptosis, angiogenesis, lymphocytes

**"RF targets which immunoglobulin?"** → **IgG**

**"RF best described as?"** → **IgM antibody against native IgG**

**"Anti-CCP used for?"** → **rheumatoid arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: AS, PsA, SLE, Reiter

**"Bone erosion in RA — true?"** → **TNF + IL-6 blockade slows erosion**\
✗ Wrong options: Wnt interference ↓ erosion · independent of synovitis

**"% adults RF positive?"** → **80–90%**

**"Ulnar drift + MCP subluxation, anti-CCP+ — immune mechanism?"** → **IgM cell-mediated**

**"Symmetric polyarthropathy + nodules — autoantibody?"** → **against IgG**

**"TNF-α therapy revolutionised?"** → **RA**

**"Biologic NOT targeting TNF?"** → **rituximab**

**"TNF blocked by?"** → **etanercept**\
✗ Wrong options: tocilizumab, anakinra, abatacept, rituximab

**"Added to MTX, better than MTX alone?"** → **doxycycline**

**"IL-1 antagonist, 2nd line?"** → **anakinra**

**"Infliximab mechanism?"** → **TNF inhibitor**\
✗ Wrong options: DHFR · CD20 · calcineurin

**"Etanercept mechanism?"** → **inhibitory binding to TNF-α**

**"DMARD on IL-1 receptor (figure)?"** → **anakinra**

**"Binds CD80/CD86?"** → **abatacept**

**"Blocks TNF–receptor binding (figure)?"** → **infliximab**

**"TKA in 2 wk on sulfasalazine, penicillamine, etanercept?"** → **continue SSZ + penicillamine, stop etanercept 1 wk before**

**"THA on etanercept + HCQ?"** → **continue HCQ, hold etanercept 2 wk**

**"Greatest SSI risk in RA foot reconstruction?"** → **prior MSSA wound infection**

**"Perioperative drug shown to ↑ infection?"** → **etanercept**

**"Why hold ustekinumab ~13 wk pre-op, restart >14 d?"** → **wound complications**

**"Most important pre-op evaluation in JRA?"** → **cervical spine assessment**

**"PADI threshold for stabilisation?"** → **14 mm**

**"RA shoulder — work-up before surgery?"** → **C-spine flexion–extension X-rays**\
✗ Wrong options: shoulder MRI

**"Multilevel subaxial listhesis + kyphosis?"** → **rheumatoid arthritis**

**"Upper-limb reconstruction order?"** → **hand > shoulder > elbow** **(TO)**

**"Wind-swept ulnar drift mechanism?"** → **ulnar extensor displacement + RCL attenuation** **(TO)**

**"Nalebuff I reconstruction goal?"** → **restore MCP extension, stabilise IP** **(TO)**

**"Can't actively extend ring finger but holds passive extension?"** → **sagittal band reconstruction**

**"Vaughan-Jackson syndrome?"** → **rupture of digital extensors**\
✗ Wrong options: FPL = Mannerfelt · caput ulnae

**"Extensor most at risk in RA wrist?"** → **extensor digiti quinti**

**"EIP transfer — effect on index?"** → **no effect**

**"FDS → EDC transfer route?"** → **radially around the radius**

**"Can't extend MCPs, tenodesis intact, can't hold passive extension — confirm with?"** → **electrodiagnostic studies** (PIN palsy)

**"Painless loss of ring + small extension — true?"** → **ulnar head prominence from DRUJ instability**\
✗ Wrong options: ring first · pain/catching · Frohse

**"Indication for RL/RSL fusion?"** → **isolated radioscaphoid arthritis, 80° arc**

**"RA forefoot, failed nonop?"** → **1st MTP fusion + lesser metatarsal head resections**\
✗ Wrong options: Keller · MTP replacement · Lapidus

**"Symmetric isolated TN arthritis, no trauma?"** → **RA**

**"End-stage RA elbow after hand reconstruction?"** → **total elbow arthroplasty**

**"NOT an indication for synovectomy ± radial head resection?"** → **female gender**

**"Early RA elbow, arc 15–110°, stable?"** → **arthroscopic synovectomy**

**"Elbow synovitis + wrist/thumb extension weakness?"** → **synovectomy + possible radial head excision**

**"Unresurfaced patella TKA in RA — more likely?"** → **anterior knee pain**\
✗ Wrong options: clunk · tendon rupture

**"Bilateral protrusio + multijoint bone scan uptake?"** → **rheumatoid arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: arthrokatadysis · DDH

**"False about 2010 RA criteria?"** → **score >8 needed** (✓ ≥6) **(TO)**

**"18 mo post-chikungunya small-joint arthritis — treatment?"** → **MTX + sulfasalazine + HCQ** **(TO)**

# Rickets

*Basic Science · 93 / 108*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — defective mineralisation of osteoid/cartilage from inadequate **Ca + PO₄** **before physeal closure** · after closure = **osteomalacia**

**Primary problem** — ✓ **low calcium** available for mineralisation (Ca/PO₄ homeostasis)\
✗ Wrong options: primarily a physeal (proliferative zone) disorder · type I collagen (OI) · EXT-1 (MHE) · dysplastic fibrous bone (FD)

**Site** — poor calcification at **zone of provisional calcification** → ↑ **physeal width** + cortical thinning/bowing · fastest-growing bones (tibia, distal femur, distal radius) · rarely hand tubular bones

**Causes** — vit D deficiency · vit D malabsorption · **renal osteodystrophy** · **X-linked hypophosphataemia** \
✗ Wrong options: **vitamin K deficiency**

**Activation** — ✓ **PTH** activates renal **1α-hydroxylase** (low Ca) → calcitriol · most potent effect: ↑ intestinal Ca absorption

### Classification

| Type | Inheritance / gene | Mechanism | Age |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Vit D-resistant (familial hypophosphataemic, XLH)** | ✓ **X-linked dominant — PHEX** (commonest **heritable** rickets) | **Renal tubules can't reabsorb PO₄** · ↑ **FGF23** → PO₄ wasting + suppressed 1α-hydroxylase · GFR normal | **1–2 yr** |
| ADHR | **AD — FGF23** (cleavage site) | ↓ FGF23 degradation · **iron deficiency triggers** expression **(TO)** | — |
| ARHR | **AR — DMP1** (also ENPP1, FAM20C) | Impaired osteocyte maturation, ↑ FGF23 | — |
| **Nutritional (vit D-deficient)** | — | ↓ intake → ↓ gut Ca → ↑ PTH → resorption → ↑ ALP | **6 mo–3 yr** |
| **VDDR I** | **AR — renal 1α-hydroxylase** (12q14; CYP27B1) | ↓ **calcitriol** | — |
| **VDDR II** | **AR — vit D receptor** (inactivating) | ↑ **calcitriol** · end-organ resistance · alopecia (2A) | — |
| HHRH | **SLC34A3** (NaPi-IIc) | FGF23-**independent** PO₄ wasting **(TO)** | — |

**XLH inheritance** — affected father → **100% daughters carriers/affected**, sons unaffected · more severe in males

**Nutritional risk factors** — premature infants · ✓ **dark-skinned (African American) breastfed >6 mo without vit D** · malabsorption (coeliac), TPN · Asian immigrants · vegetarian diets · rare since milk fortified

| FGF23-dependent hypophosphataemia (TO) | FGF23-independent (TO) |
|---|---|
| **XLH**, ADHR, ARHR · tumour-induced osteomalacia · epidermal naevus · osteoglophonic dysplasia · fibrous dysplasia · iron-infusion · **α-klotho translocation** · **Dent's** (per TO key) · NPT2a, NHERF1 mutations · post-transplant / hepatic resection | ✓ **Fanconi syndrome** · ✓ **HHRH (hereditary hypophosphataemic rickets with hypercalciuria)** |

**FGF23 (TO)** — **high** → ↓ PO₄, ↓ 1,25-D, osteomalacia · ✓ **low** → ↑ PO₄, ↑ 1,25-D, soft-tissue calcification, **hyperostosis** **(TO)** · signals via FGFR1 + **Klotho**; phosphaturia partly PTH-dependent **(TO)**

### Genes & lab markers

| Disease (Orthobullets chart) | Ca | PO₄ | ALP | PTH | 25-D | 1,25-D |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **XLH (vit D-resistant)** | **N** | **↓↓** | ↑ | **N** | N | N / inappropriately low |
| **Nutritional** | N–↓ | ↓ | ↑ | **↑** | **↓** | ↓ |
| **VDDR I** | ↓ | ↓ | ↑ | ↑ | N (TO table: ↑↑) | **↓↓** |
| **VDDR II** | ↓ | ↓ | ↑ | ↑ | N | **↑↑** (>1000 pg/mL) |
| Hypophosphatasia | ↑ | ↑ | **↓↓** | N | N | — |
| Renal osteodystrophy | ↓ | **↑** | ↑ | ↑↑ | N / ↓ | ↓↓ |
| Hyperparathyroidism | ↑ | ↓ | ↑ | ↑ | — | — |
| **HHRH (TO)** | N/↑ | ↓ | ↑ | **↓** | N | **↑** · **↓ FGF23** · **hypercalciuria** |

**Key nutritional vs XLH discriminator** — ✓ **PTH ↑ in nutritional**, **normal in XLH** · normal Ca + PTH → ✓ **XLH**

**XLH PTH** — normal (OB, Orthobullets) · N/↑ (TO table) — read the options

**Urine (TO)** — XLH/renal tubular: **↑↑ urine PO₄**, ↓ urine Ca · nutritional: ↓ urine Ca + PO₄ **(TO)**

**Routine vit D test** — ✓ **25(OH)D** · 1,25-D uninformative (normal/↑ with secondary HPT) **(TO)**

**Histology** — disordered elongated **proliferative zone** · poorly defined provisional calcification · **widened osteoid seams** · "Swiss cheese" trabeculae · collagen perpendicular to Haversian canals

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — listlessness · irritability · weakness

**Signs** — **tibial bowing** (wide proximal tibial physes) · **genu varum** (commonest) · **rachitic rosary** (costochondral enlargement) · **widened wrists** + ankles · skull flattening · **kyphosis (cat back)** · ligamentous laxity · hypotonia · waddling gait · delayed dentition, enamel defects · pathological fractures · short stature

![Tibial bowing in rickets](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9031-11-moved.jpg)

**XLH** — short stature · **genu varum** · windswept deformity · medial tibial torsion · genu valgum in some (e.g. 4-yr-old knock knees) · no myopathy

**VDDR** — like nutritional but **more severe** · type I: hypotonia, seizures, fractures in infancy · type II: + bone pain, dental caries/hypoplasia, **alopecia**

| Calcipenic vs phosphopenic (TO) | **Calcipenic** | **Phosphopenic** |
|---|---|---|
| Muscle weakness | Present | **Absent** (present in TIO) |
| Bone pain | Common | Uncommon |
| Limbs | All equal | **Predominantly lower limbs** |
| Tetany · enamel hypoplasia | May occur | Absent |
| Dental abscess | Absent | May occur |
| Ca | Low/normal | Normal |
| ALP | Markedly ↑ | Mild–moderate ↑ |
| PTH | ↑ | Normal / minimally ↑ |
| **Osteopenia, osteitis fibrosa** | Present | ✓ **Absent** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + lateral)** — ✓ **physeal widening** · **metaphyseal cupping**, irregular metaphyses · loss of zone of provisional calcification definition · osteopenia · **Looser zones** (compression side) · ✓ **anterior rib widening (rachitic rosary)** · **genu varum** · codfish vertebrae · dorsal kyphosis

![Rachitic rosary](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9031-598b04743e587575e91d114605fc6b-gallery.jpg)

| Radiographic mimic | Feature |
|---|---|
| **Hyperparathyroidism** | **Punched-out** lesions, **subperiosteal resorption**, erosion around physis edge |
| **Jansen metaphyseal chondrodysplasia** | **Rounded epiphyses**, wide epiphysis–metaphysis separation · ligand-independent **PTHR1** activation |
| **Renal osteodystrophy** | **Rugger jersey** sclerotic vertebral endplates |

**Extreme physeal widening + osteopenia + deformity** — ✓ pathognomonic for rickets → **labs (Ca, PO₄, ALP, vit D) + endocrinology first**\
✗ Wrong options: osteotomy / guided growth before medical control (high recurrence)

### Looser zones (TO)

**Looser zone** — pseudofracture; indentation of soft bone by vascular crossings **(TO)**

| Seen in (TO) | Not seen |
|---|---|
| **Osteomalacia** (characteristic) · renal osteodystrophy · fibrous dysplasia · hyperthyroidism · **Paget's** · **XLH** · OI | ✓ **Lead poisoning** |

### Oncogenic (tumour-induced) osteomalacia (TO)

**TIO** — paraneoplastic · adolescents/young adults · mesenchymal tumours secrete **FGF23 ("phosphatonin")** → phosphaturia **(TO)**

**Tumours (TO)** — **haemangiopericytoma (commonest)** · GCT · chondromyxoid fibroma · chondroblastoma · osteosarcoma · osteoblastoma · NOF / metaphyseal fibrous defect · fibrous dysplasia · MFH · fibrosarcoma · haemangioma \
✗ Wrong options: **multiple myeloma** **(TO)**

**TIO labs (TO)** — ✓ **↓ PO₄ (severe), normal Ca, low/normal 1,25-D, ↑ FGF23** · ↑ ALP · low TmP · PTH less raised than dietary osteomalacia **(TO)**

**TIO work-up + Rx (TO)** — localise: **octreotide scan**, FDG-PET/CT, MRI/CT · **excise tumour** → PO₄ normalises within hours · supplement PO₄ + vit D until normal **(TO)**

### Renal tubular rickets & Fanconi syndrome (TO)

**Renal tubular rickets (TO)** — ✓ **↑ urine PO₄, ↓ urine Ca, normal 1,25-D, ↓ serum PO₄, normal PTH** · normal Ca · low urinary pH · early infancy, severe deformity, no myopathy or hypocalcaemia · RTA, Fanconi, XLH **(TO)**

**Fanconi syndrome (TO)** — proximal tubular defect → wasting of **PO₄, amino acids, glucose, bicarbonate** **(TO)**

| Fanconi (TO) | Finding |
|---|---|
| Blood | **Metabolic acidosis** · ↓ PO₄ · ↓ 25(OH)D · ↑ ALP · ↑ PTH · (✗ Wrong options: **↑ serum Ca**) |
| Urine | ↑ PO₄ · ↑ K⁺ · calciuria |
| Bone | ↓ BMD, osteoporosis |

## 5 Management

| Type | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **XLH** | **Calcitriol + phosphate** (phosphate must be with calcitriol — prevents 2° HPT) · **burosumab** |
| **Nutritional** | **Vitamin D 5000 IU/day × 6–10 wk** |
| **VDDR I** | **Calcitriol** (physiological dose) + calcium — excellent response **(TO)** |
| VDDR 1B (25-hydroxylase) | Calcidiol **(TO)** |
| **VDDR II** | Partial resistance → **vit D** (very high calcitriol) · total → **calcium** · severe hypocalcaemia → ✓ **prolonged high-dose IV calcium** (alopecia doesn't respond) **(TO)** |
| **HHRH** | **Oral phosphate ONLY** · **active vit D contraindicated** (worsens hypercalciuria, stones) **(TO)** |
| ADHR | PO₄ + calcitriol · **iron repletion** may normalise FGF23 **(TO)** |
| ARHR | PO₄ + calcitriol · risk enthesopathy, vascular calcification (ENPP1) **(TO)** |

**Doses** — calcitriol **20–30 μg/kg/day** (children, as printed) · 0.5–0.75 μg/day adults · phosphate **20–40 mg/kg/day** in 3–5 doses (children) · 750–1000 mg/day adults

**Burosumab** — ✓ **fully human mAb neutralising circulating FGF23** → restores PO₄ reabsorption + 1α-hydroxylase · (✗ Wrong options: PHEX chaperone · Klotho agonist **(TO)**) · approved **≥1 yr** (Orthobullets) · first line **>6 months** + adults (TO) — read the options

**Conventional XLH therapy risks (TO)** — **nephrocalcinosis**, tertiary HPT → renal US · nephrocalcinosis → ✓ **switch to burosumab** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: loop diuretic · more phosphate

**XLH medical therapy** — early calcitriol + phosphate ✓ **↓ need for osteotomies**, improves height, ↓ recurrence · (✗ Wrong options: normalises mineralisation completely) · continue into **adulthood** (pain, insufficiency fractures) · (✗ Wrong options: calcium alone) · OB2930 (Loeffler): adding phosphate to vit D gave no extra benefit — read the options

### Operative criteria

| Deformity | Surgery |
|---|---|
| Growth remaining (**<11 yr**) | **Hemiepiphysiodesis / guided growth** — effective once medically treated |
| **>11 yr** / severe tibial bowing | **Multilevel corrective osteotomy** (K-wires, plates, IM nails, ex-fix) |
| Before metabolic control | No — high recurrence |

**XLH outcome** — staged surgery better, but early degenerative joint disease

## Rickets types compared

| | **Nutritional** | **XLH** | **VDDR I** | **VDDR II** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Defect | ↓ Vit D intake | PHEX → ↑ FGF23 | 1α-hydroxylase | VDR |
| Inheritance | — | XLD | AR | AR |
| PTH | ↑ | **N** | ↑ | ↑ |
| 1,25-D | ↓ | N/↓ | **↓↓** | **↑↑** |
| Treatment | Vit D | Calcitriol + PO₄ / burosumab | Calcitriol | High-dose calcitriol + Ca / IV Ca |

## How it's asked

**"Low circulating FGF23 associated with?"** → **hyperostosis** **(TO)**

**"NOT FGF23-mediated hypophosphataemic rickets?"** → **HHRH** **(TO)**

**"XLD vs nutritional rickets lab pattern?"** → **answer key not provided by source** · options: ↓P/↑ALP/normal PTH · ↓P/↑ALP/↑PTH · normal P with low ALP · gene mapping needed · per OB, XLH = ↓PO₄, ↑ALP, **normal PTH**

**"Hypophosphataemia, hyperphosphaturia, low FGF23, ↑ 1,25-D, hypercalciuria — gene?"** → **SLC34A3** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: PHEX, FGF23, CYP27B1

**"Burosumab mechanism?"** → **human mAb neutralising FGF23** **(TO)**

**"XLH on phosphate + calcitriol develops nephrocalcinosis?"** → **switch to burosumab** **(TO)**

**"Labs confirming nutritional over X-linked rickets?"** → **↓ PO₄, ↑ ALP, ↑ PTH**

**"Normal Ca + PTH found in?"** → **XLH**\
✗ Wrong options: nutritional · VDDR

**"Low PO₄ + normal Ca — common hereditary rickets?"** → **X-linked hypophosphataemic**

**"4-yr-old knock knees, ↓ PO₄, ↑ ALP, ↓ 1,25-D, normal Ca/25-D/PTH?"** → **familial hypophosphataemic rickets**\
✗ Wrong options: nutritional · renal OD

**"Deformity unresponsive to vitamins, normal Ca/PTH/vit D — inheritance?"** → **X-linked dominant**

**"Genu varum, ↓↓ PO₄, ↑ ALP, normal PTH + vit D?"** → **vitamin D-resistant rickets**\
✗ Wrong options: hypophosphatasia · renal OD

**"XLH gene?"** → **PHEX**\
✗ Wrong options: GNAS1, CLCN7, TGFBR2, COL1A1

**"3-yr-old genu valgum, coxa vara, wide physes, mother had surgeries — next?"** → **Ca, PO₄, ALP, vit D + endocrinology**\
✗ Wrong options: osteotomy · guided growth first

**"Familial hypophosphataemic rickets — true?"** → **renal tubules can't absorb phosphate**\
✗ Wrong options: 2nd commonest · ↓ physeal width · hyperphosphataemia · calcitriol normalises mineralisation

**"AD pedigree, ↓ serum PO₄, ↑ urine PO₄ — mutation?"** → **FGF23**\
✗ Wrong options: PHEX (XLD) · DMP1 (AR)

**"Man with hypophosphataemic rickets planning family — true?"** → **100% daughters carriers/affected**\
✗ Wrong options: sons carriers · FGFR3 · PTH therapy

**"PHEX child on treatment with bowing — true?"** → **Ca + PO₄ supplementation ↓ need for osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: stop at maturity · always progresses · AR

**"Genu varum, ↓ PO₄, ↑ ALP, normal Ca + 25-D — gene?"** → **PHEX**\
✗ Wrong options: COMP, COL5A1, MECP2, TNSALP

**"Vit D form measured routinely?"** → **25(OH)D** **(TO)**

**"Commonest complication of stoss vit D injections?"** → **answer key not provided by source** · options hypercalcaemia / intermittent hypercalcaemia / hyperphosphataemia / hypophosphataemia · no explanation given

**"Breastfed, no vit D, spongy gums, frontal bossing, painful chest nodules, white line of Frankel?"** → **answer key not provided by source** · options scurvy / nutritional rickets / hypophosphataemic rickets / Barton's disease · stem mixes rickets (bossing, rosary) and scurvy (gums, Frankel line) features

**"Lab consistent with nutritional rickets?"** → **↑ PTH**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ Ca, ↑ PO₄, ↓ ALP, ↑ vit D

**"Breastfed Haitian 3-yr-old with bowing — associated finding?"** → **widened wrist physes**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ PTH · ↓ ALP

**"Best radiographic characterisation of rickets?"** → **widening of anterior ribs**\
✗ Wrong options: narrow physes · rounded epiphyses (Jansen) · punched-out (HPT) · rugger jersey (renal OD)

**"NOT a cause of rickets?"** → **vitamin K deficiency**

**"US nutritional rickets commonest in?"** → **African American, breastfed** >6 mo

**"Primary problem in rickets?"** → **low calcium**

**"Nutritional rickets labs?"** → **↓ Ca + ↑ PTH**\
✗ Wrong options: normal 25-D with ↓ 1,25-D (VDDR I)

**"Vegetarian toddler with bowing + cupped physes?"** → **low vit D, low Ca, ↑ PTH, ↑ ALP**

**"1α-hydroxylase deficiency — rickets type?"** → **answer key not provided by source (TO)** · OB507: loss of 1α-hydroxylase = **VDDR type I**

**"VDDR 2A with alopecia, 1,25-D >1000, severe hypocalcaemia?"** → **prolonged high-dose IV calcium** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: rhPTH · burosumab

**"Loss of 1α-hydroxylase gene causes?"** → **hereditary VDDR type I**

**"Hormone activating 1α-hydroxylase?"** → **PTH**

**"VDDR I vs II?"** → **I = renal enzyme deficiency · II = inactivating VDR mutation**

**"Looser zone NOT seen in?"** → **lead poisoning** **(TO)**

**"Oncogenic osteomalacia — EXCEPT tumour?"** → **multiple myeloma** **(TO)**

**"TIO biochemical profile?"** → **↓ PO₄, normal Ca, low vit D, ↑ FGF23** **(TO)**

**"ADHR phenotypic trigger?"** → **iron deficiency** **(TO)**

**"NOT true of Fanconi syndrome?"** → **↓ serum PO₄ and ↑ serum Ca** **(TO)**

**"Renal tubular rickets profile?"** → **↑ urine PO₄, ↓ urine Ca, normal 1,25-D, ↓ serum PO₄, normal PTH** **(TO)**

**"NOT a feature of phosphopenic rickets?"** → **osteopenia** **(TO)**

**"Commonest deformity in renal osteodystrophy?"** → **genu valgum** **(TO)**

# Sartorius

*Basic Science · 94 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Sartorius |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | **ASIS** |
| **Insertion** | Superior medial tibial shaft near tuberosity — **pes anserinus** (with gracilis + semitendinosus) |
| **Action** | **Flexes + laterally rotates hip** · flexes knee |
| **Innervation** | **Femoral nerve (L2, L3, L4)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Muscular branches of femoral artery |

![Sartorius](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10055-sartorius.jpg)

### Femoral nerve innervation

**Femoral nerve supplies** — **sartorius** · **rectus femoris** (quadriceps) \
✗ Wrong options: obturator · tibial · peroneal · "sartorial nerve" (doesn't exist)

## How it's asked

**"Nerve to rectus femoris?"** → **femoral**\
✗ Wrong options: obturator · tibial · peroneal

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Sciatic nerve

*Basic Science · 95 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy at the hip & posterior approach

**Exit** — ✓ **greater sciatic notch** · emerges **anterior (deep) to piriformis** · passes between **piriformis and superior gemellus** (>80%) → posterior to superior gemellus, obturator internus, inferior gemellus, quadratus femoris → deep to biceps femoris

**Variants** — passes above or **pierces piriformis** · commonest rotator variant: runs **between capsule and short external rotators**, exiting below superior gemellus

**Posterior THA** — ✓ **deep to piriformis, superficial to short external rotators** · primary THA: identify by **palpation** · revision: palpation + direct visualisation

**Lesser sciatic notch** — **obturator internus tendon**\
✗ Wrong options: sciatic nerve

![Sciatic nerve course relative to piriformis and short rotators](../../MCQ/attachments/q3531-illb-nerve-course.jpg)

### Peroneal division innervation

**Thigh** — **tibial division → all hamstrings** (long head biceps, semitendinosus, semimembranosus) · ✓ **peroneal division → short head of biceps femoris only** · both gastrocnemius heads = tibial

**Peroneal division** — roots **L4–S3** (✗ Wrong options: L3–S1) · separates from tibial in upper popliteal fossa · crosses **posterior** to lateral gastrocnemius head · **deep peroneal** starts **lateral** to anterior tibial artery → crosses medial · commonest injury: **lateral** (✗ Wrong options: anterior) knee dislocation

**Localising** — thigh-level lesion → **denervates short head biceps + tibialis anterior** · knee-level lesion **spares short head biceps** → test short head biceps (EMG)

**Transection** — definitive repair at **1–3 wk** (zone of injury declares)

## How it's asked

**"Posterior THA — sciatic nerve location?"** → **deep to piriformis, superficial to short external rotators**

**"Sciatic nerve exit from pelvis?"** → **greater sciatic notch, between piriformis and superior gemellus**\
✗ Wrong options: lesser notch · obturator foramen

**"Posterolateral approach — nerve passes between?"** → **piriformis and superior gemellus**

**"Peroneal division supplies in thigh?"** → **short head of biceps femoris**

**"Peroneal division — true?"** → **thigh injury denervates short head biceps + tibialis anterior**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior knee dislocation · L3–S1

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Scleroderma

*Basic Science · 96 / 108*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — autoimmune disease of **collagen deposition + fibrosis** → hardening of skin, subcutis, muscle, internal organs

**Demographics** — **F > M (3–14:1)** · African American > Caucasian · **30–50 yr** · ✓ **rapidly progressive in children**

**Triggers** — genetic predisposition · **silica**, solvents (benzene), radiation

**Pathophysiology triad** — **collagen deposition + fibrosis** · **non-inflammatory vasculopathy** (microvascular fibroproliferation) · **autoimmunity**

### Genes & lab markers

**Genetics** — usually sporadic · HLA · **STAT4 → limited** · **IRF5 → diffuse**

| Antibody / lab | Association |
|---|---|
| **ANA** | **90–95%** · speckled / centromere · **nucleolar = specific** for systemic sclerosis |
| **Anti-centromere** | **Limited (CREST)** · ~50% |
| **Anti-Scl-70 (anti-topoisomerase I)** | **Systemic (diffuse)** · ~30% |
| Anti-histone | Also drug-induced lupus, SLE, RA |
| ESR, CRP | ↑ |
| Creatinine | Renal monitoring |
| **CXCL4** | ↑ → pulmonary fibrosis |
| **NT-proBNP** | ↑ → early pulmonary hypertension |

### Classification

| | **Limited** | **Diffuse (systemic)** |
|---|---|---|
| Skin | **Face + hands** | Widespread |
| Organs | — | **Kidney, lung, heart** |
| Course | — | **Rapidly progressive**, high mortality |
| Antibody | **Anti-centromere** | **Anti-Scl-70** |
| Subtype | **CREST** — **C**alcinosis · **R**aynaud · **E**sophageal dysmotility · **S**clerodactyly · **T**elangiectasia | — |

**Linear scleroderma (en coup de sabre)** — child with linear skin lesions + **limb atrophy** + **Achilles contracture / toe-walking** → ✓ **scleroderma** · early surgery + immunosuppression\
✗ Wrong options: arthrogryposis (non-progressive, no skin lesions) · JIA · eczema · abuse

![Linear scleroderma — en coup de sabre](../../MCQ/attachments/q2214-a-pediatric-scleroderma-en-coup-de-sabre-presenting-.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Skin** — **diffuse pruritus** · **Raynaud** (white → blue → red; cold/stress) · tightness, induration, shiny loss of wrinkles · **sclerodactyly** · digital ulcers · diuretic-resistant oedema · **hyper- + hypopigmentation** · telangiectasia · calcinosis

**Hand contractures** — **PIP flexion + compensatory MCP hyperextension** · **1st web adduction contracture**

![Sclerodactyly](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob322121-scleroderma2.jpg)

**MSK** — myalgia · arthralgia · **osteolysis (calcaneus)** · contractures, atrophy, LLD, angular malalignment (children)

**Systemic** — **acid reflux** (smooth muscle atrophy → ↓ LES pressure) · dry cough, dyspnoea (**restrictive lung disease**), dry rales · palpitations · cor pulmonale · **renal hypertension**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**CT chest** — **ground glass = early fibrosis** · honeycombing + bronchiolectasis = established interstitial fibrosis

![Honeycomb + ground glass — lung fibrosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob322121-honeycomb-and-ground-glass-appearance-indicative-of-early-lu.jpg)

**Angiography** — loss of digital flow

**Manometry** — **distal oesophageal aperistalsis + ↓ LES tone** · PFTs for early fibrosis · routine ECG

**Differential** — nephrogenic systemic fibrosis · eosinophilic fasciitis · GVHD

## 5 Management

| Problem | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Progression (esp. lung) | **Methotrexate · mycophenolate** · **cyclophosphamide** if refractory |
| **Renal involvement** | **ACE inhibitor** |
| Pruritus | Antihistamines |
| **Raynaud** | **Calcium-channel blockers** |
| Pulmonary hypertension | **Ambrisentan + tadalafil** |

### Operative criteria

| Deformity | Surgery |
|---|---|
| Symptomatic calcinosis | I&D |
| **Fixed PIP flexion** | **PIP arthrodesis** |
| **Fixed MCP hyperextension** | **MCP resection arthroplasty** |
| **1st web contracture** | **Adductor pollicis release ± trapeziectomy** |
| Digital gangrene | Amputation |

## 6 Complications

**Complications** — digital infarct/gangrene · **pulmonary hypertension** · **pulmonary fibrosis** · renal failure · pulmonary/renal/cardiac involvement = worse prognosis

## How it's asked

**"7-yr-old rapidly progressive toe-walking, limb atrophy, linear skin lesions?"** → **scleroderma**\
✗ Wrong options: arthrogryposis · JIA · abuse

**"Anti-topoisomerase I antibodies?"** → **scleroderma (diffuse)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Scurvy

*Basic Science · 97 / 108*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — severe **vitamin C** deficiency

**Epidemiology** — deficiency in 8% men, 6% women (US) · M:F 4:3 · bimodal: **infants 5–10 mo** (rare <7 mo if breastfed — breast milk has vit C) + **men >60**

**Risk factors** — elderly men living alone ("tea and toast") · overcooking · alcoholism · smokers · malabsorption (Whipple's, IBD, chemotherapy) · infants on **evaporated/condensed milk**

### Pathophysiology

**Biochemistry** — humans lack functional **L-gluconolactone oxidase** → can't make ascorbic acid · ✓ **↓ chondroitin sulfate + collagen synthesis** · impaired **hydroxylation** of collagen peptides · ↓ carnitine → myalgia

**Site** — greatest effect on **metaphysis** (physeal region) · defective **primary + secondary spongiosa** · type I collagen defect → ↓ **osteoblast** function · fragile capillaries → bleeding\
✗ Wrong options: osteoclast (osteopetrosis)

**Histology** — primary trabeculae replaced by **granulation tissue** · haemorrhage · ✓ **widened zone of provisional calcification**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — malaise · bone pain · myalgia · **bleeding gums, loose teeth** (bluish-purple gums) · haematuria · haematemesis · iron deficiency

**Signs** — petechiae, ecchymoses · **joint effusions** · **swelling over long bones (subperiosteal haemorrhage)** · massive thigh swelling in infant

**Scorbutic rosary** — costochondral separation with **angular step-off** · not rachitic rosary (**rounded, nodular**)

![Scorbutic rosary](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9034-scorbutic-rosary.jpg)

**Sites** — wrists · knees · sternal rib ends (rapid growth)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Sign | Description |
|---|---|
| ✓ **White line of Frankel** | Dense band = **widened zone of provisional calcification** between epiphysis + metaphysis |
| **Trümmerfeld zone (scurvy line)** | Transverse **lucent** metaphyseal band next to Frankel line |
| **Wimberger ring** | Dense ring around **epiphysis** |
| **Pelkin spur** | Metaphyseal spurs + fractures |
| **Corner sign of Park** | Metaphyseal clefts |
| Pencil-point cortex | Thin cortices |
| Ground-glass osteopenia | ↓ trabeculae |
| Others | Subperiosteal elevation · **epiphyseal separation** · fractures, dislocations |

![White line of Frankel and Wimberger ring](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9034-wimberger.jpg)

**Views** — wrists · knees · sternal rib ends

### Diagnostic criteria

**Diagnosis** — history + clinical + radiological picture + **response to vitamin C** · labs usually unhelpful · low fasting serum ascorbic acid

## 5 Management

**Treatment** — prompt **vitamin C** · adults **250 mg qid × 1 wk** · excellent prognosis if early

**Unanswered TO item (TO24050610)** — infant, swollen bleeding gums + massive thigh swelling, no trauma/fever · options Ewing / osteosarcoma / **scurvy** / haemophilia · **answer key not provided by source** · bleeding gums + subperiosteal long-bone swelling are scurvy features (Orthobullets)

## Scurvy vs Rickets

| | **Scurvy** | **Rickets** |
|---|---|---|
| Deficiency | Vitamin C | Vitamin D / Ca / PO₄ |
| Rosary | **Angular step-off** | **Rounded, nodular** |
| ZPC | **Widened, dense (Frankel line)** | Poorly calcified, wide physis, cupping |
| Bleeding | Gums, subperiosteal, joints | No |

## How it's asked

**"Somali 7-yr-old, purple bleeding gums, distal femur pain — true?"** → **widening of zone of provisional calcification**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ osteoclast · vit D · ↑ chondroitin/collagen · epiphysis primarily

**"Infant with bleeding gums + massive thigh swelling, no trauma/fever?"** → **answer key not provided by source** (TO) · options Ewing / osteosarcoma / scurvy / haemophilia

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Semimembranosus

*Basic Science · 98 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Semimembranosus |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | **Superolateral quadrant of ischial tuberosity** |
| **Insertion** | **Posterior medial tibial condyle** |
| **Action** | Extends hip · flexes knee · medially rotates tibia (esp. knee flexed) |
| **Innervation** | **Tibial nerve (L5, S1, S2)** |
| **Arterial supply** | Perforators of profunda femoris · inferior gluteal · superior muscular branches of popliteal |

![Semimembranosus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10070-semimembranosus.jpg)

### Cross-sectional anatomy — mid-thigh (medial → lateral, posterior)

| Structure | Position |
|---|---|
| **Gracilis** | **Most medial**, superficial to adductor magnus |
| Adductor magnus | Medial + **deep** to semimembranosus |
| ✓ **Semimembranosus** | **Medial** to semitendinosus |
| Semitendinosus | Lateral to semimembranosus |
| Biceps femoris | Most lateral |

![Mid-thigh cross-section](../../MCQ/attachments/q7467-a-79a.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"Mid-thigh cross-section — posteromedial muscle superficial to adductor magnus, medial to semitendinosus?"** → **semimembranosus**\
✗ Wrong options: semitendinosus · biceps · gracilis
