# Sickle Cell Anemia

*Basic Science · 99 / 108*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — ~**2 million** Americans carry the gene · **1 in 12 African Americans**

| | **Sickle cell anaemia (disease)** | **Sickle cell trait** |
|---|---|---|
| Genotype | **Two HbS alleles** | One HbS + one HbA |
| Severity | **Most severe** | Usually asymptomatic · **most common** |
| Key risk | Crises, infarcts, AVN, infection | **Exertional sickling collapse** → sudden death |

**Pathophysiology** — low O₂ → **sickle-shaped RBCs** → can't traverse vessels → vascular occlusion

![Sickle cells](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4114-sicklecells.jpg)

**Infection susceptibility** — **hyposplenism / functional asplenia** · sluggish circulation · ↓ opsonisation / impaired complement · GI micro-infarcts → Salmonella entry

### Orthopaedic manifestations

| Manifestation |
|---|
| **Sickle cell crisis** |
| **Osteomyelitis** · septic arthritis |
| **Osteonecrosis of femoral + humeral heads** |
| **Bone infarcts** |
| Growth retardation / skeletal immaturity |
| **Dactylitis** (acute hand/foot swelling) |

## 3 Presentation

**Sickle cell crisis** — severe bone pain · begins ~**age 2–3** · mediated by **substance P** · may cause infarcts · **hydroxyurea** → pain relief

**Sickle cell trait in athletes** — ✓ **oxygen + oral/IV hydration + rest** for exertional sickling collapse (leading cause of death in college football) · other SCT risks: compartment syndrome, lumbar myonecrosis, splenic infarct, haematuria, hyposthenuria, VTE\
✗ Wrong options: warm clothes · extra analgesia / rest days (disease, not trait) · NSAID avoidance

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **haemoglobin electrophoresis**

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **bone infarcts** · **biconcave "fish-tail" vertebrae**

![Fish vertebrae in sickle cell](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob4114-xr-fish-vertebra.jpg)

### Bone infarction vs osteomyelitis & Salmonella

| Test | **Infarct** | **Osteomyelitis** |
|---|---|---|
| **Bone scan** | Abnormal | Abnormal |
| **Bone marrow scan** | ✓ **↓ marrow uptake** | **Normal** marrow uptake |
| **MRI** | — | **Gadolinium T1** identifies infection |
| CBC, ESR, CRP | May be ↑ | May be ↑ |
| Definitive | — | **Aspirate + culture** |

**Infarct confirmed (↓ marrow uptake)** — ✓ **symptomatic care: NWB, IV hydration, haematology**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy · debridement · oral antibiotics · steroid injection

**Osteomyelitis organisms** — **S. aureus + Salmonella** (which is commoner is unclear; S. aureus still most likely per OB3500/OB4399) · ✓ **Salmonella osteomyelitis occurs most commonly / almost exclusively in sickle cell** · often **diaphyseal** · may spread from gallbladder / gut micro-infarcts

| Osteomyelitis population | Organism |
|---|---|
| **Sickle cell** | ✓ **Salmonella** (characteristic) + S. aureus |
| Neonates <4 mo | S. aureus, Enterobacter, group A/B Strep |
| **Sneaker puncture** | **Pseudomonas** |
| IVDU, dialysis | Staph, Enterobacter, Pseudomonas, Strep |

**Salmonella osteomyelitis → associated finding** — ✓ **sickled cells** on smear\
✗ Wrong options: OI, child abuse, renal failure, thalassaemia (one case)\
✗ Wrong options: myeloma plasma cells · synovial sarcoma · GCT giant cells · Ewing pseudo-rosettes

## 5 Management

**Septic arthritis** — fever, bone pain, ↑ CRP/ESR · aspirate + culture · **I&D** — consider **pre-op oxygenation + exchange transfusion**

### Osteonecrosis

**Femoral head AVN** — may be **bilateral** · mechanism ✓ **HbS → sickling → vascular occlusion**\
✗ Wrong options: SCFE slip · Salmonella · GAG accumulation · COL5A1

**Screening** — ✓ **haemoglobin : haematocrit ratio** (proxy for **MCHC**) = **strongest predictor** of femoral head ON · high-risk → hip MRI for early ON / hip preservation

| AVN stage | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Initial | **Partial weight-bearing + ROM** |
| Failed nonop / intractable pain | **THA** — **poor results** (ongoing bone remodelling) |

**Humeral head ON prognosis** — ✓ **sickle cell = most benign course / best prognosis**\
✗ Wrong options: steroids (commonest non-traumatic cause) · alcohol · radiation · malunion

## How it's asked

**"Sickle cell thigh pain, abnormal bone scan, ↓ marrow uptake?"** → **symptomatic care, NWB, IV fluids, haematology** (infarct)

**"Salmonella osteomyelitis — associated histology (figure)?"** → **sickled red cells**\
✗ Wrong options: plasma cells · pseudo-rosettes

**"Salmonella haematogenous osteomyelitis commonest in?"** → **sickle cell disease**\
✗ Wrong options: neonates · IVDU · dialysis · sneaker puncture

**"3-yr-old with Salmonella osteomyelitis also has?"** → **sickle cell anaemia**\
✗ Wrong options: thalassaemia · OI

**"Sickle cell, 2-yr progressive hip pain, AVN — mechanism?"** → **abnormal HbS alleles**\
✗ Wrong options: SCFE · Salmonella

**"Humeral head ON with best prognosis?"** → **sickle cell disease**\
✗ Wrong options: steroids, alcohol, radiation

**"Purpose of Hb : Hct ratio in sickle cell?"** → **screen for femoral head osteonecrosis risk**

**"Football players with sickle trait may need?"** → **oxygen + hydration**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Statistic Definitions

*Basic Science · 100 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Central tendency & spread

| Measure | Definition | Best for |
|---|---|---|
| **Mode** | Most frequent value | **Nominal** (categorical) data |
| **Median** | Middle value · unaffected by extremes | **Skewed** data · all levels except nominal |
| **Mean** | Arithmetic average · uses all values · most used | Sensitive to extremes |
| **Variance** | Variability of each data point from the mean · = SD² (average of squared deviations) | — |
| **Standard deviation** | Spread of the group | ±1 SD = **68%** · ±2 SD = **95%** · ±3 SD = **99.7%** |

### Diagnostic test properties (sensitivity, specificity, PPV, ROC)

| | **Disease +** | **Disease −** |
|---|---|---|
| **Test +** | **a** = true positive | **b** = false positive |
| **Test −** | **c** = false negative | **d** = true negative |

| Measure | Formula | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **Sensitivity** | **TP / (TP + FN)** = a/(a+c) | Positive in those **with** disease · **screening** (few false negatives) |
| **Specificity** | **TN / (TN + FP)** = d/(b+d) | Negative in those **without** disease · **confirmation** (avoid treating the unaffected) |
| False-positive rate | b/(b+d) = 1 − specificity | — |
| False-negative rate | c/(a+c) | — |
| **PPV** | **TP / (TP + FP)** = a/(a+b) | Positive test truly diseased · **depends on prevalence** |
| **NPV** | **TN / (TN + FN)** = d/(c+d) | Negative test truly disease-free · depends on prevalence |
| **LR+** | **Sensitivity / (1 − specificity)** | How a positive result changes likelihood |
| **LR−** | (1 − sensitivity) / specificity | How a negative result changes likelihood |

![2 × 2 table](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9073-2x2-table-jpg.jpg)

**Worked examples** — 6 of 10 diseased test + → sensitivity **60%** · 5 false + of 90 healthy → specificity 85/90 = **94.4%** · sens 90%, spec 95%, prevalence 10% → PPV 9/(9 + 4.5) = **67%** · 80/100 flu +, 5/100 healthy + → NPV 95/(20 + 95) = **83%** · 86/(86 + 4) = 95.5% → **specificity**

**Trade-off** — sensitivity and specificity usually traded off · false negative worse than false positive → choose **more sensitive** test · drug with nasty side effects, must not treat non-diseased → ✓ **highest specificity**

**Precision** — closeness of repeated measurements (random error) · precise ≠ accurate

**Post-test** — pre-test odds = p/(1 − p) · post-test odds = pre-test odds × LR · post-test probability = odds/(odds + 1)

**ROC curve** — x-axis **1 − specificity (FPR)** · y-axis **sensitivity (TPR)** · ✓ measures **responsiveness** · area under curve (**C-statistic**): **0.5 = useless**, **>0.75 adequate**, higher = better

![ROC curve](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9073-roc-curve.jpg)

| Tool | Purpose |
|---|---|
| **ROC curve** | **Responsiveness** / diagnostic ability |
| **Funnel plot** | **Publication bias** in meta-analysis |
| **Forest plot** | Relative treatment effects across studies (meta-analysis) |
| **Kaplan–Meier** | **Survivorship** |
| **Cronbach α** | **Internal consistency** of questionnaire items |
| Log rank | Compare survival curves |
| Multiple regression | Predict a variable from ≥2 others · controls confounders |
| **Cohen's kappa** | **Inter-observer** reliability |
| **ICC** | **Intra-observer** reliability |

### Incidence, prevalence & risk measures

**Incidence** — ✓ **new cases** in a period per population at risk · long-term population/cohort studies · e.g. 100 new cases among 400 at-risk workers (100 already affected excluded) = **25%**

**Prevalence** — ✓ **total existing cases** at a time point · **cross-sectional** survey (census) · e.g. 200/500 = 40% · MRSA cases on 1 day = prevalence

| Measure | Formula / source |
|---|---|
| **Relative risk** | [a/(a+b)] / [c/(c+d)] = incidence exposed / incidence unexposed · **cohort** studies · RR >1 → more outcome in exposed |
| **Odds ratio** | **(a × d)/(b × c)** · **case-control** (retrospective) + logistic regression · ≈ RR when outcome rare (<10%) · exaggerates risk when common |
| Absolute risk reduction | Risk difference between groups |
| Relative risk reduction | 1 − RR |
| Attributable risk | Risk difference attributable to exposure |
| **NNT** | **1 / ARR** (NNS 1000 → ARR 0.1%) |
| Hazard ratio | Comparison of rates over time (survival curves) |

**Hep C example** — 75/595 transfused vs 16/712 not → RR = **incidence (75/595) ÷ incidence (16/712)**

**Odds ratio in forest plot** — OR <1 = ↓ odds in treatment · **CI includes 1 → not significant** · **narrower CI = more precise, less uncertainty** · OR size ≠ effect size/clinical importance · adjusted vs crude OR differ (confounding)

**Study reporting** — cohort (e.g. BMI vs THA outcome in 500) → ✓ **relative risk** · case-control → odds ratio

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Study design & meta-analysis

| Design | Description |
|---|---|
| **Case-control** | Start with **outcome** (e.g. tibial nonunion) vs controls without → look back for risk factors → **OR** |
| **Cohort** | Start with **exposure** (e.g. daily vit D) → follow for outcome (hip fracture) → **RR** · prospective or retrospective |
| Cross-sectional | Single time point → **prevalence** |
| RCT | Random allocation, prospective |
| Case series | No comparison group |
| Meta-analysis | Combines studies · funnel plot for publication bias |

**Funnel plot** — effect size vs study size/precision · large studies less variable · ✓ **symmetric inverted funnel = no publication bias** · skewed = bias (negative studies unpublished)

![Funnel plot](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9073-funplot.jpg)

### Statistical test selection (t-test, chi-square, ANOVA, non-parametric) (TO)

**Parametric** — continuous, **normally distributed** · **non-parametric** — skewed continuous, **ordinal** or **categorical**

| Comparison | **Parametric** | **Non-parametric** |
|---|---|---|
| 2 groups, **paired** | **Paired t-test** (means in matched groups) | **Wilcoxon signed-rank** (Orthobullets) · OB1230/OB213156 call it Wilcoxon rank-sum — read the options |
| 2 groups, **unpaired** | **Student / independent t-test** | **Mann-Whitney U** (= Wilcoxon rank sum) |
| **≥3 groups** | **ANOVA** | **Kruskal-Wallis** (Friedman for repeated) |
| **Categorical, 2+ groups** | **Chi-square** | Chi-square |
| Categorical, **small sample** (<5 per cell or total <~50) | **Fisher exact** | Fisher exact |

**ANOVA** — one-way = **one independent variable** · two-way = **two independent variables** · ≥3 groups, normal data (e.g. BMI across pinning/hemi/THA; fastball velocity of 5 pitchers)

```
Choose a test
├─ Categorical / proportions → CHI-SQUARE
│      └─ small cells (<5) → FISHER EXACT
├─ Continuous, normal → 2 groups T-TEST (paired if matched) · ≥3 groups → ANOVA
└─ Continuous skewed or ordinal → 2 groups MANN-WHITNEY U · ≥3 groups → KRUSKAL-WALLIS
```

**Ordinal grade (Herring) in two unpaired populations** — ✓ **Mann-Whitney U** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: paired t · Wilcoxon signed-rank · Kruskal-Wallis

**Examples** — mean age 2 normal groups → **t-test** · CRPS rate 1% vs 9% → **chi-square** · single non-normal continuous variable → **Mann-Whitney U** · OR time robotic vs manual (normal) → **t-test** · ACL graft return-to-play scores (finite range) → chi-square per OB217862

## 5 Management

### Type I & II errors & power

| | **H₀ true** | **H₀ false** |
|---|---|---|
| **Reject H₀** | **Type I (α) error** — false positive | Correct — **power (1 − β)** |
| **Fail to reject H₀** | Correct (1 − α) | **Type II (β) error** — false negative |

![Type I and II errors](../../MCQ/attachments/q211200-illa-h0-table.jpg)

**Type I (α)** — ✓ **rejecting a true null** · α set at **0.05** (1/20) · ↓ by lowering α (0.01) or **Bonferroni** (but ↑ type II)

**Type II (β)** — ✓ **accepting a false null** · detecting no difference when one exists · commonest in **underpowered** studies · e.g. small trial negative, later 2000-patient trial positive · crossover with intent-to-treat (40% "no tourniquet" got tourniquet) · 2 groups of 5 with p = 0.079 → **may be type II**

**Power** — ✓ **1 − β** · probability of detecting a true effect · usually **80%** (β 0.2) · depends on effect size, α, β, **n** · ✓ **↑ sample size → ↑ power, ↓ type II** · 84 needed per arm, β 0.2 → chance of false-negative = **0.2**\
✗ Wrong options: prospective vs retrospective · case-control

**Power analysis** — ✓ **determines minimum sample size** to show a clinically significant difference · ✓ **before the study** · elements: α, β, effect size (± variance), n · post-hoc power discouraged

| Reduces type II | Doesn't |
|---|---|
| ✓ **↑ sample size** | ↓ α to 0.01 (↑ type II) · blinding (detection bias) · goniometer (measurement error) · randomisation (selection bias) · Bonferroni (↑ type II) · ↑ β |

**Effect size** — magnitude of difference in means between groups ÷ **pooled SD** · continuous variables (RRR for dichotomous) · not shown by p-value

**Other terms** — **Hawthorne effect** = behaviour change when observed · **confounder** = associated with both exposure + outcome · selection bias · measurement error · sampling error · **fragile p-values** with small samples\
✗ Wrong options: "error"

### P-values, confidence intervals, correlation & effect size

**P-value** — probability, **if H₀ true**, of a result **as or more extreme** · p = 0.09 → difference would arise by chance **9 in 100** times · ✓ p-value = probability of type I error (OB217456)\
✗ Wrong options: "groups statistically equal" if p > 0.05 · gives effect size · clinical significance

**Bonferroni correction** — ✓ **post-hoc correction of p-values for multiple simultaneous tests** on one data set · **α ÷ number of comparisons** (10 tests → 0.005)

| Confidence level | Mean ± SD |
|---|---|
| 85% | 1.44 |
| 90% | 1.65 |
| **95%** | **1.96** (OB218892 uses ±2 SD) |
| 98% | 2.32 |
| **99%** | **2.58** |

**Confidence interval** — range likely to contain the true parameter · shows **precision** + magnitude · ✓ **larger sample → narrower CI** (smaller margin of error) · 99% CI wider than 95% · overlapping CIs do not prove no significance — need formal test · e.g. FNA 0.5 ± 0.2 → 95% range **0.1–0.9 cm³**

**Correlation** — R² = 0.97, p = 0.03 → strongly correlated, unlikely chance, does not **validate accuracy** (both methods could be wrong) · Pearson = 2 normal continuous variables · **Bayesian** = prior → posterior probability

### Reliability, MCID, fragility index & survival

**Psychometrics** — **reliability** = ✓ **reproducibility** between repeated tests / observers · **validity** = measures what it intends · **responsiveness** = detects change (ROC)

**Inter-observer** — **Cohen's kappa** · **intra-observer** — **ICC**

**MCID** — difference in outcome that is clinically relevant · few tools have accepted MCID · statistically significant 5-point ASES difference with MCID 10–15 → ✓ **may not be clinically meaningful** (both groups' 22/27-point gains exceed MCID)

**Fragility index** — ✓ **number of outcome changes (event ↔ non-event) needed to flip significance** · dichotomous outcomes · larger = more robust

**Survivorship** — joint replacement success · all assumed operated simultaneously · **life table** (annual, changes once/yr) vs **product limit** (recalculated at each failure) · **Kaplan–Meier**: x = time, y = survival probability · higher curve at a time point = better survival

## How it's asked

**"Equation for specificity?"** → **TN / (TN + FP)**\
✗ Wrong options: TP/(TP + FN) (sensitivity)

**"Positive predictive value?"** → **TP / (TP + FP)**

**"86/(86 + 4) = 95.5% (US for meniscus tears)?"** → **specificity**

**"Sensitivity of an assay?"** → **TP / (TP + FN)**

**"Tool to determine responsiveness?"** → **ROC curve**\
✗ Wrong options: funnel plot, Kaplan–Meier, Cronbach α, forest plot

**"Test value to avoid treating people without RA?"** → **specificity**\
✗ Wrong options: sensitivity · NPV

**"Probability of finding a true association?"** → **power**

**"Accepting the null when false?"** → **type II error**

**"Statistical power defined by?"** → **1 − probability of type II error**\
✗ Wrong options: 1 − α

**"Ability to detect a true difference between interventions?"** → **power**\
✗ Wrong options: effect size · Hawthorne

**"Capsule repair study negative, later large study positive?"** → **type II error**

**"Rejecting a true null hypothesis?"** → **type I error**

**"Increase study power by?"** → **increasing sample size**

**"No difference when one truly exists?"** → **type II error**

**"Power analysis determines what, when?"** → **sample size, before the study**

**"84 per arm, β 0.2, α 0.05, p = 0.28 — chance null accepted when false?"** → **0.2**\
✗ Wrong options: 0.8 = power · 0.05

**"Tourniquet RCT, 40% crossover, ITT no difference?"** → **type 2 (β) error**

**"Purpose and timing of power analysis?"** → **minimum patients for clinically significant difference, before study**

**"Lateralised RSA study negative, later RCTs positive?"** → **type II error**

**"Reduce type II error?"** → **increase sample size**\
✗ Wrong options: α 0.01 · blinding · β 0.9

**"6300-patient trial positive, 150-patient follow-up negative?"** → **beta error**

**"Calculate power?"** → **1 − type II error**

**"Two groups of 5, p = 0.079 — statement?"** → **may have made a type II error**\
✗ Wrong options: Bonferroni · underpowered ↑ type I

**"Reduce type 2 error in TKA rehab RCT?"** → **increase sample size**\
✗ Wrong options: goniometer · blinding · Bonferroni

**"Herring grade in rural vs urban groups?"** → **Mann-Whitney U** **(TO)**

**"Paired t-test used for?"** → **difference between two means in matched groups**

**"Chi-square unreliable with small numbers — use?"** → **Fisher exact test**

**"Mean age, two normal groups?"** → **Student t-test**

**"CRPS rates 1% vs 9%?"** → **chi-square**

**"Chi-square most appropriate for?"** → **categorical data between groups**\
✗ Wrong options: <5 per group (Fisher)

**"Normal data, ≥3 independent groups?"** → **ANOVA**\
✗ Wrong options: Kaplan–Meier · log rank

**"Single continuous non-normal variable?"** → **Mann-Whitney U**

**"Ideal ANOVA scenario?"** → **mean BMI across pinning, hemi, THA (parametric)**

**"Data for chi-squared test?"** → **categorical data of two large groups**\
✗ Wrong options: two small (Fisher)

**"Mean OR time robotic vs manual TKA, normal?"** → **Student t-test**

**"Best ANOVA application?"** → **fastball velocity of top five pitchers**

**"One-way vs two-way ANOVA?"** → **one vs two independent variables, ≥3 groups**

**"Definition of incidence?"** → **new cases in a specific time period**

**"Relative risk of hep C with transfusion?"** → **(75/595) ÷ (16/712)**

**"500 workers, 100 symptomatic at start, 100 new in 5 yr — incidence?"** → **25%**\
✗ Wrong options: 20% · 40% (prevalence)

**"500 THAs followed by BMI — report effect as?"** → **relative risk**\
✗ Wrong options: odds ratio

**"Total MRSA cases on one day?"** → **prevalence**

**"Cross-sectional survey establishes?"** → **prevalence**

**"RR of dehiscence anterior vs posterior (W, X, Y, Z table)?"** → **W/(W + X) ÷ Y/(Y + Z)**\
✗ Wrong options: (W/Y)/(X/Z) (OR)

**"Effect size definition?"** → **estimated magnitude of difference in means**

**"Range likely to include the unknown parameter?"** → **confidence interval**

**"Bonferroni correction?"** → **post-hoc p-value correction for multiple tests**

**"Women vs men steps, p = 0.09 — interpretation?"** → **difference would occur by chance 9 in 100 times if no true difference**\
✗ Wrong options: statistically equal

**"R² 0.97, p 0.03 between FE and bench model?"** → **strongly correlated, not chance, but doesn't validate the model**

**"Mean ± 2.58 SD?"** → **99% CI**

**"Meaning of p = 0.03?"** → **probability of type I error**

**"1.2 ± 0.3 vs 0.5 ± 0.2 cm³ — conclusion?"** → **FNA yields 0.1–0.9 cm³ 95% of the time**\
✗ Wrong options: significance from CIs alone

**"Narrower CI in final study — cause?"** → **larger sample size**\
✗ Wrong options: 99% CI · more variability

**"Forest plot of aspirin ORs — accurate?"** → **DVT estimate less uncertain than hematoma (narrower CI)**

**"Funnel plot purpose?"** → **detect publication bias**\
✗ Wrong options: forest plot · ROC

**"Nonunion patients vs non-nonunion patients questionnaire?"** → **case-control study**

**"Daily vit D group vs none followed for hip fracture?"** → **cohort study**

**"Reliability refers to?"** → **reproducibility between repeated tests or observers**

**"Kaplan–Meier — accurate statement?"** → **group with highest curve at 1 month more likely alive**

**"Fragility index?"** → **number of outcome changes needed to change trial significance**

**"ASES +22 vs +27, p = 0.03, MCID 10–15?"** → **5-point difference may not be clinically meaningful**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Structural Properties

*Basic Science · 101 / 108*

## 1 Basics

**Implant behaviour** — **structural properties** (shape, configuration — cylinder, rectangle) + **material properties** · structural ≠ strength characteristic

### Bending rigidity (stiffness)

**Definition** — slope of **elastic range** on structural stress–strain curve · **THA stem stiffness** → proximal **stress shielding**

| Shape | Bending rigidity ∝ |
|---|---|
| **Solid cylinder** | **Radius⁴** |
| **Hollow cylinder** | **Radius³** |
| **Rectangle (plate)** | **Thickness³** |

![Bending rigidity ∝ radius⁴](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9061-rigidity-moved.jpg)

### Moments of inertia

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Area moment of inertia (I)** | Shape property predicting deflection, bending, stress · **∝ length⁴** · function of width, thickness, polar moment · = flexural stiffness |
| **Polar moment of inertia (J)** | Resistance to **torsion** |
| Moment of inertia | Resistance to change in rotation |

**Deflection** — displacement under load · ∝ (force / elastic modulus) × area moment of inertia (as printed)

### Bone anisotropy

**Cortical bone** — ✓ **anisotropic** (collagen fibre orientation) → mechanical properties **vary with orientation** · loaded along **long axis → highest stiffness + tensile strength** vs oblique/transverse\
✗ Wrong options: hydroxyapatite/water content differences

## How it's asked

**"Why orient cortical bone tensile specimens relative to femoral shaft?"** → **mechanical properties vary with orientation (anisotropy)**\
✗ Wrong options: hydroxyapatite structure · water content · easier cutting

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Subscapularis

*Basic Science · 102 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Subscapularis |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | **Subscapular fossa** |
| **Insertion** | **Lesser tuberosity** |
| **Action** | **Internal (medial) rotation** + adduction · holds humeral head in glenoid |
| **Innervation** | **Upper + lower subscapular nerves (C5–C7)** — from ✓ **posterior cord** |
| **Arterial supply** | Subscapular artery |

![Subscapularis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10016-subscapularis.jpg)

| Brachial plexus level | Branches |
|---|---|
| **Posterior cord** | ✓ **Upper + lower subscapular** · thoracodorsal |
| Lateral cord | Lateral pectoral |
| **Superior (upper) trunk** | **Suprascapular** · nerve to subclavius |
| Middle / inferior trunks | No branches |

## 3 Presentation

**Mechanism** — **forced external rotation** injury → subscapularis tear

**Tests** — **lift-off** (hand behind back, IR) → ✓ **subscapularis highest % maximal contraction** · **belly-press**\
✗ Wrong options: pec major, lat dorsi, supraspinatus

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Sagittal oblique MRI (coracoid level)** — ✓ **subscapularis = anterior** · supraspinatus superior · infraspinatus, teres minor, teres major posterior · assess **atrophy + fatty infiltration** (repair prognosis)

![Sagittal oblique MRI — subscapularis anterior](../../MCQ/attachments/q7394-a-6.jpg)

**Arthroscopy (posterosuperior portal)** — subscapularis tendon anteriorly · axial MRI fatty atrophy = chronic tear

## How it's asked

**"Muscle with highest activity in lift-off test?"** → **subscapularis**

**"Sagittal oblique MRI — anterior muscle (asterisk)?"** → **subscapularis**\
✗ Wrong options: supraspinatus · teres minor

**"Forced ER injury — tendon's nerve branches from?"** → **posterior cord**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral cord · superior trunk

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Superficial Peroneal Nerve

*Basic Science · 103 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy & entrapment

**Course** — branch of **common peroneal** after crossing fibular head → **lateral compartment** → ✓ **motor to peroneus longus + brevis** → pierces **crural fascia ~10–12 cm proximal to fibular tip** → **intermediate + medial dorsal cutaneous nerves** (dorsum of foot + toes)

**At risk** — **lateral approach to ankle** (e.g. bimalleolar ORIF)

| Nerve | Supplies |
|---|---|
| **Superficial peroneal** | **Lateral compartment** (PL, PB) · dorsum of foot except 1st web |
| **Deep peroneal** | **Anterior compartment** · **EDB, EHB** · **1st dorsal web space** |
| **Sural** | **Lateral foot** sensation |

![Superficial peroneal nerve at lateral ankle approach](../../MCQ/attachments/q7419-a-31.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Entrapment** — runner · lateral distal leg pain + **dorsal foot dysaesthesia** · worse with **active/passive plantar flexion + inversion** (traction at fascial exit) · tender fullness (fascial hernia) **~10 cm above lateral malleolus** · **Tinel +** · no weakness · normal X-ray/MRI

## 5 Management

**Entrapment** — ✓ **fascial release + neurolysis**\
✗ Wrong options: **closure of fascial defect** (worsens, risk exertional compartment syndrome) · four-compartment fasciotomy (only for established compartment syndrome) · biopsy · epidural steroid

## How it's asked

**"Nerve at lateral ankle approach — true?"** → **proximally innervates lateral compartment**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior compartment · EDB · 1st web · purely sensory lateral foot

**"Runner, dysaesthesia dorsum foot with PF + inversion, Tinel 10 cm above malleolus?"** → **fascial release + neurolysis of SPN**\
✗ Wrong options: fascial closure · fasciotomy

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Synovium & Synovial Fluid

*Basic Science · 104 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Synovium

**Function** — nutrient exchange between blood and joint fluid

**Structure** — vascularised, porous connective tissue · ✓ **no basement membrane**

| Synovial cell | Origin / features | Function |
|---|---|---|
| **Type A** | **Macrophage-derived** · non-fixed · antigen-presenting · superficial | ✓ **Phagocytosis** |
| **Type B** | **Fibroblast-like** · rich RER, dendritic processes · deeper | ✓ **Produce synovial fluid** — hyaluronic acid, fibronectin, collagen |
| Type C | Intermediate · unknown origin/function | ? multipotent precursor of A or B |

![Synovial cell types](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9018-screen-shot-2017-04-11-at-9-56-16-am.jpg)

### Synovial cells & lubrication

**Synovial fluid** — **ultrafiltrate of plasma** regulated by synovium · **~2 mL** in healthy knee · lubricates + **nourishes cartilage by diffusion**

| Component | Role |
|---|---|
| **Hyaluronan** | Synthesis needs **uridine diphosphoglucose dehydrogenase** |
| ✓ **Lubricin** | Glycoprotein (HA-binding) · **↓ coefficient of friction** · made by **superficial-zone chondrocytes** · deficiency → early arthritis |
| **Urokinase-type plasminogen activator** | Activates plasmin → **prevents intra-articular clot** (fibrinolysis) |
| Collagenases · prostaglandins | — |
| Proteolipids | Boundary lubrication |

| ✗ Wrong option | Actual role |
|---|---|
| Decorin | Commonest tendon proteoglycan · regulates collagen fibril diameter |
| Scleraxis | Tendon/ligament transcription factor · inhibits osteoblastogenesis |
| Aggrecan | Cartilage swelling pressure |
| Cathepsin | Osteoclast protease at ruffled border |

**Biomechanics** — ✓ **non-Newtonian** · viscosity not constant, not linear · **viscosity ↑ as shear rate ↓**

**Lipohaemarthrosis (supine)** — layer 1 **fat** · layer 2 **serum/synovial fluid** (✓ **uPA → inhibits clot**) · layer 3 **RBCs** (don't form fibrin clot)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Synovial fluid analysis in OA (TO)

| Parameter (TO) | Normal | **OA** | Gout | RA | Pyogenic | TB |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Appearance | Straw/clear yellow | **Clear yellow** | Yellow–turbid milky | Yellow, cloudy | Purulent | Yellow, turbid |
| Viscosity | Normal | ✓ **Normal** | ↓ | ↓ | ↓ | ↓ |
| WBC/mm³ | ≤200 | ✓ **≤2000** | 2000–50,000 | 2000–50,000 | **>50,000** | 10,000–20,000 |
| PMN | <20% | ✓ **<20%** | 60–70% | 50–60% | **90%** | 60% |
| Crystals | — | — | **Urate** | — | — | — |
| Glucose | Slightly < plasma | — | ↓ | ↓ | ↓↓ | ↓↓ |

**OA fluid** — ✓ **normal viscosity, <20% neutrophils**\
✗ Wrong options: increased viscosity · WBC >2000 · decreased viscosity **(TO)**

## How it's asked

**"Cell producing synovial fluid?"** → **type B synovial cell**\
✗ Wrong options: type A (phagocytic) · fibrochondrocyte

**"Synovial component most reducing friction?"** → **lubricin**\
✗ Wrong options: decorin, scleraxis, aggrecan, cathepsin

**"Lipohaemarthrosis layers — true?"** → **layer 2 contains uPA, inhibits intra-articular clot**

**"OA synovial fluid — true statement?"** → **normal viscosity with <20% neutrophils** **(TO)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Tendons

*Basic Science · 105 / 108*

## 1 Basics

**Function** — transfer muscle force to bone · orient along stress

| | **Paratenon-covered** | **Sheathed** |
|---|---|---|
| Examples | **Patellar, Achilles** | **Hand flexors** |
| Blood supply | **Rich** → heal better | Avascular areas · **nutrition by diffusion** |
| Injury | Trauma · fail at **musculotendinous** or **tendon–bone junction** | **Laceration** · risk of **adhesions** |

### Composition (collagen, decorin, aggrecan, enthesis)

**Structure** — fascicles separated by **endotenon**, surrounded by **epitenon** · hierarchical (microfibrils → fibrils → fascicles) · mostly **water**

| Component | % dry weight / role |
|---|---|
| **Type I collagen** | **85%** (Orthobullets, OB213905) · **95%** (OB8280) — read the options |
| Type III collagen | **0–5%** (<5%) · also V, VI small |
| Proteoglycans | 0–5% · attract water |
| ✓ **Decorin** | **Most abundant tendon proteoglycan** (small leucine-rich PG) · ✓ **regulates collagen fibril diameter / size** · cross-links fibres, transfers load |
| ✓ **Aggrecan** | Proteoglycan in **areas of compression** |
| Tenascin-C | Glycoprotein ↑ in tendinopathy |
| Elastin | Tissue recoil after stretch |
| Fibronectin | Cell adhesion, migration, differentiation |
| Fibrillin | Elastic fibre formation |

![Hierarchical tendon structure](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9015-hierarchical-structure.jpg)

**Cells** — **fibroblasts (tenocytes)**: spindle-shaped, parallel rows along load · make type I (85%) + type III (5%) collagen · drive healing

**Blood supply** — musculotendinous junction · osseotendinous junction · surrounding tissue (paratenon, mesotenon, **vincula**)

### Enthesis

| Insertion zones (↑ modulus) | Contents |
|---|---|
| **Tendon** | Type I + III collagen, elastin, PGs, fibroblasts |
| **Uncalcified fibrocartilage** | Aggrecan, types I, II, III, fibrochondrocytes |
| — **Tidemark** — | Straight basophilic line = soft–hard mechanical boundary |
| **Calcified fibrocartilage** | **Type II**, aggrecan, types I + **X**, fibrochondrocytes · interlocks with bone |
| **Bone** | Osteocytes, osteoblasts, osteoclasts, type I, apatite |

![Four zones of the direct insertion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9015-4-layers-at-insertion-site.jpg)

| | **Fibrous (indirect)** | **Fibrocartilaginous (direct)** |
|---|---|---|
| Site | Metaphysis / diaphysis | Epiphysis / apophysis |
| Structure | Perforating mineralised (Sharpey) fibres | **4 zones** |
| Insertion angle | Changes little | **Changes greatly → overuse injury** |
| Examples | **Deltoid**, adductor magnus–linea aspera, pronator teres | **Rotator cuff, Achilles** |

**Nerve endings** — ✓ **enthesis = free nerve endings (nociception)** · myotendinous junction = **Golgi organs** (steady state), **Ruffini** (prolonged, sensitive), **Pacini** (fast) · **Meissner** = skin light touch

### Vascular supply — vincula

**Sheathed tendons** — vincula = **main blood supply** to bulk of FDS/FDP · FDS + FDP each have **vincula brevia + longa** (2 each, Orthobullets) · "5 vincula: 2 short, 3 long" (OB5552)

| Vinculum | Origin → attachment |
|---|---|
| **VBS** (brevia superficialis) | PIPJ / adjacent proximal phalanx |
| **VLS** (longa superficialis) | Proximal P1 / MCPJ → 2 slips |
| ✓ **VBP** (brevia profundus) | **DIPJ / distal middle phalanx → FDP at A5** |
| **VLP** (longa profundus) | Distal P1 / PIP volar plate → through VBS → FDP |

**Jersey finger (Leddy–Packer II, fragment at A2)** — ✓ **VBP disrupted** · VLP intact · FDS vincula unaffected\
✗ Wrong options: volar plate

![Vincula of FDS and FDP](../../MCQ/attachments/q5552-illa-illus-a.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Mechanical behaviour (toe region, elastin)

**Properties** — **more collagen, less viscoelastic than ligament** · viscoelastic, non-linear · **strain-rate dependent** · ✓ **strong in tension (5–10% strain vs bone 1–4%)** · buckle in compression · **creep + stress relaxation**

**Modifiers** — exercise ↑ · **immobilisation ↓** · ↑ to maturity then ↓ · laser/heat → shrinkage · hydration, temperature, pH → **test under physiological conditions**

| Load–elongation region | Explanation |
|---|---|
| ✓ **Toe** (low load, non-linear) | **Crimped fibres straighten** · crimped elastin fibrils (OB5510) · tendon toe smaller than ligament (more parallel) |
| **Linear (elastic)** | Intermediate load · slope = **Young's modulus** |
| Yield point | Elastic → plastic |
| **Failure** | High load |

![Load–elongation curve](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9015-loadelongationcurve2.jpg)

**Soft tissue vs metal** — ✓ **toe region unique to soft tissue**\
✗ Wrong options: modulus, plastic region, strain energy density, yield point (shared)

**Mechanotransduction** — ✓ **deflection of primary cilium**\
✗ Wrong options: apoptosis · cell elongation · pressurisation (older theories)

**Training (8 wk half-marathon)** — ✓ **net ↑ type I collagen** (↑ turnover; ↓ cross-links with endurance)\
✗ Wrong options: known effect on fibril diameter / CSA

## 5 Management

### Healing & adaptation

| Stage | Events | Time |
|---|---|---|
| **Haemostasis** | **Platelets** → coagulation · fibrin clot + fibronectin → chemotaxis | 5–15 min |
| **Inflammation** | ✓ **Neutrophils + M1 macrophages** · chemotaxis of fibroblasts · fibroblasts make **type III collagen** | **1–7 days** |
| **Proliferation** | Disorganised collagen + angiogenesis · GFs: PDGF, IGF, **BMP-12/13**, TGF-β · M2 shift · ↑ type I + neovascularisation | 7–21 days |
| **Remodelling** | **Type III → type I** · fibrosis, ↓ cellularity | Up to **18 months** |

**Repair strength** — ✓ **weakest in inflammatory phase** · **7–10 days** (Orthobullets) · **5–21 days** (OB8271) · most strength by **21–28 days** · max at **6 months** · ✓ **final only ~2/3 normal**

**Early mobilisation** — earlier ROM but ↓ repair strength · ✓ **flexor tendons: prevents adhesions**

## Tendon vs Ligament

| | **Tendon** | **Ligament** |
|---|---|---|
| Collagen | More, more parallel | Less, less organised |
| Viscoelasticity | Less | More |
| Toe region | Smaller | Larger |

## How it's asked

**"Decorin controls?"** → **tendon collagen fibre size**\
✗ Wrong options: myelin · axon branching

**"Tendon matrix ratio?"** → **95% type I, <5% type III**

**"Found in areas of tendon compression?"** → **aggrecan**\
✗ Wrong options: decorin

**"Enthesis contains?"** → **free nerve endings**\
✗ Wrong options: Golgi, Pacini, Ruffini (myotendinous) · Meissner (skin)

**"Function of most abundant tendon proteoglycan?"** → **regulates collagen fibril diameter**\
✗ Wrong options: compression (aggrecan)

**"Region X on load-elongation curve?"** → **toe region with crimped fibres**

**"Stress–strain portion due to elastin structure?"** → **A — toe region**\
✗ Wrong options: B elastic · E modulus

**"Unique to ligament vs titanium curve?"** → **toe region**

**"Tendon repair weakest in which phase?"** → **inflammatory**

**"8 wk marathon training — Achilles change?"** → **net increase type I collagen**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ fibril diameter · ↑ CSA

**"How fibroblasts sense strain?"** → **primary cilium deflection**

**"Inflammatory phase hallmark (week 1)?"** → **neutrophils + M1 macrophages**\
✗ Wrong options: M2 (remodelling) · type I deposition

**"Jersey finger with fragment at A2 — vascular structure injured?"** → **vincula brevia to FDP**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Teres Major

*Basic Science · 106 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

| | Teres major |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | Dorsal **inferior angle of scapula** |
| **Insertion** | ✓ **Medial lip of intertubercular groove** |
| **Action** | **Adducts + internally rotates** arm |
| **Innervation** | ✓ **Lower subscapular nerve** — **C6, C7** (Orthobullets) · **C5, C6** (OB) · **thoracodorsal in 20%** |
| **Arterial supply** | Subscapular + circumflex scapular arteries |

![Teres major](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10009-teres-major.jpg)

### Innervation

**Lower subscapular nerve** — **posterior cord** (with upper subscapular + thoracodorsal) · also supplies **lower subscapularis** · relevant to tendon transfers for massive cuff tears

| Brachial plexus label (OB210271) | Nerve |
|---|---|
| A | Nerve to subclavius |
| B | Long thoracic |
| C | Upper subscapular |
| ✓ **D** | **Lower subscapular** |
| E | Medial cutaneous nerve of arm |

![Brachial plexus — lower subscapular nerve](../../MCQ/attachments/q210271-a-bp.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"IR muscle inserting on medial lip of bicipital groove — nerve?"** → **lower subscapular nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: upper subscapular · long thoracic

# Thromboembolism (PE & DVT) Prophylaxis

*Basic Science · 107 / 108*

## 1 Basics

**VTE** — DVT + PE · US incidence: DVT **200,000/yr**, PE **50,000/yr**

### Pathophysiology (Virchow triad, thromboplastin, fibrin, embolisation)

**Virchow's triad** — **venous stasis** · **hypercoagulability** · **endothelial (intimal) injury** \
✗ Wrong options: thrombocytopenia · platelet dysfunction

**Clot formation** — stasis → fibrin formation → clot retraction → propagation · DVT starts in **venous valve cusps**

**Surgical trigger** — ✓ **thromboplastin (tissue factor, factor III)** released during dissection → **extrinsic pathway** (VII → X → prothrombin → thrombin → fibrinogen → fibrin)

**Thrombus** — **fibrin + RBCs**, relatively **few platelets** (platelets key in **arterial** thrombi) · **fibrin = end product of coagulation cascade** (thrombin cleaves fibrinogen) · collagen starts intrinsic arm · fibrinolytic system breaks down fibrin

![Coagulation cascade](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9056-coagulation-cascade-aaos-moved.jpg)

**Cement pressurisation of femoral canal** — ✓ **greatest V/Q mismatch risk in THA** (MMA + fat emboli)\
✗ Wrong options: reaming, modular stem, SCD, posterior approach

### Thrombophilias (factor V Leiden, protein C/S)

| Inherited | Note |
|---|---|
| **MTHFR C677T/TT** | ✓ **Highest risk** (✗ Wrong options: "no impact") |
| **Factor V Leiden** | Gln-for-Arg substitution at protein C cleavage site → **factor Va resists inactivation by activated protein C** → ↑ thrombin · >50% get DVT with one extra risk (fracture, TJA) · (✗ Wrong options: platelet / endothelial / hepatic disorder · mandatory Greenfield filter) |
| **Antithrombin III deficiency** | ATIII normally inhibits thrombin + Xa |
| **Protein C / S deficiency** | Protein S = cofactor to activated protein C; inactivates Va + VIIIa · ✓ **deficiency → VTE** (✗ Wrong options: fibrinogen, VII, VIII, prothrombin deficiency) |
| Activated protein C resistance · ↑ factor VIII · hyperhomocysteinaemia · **prothrombin G20210A** | — |

| ✗ Wrong option | Actually |
|---|---|
| Familial dysfibrinogenaemia | Defective fibrin clot |
| Plasminogen deficiency | Can't lyse clots |
| Glanzmann thrombasthenia | Platelet defect → bleeding |
| von Willebrand disease | Bleeding disorder |
| Antiphospholipid syndrome | Autoimmune, antibodies to protein C/S → clotting |

### Risk factors (TO)

**Acquired** — **malignancy** (up to 20% of new VTE) · **hormones**: recombinant **EPO** (✓ thrombotic event before spinal fusion), **HRT**, OCP, late pregnancy · antiphospholipid (lupus anticoagulant, anticardiolipin) · prior VTE · **obesity** · CHF · MI · varicose veins · smoking · ↑ viscosity · thoracic outlet (UE DVT) · **type of surgery (TJA)** · **age** · family history\
✗ Wrong options: **hypercholesterolaemia**\
✗ Wrong options: protective

**Anaesthesia** — ✓ **general > spinal/epidural** (conversion spinal → GA ↑ risk) · AAOS: neuraxial anaesthesia not shown to ↓ VTE (↓ blood loss)

**Warfarin paradox** — rapid INR rise on unopposed warfarin → early **protein C fall** (t½ 6–8 h) before factor II (48–120 h) → transient hypercoagulability

**TXA** — ✓ **no ↑ DVT** (oral or IV)

**Paediatric** — rising (obesity, OCP, smoking) · ✓ **biggest risk = prior VTE** (+ thrombophilia, strong family history)\
✗ Wrong options: nephrotic, obesity, OCP, SCI (lesser)

**Caprini index** — ✓ **DVT risk assessment in trauma** · low/moderate/high/highest · **major LE fracture = highest** · **score ≥3 → prophylaxis** **(TO)**

## 3 Presentation

**DVT** — calf pain · palpable cord · pitting swelling · **50% with classic signs have no DVT** · **50% venogram-positive have normal exam** · ~20% clinically silent after TJA

**PE** — most **asymptomatic** · pleuritic pain · dyspnoea · tachypnoea · saddle embolus → death · post-vertebroplasty PE may be delayed (PMMA, fat, marrow)

**Timing** — most symptomatic VTE **after discharge, 2–6 wk** · TKA: ↑ DVT but ↓ symptomatic PE vs THA · no prophylaxis TKA → symptomatic PE ~8% · DVT ~60% without chemoprophylaxis\
✗ Wrong options: within 2 wk

**Classification** — acute <28 days · chronic >28 days · recurrent

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**DVT** — **duplex compression US** ("non-compressible vein", ~95% sens/spec) = **gold standard** (Orthobullets) · ✓ **venography = gold standard** (TO) — read the options · US best **non-invasive** test (97% proximal, ~75% calf) · symptomatic calf pain 2 wk post-TKA → ✓ **venous US** next **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · CTA · aspiration

**Screening tests (TO)** — **D-dimer**: sensitive, not specific (inflammation, malignancy, post-op) — rules out · **digital photoplethysmography** ~100% sensitive **(TO)**

**Routine post-op duplex screening** — ✓ **strong AAOS recommendation against** in asymptomatic elective arthroplasty, even with prior DVT / estrogen (cost ~$35,000 per extra DVT)

![Duplex US — non-compressible vein](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9056-dvt-us.jpg)

**PE** — ✓ **CT pulmonary angiography = gold standard** · **V/Q scan** if **contrast allergy** · CXR usually normal early (oligaemia, prominent hilum) → late wedge/plate atelectasis

![CTPA — saddle embolus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9056-ctpa-showing-saddle-embolus.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria — PE

| Finding | PE |
|---|---|
| **PaO₂ < 80 mmHg** | ✓ |
| **PaCO₂ < 35 mmHg** (hypocapnia) | ✓ · (✗ Wrong options: **PaCO₂ > 35**)|
| **A–a gradient > 20 mmHg** | ✓ |
| Tachypnoea with SpO₂ 99% | ✓ (pulse oximetry unreliable — hyperventilation) |
| Tachycardia + new **RBBB** | ✓ |
| **ECG** | **Sinus tachycardia (commonest**, 8–69%) · RAD · RBBB · **S1Q3T3 (McGinn-White)** 10–20% |
| Acid–base | **Respiratory alkalosis** (✗ Wrong options: hyperchloraemic metabolic acidosis) |

**Wells criteria** — clinical probability for DVT and PE **(TO)**

## 5 Management

### Prophylaxis guidelines (AAOS, ACCP, mechanical, combined) (TO)

**Risk stratification (ACCP 2008)** — ✓ **THA, TKA, hip fracture, major trauma, SCI = HIGH risk** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: "THR moderate risk"

**AAOS approach** — ✓ **pre-op assessment of VTE + bleeding risk** · weigh **bleeding vs PE**

| AAOS elevated risk of **major bleeding** | AAOS elevated risk of **PE** |
|---|---|
| **Bleeding disorder** (haemophilia, vWD) · **recent GI bleed** · **recent haemorrhagic stroke** · active liver disease | **Hypercoagulable state** (e.g. ✓ **protein C deficiency**) · **previous documented PE** |

### Operative criteria — who gets prophylaxis

| Procedure | Prophylaxis |
|---|---|
| **THA / TKA** | ✓ **All patients** — mechanical + pharmacological (moderate strength) · no optimal agent specified |
| Upper extremity · arthroscopy · isolated fracture knee and below (standard risk) | Not recommended |
| Foot & ankle | Not shown to ↓ VTE (20,043 patients) |
| **Spine** | No consensus · early mobilisation + IPC · chemo for long surgery, multilevel thoracolumbar, anterior TL · risk **epidural haematoma** |
| Shoulder arthroplasty | Mobilisation, mechanical, regional · LMWH only if ↑ risk |
| **Trauma** | ✓ **Combined mechanical + chemical → ↓ DVT AND PE** · standard: IPC + enoxaparin |
| **Liver failure / bleeding risk (INR 1.36)** | ✓ **Mechanical only** (✗ Wrong options: warfarin · none) |
| Contraindication to anticoagulation | IVC filter considered (✗ Wrong options: routine) |

**AAOS guideline points** — ✓ **mechanical compressive devices post-op** · aspirin post-op · ✓ **stop antiplatelets pre-op** · (✗ Wrong options: stockings alone sufficient · routine duplex · specific INR (2011)) · regional anaesthesia suggested · **early mobilisation (consensus)** · AAOS/ASH 2019: IPC > stockings, no IVC filter, aspirin or anticoagulant, DOAC > LMWH (conditional)\
✗ Wrong options: start aspirin 1 wk pre-op

**Shown to ↓ VTE after TKA** — early mobilisation · aspirin · coumadin · pneumatic boots \
✗ Wrong options: **neuraxial anaesthesia**

**IPC mechanism** — ✓ **↑ venous blood flow/return** · ↑ endothelial **fibrinolysis** · ↓ venous **compliance** + stasis\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ bleeding time · endothelial injury

**2012 ACCP convergence with AAOS** — ✓ **↑ aspirin monotherapy prescribing** · ACCP agents ≥ **10–14 days**: ASA, LMWH, fondaparinux, apixaban, dabigatran, rivaroxaban, LDUH, VKA · dual mechanical + chemo inpatient

**Compliance** — almost all receive prophylaxis but often not ACCP-compliant · LMWH compliant more than warfarin (THA 63 vs 33%) · VTE ↑ costs in- and post-discharge · most acute peri-op pulmonary events = **fat embolism / cardiac**

| Agent (TO) | Dose / start | Duration | Notes / reversal |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Aspirin** | 81 mg BID (favoured) or 160–325 mg · within 12–24 h | TKA 10–14 d · THA 28–35 d | AAOS 2007 + ACCP 2012 ✓ · **SIGN: not as sole agent** · platelets (no antidote) |
| **LMWH (enoxaparin)** | 30 mg BID (US) / 40 mg OD · 12–24 h | 10–14 d · THA up to 35 d | "Gold standard" (TO) · no aPTT monitoring · ↓ HIT, ↓ osteoporosis vs UFH |
| UFH | — | — | Monitor **aPTT** · replaced by LMWH |
| **Warfarin (VKA)** | INR **2–3, target 2.5** (ACCP) · **AAOS 2007: INR ≤ 2** | Up to 35 d | Vit K, PCC, FFP |
| **Fondaparinux** | **2.5 mg OD** | — | Anti-Xa · ✓ **no routine monitoring** |
| **Rivaroxaban** | **10 mg OD** · 6–10 h (TO) / 12–24 h (TO EPCAT table) | THA 35 d · TKA 14 d | Oral Xa · not for **hip fracture** · RECORD 1–4 · **andexanet alfa** · 20 mg = treatment dose |
| **Apixaban** | **2.5 mg BID** · 12–24 h | THA 35 d (32–38) · TKA 12 d (10–14) | Oral Xa · not for hip fracture · ADVANCE · **least bleeding** · **andexanet alfa** (✗ Wrong options: protamine) |
| **Dabigatran** | **220 mg OD** (half dose 1–4 h post-op; 110 mg if >75 or renal) | THA 28–35 d · TKA 10 d | **Direct thrombin (IIa) inhibitor** · RE-MODEL, RE-NOVATE · ✓ **idarucizumab** |

**Step-down (EPCAT II) (TO)** — ✓ **rivaroxaban 10 mg × 5 days → aspirin 81 mg** for 30 more days (THA) / 9 days (TKA) · non-inferior, less bleeding **(TO)**

**Switching rules (TO)** — do not **overlap LMWH + DOAC** · give first DOAC dose **when next LMWH dose due** (24 h after 40 mg OD; 12 h after 30 mg BID) · LMWH → aspirin day 4–6 · do not step up aspirin → rivaroxaban 20 mg · CrCl <30 → adjust/avoid · epidural: hold LMWH 12 h before catheter removal, next dose ≥4 h after **(TO)**

```
Elective THA / TKA
├─ All → MECHANICAL (IPC) + EARLY MOBILISATION
├─ Standard risk → ASPIRIN or ANTICOAGULANT (individualised)
├─ ↑ PE risk (prior PE, hypercoagulable) → PHARMACOLOGICAL ± MECHANICAL
└─ ↑ Bleeding risk (bleeding disorder, liver disease, recent GI bleed / haemorrhagic stroke) → MECHANICAL ONLY
       └─ anticoagulation contraindicated + high PE risk → CONSIDER IVC FILTER
```

### Treatment of VTE

**Isolated calf thrombus <5 cm** — rarely needs treatment → **serial US**

**Anticoagulation** — ~**3 months after DVT** · ~**12 months after PE**

**Other** — **early mobilisation** (dislodgement risk < further clot risk) · graduated compression hose → may prevent post-thrombotic syndrome · thrombolysis / thrombectomy controversial

**Suspected intra-op PE in trauma** — damage control: convert definitive (IMN) to provisional (ex-fix), get helical CT

## 6 Complications

**DVT** — PE · **post-thrombotic syndrome 43% at 2 yr** (30% mild, 10% moderate, 3% severe) · **recurrence 25%**

**PE** — sudden death · obstructive shock · PEA · arrhythmias · pulmonary hypertension · cor pulmonale · hypoxaemia · R→L shunt

**Mortality** — **6%** DVT and **12%** PE within 1 month · ~10% of PE die in first hour, 30% later from recurrence

**Bleeding on dabigatran** — ✓ **idarucizumab** **(TO)**

## DOAC comparison (TO)

| | **Rivaroxaban** | **Apixaban** | **Dabigatran** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Target | Xa | Xa | **IIa (thrombin)** |
| Dose | 10 mg OD | **2.5 mg BID** | **220 mg OD** |
| Reversal | Andexanet alfa | Andexanet alfa | **Idarucizumab** |
| Hip fracture | No | No | — |

## How it's asked

**"THA step — greatest V/Q mismatch risk?"** → **cement pressurisation of femoral canal**

**"NOT part of Virchow's triad?"** → **neither thrombocytopenia nor platelet dysfunction**

**"Molecule released in dissection causing DVT?"** → **thromboplastin**\
✗ Wrong options: prothrombin · factor XI

**"What is fibrin?"** → **product of the coagulation cascade**\
✗ Wrong options: fibrinolytic product · platelet cytoskeleton

**"Variant cofactor resistant to activated protein C?"** → **factor V Leiden**\
✗ Wrong options: ATIII · protein S

**"Variant cofactor can't be inactivated → ↑ thrombin?"** → **factor V Leiden thrombophilia**\
✗ Wrong options: Glanzmann · vWD

**"Leiden thrombophilia best described?"** → **abnormal factor V resists aPC inactivation → ↑ clotting**

**"Deficiency raising VTE risk?"** → **protein S**\
✗ Wrong options: fibrinogen, VII, VIII, prothrombin

**"Caprini index used for?"** → **DVT risk in trauma** **(TO)**

**"Pre-op EPO before scoliosis fusion — risk?"** → **thrombotic event**

**"THA patient — risk factors (BMI 34, age 70, cholesterol, son FVL)?"** → **type of surgery, age, BMI**\
✗ Wrong options: hypercholesterolaemia

**"True about VTE risk?"** → **conversion spinal → general ↑ risk**\
✗ Wrong options: MTHFR no impact · TXA ↑ · estrogen protective

**"Biggest paediatric VTE risk factor?"** → **history of VTE**

**"NOT true about VTE prophylaxis?"** → **THR patients are moderate risk** (they're high) **(TO)**

**"Step-down protocol after THA (EPCAT II)?"** → **rivaroxaban 10 mg × 5 d → aspirin 81 mg × 30 d** **(TO)**

**"GI bleed post-TKA on direct thrombin inhibitor — dose + reversal?"** → **220 mg OD; idarucizumab** **(TO)**

**"Twice-daily oral Xa inhibitor after THA — dose, start, reversal?"** → **apixaban 2.5 mg BID, 12–24 h, andexanet alfa** **(TO)**

**"Pneumatic compression devices associated with?"** → **↑ venous blood flow**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ compliance · ↓ bleeding time

**"AAOS recommendation after TKA?"** → **mechanical compressive devices post-op**\
✗ Wrong options: pre-op aspirin · screening duplex · INR 1.5–2.5

**"Elevated bleeding risk EXCEPT?"** → **protein C deficiency** (PE risk)

**"AAOS TJA prophylaxis guideline includes?"** → **pre-op assessment of VTE and bleeding risk**\
✗ Wrong options: GA · routine IVC filter · INR 2–3

**"Liver failure, INR 1.36, TKA — prophylaxis?"** → **mechanical prophylaxis in hospital**

**"Practice change after 2012 ACCP convergence?"** → **↑ aspirin monotherapy**

**"True regarding VTE after hip/knee surgery?"** → **almost all get prophylaxis, often not ACCP-compliant**\
✗ Wrong options: most within 2 wk

**"NOT shown to ↓ VTE after TKA?"** → **neuraxial anaesthesia**

**"Combined mechanical + chemical in trauma?"** → **lower DVT and PE**

**"Post-op duplex screening after THA?"** → **strongly recommended against**

**"Gold standard for DVT post-TKR?"** → **venography** **(TO)**

**"Calf pain + low fever 2 wk post-TKA — next?"** → **venous ultrasonography**

**"Indication for helical chest CT EXCEPT?"** → **PaCO₂ > 35 mmHg**

**"Dyspnoea, tachycardia 3 days post-TKA — likely finding?"** → **S1Q3T3 on ECG**

**"Collapse with JVD days after repeat vertebral augmentation?"** → **pulmonary embolism**

# Ulnar nerve

*Basic Science · 108 / 108*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

**Course** — **medial cord** → anteromedial arm → **pierces medial intermuscular septum** (upper arm) → posteromedial → **cubital tunnel** → motor branches **distal** to tunnel (FCU, FDP ring + small) → hand

**No elbow function** — ✓ transection above elbow → **no elbow limitation** (elbow movers = radial, musculocutaneous, median)

### Motor innervation (interossei, adductor pollicis, palmaris brevis) (TO)

| Region | Ulnar supply (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Forearm** ("1½ flexors") | **FCU** + **medial half of FDP** (ring, small) |
| **Hand — thenar** ("1½ muscles") | ✓ **Adductor pollicis** + **deep (medial) head of FPB** |
| Hand — other | ✓ **All interossei (palmar + dorsal)** · **medial 2 lumbricals** · hypothenars |
| **Superficial branch** | ✓ **Palmaris brevis** (only intrinsic) |
| **Deep branch** | Hypothenars (FDM brevis, ADM, ODM) · medial lumbricals · adductor pollicis · interossei · FPB deep head |

| Median supply (TO) | Radial supply (TO) |
|---|---|
| Forearm: pronator teres, PL, FCR, FDS · **AIN**: FPL, pronator quadratus, lateral FDP | Before spiral groove: triceps long + medial · in groove: lateral head, anconeus |
| Hand: APB, opponens pollicis, superficial FPB, **lateral 2 lumbricals** | Below groove: BR, ECRL (brachialis sensory only) · **PIN**: supinator + APL, EPB, ECRB, EIP, EDC, EDM, ECU |

## 3 Presentation

**Cubital tunnel syndrome** — hand clumsiness · intrinsic wasting · ✓ **Froment sign** · ↓ sensation ulnar small finger · **Tinel at medial elbow**

![Cubital tunnel — intrinsic wasting](../../MCQ/attachments/q4602-a-cubitaltunnel.jpg)

### Sensory & motor distribution after injury

**Martin-Gruber anastomosis** — median → ulnar **motor** connections in forearm · distal ulnar transection with **intact finger abduction/adduction** · sensation: ✓ **dorsal 1st web + index tip intact, small finger tip lost** (sensory loss still ulnar)

## How it's asked

**"Ulnar nerve transected excising medial intermuscular septum — elbow limitation?"** → **none**

**"Distal ulnar transection, intact abduction/adduction — sensation?"** → **dorsal 1st web + index tip intact, small finger tip lost**

**"Nerve to adductor pollicis?"** → **ulnar** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: median · both

**"Nerve to palmar interossei?"** → **ulnar** **(TO)**

**"Intrinsic supplied by superficial branch of ulnar nerve?"** → **palmaris brevis** **(TO)**\
✗ Wrong options: adductor pollicis, FDM brevis, ADM

# Brain Anatomy

*Basic Science · TO notes*

## 1 Basics

### General

**Weight** — **1300–1400 g** · \~**10 glial cells per neuron** (TO)

**Neurons vs glia** — neurons **cannot multiply**, glia can → **gliomas** among commonest brain tumours · lost neurons after head injury **not replaced** (TO)

**Coverings** — dura · arachnoid · pia (as for the cord) (TO)

**Parts** — cerebrum (2 hemispheres) · cerebellum · brainstem (midbrain, pons, medulla) · diencephalon (thalamus, hypothalamus, epithalamus + pineal) (TO)

**Contralateral control** — motor fibres **decussate in the medulla** (TO)

### Ventricles & CSF pathway

**CSF pathway (TO)** — lateral ventricle → **foramen of Monro** → 3rd ventricle → **aqueduct of Sylvius** → 4th ventricle → **foramina of Luschka + Magendie** → subarachnoid space + spinal central canal → absorbed by **arachnoid villi / granulations**

**Third ventricle** — between the thalami, above hypothalamus · **fourth ventricle** — between pons / medulla and cerebellum (TO)

**Obstruction anywhere** — **hydrocephalus** (TO)

![Ventricles and CSF pathway](../../TO notes/attachments/TOn-notes-anatomy-of-the-brain-aug-2026-p02-f004.jpg)

### Cerebrum

**Cortex** — superficial **grey matter**; white matter deep · grey grows faster → **gyri, sulci, fissures** (TO)

**Hemisphere connection** — **corpus callosum** · separated by **longitudinal fissure** (TO)

| Lobe | Position | Main functions (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| **Frontal (largest)** | Anterior; **central sulcus** separates from parietal | **Motor cortex**, personality, **Broca** (motor speech), planning, emotion |
| **Parietal** | Top of head | **Sensory cortex** (touch, pressure, temperature, pain), shapes on skin, language interpretation |
| **Occipital** | Back | **Visual cortex** |
| **Temporal** | Sides, above ears | **Auditory cortex**, hearing + **smell**; posterior part → speech comprehension |

![Lobes of the cerebrum](../../TO notes/attachments/TOn-notes-anatomy-of-the-brain-aug-2026-p02-f005.jpg)

### Cerebellum

**Size** — 2nd largest; posterior cranial fossa; **\~10% of brain weight but \~50% of neurons** (TO)

**Parts** — **vermis** (posture, sitting upright) · anterior + posterior lobes (subconscious movement control) · **flocculonodular lobe** (equilibrium, balance) · folia · white matter = **arbor vitae** (TO)

**Lesion** — **staccato ("scanning") speech** · incoordination, imbalance (TO)

| Peduncle | Connects cerebellum to (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Superior** | **Midbrain** |
| **Middle** | **Pons** |
| **Inferior** | **Medulla** |

### Brainstem

| Part | Key facts (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Midbrain** (\~2.5 cm) | Tectum: **superior colliculi = vision**, **inferior colliculi = hearing** · tegmentum (eye movements) · **substantia nigra** (dopamine; lesion = **Parkinsonism**) · crus cerebri (corticospinal + corticonuclear) · arousal, sleep–wake, temperature |
| **Pons** | Links cerebellum to brain · respiration, swallowing, **pontine micturition centre**, hearing, balance, eye movement |
| **Medulla** (\~3 cm) | Pons → foramen magnum · **motor decussation** · cardiovascular, respiratory rhythm, vasomotor centres · swallow, cough, sneeze, vomit · lower cranial nerve nuclei |

**Brainstem lesion** — drowsiness / **coma** (arousal centres) (TO)

### Diencephalon

**Thalamus** — \~**80%** of diencephalon · **gateway to cortex**: relays nearly all sensory input (TO)

**Hypothalamus** — autonomic, temperature, water balance, food intake, **circadian rhythm**, hypothalamo-pituitary axis, HR + BP (with medulla) (TO)

**Pineal** — **melatonin in darkness** → sleep · melatonin used for disturbed sleep after head injury (TO)

| Thalamic nucleus | Relays (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Medial geniculate** | **Hearing** |
| **Lateral geniculate** | **Vision** |
| **Ventral posterior** | Taste + body sensation (touch, pain, cold) |

### Circumventricular organs

**Definition** — periventricular structures **without a blood–brain barrier** ("windows of the brain"), sensory + secretory (TO)

**"Medimix SOAP" (TO)** — **M**edian eminence · **S**ubfornical organ · **S**ubcommissural organ · **O**VLT · **A**rea postrema · **P**ineal · **P**osterior pituitary

## 2 Development

### Embryology

**Neural ectoderm (TO)** — **neural tube** (brain + cord) · **neural crest** (most of PNS) · **placodes** (sensory ganglia, anterior pituitary, inner ear)

| Phase (TO) | Event | Defects |
|---|---|---|
| **Dorsal induction (neurulation)** | Neural plate **\~4.5–5 wk** → groove → folds fuse → tube; secondary neurulation → distal cord | Primary: **anencephaly, cephalocele, Chiari** · secondary: **spinal dysraphism** (spina bifida, meningomyelocele) |
| **Ventral induction** | Rostral expansion → brain vesicles (proximal **2/3 brain**, caudal **1/3 cord**; lumen = ventricles) | **Holoprosencephaly**, hydrocephalus, **aqueductal stenosis**, agenesis of corpus callosum, **Dandy-Walker** |
| **Proliferation, migration, histogenesis** | Neurons multiply + migrate to cortex | **Lissencephaly**, schizencephaly, polymicrogyria, heterotopia, micro / megalencephaly, focal cortical dysplasia |
| Myelination | Myelin sheaths form | — |

| Primary vesicle | Secondary vesicle | Adult derivative (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| **Prosencephalon** | Telencephalon · diencephalon | Cerebral hemispheres · thalamus, hypothalamus, epithalamus |
| **Mesencephalon** | Mesencephalon | Midbrain |
| **Rhombencephalon** | **Metencephalon** · **myelencephalon** | **Pons + cerebellum** · **medulla** |

![Primary and secondary brain vesicles](../../TO notes/attachments/TOn-notes-anatomy-of-the-brain-aug-2026-p05-f013.jpg)

## 3 Surface Anatomy

### Cortical surface anatomy

**Central sulcus joins Sylvian fissure** — only **\~2%** (separated by **subcentral gyrus** in 98%) (TO)

**Parietal lobules** — superior + inferior, split by **intraparietal sulcus** · **supramarginal gyrus (area 40)** caps Sylvian fissure · **angular gyrus (area 39)** caps superior temporal sulcus (TO)

**Pars opercularis, triangularis, orbitalis** — **inferior FRONTAL gyrus** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: inferior temporal gyrus

### Brodmann areas

| Area | Function (TO) |
|---|---|
| **4** (precentral) | **Primary motor** |
| 6 | Premotor / supplementary motor |
| 8 | **Frontal eye field** |
| **3, 1, 2** (postcentral) | **Primary somatosensory** |
| **44, 45** (inferior frontal) | **Broca** — **non-fluent**, comprehension preserved |
| **22, 39, 40** | **Wernicke** — **fluent**, comprehension lost |
| **41, 42** (Heschl) | **Primary auditory** |
| **17** | **Primary visual** |

### Craniometric points

**Pterion** — frontal + parietal + temporal + **greater wing of sphenoid** · 2 fingerbreadths above zygomatic arch, thumb's breadth behind frontal process of zygoma · **thinnest skull** · overlies **Sylvian fissure** (TO)

**Pterion fracture** — **middle meningeal artery** → **extradural haematoma** (TO)

**Others** — asterion · lambda · **bregma (coronal + sagittal sutures)** · stephanion · glabella · opisthion · inion (TO)

**Superior sagittal sinus** — usually **slightly right** of the sagittal suture (TO)

![Craniometric points](../../TO notes/attachments/TOn-notes-anatomy-of-the-brain-aug-2026-p06-f016.jpg)

### Homunculus

**Large areas** — hand (thumb), face, lips, tongue, eyes · small: trunk, legs (TO)

**Layout** — **leg + genitals medial** (interhemispheric) · **face + hand lateral** → explains deficits by arterial territory (TO)

## X vs Y

| | Broca | Wernicke |
|---|---|---|
| Areas | **44, 45** | **22, 39, 40** |
| Lobe | Inferior frontal | Posterior superior temporal |
| Speech | **Non-fluent** | **Fluent** |
| Comprehension | **Preserved** | **Lost** |

| | Medial geniculate | Lateral geniculate |
|---|---|---|
| Relays | **Hearing** | **Vision** |
| Midbrain partner | Inferior colliculus | Superior colliculus |

## How it's asked

**"Cortical surface anatomy — false statement?"** → **"pars opercularis, triangularis, orbitalis lie in inferior temporal gyrus"** (TO)

**"Central sulcus joins Sylvian fissure in?"** → **\~2%** (TO)

**"Cerebellar peduncles connect to?"** → **superior–midbrain · middle–pons · inferior–medulla** (TO)

**"Metencephalon gives rise to?"** → **pons + cerebellum** (TO)

**"Circumventricular organs?"** → **"Medimix SOAP"** (TO)

**"Pterion fracture → ?"** → **middle meningeal artery → extradural haematoma** (TO)

**"Substantia nigra lesion?"** → **Parkinsonism** (TO)
